## ⚡ Quick Command Reference (cheatsheet)

> The commands you reach for most, in one place. Fuller context lives in **Local Development** and the relevant Architecture sections. (Dev shell is PowerShell + Git Bash on Windows; these run in either.)

**Run locally**
```bash
composer run dev          # all-in-one: artisan serve + queue + vite  → http://localhost:8000
php artisan serve         # or run the halves separately
npm run dev
```

**Database** (dev = XAMPP **MariaDB** on `127.0.0.1:3307`, db `retab-stores`)
```bash
php artisan migrate
php artisan migrate:fresh --seed     # reset + reseed everything
php artisan migrate:status           # inspect (⚠️ db:show ERRORS on MariaDB — cosmetic; use this)
php artisan db:table <name>
```

**Quality gates** (run before shipping — these are exactly what CI checks)
```bash
php artisan test                     # PHP suite (in-memory sqlite)
php artisan test --filter=<Name>     # a single test or class
npx tsc --noEmit                     # the REAL type gate (Vite/esbuild strips types without checking)
npm run lint                         # eslint . --fix  (lints the WHOLE tree, not per-file)
npm run test:js                      # vitest (front-end helpers, e.g. consent logic)
./vendor/bin/pint                    # PHP formatter — 🔴 RUN BEFORE PUSHING (CI fails on drift since 2026-08-03)
npm run format                       # prettier over resources/ — 🔴 same, CI fails on drift
npm run build                        # client + SSR bundle (build:ssr is now an alias — see 2026-07-27)
```
> **Formatting is now a hard gate.** `lint.yml` runs `pint --test` + `format:check` + `lint:check` (all read-only), so
> unformatted code **fails CI**. Run `./vendor/bin/pint && npm run format` before pushing. _Previously these ran in
> WRITE mode with the auto-commit step commented out — CI reformatted its own checkout, discarded it, and reported
> green, so the repo drifted to 67 Pint + 92 Prettier files out of style before anyone noticed (fixed 2026-08-03)._

**End-to-end** (Playwright — see Infrastructure → "E2E suite")
```bash
npm run build && npm run test:e2e              # boots a freshly-seeded throwaway sqlite on :8100
npm run test:e2e:report                        # open the last HTML report
QA_BASE_URL=https://<site> npm run test:e2e    # run the specs against a deployment instead
```

**Catalogue + media**
```bash
php artisan catalog:import-zid                 # import the Zid CSV (--fresh wipes first · --no-images to skip)
php artisan catalog:import-images <group> --dir=<folder> --dry-run   # <group> = key in database/data/image-maps.php
php artisan catalog:clean-slugs                # dry-run: junk slugs → clean Arabic + build the 301 map
php artisan catalog:clean-slugs --apply        # ⚠️ run on PROD at launch (this is what creates the redirects)
php artisan media:variants                     # backfill responsive WebP variants (--force to regenerate)
php artisan media:push --dry-run               # preview the local→R2 media upload (no creds needed)
php artisan media:push                         # copy every stored file to R2 (idempotent; verifies at the end)
```

**Integrations readiness** (payments + shipping + email — see the command's own docblock)
```bash
php artisan integrations:check                 # per provider: env vars, webhook URL to register, live auth probe
php artisan integrations:check --offline       # env presence only (no network calls)
php artisan mail:test <address>                # send one REAL email through Resend (delivered@resend.dev = safe simulator)
```

**Git** (full workflow under Git & Deploy)
```bash
git ship <branch>        # local merge → main → dual-push both repos → re-sync branch. NEVER the GitHub merge button.
git remote -v            # must show 1 fetch + 2 push URLs on origin (dual-push)
```

**Deploy / prod** (Railway)
```bash
php artisan migrate --force                             # also runs the baseline-data migration
php artisan db:seed --class=AdminUserSeeder --force     # once, AFTER setting ADMIN_EMAIL/ADMIN_PASSWORD env
php artisan optimize                                    # config/route/view/event cache (optimize:clear to undo)
php artisan queue:work --queue=default,bulk             # 🔴 REQUIRED as its own service. The --queue list is NOT optional:
                                                        # without it the worker never serves `bulk` and campaigns never send.
```
Set **`APP_URL`** to the real domain — webhook, payment-return, and email URLs are all built from it.

**Dev logins** (seeded in local only): admin `admin@retab.com.sa` / `password` · editor `editor@retab.com.sa` / `password`

## Project Overview

**Store:** Retab Stores
**Industry:** E-commerce — premium dates (تمور) retail in Saudi Arabia
**Type:** Online store (storefront + admin/back-office), currently **migrating off [Zid](https://zid.sa)** to a custom Laravel build
**Stack:** Laravel 12 + Inertia.js v3 + React 19 + TypeScript + TailwindCSS v4 (official `laravel/react-starter-kit` v1.0.1; Inertia upgraded v2→v3 on 2026-06-28)
**DB:** Local dev runs on **XAMPP MariaDB 10.4.32** (`127.0.0.1:3307`, db `retab-stores`) — note: MariaDB, _not_ MySQL (XAMPP bundles MariaDB under the "mysql" folder/service name, hence the confusion). **MySQL 8 planned for production** (switch via `DB_CONNECTION`); dev↔prod engine parity is a pending decision (see Foundation).
**Mailer:** _TBD_ — likely Resend (mirroring Sky Amman), decision pending
**Architecture:** Single-service monolith (Laravel serves everything via Inertia). SSR build-time scaffolding present; runtime SSR not yet wired (see Build Progress).
**Hosting:** _TBD_ — likely Railway (FrankenPHP) behind Cloudflare, mirroring Sky Amman
**Branch:** `main`
**Repos (dual-push):** `origin` fetches from `https://github.com/mercenary19961/retab-stores.git`; a **second push URL** for the client's repo will be added so one `git push` updates both (see Git & Deploy).
**Languages:** Arabic (primary — Saudi market, **RTL-first**) + English (secondary). ⚠️ This flips Sky Amman's default (which is English-default).
**Theme:** _Decision pending._ The starter kit ships a light/dark **appearance toggle** (`useAppearance` hook + `settings/appearance` page) — keep, or lock to a single branded theme like Sky Amman did. Decide before heavy UI work.

## Reference Project — Sky Amman

We previously shipped **Sky Amman** (real-estate site) on essentially this same stack. It lives at `c:\Users\sabba\Desktop\projects\sky-amman\`. **When in doubt about a pattern** (locale middleware, `HandleInertiaRequests` shared props, Turnstile wiring, admin layout, Site Content CRUD, security headers, Cloudflare `trustProxies` CIDRs, SSR sidecar, CI), read the corresponding file in Sky Amman first — those patterns are proven.

Sky Amman's `CLAUDE.md` is large — read targeted sections via Grep / `offset`+`limit`, never the whole file.

### ⚠️ Differences from Sky Amman — don't blindly copy

This project is the **official Laravel React starter kit**, scaffolded fresh; Sky Amman was a hand-rolled setup. Concrete divergences to respect:

- **Inertia is now v3 here too** (`inertiajs/inertia-laravel` v3.1.0, `@inertiajs/react` ^3.0) — _formerly a divergence (scaffold shipped v2), resolved by the v2→v3 upgrade on 2026-06-28._ It now **matches Sky Amman (v3)**, so Sky Amman's Inertia patterns **port cleanly**: the 419/CSRF auto-reload listener is `router.on('httpException', …)` (v2's `invalid` was renamed), and the `resolvePageComponent` `.default` unwrap is **already applied** in `app.tsx` + `ssr.jsx` (v3 dropped auto-unwrap of the page module's default export). Note retab uses lowercase `./pages/` paths, not Sky Amman's `./Pages/`.
- **Lowercase, kebab file layout:** `resources/js/pages/` (not `Pages/`), `resources/js/layouts/`, `resources/js/components/` (+ shadcn-style Radix UI primitives under `components/ui/`), and **modular route files** (`routes/auth.php`, `routes/settings.php`, `routes/web.php`). Path-adjust any Sky Amman snippet accordingly.
- **shadcn/Radix UI is the component base** here (`@radix-ui/*`, `class-variance-authority`, `tailwind-merge`, `tailwindcss-animate`) — Sky Amman hand-built its components.
- **No bilingual/i18n, Turnstile, CMS, or admin** exist yet — those are Sky Amman patterns to **port deliberately**, not assume.

## Background — migrating off Zid

The store is **already live and selling on Zid** (hosted SaaS). We are rebuilding it on Laravel to own the codebase and add custom touches. Implications to keep in mind:

- **Data migration:** product catalogue, categories, customers, and order history likely need exporting from Zid and importing here. Plan an import path (CSV/API export → seeders or a one-off importer) before go-live.
- **Payments are ours now.** Zid bundled checkout + payment. For KSA we'll need a local gateway — **Moyasar / Tap / HyperPay** (mada + cards) and optionally **Tabby / Tamara** (BNPL). This is a **key pending decision** and shapes the orders/payments schema.
- **Feature parity first, enhancements second** — match what the Zid store does before layering "our touch."

## E-commerce Requirements & Decisions (client brief — 2026-06-29)

> Captured from the client brief. **Reference implementation:** `c:\Users\sabba\Desktop\projects\hardrock-ecom-demo\` — a mature Laravel 12 + Inertia **v2** e-commerce backend we built that ALREADY implements OTO shipping, Tamara, coupons, order activities, admin activity-log/undo, optimistic locking, roles (admin/editor), bilingual AR/EN, and notifications. Retab **adapts** these into a **tighter** build (gateway abstractions, thin controllers + service layer, explicit order state machine). Read hardrock-ecom-demo's files for proven patterns before building (e.g. `app/Services/Shipping/*`, `app/Services/Payments/*`).

### Payments — DECIDED
- **Cards via Moyasar** (mada + Visa/MC + Apple Pay + STC Pay) **+ Tamara (BNPL)** **+ bank transfer** (manual transfer to the store's **Al Rajhi** IBAN, **admin-verified** — added 2026-06-30 to match the client's current site). **No COD, no cash** — every order is prepaid (online gateway or verified bank transfer).
- **Store entity + bank** (from client): legal name **شركة مصنع رطاب الوطن للتمور**; Al Rajhi IBAN `SA9780000145608010008130` (acct `145608010008130`). Stored in `settings` (`bank_*`, `legal_name`); the IBAN is a public receiving account (fine to display).
- Both sit behind a `PaymentGateway` interface (mirror hardrock's `ShippingGateway` pattern); Tamara reuses hardrock's `TamaraClient`.
- **Capture model — DECIDED (hybrid per method):**
  - **Cards (Moyasar) = immediate capture** at checkout; **refund** on customer-cancel or admin-reject. Deliberately avoids any Moyasar delayed-capture dependency.
  - **Tamara = authorize at checkout, capture on admin confirmation**; **void** the authorization if cancelled/out-of-stock (Tamara supports auth→capture natively).
  - Both expose `authorize / capture / void / refund` via the `PaymentGateway` interface; the order flow calls the right one per method + state.
  - SLA: admin confirms within ~24–48h (Tamara auth expires); dashboard flags orders nearing auth-expiry.
  - ⚠️ Trade-off: rejected **card** orders incur a real refund (fee + "money in/out" UX) — acceptable while out-of-stock rejections stay rare (depends on inventory accuracy / أسماك sync); revisit (cards→delayed capture, if Moyasar supports it) if they become common.

### Shipping — DECIDED
- **OTO (Tryoto) aggregator** for fulfillment (reuse hardrock `ShippingGateway`→`OtoGateway`/`OtoClient` + `OtoWebhookController`). **GCC countries only. No Aramex, no DHL** (filter out). _As of 2026-08-31 the exclusions are DATA, not code — seeded rows in `shipping_carriers`, managed by the client at `/admin/shipping`. ⚠️ Until then only Aramex was actually filtered; DHL was being quoted. See Architecture → Shipping carriers portal._
- **Customer pays a SINGLE FIXED flat shipping price** across all GCC, regardless of OTO's actual carrier cost (DECIDED — client accepts absorbing the cross-GCC cost difference). One configurable number in admin.
- OTO **does** return live per-carrier rates via `checkOTODeliveryFee` (POST origin/destination/weight; auth = refresh-token→access-token, cached). Used **internally** for cost/carrier selection — NOT shown as the customer price.

### Products & Inventory — DECIDED
- **Inventory tracked by quantity (units in stock).** Weight is **descriptive only** — shown in the product title/description per product, NOT a structured field (shipping is flat, so weight never drives cost).
- ~~**No variants (DECIDED)** — each sellable item = one product = one SMACC SKU (keeps the Excel import 1:1).~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-23 by product OPTIONS** (`product_options`): one product carries a list of sellable sizes/packaging (250g / 500g / 1kg / كرتون) and the customer picks one. Weight prices auto-scale from the smallest size and are individually overridable; a **box** is priced by hand and there is at most one per product. Stock stays **one shared pool on the product**, and each option consumes `stock_units` per purchase (a box of twelve deducts 12) — so the "unit-quantity on the product" half of the old decision still holds, it is just no longer one row per pack size. The chosen option is snapshotted onto the order line (label + price + `stock_units`) so a receipt survives a later rename. ⚠️ `product_options.smacc_sku` is the seam for per-option SMACC sync and is **unused today**, so the daily Excel import is still keyed on the product's own `smacc_sku`.

### Coupons — DECIDED (+ one POSTPONED)
- Admin-controlled coupon system (reuse + extend hardrock's `Coupon`).
- **Review reward — BUILT 2026-07-29** (a second earn-a-coupon path alongside the 5-purchases loyalty reward): a verified buyer who posts a review **with a comment** earns a **one-time** percentage coupon (admin picks 10% or 20%), account-bound, valid 30 days. `App\Services\ReviewRewardService` mirrors `LoyaltyService`; **off by default** and managed on `/admin/discounts` (`review_reward_enabled` / `review_reward_percent` settings). Once-ever per customer is enforced by the `loyalty_rewards` unique key `(user_id, type='review', threshold)` — ⚠️ `threshold` is meaningless for this type but the key needs a value, so it's stored as `0`. Issuing is row-locked + idempotent (safe on the review-submit path). A **post-delivery WhatsApp nudge** (`SendReviewReminder`, dispatched with a 1-day delay from `ShippingService` on delivery) invites the review; eligibility is re-checked **at send time** and `orders.review_reminder_sent_at` stops a repeated "delivered" webhook re-sending it.
- ⏸ **POSTPONED — QR-code coupons redeemable at the PHYSICAL store** (client used to issue a QR after 5 purchases for an in-store discount). Design later: signed **single-use** token, atomic redemption (optimistic locking), validated either by أسماك or a standalone cashier "redeem" web page (decoupled from أسماك). Revisit after core build.

### Order flow (client's required flow)
1. Customer orders → pays (prepaid) → payment confirmed.
2. Admin notified via **email + WhatsApp + admin-panel notification**.
3. Admin/editor checks inventory & issues, then **confirms**:
   - **If unavailable:** button to WhatsApp the customer (apologize + suggest similar products + "back soon"); the event is logged/notified + stored for **analytics** + shown on the dashboard.
   - **If confirmed:** deduct stock → alert OTO to pick up → WhatsApp customer "confirmed, courier coming."
4. **Cancellation:** customer may cancel **only before** admin confirms → notify customer **indirectly** (don't incentivize cancelling).

### Returns & exchanges — policy from current site (to replicate)
Source: retabstore.com policy. **Defect/damage-only** (matches food/perishable norms):
- **Eligibility:** ONLY for product **defects/damage** (عيوب / تمور تالفة). Excludes late receipt, shipping-company faults, and used/tampered products.
- **Window:** request within **3 days** of receiving the product → requires tracking **`delivered_at`** on orders.
- **Channel (current):** **WhatsApp** (returns dept +966 50 384 5356) with **photos** + order number + contact details.
- **Condition:** returned items in good condition with **original labels + packaging**.
- **Shipping fees:** **non-refundable** EXCEPT when the dates arrived **damaged**.
- **Resolution:** after inspection, exchange or **refund within 14 days**.
- **Build implication — returns module:** model as a **separate entity** (not just an order status): `return_requests` (order_id, user_id, status [requested/approved/rejected/exchanged/refunded], reason, resolved_at, admin_notes) + `return_items` (partial returns) + attached **photos** (media). Enforce 3-day window via `delivered_at`; refund via the payment gateway (card→Moyasar, Tamara→Tamara); shipping fee refunded only if damaged. Customer files in-account (with photos) → admin reviews → resolves; mirror WhatsApp touchpoints.
- **Also implies:** a **content/legal pages** capability (bilingual) for the policy page itself + about/contact (port Sky Amman's Site Content CRUD pattern).

### WhatsApp + Marketing — DECIDED: direct Meta Cloud API
- Use the **direct Meta Cloud API** (no BSP) — lowest cost, full control, under the same Meta Business Manager as the client's ads. We build the integration in Laravel (send + webhooks for delivery/read status).
- **Message types:** order confirmation / apology+suggest / "courier coming" / loyalty = **Utility** templates; monthly offers = **Marketing** templates (**opt-in required**). All business-initiated messages outside a customer's 24h reply window MUST be **Meta-approved templates** (free text only inside the 24h window).
- **Admin "marketing" section = two parts:** (a) **template manager** — compose + submit to Meta + track approval status (or manage templates in Meta Business Manager initially); (b) **campaign sender** — pick an approved template → fill variables → send to the opt-in segment → track delivery via webhook.
- **🔭 FUTURE IMPROVEMENT (deferred — auto-sync template approval status).** Today the template's "Meta approval status" (`draft/pending/approved/rejected` on `whatsapp_templates`) is a **manual mirror** of the real status in Meta Business Manager — templates are authored + approved THERE, and a human flips the flag here to match (the send guardrail `WhatsappTemplate::isApproved()` + the sender dropdown trust this flag but never verify it against Meta). If the manual step becomes error-prone, wire **Meta's Template Management API** to (a) **submit** templates to Meta straight from the `/admin/marketing` screen, and (b) subscribe to the **`message_template_status_update` webhook** so the local status flips to Approved/Rejected automatically. That removes the human sync step entirely (and the drift risk of a locally-"approved" template Meta actually rejects → per-message `failed` sends). Scope it when asked.
- **Guardrails:** marketing only to opted-in contacts; plan for Meta's template-approval lag; respect Meta's daily conversation limits + quality rating.
- Synergy: **click-to-WhatsApp ads** from the client's Meta campaigns start chats + open the free 24h window.

### Loyalty — DECIDED
- **5 confirmed purchases → 15% discount coupon**; customer notified + incentivized (WhatsApp).
- Confirmed buyers → added to the **monthly WhatsApp marketing list** (must capture opt-in consent at/after purchase).

### Customers, accounts & engagement — DECIDED
- **Account creation at checkout** (if not signed in) via any of: **WhatsApp** (phone OTP via WhatsApp), **Google** (OAuth — reuse hardrock's Socialite), **email** (email+password). Minimal at signup; user **completes profile later** (name, city, phone, email…).
- **Identity model:** `users` has **nullable** name/email/phone/password (a user may have only a phone, or only Google); a `social_accounts` table links providers to one account; `otp_verifications` for phone/WhatsApp sign-in.
- **OTP delivery = WhatsApp only (DECIDED)** — no SMS provider. Phone verification is via WhatsApp OTP; sign-in methods = **WhatsApp / Google / email**.
- **Reviews** (+ helpful votes; bilingual) and **wishlist** (per user) — reuse hardrock patterns.
- **Loyalty/points tracker:** count of confirmed purchases on the user; shown on the **customer's account** (progress to reward) AND the **admin** customer view; drives the 5→15% coupon. Count-based for v1, extensible to points-per-spend.

### POS integration (SMACC / سماك) — NO API; daily Excel export→import (DECIDED)
- Physical store's POS is **SMACC** ("سماك" = how S-M-A-C-C is pronounced), by **Arab Sea Information Systems** (`arabsea.com`) — major KSA accounting/POS/inventory/e-invoicing platform.
- ⚠️ **Arab Sea confirmed: NO API integration.** Only path = **export SMACC inventory to Excel**, then **import into our admin** to re-baseline website stock.
- **Inventory model (DECIDED):** **SMACC = ledger of record**; website = **daily-synced mirror** + soft reservations for in-flight online orders. Website stock is **advisory** between imports; the **per-order admin confirm-step is the real backstop** (stale stock → at worst an apology, never a bad fulfillment).
- 🔑 **Critical rule:** online sales MUST be reflected in SMACC before each export, or every import "refills" already-sold units → upward drift + overselling. Website generates a **daily "online sales" report** so the admin batch-adjusts SMACC, then exports.
- **Daily routine:** (1) website → today's online sales; (2) admin reflects them in SMACC; (3) export SMACC → Excel; (4) import to admin (diff/preview → apply).
- **Import design:** match rows by stable key → products store **`smacc_sku`/barcode**; **diff/preview** before apply (flag unmatched rows); transactional; logged + undoable (reuse `ActivityLogService`); idempotent.
- **Mitigate the daily-commitment risk:** "stock last synced: Xh ago" indicator + banner/alert if no import in >24h; low-stock buffer; one-drag import. **Ask Arab Sea:** can SMACC schedule/email the export? → enables auto-ingest of the file (still not live sync).
- Knock-on: staler mirror → more out-of-stock rejections → more captured-card refunds (see Payments). Daily import keeps that low.

### Admin panel & i18n — DECIDED
- **Storefront AR-first** then EN; **admin panel EN-first** then AR. Admin panel supports **dark + light themes**.

### Open decisions (blocking schema design)
1. ~~Payment capture model~~ — ✅ DECIDED (hybrid: cards immediate-capture, Tamara auth→capture; see Payments).
2. ~~POS / inventory~~ — ✅ SMACC by Arab Sea, **no API** → daily Excel export→import; SMACC = ledger of record, website = daily mirror + admin confirm-step backstop (see POS section).
3. ~~WhatsApp route~~ — ✅ direct Meta Cloud API (see WhatsApp section).
4. ~~Shipping flat rate~~ — ✅ single flat GCC rate.

## Tech Stack (as scaffolded — real versions)

- **Backend:** Laravel `v12.62.0`, PHP `^8.2` (starter kit pinned to v1.0.1 because latest requires PHP 8.3), PHPUnit `11.5`, Ziggy `v2.6.3`
- **Inertia:** `inertiajs/inertia-laravel` `v3.1.0` + `@inertiajs/react` `^3.0` (installed `3.5.0`) — upgraded from v2 on 2026-06-28 (see Build Progress → Construction phase). Requires only PHP 8.2, so the starter-kit-v1.0.1 / PHP 8.2 pin was never a blocker for Inertia itself.
- **Frontend:** React `19`, TypeScript `^5.7`, Vite `^6`, Tailwind CSS `^4` (`@tailwindcss/vite`), `lucide-react`, Radix UI primitives, `clsx`/`tailwind-merge`/`cva`
- **What the starter kit already gives us:** auth flow (login / register / forgot+reset password / email verify / confirm password), `dashboard`, `settings` (profile / password / appearance), an `AppLayout`, and a `welcome` landing page. **SSR entry `resources/js/ssr.jsx` + `npm run build:ssr` exist** (runtime not enabled yet).
- **`.npmrc`** holds `production=false` — **do not delete it.** The user's shell has a global `NODE_ENV=production` that otherwise makes `npm install` silently drop devDependencies. (The starter kit dodges the worst of it by listing build deps under `dependencies`, but keep the guard.)

---

## Architecture Patterns (MUST FOLLOW)

These are the conventions we're adopting (most proven in Sky Amman). Where a pattern isn't built yet, it's marked **(to build / port)**.

### Inertia.js
- Controllers use `Inertia::render('page/name', [...props])` — no API routes for the storefront/admin UI.
- Mutations use `router.post/put/delete()` with `preserveScroll`, `onSuccess`, `onFinish`.
- Forms with files: native `FormData` + `forceFormData: true`.
- Routing is server-driven via `routes/*.php` — no client-side router.
- Auth state via `usePage().props.auth.user` (shared by middleware).
- Flash messages: server `->with('success', …)` → client reads `usePage().props.flash`.

### Bilingual (Arabic-first) + RTL — **(BUILT — foundation + storefront; deferred items below)**
- **Locale = server session, default AR** (single source of truth, no localStorage): `SetLocale` middleware → `app()->setLocale()`, `LocaleController` + `POST /locale/{locale}` (whitelisted `ar|en`), `locale` shared via `HandleInertiaRequests`. `<html dir>` is set from the locale in `app.blade.php` for correct RTL on **first paint** (no flash).
- **Client i18n = `react-i18next` + `i18next`** (`resources/js/i18n/{index,ar,en}.ts`, `lng`/`fallbackLng` = `ar`) + `LanguageContext` (`useLanguage()` → toggle does `fetch POST /locale/{lang}`, updates `document.dir`/`lang`; seeded from the shared session locale). Wired into **both** `app.tsx` + `ssr.jsx`; provider wraps the whole app.
- **DB content (`_ar`/`_en` columns):** controllers ship **BOTH** locales; the client picks via **`useLocalized()`** (`resources/js/lib/localize.ts`, AR fallback) so the toggle is **instant** — no server round-trip (mirrors Sky Amman). Stored content always wins over i18n fallbacks.
- RTL: use CSS **logical properties** (`text-start`, `ms-*`/`me-*`, `ps-*`/`pe-*`), not `text-left`/`flex-end`.
- **Server-side strings (BUILT):** flash + error messages go through `__('messages.*')` (`lang/{ar,en}/messages.php`, storefront **and** admin); Laravel validation/auth/passwords localized (`lang/ar/*` mirroring published `lang/en/*`, incl. AR field-name `attributes`). Resolved in the request's session locale. ⚠️ Server-rendered strings (these + product names) reflect the locale **at request time** — they follow on the next request, not the instant client toggle.
- **Brand web font (BUILT 2026-07-02, switched to Thmanyah 2026-07-18):** the storefront brand face is **Thmanyah Sans** (the ثمانية typeface), **self-hosted** WOFF2 replacing the old bunny.net **Tajawal**. Same wiring mechanism: override the Tailwind v4 token under RTL — `html[dir='rtl'] { --font-sans: 'Thmanyah Sans', ... }` + `--font-heading: 'Thmanyah Sans'` in `app.css` (the `font-sans`/`font-heading` utilities resolve the tokens, so the override cascades everywhere; no per-component changes). WOFF2 files live in `resources/fonts/` (weights Regular/Medium/Bold/Black), declared via `@font-face` with **`font-display: swap`**, fingerprinted by Vite (hashed URLs, `Vite::asset()`-resolvable — the manifest keys them by `resources/fonts/<file>.woff2`), and the Regular+Bold weights are **preloaded** in `app.blade.php` (`<link rel="preload" as="font" crossorigin>` — the `crossorigin` is mandatory or the browser double-downloads). Instrument Sans stays the Latin/LTR + admin default. ⚠️ **Licence caveats (thmanyah Font Licence, KSA-governed, Arabic version prevails):** commercial website use is expressly permitted, BUT (a) the font may **not be modified/subsetted** (so no glyph-subsetting to shrink it — ship as-is, ~78 KB/weight); (b) only WOFF2 is committed, **never the OTF** (desktop-installable); (c) the licence restricts making the raw file freely downloadable ("compiled/packaged/obfuscated" web-embedding only) — the Vite hashing + same-origin serving + planned Cloudflare hotlink protection cover this; (d) committing the WOFF2 to the (private) GitHub repos is fine as "your own projects" but would be redistribution if either repo goes public; (e) **written confirmation from ask@thmanyah.com is still recommended** before launch (the licence says no exception is valid unless issued by them in writing). Licence + font files: `Downloads/Thmanyah-Font-Family` (not in repo). **SSR locale (BUILT):** `ssr.jsx` calls `i18n.changeLanguage(page.props.locale)` before render.
- **Deferred:** **admin panel i18n** only (admin UI is EN/LTR-pinned by design — server flash already localizes).

### File Storage — **(BUILT — media layer + product images)**
- **Single upload path = `App\Support\Media`** (`storeImage()` / `url()` / `delete()`): validates extension AND MIME, randomizes the filename (UUID), stores public on the **`media` disk**. **Never write public files directly** — always go through `Media` (same rule as `Setting::set()`).
- **Storage provider — DECIDED: Cloudflare R2** (S3-compatible, **zero egress fees**, fits the planned Cloudflare stack). Wired as the `r2` disk in `config/filesystems.php` via Laravel's **s3 driver** (`region: auto`, path-style endpoint) — no R2-specific code, so swapping to real S3 is an env change. The active disk is chosen by **`MEDIA_DISK`** env: defaults to the local **`public`** disk in dev (no creds; run `php artisan storage:link` once), set **`MEDIA_DISK=r2`** in production (Railway's FS is ephemeral — uploads MUST NOT live on the container disk).
  - Prod env (Railway only, never commit): `MEDIA_DISK=r2`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `R2_BUCKET`, `R2_ENDPOINT` (`https://<account-id>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com`), `R2_URL` (public bucket / custom domain).
  - **Cost:** R2 free tier = **10 GB storage + 1M writes + 10M reads per month**, then **$0.015/GB-month**, **$4.50/M writes**, **$0.36/M reads**, **$0 egress**. A dates catalogue (hundreds of images, ~sub-GB) sits in the free tier indefinitely; egress being free is the reason it beats S3 for an image-heavy storefront.
- **Product images BUILT** on this: `product_images` (path/alt/sort/is_primary) managed via `Admin\ProductImageController` (upload/delete/set-primary on dedicated multipart-POST endpoints — separate from the product text form to avoid PUT-multipart issues); first image auto-primary; deleting the primary promotes the next. Storefront catalogue/product + admin list/edit render via `Media::url()` with a 🌴 placeholder fallback.
- **Return-request photos BUILT** on this layer (`returns/{order_id}/…`, shown in the admin review). **Still pending:** review photos. Seeded/structural images (logo, etc.) still go in git under `public/`.
- **Responsive WebP variants BUILT (2026-07-22).** Every image stored through `Media` auto-generates small WebP variants so listings load a few KB instead of full-res originals (measured: a 92 KB JPEG → **15 KB** `card`, **3 KB** `thumb`). `intervention/image` v3 (GD driver, needs PHP GD **with WebP**). Sizes in **`config/media.php`** (`thumb` 150px / `card` 500px / `detail` 1400px + a `variants_enabled` master switch + `image_driver`). **`App\Support\ImageVariants`** does `generate()` (best-effort, never throws — a resize failure must not break an upload), `variantPath()` (convention: `products/1/uuid.jpg` → `products/1/uuid-card.webp`, **derived not DB-stored**), and `delete()`. Wired into `Media`: `storeImage()` generates on store, `url($path, $variant)` swaps to the variant (falls back to the original when disabled/unknown so nothing 404s), `delete()` removes variants too. Served per context: catalogue/home card → `card`, search typeahead → `thumb`, product page → `detail` (full original kept for a future click-to-zoom). Backfill for pre-pipeline images: **`php artisan media:variants`** (idempotent, `--force`) — already run for all 163 product images. ⚠️ Convention-based URLs mean a variant must exist, so **run the backfill once after enabling** (or new images 404); the `variants_enabled` switch is the safety valve, and **production PHP must ship GD/Imagick with WebP** (Railpack/FrankenPHP does). Reusable pattern captured in memory (`image-variant-pipeline`) for other storefronts.
- **Production cutover to R2 — `php artisan media:push` (BUILT 2026-08-08).** _Why it exists:_ production ran with `MEDIA_DISK` unset, so uploads landed on the **container filesystem and were wiped by every deploy**. The prod DB still held all 163 `product_images` rows, so all 87 products rendered the 🌴 placeholder. _The lucky part:_ prod's `product_images.path` values are **byte-identical to the local dev paths** (verified by diffing all 163), because both came from the same import, so the fix is a **pure file upload with zero DB changes** — no re-import, no re-download from Zid's dying CDN. `media:push {--from=public} {--to=r2} {--force} {--dry-run}` streams every stored file across using the already-installed `league/flysystem-aws-s3-v3`, preserving keys exactly. Design notes: a **write/read/delete probe** runs first (a wrong key or missing bucket fails once, not 652 times — and note the `r2` disk sets `throw => false`, so a failure is a falsy return, not an exception); **dotfiles are skipped** (the public disk carries Laravel's own `.gitignore`); uploads set `ContentType` + a one-year immutable `CacheControl` (safe because filenames are UUIDs); it's **idempotent** so an interrupted transfer resumes; and it finishes by **verifying every `ProductImage` row plus its variants resolve on the target**, because "bytes moved" is not the same question as "will the storefront render". ⚠️ **R2 buckets are private by default** — serving needs either the bucket's `r2.dev` public URL (rate-limited, Cloudflare says not for production) or a **custom domain, which requires the domain to sit in the same Cloudflare account as the bucket**. Since the account holding `retab.com.sa` is not obtainable (see memory `retab-domain-dns`), create the bucket in the **new client-owned Cloudflare account** that DNS will eventually move into, launch on `r2.dev`, then swap `R2_URL` to `cdn.retab.com.sa` later — one env var, no data migration. CSP needed no change (`img-src` already allows `https:`). ✅ **DONE 2026-08-09** — bucket `retab-media`, 652 files / 162.3 MB pushed, `MEDIA_DISK=r2` live on the web service (not SSR: the sidecar never touches a disk), all 87 products verified rendering. ✅ **The `r2.dev` swap is DONE (2026-08-15) — `cdn.retab.com.sa` now serves all media.** It had been outstanding and mattered more than the docs implied: verified by curl that r2.dev responses carried **NO `CF-Cache-Status` header at all**, i.e. genuinely zero edge caching, so every image was a round trip to the bucket's **Eastern Europe** region from KSA. On the custom domain the same object returns `cf-cache-status: HIT` with an `Age`, served from the **Amman** edge. Full state in memory `retab-r2-media`. ⚠️ **Test gotcha:** `Storage::fake()` swaps the disk *instance* but leaves `config('filesystems.disks.*')` untouched, so a guard that inspects config sees the real (empty) R2 settings; and **each `expectsOutputToContain()` is matched against a separate, ordered write call**, so two substrings from one summary line can never both pass.

### Change Log + Revert — **(BUILT 2026-07-06 — v2 semantics)**
- **What:** staff audit history + per-entry **Revert** at `/admin/change-log`, backed by the existing `activity_logs` table + v2 columns `old_data`/`new_data`/`label`/`reverts_log_id` (migration `2026_07_06_100000`). Code: **`App\Services\ChangeLog\ChangeLogService`** (+ `RevertResult`); wired into **Products** (created/updated/deleted — revert doubles as the only restore-from-trash path), **Settings** (one entry per save, changed keys only), **Content pages** (update revertable; create audit-only — no delete route exists). **SMACC imports** share the table but keep their bespoke `changes` payload + own undo on the Inventory page (audit-only here).
- **Design — fixes Sky Amman's catalogued failure modes** (sky-amman CLAUDE.md → "ChangeLog / Revert semantics — compound-edit behavior catalog"): (1) **update snapshots are dirty-fields-only**, so reverting an old edit can never clobber newer edits to other fields; (2) **per-field conflict detection** — every field must still hold the entry's `new_data` value or the revert refuses with the diverged field list (also kills out-of-order resurrection); (3) **transactional reverts + honest `RevertResult` reason codes** — never a "success" that did nothing; (4) **reverts are first-class entries** — a revert writes a new semantically-typed row (`updated`/`deleted`/`restored`) linked via `reverts_log_id` and stamps the original ⇒ complete timeline and **redo = revert the mirror entry** (same machinery).
- **Deliberately NOT tracked:** orders/returns/payments (state machines with their own append-only `order_activities` audit — snapshot write-backs must never bypass them) and product images (separate media flow).
- **Usage:** updates pass `$before = $model->attributesToArray()` captured pre-save to `logUpdated()`; each write+log pair runs in `DB::transaction`. Adding a tracked section = call the right `log*` method + add the subject to `REVERTABLE`/`SUBJECT_LABELS` in the service.

### Building a designed section from a Figma composite — **(reusable recipe, distilled 2026-08-10)**

> Four About Us sections were built this way (hero, journey, values, why-us). **Measure the composite; do not eyeball it.** Every client-reported miss on these sections came from a value that was guessed, and every guess that looked right in code turned out wrong on screen. Follow the order below.

1. **Inventory the hand-off first.** Get dimensions and alpha of every file (`getimagesize` + an alpha histogram). Frames so far are all **1440×N**. An accompanying `.zip` has been an **empty 210-byte archive three times** — the real files always arrived loose alongside it, so don't debug the extraction. Filenames have twice contained a **non-breaking space (U+00A0)**, which is why Explorer silently skips them; check with `od -c`.
2. **If an SVG came too, read the geometry out of it** (`<rect>` x/y/width/height/rx, `<path fill>`). It is exact and beats any pixel measurement. But ⚠️ **Figma does not export effects to SVG** — a `fill="black"` rect whose PNG shows a white interior means an inset shadow or blur, and the SVG will also carry **zero `<text>`** because type is outlined, so copy must be rebuilt as live HTML.
3. **If the designer also supplied the bare background plate, diff it against the composite.** This is the highest-leverage trick available: agreement in a card-free region *proves* the plate is genuinely the background (why-us agreed to 0.17/255 in the gutter), and the difference then isolates the glass fill, the watermark, and the type, each measured **at the same pixel so the background gradient cancels**.
   - **Given an overlay asset instead of a plate, invert it: PREDICT the background and diff that.** Composite the supplied watermark over the flat page colour at a candidate placement and compare against the composite's background pixels. This turns "where does it go?" into a single number per hypothesis and settles it outright: contact-info's watermark scored **0.02/255** at natural size at (0,0) against 1.28 stretched, 3.45 mirrored and 3.08 absent, proving the designer had exported the already-rotated layer at frame scale. It also confirms the opacity for free (that asset is pre-faded to 7.9%, so it needs none of its own).
   - ⚠️ **When comparing the render back to the composite, compare a QUANTITY, not an impression.** Tint and coverage are independent: contact-info's watermark matched the design's tint at every size (12.9 vs 13.6 of 255) while covering only 16.5% of the visible background against 20.9%, because a vertically compressed section scales a height-anchored image down. Sweep the size, measure coverage at each step, and pick from the curve.
4. **Find element edges on scan lines chosen to be clear of type.** Generic step-detection locks onto glyphs and watermarks. Borders show up as sharp luminance spikes; sample *inside* a card, away from its text.
5. **Derive type size from ink, and only trust it when two independent dimensions agree.** Compute the size implied by the design's ink **width** and by its ink **height** (`canvas measureText` → `actualBoundingBox*` at 100px in the real font, then scale). ⚠️ They only agree on lines with **no kashida**; everywhere else the width figure is corrupted by the designer's elongation, so **height is the tatweel-invariant one**. In why-us the two no-kashida lines were the only usable anchors, and they proved the card title and body are the *same* size, with weight and colour carrying the hierarchy. Contact-info reached the same conclusion from three anchors (two Latin strings plus one un-elongated Arabic line, agreeing to 0.4px), and once the size was fixed **every one of seven strings matched the design's ink height to within 1px** — that agreement is the signal the whole spec is right, so compute it before writing any CSS.
   - **Ink height barely discriminates WEIGHT (a 5% spread across 400→900). Stroke thickness does.** Take the **median horizontal ink-run length** on scan lines through the middle of the x-height band, on the design and on canvas renders of the candidates. Contact-info's 18 / 6 / 3px stems mapped cleanly onto w900 / w700 / w400, which pinned a hierarchy the sizes alone could not (all three elements are ~the same size).
   - 🔴 **Use the SAME ink threshold on both sides or the comparison lies.** A strict "near the ink colour" test on the composite against canvas's 50% cut under-measured every design stem by ~1px, which pointed at Medium instead of Bold. **Nearest-colour classification** (is this pixel closer to the ink colour or the background colour?) is the 50% criterion and is directly comparable to canvas.
   - **Solve line-height from a two-line ink block:** `blockInkHeight = pitch + ascent(line 1) + descent(line 2)`, with the ascent and descent measured per-string in the real font. Cross-check on a second block — contact-info's two independent blocks agreed at 69.0 and 68.0px, i.e. 1.30.
6. ⚠️ **The composite's copy can be out of date, so decide per field where the value comes from.** Contact-info's email card reads `info@retabstore.com`, the **old Zid domain**, already wrong before the section was built. Anything the client can edit in the admin (here the phone and email, which the footer already reads from settings) must resolve at render time from that same source; only fixed display copy belongs in i18n. Transcribing a composite verbatim ships whatever was stale in it.
7. **Reproduce the constraint, not the numbers.** The values section's `181+376+326+376+181=1440` is exact yet unusable as a grid, because the columns were narrower than the text. What had to be preserved was the **element centres**; for a 2-column grid that is `(column + gap) / 2`, so the pair can be traded off freely. Ask what the number is *for* before copying it.
8. 🔴 **Compress the height. Every frame so far has been ~1136px, which cannot fit a laptop viewport once the 114px navbar is counted, and the client has flagged it.** Budget the frame first: if padding and gaps are a large share (values: 531 of 1136), halve them and scale type lightly; if the content dominates (why-us), the content has to scale too. Target **≲800px** and make every `clamp()` **cap at 1440px** so the section stops growing on larger monitors instead of scaling itself back out of view.
9. **Verify by measuring the render, not by looking at it.** Screenshot the section and run the *same* pixel analysis over it as over the composite, so the two are compared on identical terms. Then check the things a screenshot hides: section height and `scrollWidth > clientWidth` overflow **at 1920/1440/1280/1024/768/390 in both locales**.
10. **Recurring traps, all of which have already cost time here:**
   - **`text-end` is inverted in both locales at once.** It resolves against reading direction, so it means left in AR and right in EN. A heading almost always wants **`text-start`**.
   - **Physical vs logical positioning is a real decision.** Anything whose crop is baked to bleed off a specific corner (watermarks) must stay `left-0`/`top-0` in both locales; anything that should follow the reader uses `ps-`/`text-start`. Moving an absolutely positioned element into flow silently makes it direction-relative.
   - **Never use a negative z-index for a background image.** `position: relative` does not create a stacking context, so `-z-10` paints the image behind the nearest *ancestor* background and the section renders bare. Plain `absolute` first in DOM order plus a `relative` content wrapper is enough.
   - **Give any dark section a fallback `bg-` colour** sampled from its plate. White and gold type over a failed background image is an invisible section.
   - **Symmetric padding centres the em box, not the ink**, and Thmanyah reserves a large ascent for diacritics these labels never use. Correct with an asymmetric `--nudge`, measured by the **alpha-weighted pixel centroid** (the ink bounding box understates the error by more than half), and scope it per direction — Arabic needed 0.200em where English needed 0.065em.
   - **Kashida breaks string matching:** `'قيــــــمنا'.includes('قيم')` is **false**. Don't select or assert on elongated copy; and a fixed-width pill needs `whitespace-nowrap`, or a wrapping label grows the pill and spills past its rounded ends.
   - 🔴 **`ltr:` resolves against the element's OWN direction, not the page's.** Putting `dir="ltr"` on an element to stop RTL reordering a phone number or an email **also switches on every `ltr:` utility on that element** — in contact-info that silently gave two of the four cards the smaller English type size while the page was still in Arabic. Keep the `ltr:` size on the block and move the `dir` down onto the value's own `<span>`.
   - ⚠️ **A colour-keyed ink test inside a rounded rect measures the CORNER ANTIALIASING as ink.** With `rx=73` the naive scan reported each card's title as spanning the full 591px card width. Test membership of the rounded-rect interior (inset a few px) before classifying a pixel, the same way border scan lines have to dodge glyphs.
   - **One over-long English line does not just look wrong, it resizes the section.** With `auto-rows-fr` a single wrap grows *every* card in step: contact-info went from 797px to 910px on one 545px line in a 520px box. Budget the inner width, shorten the EN copy, and give English its own slightly smaller size so the copy has headroom rather than a 25px margin.
   - **PHP caches `stat()`**: after writing a file you already stat'd, `filesize()` returns the old value. `clearstatcache(true, $path)`, or compare md5.
11. **Ship copy as an array of objects in i18n** (`items: [{ key, … }]`), so a fifth card is a copy change rather than a code change, and **convert plates to WebP** (a 1.27 MB gradient PNG became **8 KB** at q84, verified at mean 0.724/255 with no pixel off by more than 3 — PNG is simply bad at smooth gradients). Copy already-lossy WebP **byte-for-byte** rather than re-encoding it.

### Coming Soon + Demand Capture — **(BUILT 2026-07-22)**
- **Purpose:** turn incomplete draft products (the Zid import left 22 hidden as `is_active=false`) into a demand funnel instead of dead weight. A draft can be surfaced on the store as **"Coming Soon"** — visible + describable but **not buyable**; customers tap **"I want this"** to register interest, which notifies staff for WhatsApp follow-up.
- **State model — the key rule:** `products.is_coming_soon` (boolean) is a *display* flag, NEVER a buy flag. **Buyability stays gated purely by `is_active` everywhere** (cart/checkout unchanged — `CartController` still `Product::where('is_active', true)->findOrFail(...)`, so a coming-soon item 404s the cart). `Product::isComingSoon()` = `!is_active && is_coming_soon`. `scopeVisibleOnStore()` = `is_active OR is_coming_soon` and is what the **storefront catalogue/detail** query on (replacing the old bare `is_active` filter); best-sellers / new-arrivals / Offers stay `is_active`-only. `card()` ships a `coming_soon` bool → the card shows a "Coming Soon" badge + "I want this →" instead of price; the product page hides price/Tamara/add-to-cart and renders the request control.
- **Request flow:** `POST /products/{slug}/request` → `ProductRequestController` (storefront, `throttle:5,1`, **guests allowed**). Guests supply a phone + pass **Turnstile** (reuses `TurnstileVerifier` + the `turnstileSiteKey` shared prop, mirrors `OtpAuthController`); signed-in users are one click (their account carries the contact). `abort_unless($product->isComingSoon())` so the button can't be replayed against live/plain-hidden products. One open (unhandled) request per customer per product is enforced (collapses double-taps). Rows land in **`product_requests`** (`product_id`, nullable `user_id`, `phone`, `ip`, `handled_at`); staff are notified via `ProductRequestedNotification` (database channel → bell, like `ReturnRequestedNotification`).
- **Admin surfaces:** (1) **Drafts workspace** — the Products list gained a `status` filter (`active`/`draft`/`coming_soon`) with a live draft count + per-row **completeness badges** (`needs_price`/`needs_image`/`needs_description`) so staff see what each draft still needs; the product form has the **"Show on store as Coming Soon (request only)"** toggle. (2) **Dashboard** — a `draftsToComplete` action-queue item (count of `is_active=false`) → `/admin/products?status=draft`. (3) **Dedicated page** `/admin/product-requests` (`ProductRequestController@index` + `markHandled`, gated by the **new `product_requests` permission** section in `App\Support\Permission` — view/manage; ⚠️ existing editors won't have it until an admin grants it, admins bypass) — list of requests (product, account/guest phone, when) filterable open/handled + a sidebar entry.
- **Descriptions:** the 22 inactive drafts were given draft bilingual descriptions (client refines); price/photo for the zero-price / photo-less ones remain client-supplied. Only-empty fields were filled (the importer's `name_en`/`description_*` guards keep them safe on re-import).
- **Known minor:** a coming-soon product's `Product` JSON-LD still emits `price: 0.00` (transient; suppress the offer block if Google flags it).

### Admin UI conventions — **(BUILT 2026-07-28/29 — use these, don't re-roll them)**
The admin panel got a consistency pass; these are now the shared primitives. **Reach for them before hand-rolling a badge, a toggle, or a flash message.**
- **Status tags = `components/status-pill.tsx` (the look) + `components/admin/status-badge.tsx` (the registry)** — rebuilt 2026-08-20, see the log entry for the reasoning. 🔑 **Tones are named for what the reader should DO, never for a colour:** `attention` (a decision or verification is owed by staff — the only loud one, and it maps to the dashboard's action queue), `active` (in flight / live), `done`, `stopped`, `idle`. **Colour lives in the ICON, not the fill** — every tone shares one faint surface so a table reads calm, and `attention` is the single exception that also tints. **A page never picks an appearance:** `<StatusBadge domain="order" value={…} />` looks up tone + icon + i18n prefix from the one registry, which covers `order · payment · return · coupon · discount · changeLog · template · inquiry`. Add a value **there**, not on the page (an unknown value degrades to `idle`, no icon, label still rendered). Use the raw `<StatusPill tone icon>` only for one-off booleans (published/hidden, guest, unread). Dark-palette only, no `dark:` variants — the admin shell is hardcoded `dark` and nothing outside it renders a pill. `data-status-tone` is on every pill, so browser checks and E2E select on that rather than a colour class.
- **Panel chrome = `resources/js/lib/admin-ui.ts`** — `CARD` (the panel surface, `rounded-xl`, caller adds padding) and `THEAD` (the table header row). 🔴 **`text-start`, NEVER `text-left`, anywhere in the admin.** The panel flips to `dir="rtl"` in Arabic, so a physical `left` pins a header to the opposite side from its own column's data — which is how all 17 tables looked in Arabic until 2026-08-20.
- **Filter chips = `components/admin/filter-chips.tsx`** — the status filter row above a list (`options`/`value`/`onChange`, null = "All", optional per-option `count`). Three pages had each hand-rolled it. ⚠️ Deliberately `rounded-full` where status pills are `rounded-md`: **round means "press me", square means "read me"**, and they sit inches apart. The active chip is brand teal, never white — on a dark panel white outshouts the amber status pills and puts a filter selection above work that needs doing.
- **Resizable columns:** saved widths are **clamped to the column's constraints on hydration** (`use-resizable-columns.ts`). Before that, raising a `minWidth` had no effect on anyone who had already dragged that column narrower. ⚠️ When a column-width bug appears only on your own account, check `ui_preferences.tableWidths` before treating it as a shipped default.
- **One-click state flips = `components/admin/status-toggle.tsx`** — a `StatusPill` that doubles as a toggle button (product visibility, coupon active), fires `router[method](url)` with `preserveScroll/preserveState`, shows a busy state, controller back-redirects to refresh the row. **No confirm step** (the flip is trivially reversible). ⚠️ When blocked it uses **`aria-disabled`, not the native `disabled`**, so the button stays hoverable and the `hint` tooltip can still explain *why*.
- **Flash = overlay toasts (`components/admin/admin-toasts.tsx`)**, not an in-flow banner (the old banner shifted the page on every save). Pinned top inline-end (top-left in RTL), success auto-dismisses after 4 s (hover pauses), **errors linger until closed**, stack capped at 4. ⚠️ **Gotcha:** the effect keys on a **per-response `flash.id` nonce** added in `HandleInertiaRequests` — without it, two consecutive actions flashing the *same* string wouldn't re-fire (an identical value doesn't change a value-compared effect dep), so the second toast silently never appeared. Any new flash surface must keep that nonce.
- **List pagination = `Controller::perPage($request, $default)`** (`app/Http/Controllers/Controller.php`) + `components/admin/pagination.tsx`. Page size comes from `?per_page=N` **whitelisted against `PER_PAGE_OPTIONS` [10,25,50,100]** (guards arbitrary/huge page sizes); a caller default outside the list is merged in by the UI as an extra option. Wired on products / orders / customers / client-reviews / product-requests.
- **Per-user view preferences:** the products card⇄table toggle persists in **`localStorage`** (`admin.products.view`) — a pure display preference, deliberately not a server round-trip. Column widths still use the server-side `ui_preferences.tableWidths`.
- **Media viewing:** `components/image-lightbox.tsx` (full-screen viewer) + `components/store/product-gallery.tsx` (storefront hover-zoom) are shared; the admin product image opens the same lightbox for zoom/swap. Thumbnails must request the **`card`** variant, not the original (see File Storage → variants).
- **Password fields = `components/password-input.tsx`** (BUILT 2026-08-08) — one show/hide-toggle input used by both the storefront login and the admin. Deliberately a **thin wrapper that owns only the `type` attribute** and spreads the rest through, so it works **uncontrolled** (login submits raw DOM values through Inertia's `<Form>` to stay immune to password-manager autofill desync — making it controlled-only would reintroduce that bug) *and* controlled (`useForm`). ⚠️ **Labels are props, not looked up inside**: a plain `useTranslation()` in a component rendered inside the admin panel resolves against the **storefront** i18n instance (see `i18n/use-admin-t.ts`), so each caller passes strings from its own instance. RTL-safe via `end-0`/`pe-10`, and the button is `type="button"` so it can never submit the form.
- **Own-account password change = `components/admin/change-password-form.tsx`** (BUILT 2026-08-08). Posts to the **shared `password.update` route**, not an admin-specific endpoint, so there is exactly one place a password changes; `back()` lands the caller wherever it submitted from. Mounted twice: inline on `/admin/users` for **your own row only** (`selected.id === auth.user.id` — `current_password` is by definition nobody else's to supply, so an admin cannot set an editor's password here) **and** as a modal behind the top-bar key icon, because 🔑 **`/admin/users` is `admin`-only middleware, so editors could never reach a Staff-page-only section.** The route gained `throttle:10,1,password-update` (a `current_password` rule is a credential-guessing oracle for a stolen session) — note the **bucket prefix**, without which it would share one counter with every other throttled route.

### Notifications & alerts (staff) — **(bell + WhatsApp BUILT; email + live bell added 2026-07-30)**
- **Three triggers, three channels.** New order (`CheckoutController`), return filed (`ReturnService`), coming-soon product requested (`ProductRequestController`). Each does `Notification::send(User::staff()->get(), …)` **plus** a separate WhatsApp fan-out (the first two only) to the phone list in `config('services.whatsapp.admin_recipients')`.
- **Channels per notification:** `database` (the bell) + `mail`. ⚠️ **Mail is conditional per recipient** — `via()` returns `['database']` alone when `$notifiable->email` is null, because `users.email` is **nullable** under the OTP identity model and the mail transport throws on an empty address.
- **`viaConnections(): ['database' => 'sync']`** on all three: the bell row is written **in-request** (instant, and it keeps working even with no queue worker), while only the email goes to the queue — off the customer's checkout request. Framework support is real (`NotificationSender` honours `viaConnections`/`viaQueues`).
- **Admin emails are pre-rendered ENGLISH literals, not `__()`** — deliberate. The admin's panel language lives in their browser (localStorage + the `admin_locale` cookie), so a notification triggered by a **customer's** request has no way to know it, and a queued notification would otherwise capture the customer's AR session locale. The bell payload stays structured/client-rendered for exactly the same reason.
- **The bell is live without websockets:** `router.poll(45s, { only: ['notifications'] }, { keepAlive: false })` — an Inertia **partial** reload, so the server recomputes only the `notifications` closure (two indexed queries) and scroll/state are preserved for free; a hidden tab is throttled to one poll in ten ticks. Deliberately polling, not broadcasting — no Reverb/Pusher service to run. Plus a tab-title unread badge and an optional chime (WebAudio, no asset to ship; muted per browser under `admin.notifications.sound`).
  - ⚠️ **Gotcha — Inertia owns `<title>`.** It rewrites the title on hydration and on every navigation, so a badge written once (even one frame late via `requestAnimationFrame`) is silently dropped. The fix is a **`MutationObserver` on `document.head`** that re-applies the prefix whenever anything touches it, with an equality guard so our own write doesn't loop the observer.
- **WhatsApp transport is QUEUED** — `App\Jobs\SendWhatsappMessage` (`tries = 3`, backoff 30/120/600s). The ledger row is written synchronously as `queued` (so the admin log shows the attempt immediately) and only the Meta call is deferred; the job flips it to sent/failed and is idempotent (a retry after a landed send no-ops). Before this, every transactional send was an **inline HTTP call per recipient inside the customer's request** — seconds of checkout latency, and a transient failure lost the alert to a log line.
  - ⚠️ **Params are passed to the job, never read back off the row** — the ledger deliberately **redacts** sensitive params (OTP codes), so the stored payload is not a faithful send payload.
  - ⚠️ **OTP sends inline** (`dispatch(..., queue: false)`): the customer is staring at the code field, and it keeps plaintext codes out of the `jobs` table.
  - ⚠️ **The job rethrows to trigger retries, EXCEPT under the `sync` driver** (`$this->job?->getConnectionName()`), where the exception would surface inside the caller's request. A Meta outage must never break a checkout — that guarantee predates the queue and is preserved.
- 🔴 **DEPLOY PREREQUISITE — a queue worker MUST run in production** (`php artisan queue:work --queue=default,bulk`, a second Railway service off the same image). ⚠️ **The `--queue` list is load-bearing** — see Architecture → Queue isolation; a worker started without it serves only `default`, so every marketing campaign would sit in the table forever. `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database`, so without a worker the jobs table just fills: admin **emails**, **customer order emails** (receipt / confirmed / shipped) and **WhatsApp messages** would never send (the bell still works — that's what `viaConnections` buys). This is **not new** with these changes: `SendReviewReminder` (the 1-day-delayed review nudge) and `SendWhatsappCampaign` already depended on it silently.
- **History page — `/admin/notifications`** (BUILT 2026-07-30): the bell only carries the latest 10, so everything older was unreachable. Paginated own-notifications list with read-state + type filters, reached via **"View all"** in the dropdown (no sidebar entry — notifications belong to the navbar bell). Rows reuse the bell's open route, so clicking still marks read + redirects to the subject.
  - **Payload→text rendering is shared**: `components/admin/notification-content.tsx` (`useNotificationText()` + `iconFor()` + `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`), imported by BOTH the bell and the page, so a new notification type can't render in one and not the other. A hook rather than plain functions only so callers don't pass `t` around.
  - ⚠️ **Gotcha — the page paginator is called `entries`, NOT `notifications`.** `notifications` is the **shared bell prop** (`{unread, items}`); a page prop of the same name silently overrides it, and the bell's `items.filter(...)` would then throw on this very page. Pinned by `NotificationBellTest::test_history_does_not_shadow_the_shared_bell_prop`.
  - Type filter uses a JSON-path where on the structured payload (`where('data->type', …)`, whitelisted so it's never raw input) → `json_unquote(json_extract(...))`. **Verified on SQLite (tests) AND MariaDB 10.4 (dev)**; MySQL 8 (prod) is strictly better.
- **Mail provider = Resend (WIRED 2026-07-30).** `resend/resend-php` installed (the framework's `ResendTransport` needs it for `Resend::client()`); `config/mail.php` already had the `resend` mailer and `config/services.php` the `RESEND_KEY` binding, so no config changes were needed. Verified with a real send to Resend's simulator (`delivered@resend.dev`).
  - **The client's key is SEND-ONLY (restricted)** — good practice, but it means it can't read any other endpoint. `GET /domains` answers **401 `restricted_api_key`**, which `App\Support\ResendProbe` treats as **OK** (an invalid key answers **400 `validation_error`** instead, and a full-access key answers 200 with each domain's verification status — three cleanly distinguishable states). Reported by `integrations:check` alongside the payment providers.
  - 🔴 **Remaining step: verify `retabstore.com` in Resend** (add the domain, paste DKIM/SPF into Cloudflare DNS). Confirmed empirically — sending from `no-reply@retabstore.com` today fails with *"The retabstore.com domain is not verified"*. **Until then the only accepted from-address is `onboarding@resend.dev`**, which is what `.env` uses.
  - **Dev deliberately stays on `MAIL_MAILER=log`.** The seeded dev staff use fake `@retab.com.sa` addresses, so pointing dev at Resend would bounce them off a brand-new account and dent its sending reputation. `php artisan mail:test <address>` sends through the **resend** mailer explicitly (`--mailer=` to override), so a real send never requires flipping the global default. Production sets `MAIL_MAILER=resend`.
  - ⚠️ Mail is **queued** (see `viaConnections` above), so nothing sends without the worker.

### Queue isolation — **(BUILT 2026-08-20)**
- **Two queues, split by Meta's message category.** `marketing` sends go to **`bulk`**; everything else (order confirmations, receipts, shipping notices, OTP fallbacks, the review nudge, admin alerts) stays on the default queue. Names live in **`App\Support\Queues`**.
- 🔑 **Why:** a campaign dispatches **one `SendWhatsappMessage` job per recipient** (`SendWhatsappCampaign` chunks the audience 100 at a time but calls `sendCampaignMessage` per user). On a single shared queue a 5,000-person blast therefore put every order confirmation behind 5,000 marketing messages, processed one at a time by one worker. A customer who paid mid-campaign waited for the whole blast to drain. Nothing errored — it was purely an ordering failure, which is why nothing caught it.
- **Keyed off the existing `category` argument, not a new flag.** `'marketing'` vs `'utility'` is Meta's own distinction and already governs opt-in rules and template approval, so the queue choice cannot drift from the thing it describes. The **orchestrator job itself** is on `bulk` too (it walks the entire segment, so leaving it on `default` would block transactional work for the length of the run).
- ⚠️ **Only the exception is named.** Transactional work never calls `onQueue()`, so it lands on whatever `DB_QUEUE` says. Hardcoding `'default'` would silently break if that env var were ever changed — hence the test asserts `$job->queue === null` for transactional, not a literal string.
- 🔴 **The worker MUST be started with `--queue=default,bulk`.** Laravel re-checks the list in order after every job, so naming the default first is exactly what lets a transactional message jump ahead of a running campaign. **Omitting `--queue` entirely means the worker only serves `default` and bulk jobs never run at all** — the campaign would sit in the table forever with no error anywhere. Reversing the order would reintroduce the original bug.
- **`QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` is unaffected** (the interim no-worker option): `onQueue()` is a no-op under the sync driver, so everything still runs inline.
- **Pinned by `tests/Feature/QueueIsolationTest.php`** (5 tests), including the worker's priority order and that OTP still bypasses the queue entirely. The two isolation tests were **proven to fail** against the old code before being accepted.
- ⏭ **Deliberately NOT moved to bulk:** `SendReviewReminder`. It is one delayed job per delivered order — low volume, and it is a customer-facing nudge rather than a blast. Revisit if delivery volume ever makes it bursty.

### Customer-facing email — **(BUILT 2026-08-03)**
Everything above is STAFF alerting. Customers now get their own mail, and the design decisions are close to the inverse of the staff ones.
- **Three emails, one per lifecycle beat:** `OrderPlacedMail` (the receipt), `OrderConfirmedMail`, `OrderShippedMail` (carrier + tracking) — `app/Mail/`, all extending **`OrderMail`**, all `ShouldQueue`. Sent through **`App\Services\CustomerMailer`** (`orderPlaced/orderConfirmed/orderShipped`), shaped after `WhatsAppService`: call sites state what happened, and every "can we reach this customer?" decision lives in one place. Returns a bool so callers/tests can assert rather than guess.
- **Where they fire:** confirmed + shipped sit beside the existing WhatsApp calls in `Admin\OrderController`. **Placed is split by payment method on purpose** — bank transfer sends at checkout (`CheckoutController`, ⚠️ *after* the `payment_method` update, since the mail reads it to decide on the bank block), while **card/Tamara send on payment confirmation** (`PaymentService::markOrderPaid`, `TamaraService::markAuthorized`), never at checkout: a customer who merely reached the hosted gateway and abandoned it must not get a receipt for an order that was never paid.
  - ⚠️ **Idempotency differs between the two gateways.** `markOrderPaid` already early-returns when the order is paid, so a repeated webhook can't double-send. **`markAuthorized` had no such guard** and re-runs on every delivery attempt — it now captures `$firstAuthorization` (was the order still `pending_payment`?) before the write and only mails on that transition.
- 🔑 **Language: `orders.locale`, snapshotted at checkout** (new column, migration `2026_08_03_100000`). This is the whole reason the base class exists. The mails are **queued**, so at render time `app()->getLocale()` is the WORKER's locale (AR, the app default) — an English shopper would silently get an Arabic receipt. `OrderMail::__construct` pins `$this->locale` from the order; Laravel wraps both the envelope and the view render in `withLocale`, so **the subject line follows too**. Guests have no user record to read a preference from, which is why this is on the order and not on `users`.
  - ⚠️ **Testing gotcha:** asserting the subject by calling `envelope()` **directly** bypasses that wrapper and resolves `__()` in the ambient locale — a correctly-localized subject then looks broken. Use **`assertHasSubject()`**, which goes through the same path a real send does. Pinned in `CustomerEmailTest`.
- **Templates are hand-rolled, not Laravel's markdown theme** (`resources/views/components/mail-layout.blade.php` + `emails/*.blade.php` + a shared `partials/order-summary`): the store is Arabic-first and the whole document has to flip, which means fighting the published theme's LTR-baked CSS on every component. Table layout + inline styles only (Outlook ignores flex/grid, several clients strip `<style>`); `dir` is set on `<html>` **and** the outer table because Gmail drops `<html>` attributes when it re-wraps the body; **no web fonts** (Thmanyah is licensed for embedding on our own origin, not for redistribution through mail clients). Copy lives in `lang/{ar,en}/emails.php`. IBAN / account / tracking numbers are pinned `direction:ltr; unicode-bidi:embed` so RTL reordering can't visually scramble a number mid-copy.
- **Why the receipt matters most for bank transfer:** it's the one method with no gateway receipt of its own, and the IBAN plus the order number the customer must quote as the transfer reference otherwise exist only on a page view that's gone the moment they close the tab.
- ⚠️ **`customer_email` is nullable and that's normal** — phone-only accounts (WhatsApp OTP) and guest checkout only require a phone; those customers are reached over WhatsApp, so no address is a silent no-op, not an error. Falls back to the account address when the order snapshot has none.
- ⚠️ **Guests get no "View your order" button.** The storefront gates `/orders/{number}` on session state, so a link opened days later in another browser would 403. The body carries everything they need instead. _Follow-up if wanted: a signed URL would let guests back in._
- **Deliberately deferred:** return-status emails (the returns flow has four WhatsApp touchpoints and its own status vocabulary — a second template family, not a variant of these).
- **Preview them** by rendering a mailable to HTML in tinker (`(new OrderPlacedMail($order))->render()`); there's no preview route.
- **Not built yet** (ranked next steps): alerts for **Tamara authorizations nearing expiry** (money — auths expire in 24-48h; the dashboard already computes `tamaraExpiring`, so it's a scheduled command away — but it would be the project's FIRST scheduled task ⇒ a `schedule:work` service) and low-stock / sync-gone-stale; **per-recipient routing** (admin phones/emails are env-only today, and every staff member gets everything).

### Shipping carriers portal — **(BUILT 2026-08-31)**
Admin page at **`/admin/shipping`**: which couriers the store will ship with, what each charges today, and who to call when a parcel goes wrong. Code: `App\Models\ShippingCarrier` + `App\Services\Shipping\CarrierDirectory` + `CarrierOption` + `Admin\ShippingController`, page `admin/shipping/index.tsx`, new `shipping` permission section.

- 🔑 **The page is two halves from two different owners, and keeping them apart is the whole design.** **Availability and prices are OTO's**, read live and never stored (a cached price is a wrong price). **The enable switch and the contact card are ours**, stored and never overwritten by a refresh. They are shown as separate things on every card — "OTO is not offering this today" is somebody else's outage to wait out, while "we switched this off" is a decision made on this page, and one green dot covering both would make an outage look like a setting.
- 🔴 **The switch is NOT cosmetic, and that is the one thing to preserve.** `OtoGateway::getDeliveryOptions()` filters every rate quote through `ShippingCarrier::allows()`, which is the single path **both** the manual carrier picker and the automatic cheapest-carrier choice go through (`ShippingService::resolveOption` re-quotes even for a manual pick). So switching a carrier off removes it from the Ship dialog and makes it unselectable by the automatic pick at the same time. Pinned by three tests that were **proven to fail** against a disabled filter.
- 🔑 **It replaces a hardcoded `stripos($carrier, 'aramex')`, which only implemented HALF the recorded decision.** The brief has always said *"GCC only. No Aramex, no DHL"*, but only Aramex was ever filtered — so **DHL was being quoted and could win the automatic cheapest pick**. Both exclusions are now seeded rows (in the migration, so they hold on a fresh production deploy) and the client can change either without a deploy. ⚠️ **Seeding them in the migration is mandatory, not tidiness:** the register fails open, so an empty table would have silently un-banned Aramex the moment this shipped.
- **Key matching:** the register keys on `ShippingCarrier::normalizeKey()` (lowercased, punctuation stripped, noise words like `express`/`logistics` removed) because **OTO names the same courier differently on different endpoints** — the rate check says "SMSA Express" where the listing says "SMSA". Keying on the raw name would let a disabled carrier come back as an unrecognised, and therefore allowed, one. `DHL` and `DHL Express` both resolve to `dhl`. ⚠️ Keep the noise list TIGHT; every word added risks collapsing two real couriers onto one key.
- ⚠️ **Fails OPEN, deliberately and twice over:** an unknown carrier is not in the disabled set so it is allowed, and a cache or DB failure yields an empty set rather than an exception. A surprise courier on a label is recoverable; an order that cannot be shipped is not. The counterweight is the **last-available-carrier guard** in `toggle()` — switching off the final usable carrier would not fail here, it would fail days later as `resolveOption()` throwing "no delivery options" on somebody else's shipment, with nothing linking it back to the click.
- **Two OTO endpoints, tried in order** (`OtoGateway::listServices`): `GET /getDeliveryOptions` is the real catalogue (logo, pickup cut-off, weight allowance, return fee) but is **gated on the account's OTO plan tier**; `POST /checkOTODeliveryFee` is the rate check we already ship with and works on every plan. ⚠️ OTO documents `getDeliveryOptions` as available to *"Starter Package, Scale Package, Enterprise Package, Marketplaces"* — **not Free** — and **this account's tier is UNCONFIRMED** (see the 2026-08-31 correction in the log), so the fallback may well be the live path. Treating the refusal as "no carriers" would have shown the client an empty portal implying their shipping was broken. The page says which kind of data it got, derived from the payload rather than claimed.
- ⚠️ **A refresh must NEVER write `is_enabled` or a contact field on an existing row.** That would be the worst bug this feature could have and it would present as the toggle simply not saving. `sync()` only creates rows and stamps `last_seen_at`; pinned by two tests, also proven to fail against an unsafe version.
- ⚠️ **Nothing here changes anything inside OTO, and the page says so.** OTO's API can list (`dcList`) and activate (`dcConfig`/`dcActivation`) carriers but exposes **no deactivation endpoint**, and activation there means attaching the merchant's own carrier contract with its credentials — an OTO-dashboard job. So "off" means "Retab will not use it", never "OTO switched it off".
- **Seeded contact details** (`CarrierDirectory::SEEDS`: SMSA, Naqel, SPL, iMile, AyMakan, J&T, Zajil) are **public best-effort defaults, applied once on discovery and never re-applied**, so an admin's correction is permanent. 🔴 **Support phone and email are deliberately left blank** — a wrong website is obvious the moment someone clicks it, a wrong support number sends staff to a stranger. The client fills those in from their account manager, and should give the seeded websites a once-over.
- ⚠️ **`{tracking}` in `tracking_url`** builds a parcel link via `ShippingCarrier::trackingLink()`. `oto_url` is blank by default: OTO's per-carrier URLs are account-specific, so the admin pastes the real one rather than us guessing a path that 404s.
- ⚠️ **RTL gotcha found only by looking at the Arabic page:** OTO's delivery-time strings ("1 to 2 Working Days") were bidi-reordered into "to 2 Working Days 1", because the leading digit is neutral so the run laid out right-to-left around it. Fixed with **`dir="auto"`** (not `ltr`) on that span, so a Latin string reads LTR and an Arabic one still reads RTL — and note this is only safe because the element carries no `ltr:` utilities (the 2026-08-11 contact-info trap).
- **Permission:** `shipping.view` = "see what it costs and who to phone"; `shipping.manage` = change what the store ships with. Default editors get view only, the same split as `settings.view`/`settings.edit`; `shipping` is in the `operations` and `manager` presets.
- **UI is TWO MODES, rebuilt 2026-08-31 once the real account turned out to offer 15 carriers.** **Grid** is the default: one compact card per carrier (logo, name, delivery time, price, availability, on/off), **sorted cheapest first**, four across. **Detail** replaces the grid in place when a card is clicked, with a 280px rail listing every carrier so two couriers can be compared closely without going back out; the ✕ or Escape returns to the grid. 🔑 The first build used tall two-column cards, which was fine at two carriers and became ~2,400px of scrolling at sixteen, with no way to compare prices at all.
  - 🔑 **`selectedKey` is one piece of state and `current` is DERIVED from the filtered list**, not stored. So when a filter or search hides the open carrier, the grid returns on its own — there is no effect to keep in sync and no way to be left reading a carrier that is no longer listed.
  - ⚠️ **The card is a `div` with a click handler, not a `button`**, because it contains the enable toggle and a button inside a button is invalid HTML. The carrier NAME is the real button, so keyboard access is intact and the whole-card click is a mouse convenience. The toggle is wrapped in a `stopPropagation` span, or a single press would switch the carrier **and** open it.
  - ⚠️ **The rail is hidden below `lg`**, which is exactly where the admin sidebar stops being a drawer; a 280px rail plus a detail pane does not fit a phone. Below that the detail is full width and the ✕ is the only way back, which is why that button is never optional.
  - **Display fixes on OTO's raw output:** `1to4WorkingDays` → "1 to 4 days", and 🔑 rebuilt **through i18n from the extracted numbers** (`daysRange`/`daysSingle`) rather than tidied in place, so Arabic reads «من 1 إلى 4 أيام» instead of spaced-out English.
