Prices are `font-mono tabular-nums` so decimals line up down a column. A summary strip carries cheapest / fastest / switched-on above the data, and a name filter earns its place past ten carriers.
  - 🔴 **`prettyName()` splits camelCase carrier names ONLY when both sides are 3+ characters.** OTO sends `adwarLogestechs` and `delexLogestechs`, but a naive lowercase-to-uppercase split also turns **`AyMakan` into "Ay Makan" and `iMile` into "I Mile"**, which is not how either company writes itself. The leading capital is added only when a split actually happened, for the same reason. Display-only; Edit details is the permanent fix for a name it gets wrong.
- **Destination city is a bilingual combobox** (`components/admin/city-input.tsx` + `lib/saudi-cities.ts`, ~95 Saudi cities with Arabic and English names). Focusing it offers the major destinations; typing filters in either language.
  - 🔑 **The English name is the VALUE; the Arabic name is only how you find it.** OTO matches the city string on their side and their documented examples are English transliterations ("ar rass", "Riyadh"), and `OTO_ORIGIN_CITY` is English too — so selecting «جدة» submits `Jeddah`. Typing Arabic by hand and pressing Enter resolves through `exactCity()` for the same reason. Sending Arabic would be a guess about a matcher we cannot see.
  - 🔑 **Matching reuses `normalize()` from `lib/search.ts`**, the storefront product search's Arabic folder, so «الاحساء» finds «الأحساء» and «جده» finds «جدة». Those are spelling differences, not typos, so no fuzzy layer would rescue them — and it means one normalizer serves both features rather than a second copy drifting.
  - ⚠️ **Deliberately free text, not a select.** An unrecognised value is submitted as typed, so a city missing from the list is an annoyance rather than a wall, and there is no "invalid city" state because we do not know OTO's full vocabulary. ⏭ OTO publishes **`getCities`** (documented on every plan) if the spellings ever mismatch; it is not used because a typeahead that waits on the network is worse than one that answers instantly, the Arabic names have to be ours regardless, and the page must keep working when OTO is unreachable.
  - ⚠️ **Escape is scoped:** the field swallows it only while its list is open, so a second press still reaches the page handler that closes the carrier detail view. Suggestions commit on **`onMouseDown`**, not `onClick` — the input's blur would otherwise close the list before a click could land.
  - Pinned by 14 vitest cases in `lib/saudi-cities.test.ts`, including the letter-folding pairs and the Arabic-to-English resolution.
- **Pickup-point ("PUDO") services — BUILT 2026-09-01.** `PUDO` = pick up / drop off: the customer collects the parcel from a branch, counter or locker instead of it being delivered. It is materially cheaper (SMSA quotes **13.92 vs 24.36 SAR** for the same destination), which is exactly what would have made the automatic cheapest-carrier pick choose it — handing a customer who paid the flat home-delivery fee an errand they never agreed to.
  - 🔑 **One definition, `App\Services\Shipping\PickupPoint`**, read by both the admin portal (which badges it) and the quote path (which keeps it off the automatic pick). ⚠️ Deliberately an **OR** of OTO's `pickupDropoff` flag and the service name, not "trust the flag, fall back to the name": a false positive only costs a cheap option staff can still choose by hand, while a false negative strands a customer — and the flag is absent entirely from the rate-check payload, where the name is all there is. Keep `HINTS` tight; `collect` is excluded because it would swallow "collect on delivery".
  - 🔴 **`ShippingService::preferredOption()` is public and static so the Ship dialog can badge the row it returns.** Letting the UI re-derive it — as it did, by labelling whichever row sorted first — is how the two drift apart the moment the rule gains a nuance, which is exactly what happened. It picks the cheapest **door delivery**, and ⚠️ **falls back to the cheapest overall when every option is a pickup point**: refusing there would surface days later as "no delivery options" on somebody else's shipment.
  - **`DeliveryOption` now carries `service` and `pickupDropoff`.** Both were being dropped, so one company quoting several services under a single name (both SMSA rows arrive as `smsaV2`) produced rows that were indistinguishable to the operator.
  - ⚠️ **Most apparent price gaps against the live Zid store are NOT errors**: Zid keeps carton and single as variant *options* on one product and its JSON-LD reports the base price, so our flattened `- كرتون` rows legitimately cost 7–12× more. Verified on a Zid product page (it mentions both كرتون and حبة).
- ⏭ **Known limitation:** if OTO ever reports one courier under two genuinely different company names ("SPL" and "Saudi Post"), they register as two rows and disabling one will not disable the other. Inherent to name matching without stable company ids from OTO; `last_seen_at` and the notes field make it visible.
- ⏭ **Not built:** no per-carrier performance stats (`orders.carrier` + `shipping_cost` are recorded, so on-time rate and average cost per courier are a query away and would make the enable decision evidence-based); the tracking link is not yet surfaced on the order page; and there is no dashboard tile for "a carrier we rely on stopped being offered".

### Product publish guard — **(BUILT 2026-09-01)**
A product cannot be shown on the storefront until it has a **price, an Arabic name, and at least one image**. Code: `Product::PUBLISH_REQUIREMENTS` + `missingForPublish()` / `isPublishable()` / `syncPublishability()` / `scopeIncompleteForPublish()`, enforced by a `saving` hook in `Product::booted()`.

- 🔑 **Enforced on the MODEL, not in the controller, and that is the whole point.** `is_active` is written from at least four places: the product form, the one-click list toggle, a **change-log revert** (which writes an entire previous row back, so reverting an old edit would otherwise re-publish a product that has since lost its image), and the catalogue importers. A controller-side check covers the first and silently misses the rest. Pinned by a test that drives the revert path specifically.
- 🔑 **It DOWNGRADES, it does not refuse the save.** Staff can always save work in progress; the storefront just does not show it. ⚠️ This **fixed a live bug**: `update()` used to throw when a product had no images, so the five image-less products — precisely the ones needing attention — **could not be edited at all**. `AdminProductControllerTest::test_update_is_allowed_without_images_but_the_product_stays_hidden` is the renamed test that used to assert the opposite.
- 🔑 **An ENGLISH NAME is an advisory, not a gate** (decided 2026-09-01, after measuring). The storefront is Arabic-first and `useLocalized()` falls back to the Arabic name, so an English visitor sees a correctly-named product either way — blocking on it would be stricter than this app's own bilingual contract ("AR required, EN nullable, falls back to AR"). Kept in a **separate `publishAdvisories()`** so a gate and a nudge can never be confused; still badged on the row. _The measurement that settled it: requiring it reddened **59 tests across 19 classes**; not requiring it, **7**. Production was unaffected either way — all 91 products already have an English name._
- ⚠️ **A record that does not yet exist is treated as SATISFYING the image rule.** Images are a separate table and can only be attached after the insert, so judging a new row on them would make `Product::create([... 'is_active' => true])` impossible to satisfy in one statement for every seeder, importer and test. The rule still bites where it matters: the create form requires images before it accepts the request, every later save re-checks, and `store()` re-applies the requested visibility once the images are on. What is deliberately NOT covered is a script that inserts an active product and never attaches an image — trusted code, and the dashboard tile lists it if it happens.
- ⚠️ **Deleting the last image re-checks explicitly** (`ProductImageController::destroy` → `syncPublishability()`), because images live on their own endpoints: removing one leaves the product row untouched, so nothing else would notice and a live product would keep selling with no picture. The admin is told the product just went hidden rather than it happening silently.
- 🔴 **`scopeIncompleteForPublish()` is the SQL twin of `missingForPublish()` and the two MUST agree** — a product the scope omits but the guard rejects would be missing from the "needs completing" list while refusing to go live, which reads as a broken toggle. Pinned by a test that walks every product and asserts the two sets are identical. Change one, change both.
- **Surfaces:** a **"Products that cannot go live yet"** tile in the dashboard action queue (deliberately NOT `urgent` — that tint is reserved for queues where waiting costs money) linking to a new **`/admin/products?status=incomplete`** filter. Both count the same set, by construction. The tile self-hides at zero, which is what makes it appropriate here where the draft backlog was not (see the 2026-08-20 entry that moved drafts out of this queue).
- ⚠️ The list toggle refuses **up front** with a message naming everything missing at once, rather than letting the model guard silently spring the toggle back — and it names the whole list so an admin fixing a product learns all of it on the first attempt.

### Store events (named campaigns) — **(BUILT 2026-09-08)**
A **named, time-boxed** campaign with its own homepage strip above Best Sellers, headed by the event's own name («اليوم الوطني السعودي»), two offers per row. Code: `App\Models\StoreEvent` + `App\Support\StoreEventCards` + `Admin\StoreEventController`, storefront `components/store/store-event.tsx`, admin `admin/store-events/{index,show,event-fields}.tsx`, new `store_events` permission section.

- 🔑 **A table, not a flag, and the client's own requirement is what settles it.** The brief was originally "a Special Offers section"; the follow-up was *"sometimes we want the event to be named, for example اليوم الوطني السعودي"*. A name, a subtitle and a window cannot live on a boolean, and the section heading has to be read from somewhere — so `store_events` + an `event_product` pivot, rather than the `is_special_offer` column the first design proposed. **The heading is data**, which is the whole point: the section reads اليوم الوطني السعودي in September and عروض رمضان in March with no deploy.
- 🔴 **THE RULE THE FEATURE RESTS ON: `Product::scopeNotInRunningEvent()`, and it has THREE call sites that must agree.** The client's instruction was *"this section shouldn't mix with the discounts section"*. A discounted product attached to a running event would otherwise appear in the event strip **and** in the العروض strip lower down the same page. The scope excludes it from (1) the homepage discounts strip, (2) `/shop?on_sale=1`, and (3) **`hasOffers`** — which is the one easiest to forget, because it drives the Offers nav item, the mobile drawer entry *and* the cart's empty-state link, so missing it means the nav offers a link to an empty page. **Proven, not assumed:** disabling the scope reddens 4 tests covering all three.
- ⚠️ **The exclusion is scoped to the ON-SALE surfaces only.** An event product keeps its category and stays in ordinary browsing, search and its own product page. This is deliberately unlike the "move it into a Special Offers category" design that was rejected: `products.category_id` is a single FK, so that would have emptied a product's real category for the length of the campaign.
- 🔑 **An event sets no prices.** The discount is still the product's own `sale_price` + `sale_starts_at`/`sale_ends_at`, which already existed and already expires by itself — set the sale window to the event's window and the price handles itself. That separation is why an event can be built, paused and deleted without ever touching the catalogue. The admin page surfaces each offer's `saleStatus()` (scheduled / active / expired) precisely because a lapsed discount is the commonest way an event looks wrong on the storefront.
- **Per-event accent colour** (`accent_color`, nullable → brand teal). Drives heading, rule, badges and prices through one `--ev` custom property; the section tint is **`color-mix`d from the accent** rather than stored separately, so an event can never be given a background that fights its own heading. ⚠️ The admin offers **presets, not an open colour picker** — a free field is a licence to put a clashing colour on the homepage — with a hex box behind them for a one-off. A preset equal to brand teal is filtered out of the swatch row, or it renders an identical twin beside "default" storing a different value (`''` vs `#1b4e53`).
- **Cards are 16:9 banners** (client picked treatment A). `event_product.banner_image` is optional designed artwork; with none, the card crops the product's own primary photo (`detail` variant, 1400px — `card` at 500px is too small for a ~780px-wide card), so **an event is never blocked waiting on a designer**. The gold pill is `badge_ar`/`badge_en` on the pivot, falling back to a **derived** `discount_percent` — so «خصم ٢٠٪» needs nothing typed and can never disagree with the price beside it, while «كرتونين + الثالث مجاناً», which no discount can express, is typed once.
- ⚠️ **An event whose offers are all hidden renders NOTHING**, not a titled section over an empty carousel. Realistic: a product can be deactivated, or fail the publish guard, while still attached. Two overlapping events render two titled sections in order, which needs no "which one wins" rule and handles the ordinary single-event case for free.
- ⚠️ **`dateTime`, NOT `timestamp`, on the window columns.** MariaDB gives the first non-nullable TIMESTAMP an implicit `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` default and every later one `'0000-00-00'`, which strict mode rejects: *"Invalid default value for 'ends_at'"*. The products table only dodges this because its sale window is nullable. Cost one migration failure.
- **The العروض الخاصة category is seeded in the MIGRATION** (permanent home for campaign bundles, so they stay browsable between events). Categories have no admin UI and Railpack runs `migrate --force` but never `db:seed`, so a fresh production deploy would otherwise have nowhere to put a bundle. Deliberately **no image**: the homepage tile query is `whereNotNull('image')`, and a tile for a category that is empty between events is a dead end.
- **Carousel logic is SHARED, not copied** — `hooks/use-carousel.ts`, extracted from `product-carousel.tsx`. The RTL scroll handling (`scrollLeft` runs `0..max` in LTR and `-max..0` in RTL) was got wrong once already; a second copy would drift. `initialScrollable` preserves what the SSR sidecar renders so the arrows do not flash in on hydration.
- **Permission:** `store_events.view` / `store_events.manage`, defaulting view-only for editors (mirroring `discounts` — a campaign fronts the homepage and moves money), in the `catalogue` and `manager` presets.
- ⏭ **Not built:** a bundle product does NOT decrement the products it contains (see the log entry — it needs a components table and a decision from the client), no per-event sales reporting, and no dashboard tile for a running event.

### Auth & Security — **(harden before launch)**
- Session-based auth (starter kit default). Use `Auth::id()`/`Auth::user()`, never the `auth()` helper in new code (Sky Amman convention).
- Rate-limit all public POST endpoints; add a bot gate (Cloudflare Turnstile) on public forms (port from Sky Amman).
- Server-side validation on ALL endpoints; validate file types by extension AND MIME; parameterized queries / Eloquent only.
- Add a `SecurityHeaders` middleware (CSP allowlist, Permissions-Policy) — port from Sky Amman, skip CSP in local dev.
- Production HTTPS behind a proxy: `URL::forceScheme('https')` when `APP_ENV=production` + correct `trustProxies` CIDRs (see Sky Amman's `bootstrap/app.php`).

### Storefront search — **(BUILT 2026-08-20)**
Site-wide product search from the navbar, Arabic-aware, typo-tolerant and cross-language. **Read this before touching any of the four files below.**

- **The pieces.** `App\Support\SearchText` (normalisation + synonyms + the server-side predicate) · `ShopController::cachedIndex()` (the cached index, now carrying `id` and a pre-built `terms` haystack) · `resources/js/lib/search.ts` (normalisation + Damerau-Levenshtein + ranking) · `hooks/use-search-index.ts` (module-level one-shot fetch) · `components/store/search-overlay.tsx` (navbar) and `catalogue-search.tsx` (the `/shop` box) — **both render the same matcher**, and on `/shop` they are on screen together.
- 🔑 **Letter folding is the feature; typo tolerance is the garnish.** أ/إ/آ→ا, ة→ه, ى→ي, ؤ→و, ئ→ي, ء→∅, harakat and kashida dropped, Arabic-Indic digits to ASCII, everything else a word boundary. None of that is about misspelling — it is the same word written the way different people write it, and it is what makes «علبه» find «علبة».
- 🔴 **`normalize` is DUPLICATED in PHP and TS on purpose** (the server normalises to build the index, the client normalises the query). They must produce identical output or the typeahead silently stops matching what the results page finds. **Pinned by the same 18-case table in `tests/Feature/SearchTest.php` and `resources/js/lib/search.test.ts` — change one, change both.** Latin diacritics are deliberately stripped by neither: JS could and PHP could not without ext-intl, and one-sided folding is exactly the drift being guarded against.
- 🔑 **The `?q=` results page resolves ids from the SAME cached index**, not a SQL `LIKE`. Making the typeahead smart while the results page stayed dumb would be worse than leaving both dumb — the shopper sees five suggestions, presses Enter, and gets nothing.
- **Typos are the one asymmetry** (client ranks fuzzily, server is substring-only). The overlay calls `serverWouldMatch()` and, when false, `correctQuery()` rewrites the term so "see all results" reads *«… عن «sukkari»»* and lands on real results. **No correction available ⇒ no link rendered**, rather than a link to an empty page.
- **Synonyms live on the PRODUCT side** (`SearchText::SYNONYMS`, expanded at index time), never on the query — one list, and the fuzzy layer inherits it. Add a group there when a word shoppers type appears nowhere in the catalogue text (the reason it exists: the catalogue writes "box" as «بوكس»).
- **Scoring:** exact word 100 · prefix 80 · substring 55 · fuzzy 34−6·distance. Tokens are **ANDed** (OR returns most of the catalogue for two words). ⚠️ **No fuzz under 4 characters** — at 3, one edit reaches a large share of the vocabulary and «لوز» starts matching «جوز»/«روز», which reads as broken rather than forgiving.
- ⚠️ **Cache busting:** `Product`, `ProductImage` **and `Category`** all forget `Product::SEARCH_INDEX_CACHE`; the 1h TTL is the safety net. Anything new folded into `terms` needs its model added here.
- 🔴 **The overlay is portalled to `<body>`.** The header writes `transform` on every scroll tick, so a `fixed` descendant of it resolves against the header's own ~69px box. Same trap the mobile drawer shipped with. Pinned by an E2E test that scrolls before opening.
- ⚠️ Escape is handled on the **document**, not the input — focus is set on a timer, so an input-scoped handler misses a fast press and leaves the page scroll-locked. `input[type=search]`'s native clear ✕ is suppressed app-wide (it sat beside our close button).
- ⚠️ **Entry points:** the icon sits beside the burger at every width, plus a labelled field at the top of the mobile drawer. 🔑 **Row 1's start cell is now full on phones** — burger + search + the sign-up pill, with 9px to spare at 390px in Arabic. Anything else added there collides with the logo; measure before assuming otherwise, and see the log entry for what has already been tried.
- ⏭ **Levers if it needs to be better:** log queries that return nothing (the best possible input to the synonym list, currently discarded), a recent/popular panel in the empty state, and indexing descriptions (deliberately excluded — long prose, and a fuzzy hit inside one is usually a false positive).

### SEO — **(BUILT — sitemap/robots/JSON-LD 2026-07-02; 301s + slug cleanup 2026-07-25)**
- Storefront SEO matters (organic product discovery). Server-render public pages (enable SSR — see Build Progress), per-page/per-product `<Head>` meta + OG, dynamic `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt` disallowing admin, JSON-LD (`Product`/`Offer`/`BreadcrumbList`), and `hreflang` AR↔EN. _(hreflang deliberately skipped — session-based locale shares one URL per language; see the 2026-07-02 Build Progress entry.)_
- 🔴 **CORRECTION 2026-09-01 — the premise of the 301 plan below no longer holds. Re-check it before launch.** It assumes our `products.slug` values still match the URLs Google has indexed for the Zid store, because the importer seeded ours from Zid's. **Zid has since changed its own URLs and SKUs.** Scraping the live store today: slugs are now partly English transliterations (`/products/Khlass-N1-250g`, `/products/Najdi-coffee`) where our export has Arabic ones, and Zid's SKUs are now `RETAB###` rather than the `Z.30547.*` in the export. So the redirect map built from `old_zid_slug` may point from slugs Zid itself no longer serves, and a slug we never cleaned can still 404 on their side. Also note **`catalog:clean-slugs` has never been run on production** (`redirects` is empty there). Before launch: re-derive the redirect map from what is actually indexed (Search Console), not from the July export.
- **301 redirects from the old Zid URLs + slug cleanup — BUILT 2026-07-25 (run `catalog:clean-slugs --apply` on prod AT LAUNCH).** _Why it matters:_ the live Zid store is already indexed by Google; each ranked result points at an old Zid slug. Zid used the **same** `/products/{slug}` path we do (verified: `retabstore.com/products/{old-slug}` → 200), and the importer seeded our `products.slug` **from** `old_zid_slug` — so today the old URLs already resolve. The moment we clean a slug, its old URL would 404 and the ranking (link equity) is lost. _What was built:_
  - **`redirects` table** + `App\Models\Redirect` (`from_slug` unique, nullable `product_id` FK, static `to_url` fallback, `status` default 301, `hits`). `target()` resolves to the product's **current** slug (so a later re-clean still lands right) or the static URL, else null → 404.
  - **`catalog:clean-slugs {--apply}` command** — dry-run by default. Regenerates only **junk** slugs (ar-prefixed / English / uppercase / leading-hyphen — detected as "contains a Latin letter or leading `-`") into clean **Arabic** slugs from `name_ar` via **`App\Support\ArabicSlug`** (preserves Arabic + Arabic-Indic digits, strips tatweel/harakat, lowercases Latin; Str::slug would transliterate Arabic away). Already-clean Arabic slugs are **left untouched (no redirect)** → fewest redirects, best ranking preservation. Records a 301 from each changed slug; transactional; idempotent (cleaned slugs aren't junk on re-run; `firstOrCreate` redirects); dedupes collisions with `-2`. On the real catalogue: **27 of 87** slugs cleaned.
  - **Resolution:** the `/products/{product:slug}` GET route has a **`->missing()`** hook → `RedirectController@missingProduct` (301 to `target()`, increments `hits`, else 404). Non-breaking (keeps the `{product:slug}` binding; `route('shop.product', …)` still generates by slug) and **`route:cache`-safe** (the missing closure serializes via `SerializableClosure` — verified). Chose Arabic slugs over English (client decision 2026-07-25).
  - **Launch step:** after final slugs are settled, run `php artisan catalog:clean-slugs` (preview) then `--apply` **on prod**. Future legacy paths (e.g. old category URLs) can be added as manual `redirects` rows (set `to_url`). _Deferred:_ the 16 Zid **variant-parent** URLs (indexed but not 1:1 with a product) — add hand-mapped `redirects` rows if they show in Search Console.
- **Brand assets: favicons, PWA manifest + OG share card — BUILT 2026-08-08.** `public/` now carries `favicon.ico` (multi-res 16+32, hand-assembled — PHP GD cannot write ICO, but the format accepts raw PNG blobs: 6-byte header + 16-byte directory entries + payloads), `icon.svg`, `apple-touch-icon.png` (180), `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png`, `site.webmanifest` (theme `#1b4e53`), and `og-image.{jpg,webp}` (1200×630). Linked from `app.blade.php`. _Prior state: `public/favicon.ico` was the scaffold's **0-byte** placeholder and there were **no icon links at all**, so the site had no favicon anywhere and no default share card._
  - 🔴 **THE GOTCHA THAT MATTERS: Inertia `<Head>` tags do NOT exist in the server HTML unless SSR is running.** They are applied client-side after hydration, and **WhatsApp's and Facebook's link-preview crawlers do not execute JavaScript** — so with SSR off, every `og:*` tag on the site is invisible to them and shared links preview blank. Verified empirically here: `curl` with SSR off returned only the Blade-level tags; with the sidecar up, all seven `og:*`/`twitter:*` tags appeared with `data-inertia`. **This promotes the SSR sidecar from a performance nicety to a hard requirement for the store's main marketing channel.**
  - ⚠️ **A running SSR sidecar keeps serving the OLD bundle after a rebuild.** Cost real debugging time here: `npm run build` refreshed `bootstrap/ssr/ssr.js`, but a sidecar left running from an earlier session still rendered the previous markup (new tags missing, old ones present — which looks exactly like a broken code change). A second `node bootstrap/ssr/ssr.js` **crashes with `EADDRINUSE` on 13714 rather than taking over**, so the stale one silently wins. **On deploy, the SSR service must restart too** — redeploying only the web service leaves stale markup being served.
  - **`og:image` is deliberately NOT in `app.blade.php`.** A layout-level tag renders *before* `@inertiaHead`, and crawlers treat the **first** `og:image` as primary, so it would override the product page's own photo. Instead the absolute URL is a shared Inertia prop (`ogImage`, built from `APP_URL` server-side because crawlers do not resolve relative paths) consumed by each page's `<Head>`; the product page falls back to it only when a product has no photo.
  - **The JPEG twin is intentional.** WhatsApp's preview crawler is unreliable with WebP and WhatsApp is this store's primary channel, so `og:image` points at `og-image.jpg` (113 KB) while the WebP is kept alongside it.
  - ⚠️ **Designer hand-off gotchas (both cost time):** every exported icon was **one pixel oversized** (17/33/181/193/513 — a Figma fractional-frame rounding artifact) which makes browsers resample favicons and makes Chrome reject a maskable icon for a declared-vs-actual size mismatch, so all were resized to exact powers; and two filenames contained a **non-breaking space (U+00A0) instead of a space**, which is why Windows Explorer silently skipped them during extraction (leaving an empty `SVG/` folder that looked like a corrupt archive) and why any hardcoded path with a normal space fails to match. The archive was fine — .NET extracted it perfectly. Also, the master SVG's viewBox was **not square** (1133.94×1341.15), which browsers letterbox in a square favicon slot, so it was widened to `-103.605 0 1341.15 1341.15` (centres the artwork without touching a path).
  - 🔑 **Transparency is deliberately NOT uniform across the set** (designer supplied a transparent PNG set on 2026-08-08; the original white-backed one is only kept where opacity is required):
    - `favicon.ico` (16+32), `icon.svg`, `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png` → **transparent**, so the mark sits correctly on both light and dark browser chrome.
    - `apple-touch-icon.png` → **flattened onto white**. iOS composites a transparent home-screen icon onto **black**, which would put the gold mark on a black tile instead of the intended white one.
    - `icon-maskable-512.png` → **the designer's original white-backed export, kept as-is**. A maskable icon must be opaque *and* keep its content inside the inner-80% safe zone because Android crops it to the launcher's shape; that export already has the padding, so synthesising one from the plain transparent icon would risk cropping the mark.
    - Rebuilt with plain GD (`imagecopyresampled` + the `imagealphablending(false)`/`imagesavealpha(true)` recipe) so alpha survives the downscale; transparent PNGs also compress better, which took `favicon.ico` from 2208 to 1457 bytes.

### Deployment + SSR
- Likely Railway (Railpack, PHP 8.2 + Node) behind Cloudflare — port Sky Amman's startup (`migrate --force` → `storage:link` → `optimize`).
- Use `SESSION_DRIVER=database` in production (ephemeral FS).
- **SSR:** the starter kit ships `resources/js/ssr.jsx` + `npm run build:ssr`. To actually enable it we still need to: publish `config/inertia.php` (env-driven `INERTIA_SSR_ENABLED`), add a timeout/graceful-fallback HTTP gateway (so a hung SSR process falls back to CSR instead of 502-ing), and stand up a production SSR sidecar. **Port Sky Amman's `app/Ssr/TimeoutHttpGateway.php` + sidecar setup.** (Sky Amman docs two gotchas: Railpack pruning Node from the runtime → `RAILPACK_DEPLOY_APT_PACKAGES=nodejs`; and the SSR URL host must match the private domain exactly + is baked at `config:cache` time.)

## Database Schema (BUILT — schema v1 complete on `construction_phase`)

> **Source of truth = the migrations** in `database/migrations/` (`2026_06_29_1000xx_*`) + Eloquent models in `app/Models/`. Migrated clean on **MariaDB (dev)** and **SQLite (tests)**; 26 tests green. Conventions: bilingual `_ar`/`_en` (AR required, EN nullable, app falls back to AR); quantity `stock` (no variants); `smacc_sku` import key; soft-deletes on `products` + `users`; money `decimal(10,2)` SAR; **order/payment/coupon/return state are backed enums** in `app/Enums/`.
>
> **Tables by domain:**
> - **Catalog:** `categories`, `products`, `product_images`
> - **Accounts:** `users` (extended: phone, role, locale, admin_theme, whatsapp_opt_in, confirmed_purchases_count; name/email/password now nullable), `social_accounts` (Google/OAuth), `otp_verifications` (WhatsApp OTP), `addresses` (GCC)
> - **Cart:** `carts`, `cart_items`
> - **Orders:** `orders` (state machine via `OrderStatus`; `delivered_at`; OTO + payment fields), `order_items` (snapshots), `order_activities` (append-only audit)
> - **Payments:** `payments` (auth/capture/void/refund ledger)
> - **Coupons/Loyalty:** `coupons` (admin-controlled; `channel`/`source`), `coupon_redemptions`, `loyalty_rewards` (5 buys → 15%)
> - **Returns:** `order_returns` (defect-only; photos; `ReturnStatus`), `return_items`
> - **Engagement:** `reviews`, `review_helpful_votes`, `wishlists`
> - **Platform:** `content_pages` (bilingual CMS), `whatsapp_messages` (Cloud API log), `notifications` (Laravel bell), `demand_events` (unavailable analytics), `settings` (`Setting::get/set`), `activity_logs` (admin audit + revert — change-log v2, see Architecture → Change Log), `shipping_carriers` (which couriers the store ships with + their support contacts — see Architecture → Shipping carriers portal)
> - **Enums (`app/Enums/`):** `OrderStatus` (+enforced transitions), `PaymentStatus`, `PaymentMethod`, `CouponType`, `PaymentTransactionType`, `ReturnStatus`
>
> **Deliberately NOT built (schema-ready, deferred):** ~~product variants~~ (BUILT 2026-08-23 as `product_options` — see E-commerce Requirements → Products & Inventory); QR in-store coupons; the payment/shipping/WhatsApp **service** layer (gateways/OTO/Cloud-API clients) — that's the next phase.

_Original roadmap (superseded by the BUILT inventory above — kept for historical context):_

- `products`, `categories` (+ pivot), `product_variants` (size/weight/grade — relevant for dates), `product_images`
- `inventory` / stock tracking, `prices` (incl. sale price), maybe `coupons`/`discounts`
- `carts` + `cart_items` (guest + logged-in), `orders` + `order_items`, `order_status` history
- `customers`/extended user profile, `addresses` (KSA address format), `payments` (gateway txn refs), `shipping`/`fulfillment`
- Bilingual product content (AR/EN) — decide column-per-locale vs. a translations table early.

> **Document every real schema decision here as it's made**, Sky-Amman-style (the "why", not just the "what"), so future agents don't re-litigate it.

## Key File Locations (as scaffolded)

```
routes/web.php, auth.php, settings.php, console.php   → route definitions (modular)
resources/js/app.tsx            → client entry
resources/js/ssr.jsx            → SSR entry (build-time only until runtime is wired)
resources/js/pages/             → page components (welcome, dashboard, auth/*, settings/*)
resources/js/layouts/           → AppLayout + auth/settings layouts
resources/js/components/         → shared components (+ ui/ shadcn-Radix primitives)
resources/js/hooks/             → e.g. useAppearance
resources/js/lib/               → utils (cn, etc.)
resources/js/types/             → shared TS types
resources/css/app.css           → Tailwind v4 entry + theme tokens
database/migrations/            → starter-kit defaults only so far
.npmrc                          → production=false (keep — NODE_ENV gotcha)
```

---

## Code Quality Rules (adopted from Sky Amman)

- If a model has a dedicated method (`Media::storeFile()`, `Setting::set()`), **always use it** — never bypass with raw `::update()`/`::create()`.
- The same write (create/update/restore) must go through one shared model method.
- **Never query inside a loop** — batch-fetch with `whereIn()`/`pluck()` first. Paginated views query related data only for the current page's IDs.
- Guard no-op operations (short-circuit when nothing changed).
- If a method returns a meaningful value (bool/count/status), use it — don't call-and-ignore.
- Match the surrounding code's style, naming, and comment density.

## Off-Repo Asset & Backup Archive — `Desktop\Project files\retab\`

Everything the project needs that is **not in git** and could not be recreated lives in one place (consolidated 2026-09-01 from the scattered `Downloads\Retab`). **Look here before asking the client to re-send anything.** The folder carries its own `README.md`.

| Folder | Contents |
|---|---|
| `backups/` | Production MySQL dumps + demo seed/rollback SQL. **`retab-prod-20260901-110115.sql` = the full 43-table dump taken immediately before the demo orders/customers were deleted from prod on 2026-09-01.** |
| `data/` | **`products_export_*.xlsx` — the Zid catalogue export** (the migration source of record) + `retab-products-clean.csv` |
| `design/` | Designer hand-offs: navbar / homepage / footer / footer-banner / favicon / about-us / misc. For several sections this is the ONLY copy of the composite that shipped code was measured against (see Architecture → "Building a designed section from a Figma composite") |
| `docs/` | Registration certificate, invoices, Zid WhatsApp pricing, integrations guide, and **`googlee5b4a3ebe46a90cc.html`** (Search Console domain verification) |
| `fonts/` | **Thmanyah Sans + `LICENSE.pdf`** — the licence home this file's Bilingual section points at |
| `photography/` | Raw client photo deliveries: `rusks/`, `category-tiles/`, `product-shots/`. ⚠️ Not all of it is in use — see the note below |

- 🔴 **It is NOT a backup, and the distinction matters.** `Desktop` is local only (it is *not* inside `OneDrive`), so this archive shares a disk — and a failure — with the working copy. The git remotes are the only offsite copies and they carry none of this. Copy the folder to OneDrive or an external drive to make it real. ⚠️ **Never push `fonts/` anywhere public** — the Thmanyah licence forbids redistribution (see Architecture → Bilingual for the full terms).
- **Taking a fresh prod dump:** `mysqldump` is unavailable (the XAMPP MariaDB client can't auth to MySQL 8's `caching_sha2_password`), so use PHP PDO or TablePlus over the public proxy — `railway variables --service "MySQL" --kv | grep MYSQL_PUBLIC_URL`. ⚠️ The Railway service is **`MySQL`**; `mysql` answers "Service not found", and the internal `mysql.railway.internal` host is unreachable from a laptop. Name dumps `retab-prod-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>.sql`. **Always take one before any destructive production change.**
- Originals were **copied, not moved**, so `Downloads\Retab` still holds a duplicate — but Downloads is where files get cleared, so treat the archive as canonical.
- ⚠️ **Photography status, corrected 2026-09-01 (client-flagged) — do not assume the archive mirrors the live catalogue.** The images the importer actually uses are **committed to the repo** (`database/data/product-photos` 24 · `product-photos-light` 10 · `rusk-photos` 7, all compressed WebP), so the live set is covered by git, not by this archive. What the archive holds is the raw high-res deliveries, and they do **not** line up 1:1:
  - 🔴 **`photography/product-shots/` (13 × `prod (N).png`, 83 MB) feeds NOTHING** — grepped, it matches no matcher in any `database/data/image-maps.php` group. An older delivery, kept pending review rather than deleted.
  - **Not yet archived:** the raw masters behind the *current* photos — `Downloads\products imgs\` (the `retab-NN` dark set) and `Downloads\light imgs\` (`dates image (N).png`, the light set). Deliberately left for later: the client is still delivering replacement photography, so archiving now would capture a half-finished set. Sweep both into `photography/` once the re-shoot is done.

## Local Development

- **Start (all-in-one):** `composer run dev` — runs `php artisan serve` + queue listener + `npm run dev` concurrently.
- **Or separately:** `php artisan serve` + `npm run dev`.
- **URL:** `http://localhost:8000`
- **Database:** XAMPP **MariaDB 10.4.32** on `127.0.0.1:3307`, db `retab-stores` (user `root`, empty password) — set in `.env`. Reset: `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed`. ⚠️ `php artisan db:show` errors on MariaDB (queries `performance_schema.session_status`, which it doesn't expose) — cosmetic only; use `migrate:status` / `db:table <name>` to inspect.
- **Build:** `npm run build` — emits the client **and** the SSR bundle (`build:ssr` is now an identical alias, so Railway's default build produces the sidecar bundle without extra config).
- **Backend tests:** `php artisan test` (PHPUnit, in-memory SQLite).
- **Mail in dev:** default `MAIL_MAILER=log` writes to `storage/logs/laravel.log` — no provider key needed.
- **`.npmrc` guard:** if `npm install` ever drops vite/devDeps, `.npmrc` (`production=false`) should prevent it; fallback `npm install --include=dev`.
- **⚠️ Composer gotcha — `composer update <package>` can silently install a DEV branch and "fix" nothing (hit 2026-07-30).** _Symptom:_ `composer update guzzlehttp/guzzle` reported `Upgrading guzzlehttp/guzzle (7.12.3 => 7.12.x-dev 9aa17bc)` — a **dev-branch commit of a production HTTP library** — and `composer audit` still listed the same 4 advisories afterwards. _Root cause (two things combined):_ (1) the starter kit ships **`"minimum-stability": "dev"`** (with `prefer-stable: true`), so dev branches are *allowed* whenever no stable version satisfies the resolution; (2) `composer update <one-package>` deliberately **will not touch that package's own dependencies** — guzzle ≥7.15 requires `guzzlehttp/psr7 ^2.13` + `guzzlehttp/promises ^2.5.1` (installed: 2.12.3 / 2.5.0), so the stable target was unreachable and composer quietly settled on the highest thing it *could* take: the 7.12 dev branch. Diagnose with **`composer why-not guzzlehttp/guzzle 7.15.1`** (names the blocking requirements). _Fix:_ **`composer update guzzlehttp/guzzle --with-dependencies`** → 7.15.2 stable + psr7 2.13.0 + promises 2.5.1, `composer audit` clean, 348 tests green. _Lesson for any security update:_ afterwards, confirm the resolved version is **stable AND above the advisory's "Affected versions" bound** — re-run `composer audit` rather than trusting that the update did something, and reach for `-W` before concluding no fix exists.
- **⚠️ Intelephense gotcha — P1005 "Expected 4. Found 2." on `Model::where(...)` (solved for good 2026-07-02, machine-level).** _Symptom:_ every static Eloquent call (`Product::where(...)`) red-squiggled with P1005, in **every** Laravel project, surviving even `intelephense.diagnostics.argumentCount: false`. _Root cause (three parts):_ (1) the **official Laravel VS Code extension** (`laravel.vscode-laravel`) generates `vendor/_laravel_ide/_model_helpers.php` in every project, redeclaring each model with **defaults-less** `@method static mixed where($column, $operator, $value, $boolean)` — class-level tags shadow the real optional-param signatures, so Intelephense counts all 4 as required; (2) a **second embedded Intelephense** was running — the `porifa.laravel-intelephense` fork bundles its own server, labels diagnostics `intelephense`, but only reads `laraphense.*` settings with **no diagnostics toggles** — that's why the global `argumentCount: false` "didn't work"; (3) the separate P1013 (`session()->push` "undefined") is a framework docblock gap — `Request::session()` advertises the Session **contract**, which lacks `push()` (only the concrete `Store` has it). _Fix (machine-wide, covers all sibling projects):_ uninstalled `porifa.laravel-intelephense`; added `**/vendor/_laravel_ide/**` to **`intelephense.files.exclude`** in global VS Code settings (kept the default excludes — the setting replaces, not merges); removed the now-unneeded `argumentCount: false` so real arg-count checking is back. _Project-level companion:_ `barryvdh/laravel-ide-helper` (dev dep) supplies the **correct** signatures + model `@property` autocomplete — `write_eloquent_model_mixins => true` puts `@mixin \Eloquent` on vendor's base `Model` (wiped by `composer update` → re-applied by the `post-update-cmd` hook); `ide-helper:models --write-mixin` needs the DB up, re-run after schema changes; `_ide_helper*.php` gitignored — regenerate after fresh clone. Contract-vs-implementation gaps (session `push`) still need a one-line inline `/** @var \Illuminate\Session\Store $session */`. After changes: fully restart VS Code.
- **⚠️ Editor gotcha — `tailwindcss(invalidConfigPath)` reported on CLAUDE.md (fixed 2026-08-03).** _Symptom:_ a red squiggle **in the Markdown doc**, on the prose describing `config('retab.admin.email')`: *"'retab.admin.email' does not exist in your theme config."* Nothing is wrong with the file. _Root cause:_ **Tailwind CSS IntelliSense**, not PHP tooling or Markdown — the extension validates every `config()` / `theme()` call it can see against the Tailwind theme, and it doesn't exclude Markdown code spans, so a documented **Laravel** `config()` helper looks like a broken Tailwind theme path. (Read the source in parentheses on any diagnostic before chasing it: `tailwindcss(...)` vs `intelephense(...)` points straight at the culprit — same lesson as the P1005 saga above, where the reporting extension wasn't the one being configured.) _Fix:_ `"tailwindCSS.lint.invalidConfigPath": "ignore"` in **`.vscode/settings.json`** (alongside the existing `unknownAtRules` silencers). Safe wholesale here: Tailwind **v4** is CSS-first (`@theme` in `app.css`, no `tailwind.config.js`) and the `theme()`/`config()` helpers appear **nowhere** in `resources/css` or `resources/js` — verified — so the rule has zero true positives in this repo. Reload the VS Code window for it to take. The pseudo-syntax `config('a'|'b')` that triggered it was also rewritten as two real calls (it was never valid PHP). **Applies to every Tailwind v4 project on this stack** (Sky Amman, HardRock, …) whose docs mention Laravel's `config()`.

## Git & Deploy

- `origin` is wired to **your** repo: `https://github.com/mercenary19961/retab-stores.git`.
- **Dual-push is ACTIVE** (configured 2026-06-28; **re-fixed 2026-07-02** — ⚠️ gotcha: the config had drifted onto a redundant remote named `upstream`, so plain `git push` — which uses `origin` — was NOT reaching the client repo while this doc said it was. _Symptom:_ client repo stays empty/stale despite pushes. _Check:_ `git remote -v` must show the 2 push URLs on **origin**, not on some other remote. _Fix:_ the two `set-url --add --push origin` lines below + `git remote remove upstream`) — one `git push` → **both** repos. ⚠️ **Second gotcha (2026-07-02): `mercenary19961` has READ but NOT WRITE on the client repo** — pushes 403 on the second URL (`Permission ... denied to mercenary19961`) while the own-repo push succeeds; VS Code's fork-dialog misleadingly names the OWN repo when this happens. The June-28 "access verified" note only ever verified READ (`ls-remote`). ✅ **RESOLVED (verified 2026-07-04):** the client granted Write, the first push landed, and the client repo is now **fully populated and in sync** with origin (`main` + `construction_phase` at identical SHAs; `git push --dry-run https://github.com/retab-dates-dev/retab-website.git construction_phase` → "Everything up-to-date"). The client repo **`https://github.com/retab-dates-dev/retab-website.git`** is the **Railway production source**. Configured via:
  ```bash
  git remote set-url --add --push origin https://github.com/mercenary19961/retab-stores.git    # re-add own repo FIRST
  git remote set-url --add --push origin https://github.com/retab-dates-dev/retab-website.git   # client / prod repo
  git remote -v   # → 1 fetch URL + 2 push URLs
  ```
  ⚠️ The **first** `--add --push` line (re-adding your own repo) is required — adding any explicit push URL drops the implicit default, so without it pushes would go **only** to the client repo. Read access to the client repo is verified (`git ls-remote` → exit 0).
- **Merge workflow — REVISED 2026-07-23: local-merge + dual-push via the `git ship` alias; NEVER use GitHub's merge button.** _History:_ the old rule (2026-07-04) was "merge via the PR button in BOTH repos" because the client repo had a **branch-protection ruleset requiring PRs to `main`** (a direct `main` push 403'd with `GH013 … Changes must be made through a pull request`). That button-in-both-repos habit is exactly what made the two `main`s **permanently SHA-divergent** (each Merge button mints its own merge commit) and produced the endless "N commits behind/ahead" churn. **On 2026-07-23 the client removed that ruleset**, so direct pushes to client `main` work again — which lets `main` have a **single writer** (your machine) and stay byte-identical on both repos.
  - **The rule now:** advance `main` by merging **locally**, then one `git push` dual-pushes the **same commit** to both repos → the two `main`s can't diverge. **Do not click GitHub's Merge button** (two independent buttons = two different merge commits = divergence returns).
  - **Tooling:** a repo-local git alias **`git ship <branch>`** (in `.git/config`, not committed) does the whole thing: `switch main → pull → merge --no-edit <branch> → push` (dual) `→ switch back → merge --ff-only main → push` the branch. It **keeps + re-syncs the branch** (branch ends at 0 ahead / 0 behind, no "behind main" churn) and **refuses to ship `main` into itself**. So the loop is: commit on a branch → `git push` (CI runs) → `git ship <branch>` once green. PRs are optional, for reading CI only — the merge never happens through the button.
  - ⚠️ **Tradeoff of removing the ruleset:** production `main` (client repo = Railway source) no longer has a server-side PR gate — anyone with write can push straight to prod `main`. The gate is now **discipline**: only `git ship` after CI is green. (Re-add a ruleset later if the client wants a hard gate; then the deploy step becomes a PR merge on the client repo again and the mains go back to harmless SHA-divergence.)
- Dual-push config (origin fetches your repo, pushes to both) — `git remote -v` must show **1 fetch + 2 push URLs on `origin`**:
  ```bash
  git remote set-url --add --push origin https://github.com/mercenary19961/retab-stores.git    # re-add own repo FIRST (an explicit push URL drops the implicit default)
  git remote set-url --add --push origin https://github.com/retab-dates-dev/retab-website.git   # client / prod repo (Railway source)
  ```
- Commit/push **only when the user asks** (don't auto-push). Branch off `main` for feature work.

## Commit Message Convention

Format: `type(scope): short description`

Types: `init` (scaffolding/setup) · `feat` · `fix` · `refactor` · `style` (visual only) · `doc` · `chore`

Rules: lowercase subject, no trailing period, present/imperative tense ("add" not "added"), under 72 chars, be specific ("fix: resolve 419 on locale toggle", not "fix: fix bug").

**Do NOT add any attribution/co-author trailer to commit messages** — no `Co-Authored-By:` line, no "Generated with" line. _(User preference set 2026-06-30; overrides the harness default.)_

## Collaboration — Commit Message Suggestions

After completing any task that touches code, end the reply with a **one-line suggested commit message** in the convention above (don't run the commit — just suggest it). Skip this for purely exploratory work (reading/answering) or when no files changed.

Also: 5 zero-price drafts, ~8 odd fractional prices, and 44 "Infinite"-stock rows to sanity-check. See the 301-redirect note under Architecture → SEO._
- [x] **Security hardening** (2026-07-02, ported from Sky Amman) — `SecurityHeaders` middleware (nosniff/frame/referrer/permissions always; **CSP + HSTS skipped in local only** — Vite HMR's bracketed-IPv6 origin breaks CSP; allowlist = challenges.cloudflare.com, cloudflareinsights, fonts.bunny.net; no gateway origins needed since checkout redirects OUT to hosted pages); ~~**trustProxies locked to Cloudflare CIDRs + RFC 1918** (never `*`)~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-09: now `at: '*'`.** That allowlist assumed Cloudflare would sit in front; production is a **direct Railway CNAME**, so it matched nothing and Laravel ignored every `X-Forwarded-*` header (collapsing all per-IP throttle buckets into one). ⚠️ **This note's original advice — "re-narrow it if Cloudflare is ever put in front" — is WRONG and was corrected on 2026-08-11: do NOT narrow it.** Cloudflare *is* in front now, and an allowlist neither helps (the visitor IP is absent from `X-Forwarded-For` entirely — Railway overwrites that header) nor is harmless (it would drop `X-Forwarded-Proto` and make Laravel read real HTTPS traffic as http). The client IP is resolved by `TrustProxiedClientIp` from `X-Real-IP` instead; full diagnosis in the 2026-08-11 log entry; + `URL::forceScheme('https')` **and `URL::forceRootUrl(config('app.url'))`** in production; **Turnstile** (`TurnstileVerifier` service — no-ops without `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`, fails CLOSED on CF outage; `turnstile.tsx` widget renders nothing without the site key, shared via `turnstileSiteKey` prop) gating **WhatsApp OTP send** (each send costs a real message — the highest-abuse surface); **rate limits** on every public POST (locale 30/m, cart 60/m, checkout 10/m, reviews 10/m, helpful+wishlist 30/m, returns 5/m, register 10/m; OTP already 6/m). Prod env needed: `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`/`TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`.
  - 🔴 **⚠️ GOTCHA — every `throttle:` MUST carry a prefix, or all of these share ONE bucket (fixed 2026-08-06).** _Symptom:_ a guest gets **429 Too Many Requests** at checkout after ordinary shopping; surfaced when new cart E2E specs pushed a shared counter over and the checkout spec started failing. _Root cause:_ Laravel's unnamed `throttle:max,decay` keys its counter via `ThrottleRequests::resolveRequestSignature()`, which returns **`sha1(routeDomain|IP)` — the route URI is NOT in the key**. So *every* rate-limited route shared a single per-visitor counter while each compared that shared count against its own limit, meaning the **strictest** route rejected once the visitor's **combined** requests passed it. **This was a live revenue bug: a shopper who added ~11 items to their cart (limit 60, allowed) then clicked checkout (limit 10) got a 429 and could not pay.** _Fix:_ pass the middleware's 3rd argument as a namespace — `throttle:60,1,cart`, `throttle:10,1,checkout`, `throttle:6,1,otp-send`, … — applied across **`routes/web.php` and `routes/auth.php`** so each group counts independently (which is what the per-route numbers were always meant to express). Pinned by `SecurityHardeningTest::test_cart_requests_do_not_consume_the_checkout_rate_limit` (+ the OTP twin). **Any new throttled route must include a prefix** — a bare `throttle:N,1` silently rejoins the shared bucket.

Verified by simulating the vendor-less CI locally (vendor path pointed at a nonexistent dir → `tsc` exit 0).

  - ⚠️ **Railpack build gotcha (hit 2026-07-23, FIXED): missing PHP extensions → build dies at `php artisan config:cache`.** _Symptom:_ first Railway build failed; Railpack's `install-php-extensions …` list installed `pdo_mysql redis` etc. but **not `zip`** (the FrankenPHP base image omits it), so composer's `vendor/composer/platform_check.php` fatals ("require ext-zip") on the first artisan call in the build. _Root cause:_ **`openspout/openspout`** (prod dep, Excel import/export) requires `ext-zip`/`ext-xmlreader`/`ext-xmlwriter`, and Railpack only installs the ext-* it detects, **not the transitive requirements**. Local (XAMPP) + CI (`setup-php` bundles them) both have these, so it only surfaced on Railway. _Fix:_ declare them in the **root `composer.json` `require`** — Railpack installs exactly the ext-* listed there. Added `ext-zip`, `ext-xmlreader`, `ext-xmlwriter`, and **`ext-gd`** (needed anyway for the `intervention/image` variant pipeline — `install-php-extensions gd` bundles WebP). Reproduce build cache locally with `php artisan config:cache && route:cache && view:cache && event:cache` then `optimize:clear`. _Still to verify at deploy:_ whether Railpack's start runs `migrate --force` automatically; if not, set a Railway **Pre-deploy Command** `php artisan migrate --force` (this also runs the baseline-data migration). Admin is one console step post-deploy: `php artisan db:seed --class=AdminUserSeeder --force`.

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-08 — 🎉 **Store events: named, time-boxed homepage campaigns («اليوم الوطني السعودي») as their own strip above Best Sellers.** Full design in Architecture → Store events.
> - 🔑 **The client's second message is what decided the model, and it is worth recording why.** The first ask was "a Special Offers section"; the follow-up was *"sometimes we want the event to be named… this section shouldn't mix with the discounts section, it's a section on its own"*. A name and a window cannot live on a product flag and the heading has to come from somewhere — so this is a real `store_events` table, not the `is_special_offer` boolean the first proposal recommended. **The proposal was published as an artifact and revised before any code was written**, which is what turned a two-week feature into one with no rework.
> - 🔴 **The whole feature rests on ONE scope with THREE call sites: `Product::scopeNotInRunningEvent()`.** "Shouldn't mix" has to be enforced, or a discounted product in a running event appears twice on one page. It is excluded from the discounts strip, from `/shop?on_sale=1`, and from **`hasOffers`** — the easy one to miss, since that prop drives the Offers nav item, the mobile drawer and the cart's empty-state link, so skipping it means the nav links to an empty page. **Proven to fail against broken code**: disabling the scope reddens 4 tests covering all three.
> - 🔑 **Two things already existed and were reported rather than rebuilt.** `sale_price` + `sale_starts_at`/`sale_ends_at` with a window-honouring `onSale()` scope means "a discount for the event duration only" was already shipped; and a bundle with its own stock is just a product. **Also found and fixed a naming collision I was about to create**: the existing discounts strip was titled «عروض خاصة» / "Special Offers", exactly the name the new category takes, so it was renamed to match its own nav item («العروض» / "Offers"). One word to revert.
> - 🔴 **A `BadMethodCallException` reached the browser with 17 tests green: I called `Product::saleState()`, which does not exist (it is `saleStatus()`).** I had read that method's DOCBLOCK and never its signature. Nothing caught it because the admin screens were covered only through their write endpoints — no test had ever asked the controller to render an offer row. Three render tests added, and the lesson generalises: **a controller with no test that renders it is untested, however many tests hit its writes.**
> - 🔴 **Found a PRE-EXISTING bug while fixing my own: `admin.common.save` has never existed** (the `save` key sits under `admin.contextMenu`), so the shipping carrier dialog has been rendering the literal string `admin.common.save` on its button since 2026-08-31. i18next renders a missing key verbatim, which no static gate can see. Key added; my own two call sites moved to labelled saves per the panel's convention ("Save event", "Save badge").
> - ⚠️ **The shared admin `Button` accepted no `aria-label` at all**, so every icon-only button in the panel had no accessible name and could not be found by role. Added and forwarded on both the `<button>` and `<Link>` branches.
> - ⚠️ **MariaDB rejected the migration outright:** a second non-nullable `timestamp` column gets an implicit `'0000-00-00'` default. `dateTime` instead — the products table only dodges this because its sale window is nullable.
> - ⚠️ **An existing test broke and the fix was to make it say what it means.** `ZidCatalogImporterTest` counted ALL top-level categories; the migration seeds a standalone one. Now asserts `top-level AND has children`, which is what "nav parent" actually means and is immune to the next seeded category.
> - **761 tests / 3542 assertions** ✓ (+23), **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓ — all run **bare, as `lint.yml` invokes them**. **Verified in a real browser in both locales at 1440 and 390** against a seeded National Day event: 2 cards per row at every width, 16:9 crop measured at 1.78, green accent resolved to `rgb(0,108,53)`, typed badge beside three derived «خصم N٪», paging confirmed by scrollLeft (0 → −1268, correctly negative in RTL), and the arrow enabled-states **inverting between locales** (`[false,true]` RTL / `[true,false]` LTR), which is the single strongest signal the shared RTL hook is right. Reorder verified against the DATABASE, not the DOM. Zero page errors, zero raw i18n keys, zero overflow. Demo event and prices removed afterwards, verified back to 0.
> - ⚠️ **Three of my own probe's "bugs" were the probe's**: `.last()` clicked the correctly-disabled RTL arrow, `page.request.post('/locale/en')` used a separate request context so the "English" run silently re-measured the Arabic page, and a wrong ancestor selector made a working reorder look like a no-op. **Same lesson as every previous sweep: assert on the thing, not a proxy for it.**
> - ⏭ **Open, and it needs the client: selling a bundle does not decrement the products inside it.** Fine if staff pack bundles as separate stock; if they pull from the same shelf, SMACC and the site drift apart on the most visible products in a national campaign. That is a components table, deliberately not built on a guess.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🔴 **Checking PRODUCTION before applying the merge found a defect dev could never have shown: the box option was hardcoded `is_active => true`.**
> - 🔑 **The assumption was "both halves always share the same visibility". True for all 12 pairs on dev, FALSE on production** — `catalog:hide-unphotographed` hides on photography, not on intent, so `سكري فاخر ١ كيلو - حبة` is live while its carton is hidden. Applying as written would have **put a hidden carton back on sale at 160.00**, eight times the unit price, with nothing in the output mentioning it. The option now **inherits the carton's `is_active`**, and the dry-run table gained a **"Box sold?"** column so the consequence is visible before committing to it. Proven to fail against the old behaviour.
> - **The general lesson, and it is the one worth keeping: a rule validated only against the dev catalogue is a rule validated against ONE sample.** Dev and prod diverge because *different commands have been run on each* — `hide-unphotographed` and `clean-slugs` on one, the demo purge on the other. Before any catalogue command's first production run, **dry-run it there and read the output**, rather than trusting a dev result.
> - **Production state, measured not assumed:** all **12 pairs + 4 orphans still present**; `product_options` and `redirects` both **empty**, confirming the merge (and `catalog:clean-slugs`) have never run there. **30 products are publicly visible and 5 of them are flattened halves**, including **two products listed TWICE** (a Single and a Carton side by side) — so this was customer-facing duplication, not just admin clutter. After the merge 2 pairs get a live box option and 10 get a hidden one; the visible catalogue goes 30 → 28.
> - ⚠️ **Prod slugs are NOT dev slugs**, so a 404 on a dev-derived product URL proves nothing — `clean-slugs` has run on dev and never on prod. Query `/shop/search-index` (the whole visible catalogue as JSON, no auth) instead of guessing URLs; it is the cheapest way to ask a live storefront what it is actually showing.
> - **Backup taken first, per the rule in this file:** `retab-prod-20260902-000935.sql` (43 tables, 171,937 bytes) in `Desktop\Project files\retab\backups\`, verified before proceeding — ends with its FK re-enable, Arabic survived byte-exact, and row counts match the live DB (91 products / 115 images / 8 categories / 3 users). ⚠️ `mysqldump` is still unavailable (the XAMPP MariaDB client cannot auth to MySQL 8's `caching_sha2_password`), so the dump is built with PDO over `MYSQL_PUBLIC_URL`; the service name is case-sensitive **`MySQL`**. ⚠️ Gotcha in that script: `$row` is **false** once a `while ($row = $stmt->fetch())` loop ends, so capture the column list from the FIRST row, not after the loop.
> - **724 tests / 3265 assertions** ✓ (+2), Pint ✓. No JS/TS touched, so those gates are unchanged.
> - ✅ **APPLIED TO PRODUCTION 2026-09-02**: 12 pairs merged, 4 orphans renamed, 12 redirects written, 12 carton rows soft-deleted; the publicly visible catalogue went **30 → 28** and a re-run reports "Nothing to do". **Verified in a real browser against the live site**, which is where the fix proved itself on real data: `خلاص أشيقر درجة أولى وزن 250 جرام` offers «الأصلي 5.75» + «كرتون 69.00», `سكري فاخر ١ كيلو` renders as a **plain single-price product with no box option** (its carton was hidden — under the old code that page would now be selling a 160.00 box), and the absorbed carton URL **301s to the survivor and returns 200, not 404**. Zero page errors.
> - ⏭ **Two follow-ups the merge deliberately does not decide:** `RTB-0077` Khalas Sajir's **8 units per box is the rounded guess** (184 ÷ 24 = 7.667), and `RTB-0004` / `RTB-0044` are now **two live products with the identical name** in different categories (5.00 with a box of 24, and 5.75 with a box of 12). Both are pricing decisions for the client, not code.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🖼️ **RTB-0068 and RTB-0069 are PROMOTIONAL BUNDLES, not gift boxes — and the only way to find that out was to look at the photograph.**
> - 🔑 **The technique, which is the transferable part: when the data cannot explain a product, OPEN ITS IMAGE.** Both rows looked like debris — names identical to the plain 500g/1kg products but priced **240.00** and **280.00**, no options, no description, sitting alone in an Occasion Gifts category. Every database angle said "duplicate or junk". The image says «**لا تفوت العرض — كرتونين + والثالثة مجاني**» (*don't miss the offer, two cartons + the third free*) with the terms printed underneath. Downloading from `cdn.retab.com.sa` and reading it settled in one step what querying could not.
> - **The arithmetic then confirms it exactly:** the 500g carton is 120.00 and the bundle is **240 = 2 × 120**; the 1kg carton is 140.00 and the bundle is **280 = 2 × 140**. So the prices were never wrong — the NAMES simply never said these were bundles, which is the entire reason they read as duplicates of the plain products.
> - ⚠️ **Their images are marketing banners with the PRICE baked into the artwork** (240 / 280 / «مع الضريبة»). That is a maintenance trap: change either bundle price and the picture silently lies. Worth re-shooting as plain product photos with the offer expressed as a coupon or a named bundle, which this store already has the machinery for.
> - ⏭ **Left as-is pending the client:** whether the 2+1 offer runs on the new store at all. If it does, they need names that say so; if not, they are deletable.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🧹 **Product-list filters now survive a save or a delete, and there is a visible button to clear them.**
> - **The bug:** `toggleActive` used `back()` (which keeps the query string) but `store`, `update` and `destroy` all did `redirect()->route('admin.products.index')`, which **drops it**. So an admin filtered to "Hidden · Premium Dates", deleted one row, and was thrown back to an unfiltered page 1 — then had to rebuild the filters for every single row they were working through.
> - 🔑 **Fixed with a SESSION mirror of the last list view, not `back()`.** `back()` works for delete but not for edit, where the referer is the edit screen rather than the list — and that screen is also reached from the dashboard and from global search, where there is no filter state to carry. `index()` records the whitelisted query keys, and `listRedirect()` replays them.
> - ⚠️ **It is a mirror, never a sticky preference.** `index()` OVERWRITES it on every visit, so arriving from the sidebar with no query string records none and the admin is never shown filters they did not just set. Pinned by a test.
> - ⚠️ **Only whitelisted keys are stored** (`search`, `category`, `status`, `sort`, `direction`, `per_page`, `page`), so a stray query param can never be replayed into a later redirect. Also pinned.
> - **Plus a visible "Clear search and filters" button** in the filter bar, shown only when something is active. It shares one `clearFilters()` with the empty state's link, so the two cannot diverge.
> - **741 tests** ✓ (+4), all gates ✓. **Verified in a browser on a throwaway product**: filtered to it, deleted it through the real confirm dialog, confirmed it was genuinely soft-deleted, and all three filters survived.
> - ⚠️ **A verification lesson worth keeping: my first browser run "passed" without deleting anything.** The URL came back reordered (`category` before `status`), which I read as proof the redirect had run — but that ordering is just what the page's own `query()` helper emits, and the modal had never been confirmed. Checking `deleted_at` in the database is what exposed it. **A URL that merely looks different is not evidence that the write happened.**
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🔍 **"I search the SKU and can't find it" — the search was never broken; a category filter was silently hiding the match.**
> - **Proven, not assumed** (`tests/Feature/Admin/ProductSearchFilterTest.php`): the predicate does cover `sku` and `smacc_sku`, and searching `RTB-0068` alone finds it. But the list **keeps the category filter when you search**, so the same query under a Premium Dates filter returns nothing — and RTB-0068 is in Occasion Gifts. The empty state then said only *"No products."*, which is true and useless.
> - **Fix is the empty state, not the query.** Scoping a search to the active filter is correct behaviour; failing to say so is not. It now names the likely cause and offers one action.
> - 🔑 **That action keeps the SEARCH and drops the FILTERS** ("Search all categories instead"), rather than clearing everything. Verified in a browser: the first version cleared the search too, which threw away the SKU the admin had typed and dumped them on page 1 of the whole catalogue — technically a working "clear filters" button that does not solve the problem it appears next to. One click now finds the product with the query intact.
> - **737 tests** ✓ (+3), all gates ✓. Also corrected RTB-0044's live description, which claimed *"24 عبوة … 138 ريال"* against an actual 12-pack carton at 69.00 — now 12 packs, 3kg, 69 riyal, on production and dev. ⚠️ Note the copy quotes a PRICE, so it goes stale again the next time the carton is repriced; the durable fix is to drop the price sentence, since the page already shows it.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🧬 **`catalog:absorb <from> <into>` — folding the duplicate Zid LISTINGS that a human has to arbitrate.** Built for the type-by-type catalogue cleanup; more of these are coming (Qassim, Sukkari, Shishi).
> - 🔑 **Different job from `catalog:merge-variants`.** That one was mechanical: a "- Carton"/"- Single" pair is provably one product. These are the ones where **both listings are genuinely live on Zid at different prices with different cartons**, so only the client can say which is current. The winner is therefore **stated as an argument, never inferred**, and the keeper's price, stock and options survive completely untouched — those are the whole question.
> - **What moves:** only-empty text fills (the importer's convention), images **only with `--with-images`** and appended after the keeper's own so its primary stays primary, plus a **301 from the retired slug** because a live listing may be indexed. Soft delete, so reviews/wishlists/order lines stay valid on the retired row — and it **reports** those counts rather than moving them.
> - 🔴 **A test caught a real bug: `products.smacc_sku` is UNIQUE and a soft-deleted row still occupies the index**, so inheriting it blew up on a constraint violation. It has to be released from the retiring row *before* the keeper takes it, inside the same transaction. Worth transferring rather than dropping, since it is the key the daily SMACC import matches on — a keeper without one cannot be stock-synced.
> - **Applied to the Khalas Ushaiger line:** RTB-0004 → RTB-0044 (250g) and RTB-0009 → RTB-0003 (1kg, `--with-images`), leaving one product per weight: **250g 5.75 · 500g 10.00 · 1kg 20.00**.
> - ⚠️ **CORRECTING MY OWN REASONING, because the client had to fix it:** I recommended keeping the 5.00 listing partly because it made a flat 20 SAR/kg ladder across the three sizes. **That is not evidence** — a smaller pack costing more per kg is completely ordinary retail, so 5.75 for 250g beside 10.00 for 500g is perfectly coherent. The actual price is **5.75**. Treating tidy arithmetic as proof of correctness is the same error as deriving pack counts from price ratios.
> - ⏭ **Left live and contradictory:** RTB-0044's own description still says *"الكرتون يحتوي على 24 عبوة و سعره بـ 138 ريال"* while it sells a **12-pack carton at 69.00** — and it now sits directly above the new «الكرتون يحتوي على 12 عبوة» note on the same page. Correcting those numbers is a content edit, not a code one.

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🏷️ **The picker's default choice now names its own size («250 جم») instead of the generic «الأصلي», via a new `products.base_weight_grams`.**
> - 🔑 **This is the missing piece the whole naming discussion was blocked on.** The product price had **no weight attached to it anywhere** — `products` has no weight column (a deliberate 2026-06 decision: *"weight is descriptive only, shown in the title/description"*) and the Zid export's `weight` column is unusable (values of `1`, `0.25`, `500`, `700`, `3`, `16` all under unit "kg"). So the size lived ONLY in the product name, which is exactly why the name could not safely drop it. With the base choice self-labelling, **the weight is now free to leave the name**.
> - **Grams, not a free-text label**, matching `product_options.amount`: one number formats identically in both languages from one i18n key, where typed labels drift ("250 جرام" / "250غم" / "250g" for one thing). Nullable, and null keeps the old generic label — that is what makes it optional for the many products (nuts, boxes, pastes) with no single meaningful weight.
> - **Seeded for 23 products** by `2026_09_02_120000_seed_product_base_weights`, derived once by parsing English names then **frozen as an explicit SKU map** rather than re-parsed at run time (same reasoning as the category-tile backfill, and a reviewable list beats a regex over 79 names). Fills only nulls, so an admin correction is never overwritten.
> - ⚠️ **The seeded values are PACK SIZES read off the name, so they are right even where the PRICE is wrong** — RTB-0038/0039/0068/0069 carry carton-scale prices on single-pack names. Labelling those "250 جم" beside 240.00 makes the mismatch *more* visible, which is the right direction. All four are hidden.
> - ⏭ **It is also the missing anchor for price auto-scaling, deliberately not wired yet.** `scaleFromBasePrice` treats the SMALLEST OPTION as the base, so on a 250g product priced 5.75 the "+ 500g" preset yields **5.75 instead of 11.50** — the admin has to add 250g via "+ Custom" first, and there is no 250g preset. Pointing that function at `base_weight_grams` fixes it and is its own change.

> - ⚠️ **Harness note:** the admin form's `text()` helper renders **no `name` attribute** — it puts `id="field-<name>"` on the wrapping `<label>`. Select `#field-<name> input`, or a correct field looks missing.

> - 🔑 **It is NOT a new column. It IS `stock_units`**, the same number a box sale deducts from the shared pool. So the count a customer reads and the count the shop deducts cannot drift apart — a separate `pieces_per_box` field would be two records of one fact, and they would disagree the first time somebody edited one.
> - **"Optional" falls out of the data rather than needing a toggle:** the server ships `pieces` as null unless the option `is_box` **and** `stock_units > 1`. 1 is the default for a new box and means the count was never entered, and "contains 1 pack" tells nobody anything. The admin hint now says so in both languages, since that field became customer-facing.
> - ⚠️ **Gated on `is_box`, never on `amount === null`.** Inferring a box from a missing weight is exactly what the `add_is_box_to_product_options` migration warns against: a Custom option with no grams would be advertised as a carton.
> - ⚠️ **A test caught that the shared fixture in `ProductOptionsTest` predates the `is_box` column** — its «كرتون» option had `amount => null` but was never flagged as a box, so no test had been exercising the box path at all. Fixed in the fixture.

Five products carry that contradiction and it is now visible on one screen, so the copy needs correcting rather than just noting.

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🔴 **A pack count CANNOT be derived from price, and the merge's `stock_units` were wrong wherever a carton carries a bulk discount.** Caught by the client; confirmed against their live Zid store.
> - **The flaw:** `catalog:merge-variants` derived `stock_units` as `carton price ÷ unit price`. That equals the pack count **only when the carton has no bulk discount**. Khalas Ushaiger, verified against retabstore.com: 250g `69.00 ÷ 5.75 = 12` ✓, 500g `120.00 ÷ 10.00 = 12` ✓, but 1kg `140.00 ÷ 20.00 = 7` ✗ — it is really **8 packs**, sold at 140 against a 160 list, a **12.5% carton discount**. Under-deducting stock is the expensive direction, so this is an inventory fix rather than tidying.
> - 🔑 **It also rescues the one figure that had been flagged as an unreliable guess.** Khalas Sajir's `184 ÷ 24 = 7.667` was never a rounding problem: `8 × 24 = 192` list sold at 184 is the same discount. So 8 was right, for a reason the command had wrong. **A non-integer ratio is evidence of a DISCOUNT, not of bad data** — the command's warning wording should be read that way.
> - ⚠️ **The prices were never wrong.** Scraping the live Zid store returned 5.75/69.00, 10.00/120.00 and 20.00/140.00, matching our stored values exactly. That also **reverses an earlier reading in this file**: the product DESCRIPTIONS are the stale half, not the prices (RTB-0044's says *"24 packs at 138"* while the store sells **12 at 69**, i.e. the carton was halved and the copy never updated). Five products carry that contradiction, one of them live.
> - **Fixed by data migration `2026_09_02_100000_correct_khalas_ushaiger_carton_data`** (idempotent, SKU-scoped), which also activates the box option on the three confirmed products — their cartons all sell on Zid, and the options were only created inactive because `hide-unphotographed` had hidden the carton ROWS for lacking photography, which is a photography decision rather than a decision to stop selling cartons.
> - 🔑 **CLIENT DECISION (2026-09-02): the three sizes stay THREE SEPARATE PRODUCTS**, each keeping its own carton, rather than merging into one product with several sizes. That closes the "one box per product" blocker without needing the model changed, because each product keeps exactly one box.
> - ⚠️ **DEV AND PROD DIVERGE on these options and dev is NOT a faithful preview.** Dev was merged with the pre-fix command (every box forced `is_active = true`); production was merged after it (boxes inherit the carton's visibility, so 2 active / 10 inactive). Read the prod dry-run output, not the dev database, when predicting what a catalogue change will do there.
> - ⏭ **Two duplicates on the CLIENT'S OWN Zid store, not our import's doing:** `خلاص أشيقر درجة أولى وزن 250 جرام` at 5.75 and `خلاص أشيقر درجة أولى 250 جرام` at 5.00 (Zid SKU `RETAB078`) are both live, differing only by the word «وزن». And RTB-0009 is an unresolved near-duplicate of RTB-0003 — same price and carton, but it carries the only description and the better photos, so it must not be deleted blind.
> - ⚠️ **Useful technique: `/shop/search-index` and Zid's product JSON-LD both answer "what is this store actually selling right now" with one unauthenticated request.** Zid's JSON-LD reports only the BASE price (variants are not in it), so carton prices have to be read from the embedded payload's `slug`+`price` pairs instead.
