> **Last updated:** 2026-09-02 — 🗓️ **The `Schedular` service: a failed build that was NOT the code, and a missing credential that was.** Two unrelated problems reported as one.
> - **The build failure was a transient GitHub outage.** `composer install` died on `api.github.com/repos/vlucas/phpdotenv/zipball/… (HTTP/2 504)`. 🔑 **The diagnostic that settles this class of failure in one step: the SAME commit built and deployed fine on `retab-website`.** A code or lockfile fault cannot be selective across services building the same image, so a per-service failure on a network fetch is transient by construction. Note 504 is GitHub failing, not a rate limit (403) and not a bad lockfile (a checksum or resolution error). **Fix is simply to redeploy**, which worked.
> - ⚠️ **A failed build leaves the service reading "Online", because Railway keeps the last good deployment running.** So the symptom is not downtime, it is a service silently running OLD code. Do not read "Online" as "up to date".
> - 🔴 **The real defect a redeploy would NOT have fixed: `Schedular` was missing `TAMARA_API_TOKEN`.** Found by diffing its variable NAMES against `retab-website`. It matters more than it looks: `payments:alert-expiring` does not merely read local rows — `reconcileLapsed()` **calls Tamara**, the call is deliberately un-caught (Tamara's answer, never our clock, decides a hold is dead), and reconciliation runs **before** the alerting. So a missing token throws and takes the alert down with it. Latent only because production currently has **zero orders** after the demo purge. Token copied across and the service redeployed; `payments:alert-expiring --dry-run` now runs clean there.
> - ⚠️ **Also divergent on that service, deliberately left:** `MAIL_MAILER=log` where web has `resend` (harmless today because mail is queued and the WORKER sends it, but a footgun the moment anything sends inline), and no `MEDIA_DISK`/`R2_*` (nothing scheduled touches media yet — the same shape as the bug that wiped prod uploads).
> - ⚠️ **Three Railway CLI gotchas, all of which cost time here.** (1) **`railway logs` STREAMS and never exits**, so it hangs any timeout-bounded call; check state a different way. (2) **`RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` is not injected in this project**, so "which commit is this service on?" cannot be answered from variables — **grep the deployed file for a marker unique to the commit** instead (`railway ssh --service X -- grep -c INHERIT app/…`), which confirmed all three services were current. (3) **`railway ssh -- grep -c "two words" file` splits on the space** despite the quotes and treats the second word as a filename; use a single-word pattern. (The older `tinker --execute` mangling is unchanged and still applies.)
> - ⚠️ **The CLI's project link drops between sessions and fails QUIET**, returning scaffold defaults rather than an error — re-link with `railway link -p "Retab Stores Main Website" -e production -s <service>` and confirm with `railway status` before trusting any variable read. Already documented on 2026-08-11; it happened again.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-09-01 — 📦 **The Zid catalogue's flattened "- Carton"/"- Single" product pairs merged into one product with a Box option: 91 → 79 products.** New `php artisan catalog:merge-variants {--apply}` (dry-run by default), applied to dev.
> - 🔑 **Correcting this file, which was actively misleading: "No variants (DECIDED)" has been FALSE since 2026-08-23.** `product_options` shipped that day — sizes, a box, auto-scaled prices, shared stock deducted by `stock_units`, admin editor, storefront picker, cart, order snapshots, tests — and the decision it supersedes was never struck out. **Zero of the 91 products actually used it**, so the catalogue was still selling the same dates as two rows at wildly different prices (Sukkari 500g at **11.50 and at 138.00**). The feature was built; only the data had never been migrated.
> - 🔑 **The SINGLE survives, never the carton**, because a product's own `price` means "the always-present default choice the storefront opens on" once options exist — which is exactly what a per-unit price is. The carton becomes a hand-priced Box option beside it. The survivor also **keeps its slug** (already indexed, and renaming costs ranking for nothing); only the absorbed carton's slug needs a home, and it gets a **301 to the survivor** rather than a 404.
> - ⚠️ **`stock_units` is DERIVED, not known, and it is real money.** The two rows each counted the same physical dates, so only the single's stock survives as the shared pool and the box then consumes N units per sale — N taken from the price ratio, the only evidence in the data. `OrderConfirmationService` decrements by `stock_units × quantity`, so leaving it at the default 1 would sell a carton of twelve and deduct one. One of the twelve pairs does not divide cleanly (Khalas Sajir, **184 ÷ 24 = 7.67 → 8**) and the command reports every such guess for the client to correct.
> - **So `stock_units` is now editable in the options editor — on the BOX row only.** It was in the DB and consumed at confirm time but hidden in the UI ("stock handling is deferred"), which is fine while nothing has a box and wrong the moment something does. ⚠️ A weight option still consumes 1, so a 1kg size under a 250g base under-deducts — pre-existing, unchanged here, and worth closing when size stock is built.
> - ⚠️ **Category is part of the grouping key, and that is not tidiness:** "Khalas Ushaiger Grade 1 250g" is a real pair in **both** Premium Dates (5.75 / 69.00) and Assorted Products (5.00 / 120.00). Grouping on the name alone collapses four rows into one product. **Proven by test** — removing the category from the key reddens that case.
> - 🔴 **THE WORD "BOX" AND THE WORD "CARTON" ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE HERE, and conflating them would wreck nine real products.** The catalogue uses **بوكس** for **9 genuine gift-box products** (بوكس عجوة المدينة, Golden Date Box, Premium Sagai Wooden Box…) where the word *is* the product's identity, and **كرتون** only ever for a shipping carton, i.e. the flattening artifact. So the match is anchored to a trailing ` - <word>` (which accepts the full vocabulary, since a trailing suffix is unambiguous), with a **mid-name fallback that accepts `carton`/`كرتون` ONLY**. Pinned in both directions: adding `box`/`بوكس` to the mid-name list reddens the gift-box test. The command also refuses, rather than guesses, when a group holds two cartons or two singles.
> - ⚠️ **A miss I shipped and the client caught within the hour: the Zid export puts the marker mid-name in Arabic with no dash at all** — `سكري درجة اولى كرتون ١،٥٠٠ كيلو` against a tidy `Grade 1 Sukkari 1.5kg - Carton` in English. The first pass cleaned only the English half, leaving **the two languages disagreeing on the storefront's DEFAULT language**. Worth internalising: *a rule derived from the English names does not automatically hold for the Arabic ones*, and on an Arabic-first store the Arabic half is the one that matters. Token matching, not a `\b` regex — PHP's `/u` does not imply `PCRE_UCP`, so `\w`/`\b` stay ASCII and land in the wrong place in Arabic.
> - ⚠️ **The orphan table printed identical "was" and "becomes" values** for that row, because it preferred the English name and only the Arabic changed — a real fix reading as a no-op. It now shows whichever name actually changes.
> - **4 unpaired cartons are RENAMED ONLY** — no per-unit price exists for them, and inventing one would be making data up. They keep a carton-scale price (Shishi 1kg at 161.00) and the command says so; all four are hidden, so nothing misleading is live. 🔴 They need a unit price + a Box price by hand before publishing.
> - **722 tests / 3261 assertions** ✓ (+10), `tsc` ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, eslint ✓ — all run **bare, as `lint.yml` invokes them** — client+SSR builds ✓, **41/41 E2E** ✓. Four claims were **proven to fail against broken code** before being accepted (category scoping; derived `stock_units`; mid-name detection; and the reverse, that including `box` breaks the gift boxes). **Verified in a real browser in both locales**: the admin's new Units/box column lines up with its header to the pixel (`[281,423,565,653,757,845]` on both rows), the box input round-trips 12, the storefront renders «الأصلي 5.75» / «كرتون 69.00» (and `Original`/`Box` in English) with the Tamara estimate following the selection, and adding the box lands a **69.00** cart line labelled «كرتون». Zero raw i18n keys, zero overflow, zero page errors.
> - ⚠️ **Bash heredoc trap, second occurrence** (first was 2026-08-31): a `cat <<'PHP'` carrying mixed quotes died with `unexpected EOF while looking for matching '` and wrote **nothing**. The Write tool sidesteps shell quoting entirely — reach for it rather than fighting the heredoc. `sed`/`perl` substitutions against lines containing `|` and `$` silently no-op'd for the same reason; use Edit for a precise one-line change.
> - ⏭ **Left for the client, deliberately:** RTB-0004 and RTB-0044 are now **two live products with the identical English name** in different categories (5.00 vs 5.75, box 24 vs 12) — the duplicate family the 2026-09-01 catalogue diagnosis already flagged, and a naming/pricing decision rather than a code one. Survivor slugs still read `…-حبة` ("single"); `catalog:clean-slugs` is the tool if that is ever worth a redirect. **Not yet run on production.**

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-01 — 🛡️ **Publish guard: an incomplete product can never be live, and the dashboard says how many are stuck.** Full design in Architecture → Product publish guard.
> - **Enforced on the MODEL, not the controller**, because `is_active` is written from four places and a controller check covers one of them. The one that would have bitten silently is a **change-log revert**, which writes a whole previous row back and would re-publish a product that has since lost its image. There is a test for exactly that.
> - 🔴 **It fixed a live bug rather than only adding a rule:** `update()` used to THROW when a product had no images, so the five image-less products — precisely the ones needing attention — **could not be edited at all**. The guard hides instead of refusing, so work in progress always saves. The test that asserted the old behaviour was renamed and inverted, which is the honest signal the contract changed.
> - 🔑 **The English name is an advisory, not a gate, and that was decided by measuring rather than by taste.** Requiring it reddened **59 tests across 19 classes**; not requiring it, **7**. It also contradicts this app's own bilingual contract (AR required, EN nullable, storefront falls back to AR), and production was unaffected either way since all 91 products already have one. It is still badged and still counted — kept in a separate `publishAdvisories()` so a gate and a nudge cannot be confused.
> - ⚠️ **A not-yet-existing record satisfies the image rule on purpose.** Images are a separate table attached after the insert, so enforcing at insert time makes `Product::create([... 'is_active' => true])` unsatisfiable in one statement for every seeder, importer and test. The rule still bites on the create form (images required), on every later save, and when the last image is deleted.
> - 🔴 **The SQL scope and the PHP check are twins and must agree** — a product one flags and the other does not would be absent from the "needs completing" list while refusing to publish, which reads as a broken toggle. Pinned by a test asserting the two sets are identical.
> - **691 tests / 3164 assertions** ✓ (+13), and the guard tests were **proven to fail against the old code** (removing the hook reddens 7 of 12). `tsc` ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, eslint ✓ — all four run **bare**, as `lint.yml` invokes them — **41/41 E2E** ✓. **Verified in a real browser in both locales**: the tile reads 9, the filter returns those same 9, all nine toggles are `aria-disabled`, and a forced toggle against `RTB-0074` left it hidden with `missing=price,image`. Zero raw i18n keys, zero page errors, zero overflow.

> - 🔑 **Third ask was "make sure the courier collects from this shop", and the portal now SHOWS the answer rather than asserting it:** the pickup address lives in OTO, not here. ⚠️ **Correcting a claim made in the same session:** OTO *does* expose `createPickupLocation` and `updatePickupLocation`, so this is read-only **by choice, not by necessity** — moving where a courier turns up is a one-time account decision, and a panel that can silently change it is a worse failure than one that only reports it. Implementation: `getPickupLocationList` is read once per cached listing and rendered as *"Couriers collect from …"* under the city controls. Showing it beats asserting it, and it stays true without anyone remembering to re-check.
> - 🔑 **The COUNT is displayed as deliberately as the address.** With exactly one location OTO assigns it automatically, which is the only reason `pushOrder()` can safely omit `pickupLocationCode`. More than one makes that omission a real ambiguity, so the panel warns when it sees a second. That was already flagged as a watch item on 2026-08-15; it is now visible rather than filed away.
> - ⚠️ **Folded into the SAME cached payload as the rates**, so the collection address costs no extra round trip on a page load, and wrapped in its own catch: 🔴 **a pickup-location failure must never cost a good carrier listing**, since it is a separate OTO call on the less important half of the page. **Proven by removing the guard and watching the test go red**, not assumed.
> - ⚠️ **The mock had to stub `pickupLocations()` explicitly.** `safePickupLocations()` catches everything, so an unmocked call was silently swallowed and every assertion about the collection address would have passed vacuously — a test green for the wrong reason, which is worse than a red one.
> - ✅ **BOTH open questions answered the same day, from the client's OTO account.** It lives at **Settings → Management → Sender Locations** — OTO's own name for a pickup location, which is why neither their API docs nor a search for "pickup" found it in the interface. Worth writing down: **Sender Locations, not Pickup Locations.**
>   - **The collection address is already correct.** Exactly **one** entry, Active: *RRMD7708, 7708, King Fahad Rd, 4193, Al Malqa, 13521, Riyadh*, contact سعود الجلعود. That is the Al Malqa shop, the city matches `OTO_ORIGIN_CITY`, and 🔑 **being the only one is what makes `pushOrder()` safe to omit `pickupLocationCode`** — the auto-assignment has nothing to be ambiguous about. Nothing to change.
>   - **The phone question resolves too:** `966550883845` is the contact on that sender location, so it is the company's operational number rather than the closed Al Aziziyah branch's. `store_phone` and the footer `contact_phone` are fine as they are.
> - ⏭ **One small discrepancy left, customer-facing:** OTO records the shop's postcode as **13521** while `BranchController` says **11564**. 13521 is Al Malqa's national-address code and 11564 is an older Riyadh-wide one, so OTO's is probably the right one, but the branches page shows this address to customers — worth confirming with the client before editing rather than guessing. Also optional: the sender location is named «موقع الإرسال الخاص بي» ("my sending location"), which tells a reader nothing and is what `/admin/shipping` displays; renaming it in OTO to «رطاب للتمور، الملقا» would make that line self-explanatory.
> - **659 tests / 3104 assertions** ✓ (+2), **96 JS** ✓, **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. **Verified in a browser in both locales**: exactly one card, zero mentions of Al Aziziyah, the map iframe and the Directions link both pinned to the listing's coordinates, the card centred (385px gutters either side) rather than stranded in a half column, no overflow at 390px, and the collection line rendering the Arabic address with correct bidi.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — 🏙️ **Destination city becomes a bilingual combobox: ~95 Saudi cities, searchable in Arabic or English, always submitting the English name.**
> - 🔑 **The load-bearing decision is that the two languages are not symmetric.** Arabic is how you FIND the city; English is what gets SENT. OTO matches the string on their side, their documented examples are English transliterations ("ar rass", "Riyadh"), and `OTO_ORIGIN_CITY` is English — so «جدة» submits `Jeddah`, whether it was picked from the list or typed by hand and resolved through `exactCity()`. Sending Arabic would be a guess about a matcher we cannot inspect.
> - 🔑 **Reuses `normalize()` from `lib/search.ts` rather than re-rolling Arabic folding.** «الاحساء» finds «الأحساء», «جده» finds «جدة», "Al-Khobar" finds "Al Khobar". Those are spelling differences, not typos, exactly as the storefront search entry argues — and one normalizer serving both features is what stops a second copy drifting.
> - ⚠️ **Free text, not a select, and that is the safety valve.** An unrecognised value is submitted as typed, so a city missing from our list is an annoyance rather than a wall. There is deliberately no "invalid city" state: we do not know OTO's vocabulary, so refusing a value on our own authority would be wrong. ⏭ **`getCities` exists on every OTO plan** and would guarantee the spellings match theirs — not used because a typeahead that waits on a network round trip is worse than one that answers instantly, the Arabic names have to be ours regardless, and the page has to keep working when OTO is unreachable (the permanent state in dev).
> - ⚠️ **Escape had to be scoped, or it would have broken the page it sits on.** The field stops propagation only while its list is open, so one press closes the suggestions and a second reaches the window handler that closes the carrier detail view. Verified in that exact order rather than assumed. Suggestions also commit on **`onMouseDown`**, not `onClick`, because the input's blur closes the list before a click can land.
> - **96 JS tests** ✓ (+14), **657 PHP** ✓, **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. **Driven in a browser in both locales at 1440 / 1024 / 390**: majors on focus, "jed" narrowing to one, three Arabic queries including the hamza-less spelling, a non-Saudi city offering nothing, clicking an Arabic row producing `?city=Jeddah`, arrow-key navigation, both Escape cases, dropdown aligned to the input at every width, zero overflow, zero page errors.
> - ⚠️ **My probe reported two navigation failures that were its own impatience** — it read `page.url()` after a fixed 1200ms sleep, and the Inertia visit lands at ~600ms only sometimes within that window. `waitForURL` on the actual condition, not a timer. That is the third distinct false positive this probe has produced (after `parseFloat('—')` and `innerText` applying `text-transform`), and all three shared a cause: **asserting on a proxy rather than on the thing itself.**

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — 🗂️ **Shipping portal rebuilt as a card grid that opens into a list-and-detail view, once production turned out to offer 15 carriers rather than 2.** Design agreed from a mockup first (three directions, client picked compact cards plus the detail pane).
> - ✅ **THE PLAN QUESTION IS SETTLED, and the answer is do nothing: the live account lists 15 available carriers.** Carrier choice already works, the picker has real options, and logos and pickup cut-off times are coming back, which means `getDeliveryOptions` is answering rather than the rate-check fallback. Everything above about the 1-vs-3 own-contract cap was right in the only sense that mattered: it never touched this store.
> - 🔑 **The redesign was forced by that number, not by taste.** The original two-column cards were sized for the two carriers dev could show. At sixteen they were roughly **2,400px of scrolling** with no sort, no summary and no way to compare two prices without holding them in your head — on a page whose entire job is "which courier is cheapest to this city, and may we use it".
> - **Grid is the default and detail opens in place.** One state (`selectedKey`), and 🔑 the open carrier is **derived from the filtered list**, so hiding it with a filter returns you to the grid automatically rather than stranding you on a row nobody can see. The ✕ and Escape both close.
> - ⚠️ **Three interaction traps, all handled deliberately:** the card cannot be a `<button>` because it contains the toggle (nested buttons are invalid HTML, so the NAME is the button and the card click is a mouse extra); the toggle needs `stopPropagation` or one press both switches and opens; and the rail hides below `lg`, exactly where the sidebar stops being a drawer.
> - 🔴 **`prettyName()` is deliberately conservative and the naive version is wrong.** Splitting every lowercase-to-uppercase boundary fixes `adwarLogestechs` but breaks `AyMakan` → "Ay Makan" and `iMile` → "I Mile". Requiring 3+ characters on both sides separates the first and leaves the other two alone, and the leading capital is only applied when a split actually fired, so `iMile` keeps its lowercase i. **All four cases are asserted in the browser pass**, not reasoned about.
> - 🔑 **Delivery times are rebuilt through i18n, not reformatted.** Pulling the numbers out of `1to4WorkingDays` and feeding `daysRange` means Arabic reads «من 1 إلى 4 أيام»; tidying the English string in place would have left English on the Arabic panel. Uses `{{n}}`/`{{from}}`/`{{to}}`, never `{{count}}`, which i18next reserves for pluralization.
> - ⚠️ **Two nits only the screenshots caught.** `dir="rtl"` on a **block** element also right-ALIGNS it, so the Arabic carrier name jumped to the far edge of the detail header instead of sitting under the Latin one — the dir belongs on the value's own `<span>` (the same contact-info lesson as 2026-08-11). And the summary read *"Switched on 11 of 12 available carriers are switched on"*, because a label was placed in front of a value that was already a full sentence; `usableCount` is now a bare `{{n}} of {{total}}`.
> - ⚠️ **My browser probe reported three bugs that did not exist, and both causes generalise.** `parseFloat('—')` is `NaN` for the one carrier with no price, and every `NaN <= x` is false, so a correctly sorted list failed a monotonic check — that carrier sorting **last** is the right behaviour and now has its own assertion. And **Chrome's `innerText` applies `text-transform`**, so section headings styled `uppercase` render as "SERVICES" and a case-sensitive `/Services/` reported two missing sections that were plainly on screen.
> - **657 tests / 3076 assertions** ✓, **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓ — all four static gates run **bare**, as `lint.yml` invokes them. **Verified in a real browser at 1440 / 1024 / 390 in both locales**: 13 cards price-sorted, all six display fixes, the toggle firing without opening, rail switching, ✕ and Escape, the filter and search carrying into the rail, the rail's visibility flipping at exactly `lg`, zero overflow and zero page errors.
> - ⚠️ **Verification method worth reusing: the populated page is inspected by PRIMING THE CACHE, never by stubbing code.** `CarrierDirectory::live()` reads the cache first, so a realistic listing written straight into it renders the populated branch with no outbound request and no application code touched. 🔴 Dev deliberately holds no `OTO_*` vars and the live token must never be pasted in — it points at the client's real account, where `createOrder` dispatches an actual courier. Scripts are `prime-carriers.php` / `unprime-carriers.php`; the register was restored to its two seeded exclusions afterwards.
> - ⏭ **Not built:** no card/table toggle (direction A from the mockup, a sortable comparison table, is the natural next step if the grid ever feels loose — the products page's localStorage view switch is the precedent), no keyboard arrow navigation in the rail, and the tracking link still is not surfaced on the order page.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — 🚚 **Shipping carriers portal at `/admin/shipping`: live prices from OTO, an enable switch that actually bites, and the support contacts nobody could find.** Full design in Architecture → Shipping carriers portal.
> - 🔑 **The design question was what "activate a shipping service" can honestly mean, and OTO's own docs settle it: there is NO deactivation endpoint.** Its API can list carriers (`dcList`) and activate one (`dcConfig`/`dcActivation`), but activation means attaching the merchant's own carrier contract with its credentials — a dashboard job, not something to drive from this panel. So availability and prices are read live from OTO, and the switch is **ours**: whether Retab will quote and ship with a courier. The page says exactly that rather than implying a reach it does not have.
> - 🔴 **A real pre-existing bug fell out of it: only HALF the recorded shipping decision was implemented.** The brief has said *"No Aramex, no DHL"* since the OTO integration was designed, but `OtoGateway` carried a hardcoded `stripos($carrier, 'aramex')` and nothing for DHL — so **DHL was being quoted and could win the automatic cheapest-carrier pick**, with no way to change either exclusion without a deploy. Both are now seeded rows the client controls. ⚠️ **Seeding them in the migration is load-bearing**, because the register fails open: an empty table would have silently un-banned Aramex the moment this shipped.
> - 🔑 **The switch had to bite, or this is a settings page that repaints a pill.** The filter sits in `getDeliveryOptions()`, the one path **both** the manual picker and the automatic cheapest pick go through — `ShippingService::resolveOption` re-quotes even for a manual choice. **Three tests were proven to fail** against a disabled filter (including one where the banned carrier is also the cheapest, which is the case that costs money), and two more against a `sync()` that re-enabled a disabled carrier. A test that passes either way would have been worthless here.
> - ⚠️ **Keying on the carrier NAME is the fragile part, and it is handled explicitly.** OTO reports the same courier differently across endpoints ("SMSA" vs "SMSA Express"), so a raw-name key would let a disabled carrier return as an unrecognised, and therefore allowed, one. `normalizeKey()` strips punctuation and noise words; `DHL` and `DHL Express` both land on `dhl`. Keep the noise list tight — every word added risks collapsing two real couriers onto one key.
> - ⚠️ **Two OTO endpoints, and the fallback matters more than it looks.** `getDeliveryOptions` is the rich catalogue but is **gated on the account's plan tier** (Starter and above); `checkOTODeliveryFee` works everywhere. Treating a plan refusal as "no carriers available" would have shown the client an empty portal implying their shipping was broken, when orders ship fine. The page reports which kind of data came back, derived from the payload rather than asserted.
> - 🔑 **OTO being unreachable is DATA, not an exception.** Dev has no OTO credentials at all, so the failure path is the default local experience — and it is a usable page: the switches, the notes and the support numbers all still render under a banner explaining the outage. Verified in a browser, because that is the state a client is most likely to hit.
> - 🔴 **A bug only the Arabic browser pass could find: OTO's delivery times were bidi-scrambled**, "1 to 2 Working Days" rendering as "to 2 Working Days 1" because the leading digit is neutral. Fixed with **`dir="auto"`** rather than `ltr`, so an Arabic string from a future account still reads correctly — and safe only because that element carries no `ltr:` utilities.
> - ⚠️ **Support phone and email are deliberately seeded BLANK** while websites are pre-filled. A wrong website is obvious the moment someone clicks it; a wrong support number sends staff to a stranger. 🔴 **The client should give the seeded websites a once-over** — they are public best-effort defaults, not values verified against their own OTO contracts, and every field is editable.
> - **657 tests / 3076 assertions** ✓ (+16), **41/41 E2E** ✓ (the admin layout is shared by every panel page), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓ — all four static gates run **bare**, as `lint.yml` invokes them. **Verified in a real browser in both locales at 1440 and 390**: the toggle round-trips end to end (pill flips, flash names the consequence), the edit dialog renders its eight fields, no raw i18n keys, no horizontal overflow, zero page errors.
> - ⚠️ **Verification note worth reusing: the populated page was inspected by PRIMING THE CACHE, not by stubbing code.** `CarrierDirectory::live()` checks the cache first, so a realistic listing written straight into it renders the populated branch with no outbound request and no application code touched. 🔴 Dev deliberately has no `OTO_*` vars, and the live token must never be pasted in — it points at the client's real account, where `createOrder` would dispatch an actual courier. The primed rows were removed afterwards and the register is back to the two seeded exclusions.
> - ⚠️ **My own probe reported four bugs that did not exist**, matching `admin.shipping.index`/`refresh`/`toggle`/`update` as raw i18n keys when they are **Ziggy route names** in a JSON script tag — scan `body.innerText`, not `page.content()`. Likewise `document.documentElement.dir` is the STOREFRONT session locale (always `rtl` here); the panel sets its own `dir` on `.admin-shell`.
> - 🔴 **CORRECTION, same day: I asserted "the store is on OTO's Free tier" and that was NOT established.** It came from reading a sidebar button labelled *"OTO Modules — Free"* in the client's OTO screenshot as a plan badge; it sits in the nav as a promo for an add-on, and a plan badge would normally live by the account name. **The tier is unconfirmed** — corrected in `OtoGateway::listServices` and above. The reasoning it supported (fall back rather than treat a refusal as "no carriers") stands on its own, since the endpoint is plan-gated either way.
> - 📋 **OTO plan facts, from their own docs, worth not re-deriving.** Tiers: **Free 0 · Starter 59 · Scale 279 · Pro 1,599 SAR/month**, Enterprise custom, **−15% yearly**. `getDeliveryOptions` is documented for *"Starter Package, Scale Package, Enterprise Package, Marketplaces"*, i.e. **not Free**, which is exactly why `listServices` falls back.
> - 🔑 **THE STRUCTURAL FACT THE WHOLE PLAN QUESTION TURNS ON: every plan limit OTO publishes is measured in YOUR OWN CARRIER CONTRACTS, not in carriers you can ship with.** The tiers grant *"30 / 200 / 1,000 fee-free shipments per month using your own contracts"* (then 0.9 / 0.7 / 0.5 SAR per extra) and *"1 / 3 / unlimited shipping contracts"* — that last figure is the same *"1 DC for the Free package, 3 DC for the Starter package"* the carrier-integration docs quote against `dcActivation`, which exists to attach a contract **you** hold. Shipments on **OTO's own rates** are described as unlimited on every plan, because OTO earns its margin in the shipping price rather than the subscription.
> - 🔴 **Retab has no carrier contracts and ships on OTO's rates**, so on current evidence **none of those caps bite** and the Free plan may be entirely adequate. `OtoGateway` calls **`checkOTODeliveryFee`**, documented as *"shipping prices offered by OTO rates"*; the other endpoint, `checkDeliveryFee`, is *"contract based pricing using your own rates"* and we never call it. ⚠️ **Correcting an earlier note in this same entry**, which read the 1-DC cap as a limit on carrier choice and concluded Free "makes the whole carrier-choice premise moot" — that conflated the two rate models. What Starter actually buys Retab is `getDeliveryOptions` (richer portal metadata), and nothing else that applies today.
> - ⚠️ **Two number caveats before quoting any of this at the client.** The per-shipment fees (0.9 / 0.7 / 0.5 SAR) and the 30 / 200 / 1,000 allowances come from a help article whose PRICES are stale (it lists Starter at 99 and Scale at 399 where the live pricing page says 59 and 279), so the allowances want confirming too. And **the count of carriers actually offered on OTO rates for this account is still unverified** — see the diagnostic below, which settles it in one page load.
> - 🔑 **The new portal is itself the diagnostic for all of this.** Production has the OTO credentials, so opening `/admin/shipping` there answers three questions at once that no amount of reading can: how many carriers the account actually gets, whether rate lookups work at all (never yet verified against the live account — the picker has only ever been exercised against mocks), and whether `getDeliveryOptions` is refused (the page shows its "basic rates only" notice when it is).
> - ⏭ **Not built:** per-carrier performance stats (`orders.carrier` + `shipping_cost` are already recorded, so on-time rate and real cost per courier are a query away and would make the enable decision evidence-based), the tracking link is not yet surfaced on the order page, and there is no alert when a carrier the store relies on stops being offered.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — 🤍 **Premium lines re-shot on a light backdrop: 10 images, 13 SKUs, new `photoshoot-light-2026-08` map group.** Same products, same props, same framing as `photoshoot-2026-08`; only the backdrop changed from grey stone to pale plaster.
> - 🔑 **The whole job was the MAPPING, because this delivery arrived with generic filenames** (`dates image (1..11).png`) where the rusks set was SKU-keyed. That difference is exactly what `image-maps.php` already praises SKU naming for, and it is worth asking the client for next time: it turned a copy-the-map task into an identification task. Every file had to be matched to the shot it replaces by pack, prop placement and framing, against the 24 existing sources.
> - **Method that made it tractable: two GD contact sheets** (11 new, 24 old) read side by side, then higher-resolution side-by-sides for the pairs the sheets could not separate. Most matched instantly on packaging; three needed the second pass.
> - 🔴 **The hard pair, and where to look first if photos ever appear swapped: `dates image (6)` and `(7)` are the SAME yellow-label vacuum packaging for two DIFFERENT varieties** (دخيني RTB-0091 vs خلاص أشيقر RTB-0004/0044). The printed variety name is illegible even cropped to native resolution and upscaled, so they were separated on **composition instead**: (7) reproduces retab-20 exactly (label upright, green nutrition table to its left, squarer pack) and (6) reproduces retab-21 (label tilted, taller pack), with the dallah, glass and palm frond in identical positions. These are the same setups re-lit, so the pairing is sound — but it is an inference from framing, not from reading the pack.
> - ⚠️ **`dates image (2)` is deliberately UNUSED.** It is the same سكري فاخر shot as (3) in a warmer treatment; the client picked the lighter (3). Two near-identical photos on one product would only make a shopper swipe between the same picture twice.
> - **Three images each serve TWO SKUs**, which is why the files are named for the shot they replace (`light-20`) rather than for a SKU — a SKU filename could never be 1:1 here. Those pairs are the كرتون/حبة duplicates the Zid variant flattening left behind, exactly as in the dark set. They live in their own folder, so `light-20` cannot collide with the `retab-NN` matchers despite both being two digits.
> - **Encoding decided by measurement, not habit:** sources are **1254×1254, BELOW the `detail` variant's 1400px**, so they are converted at native size — upscaling would invent detail. WebP **q82** (matching the rusks pipeline) gives **22.5 MB → 1.6 MB**. q88 and q92 were measured and rejected: q88 costs +40% size for a 17% lower mean deviation and q92 another +35% for 14%, while the worst-pixel error barely moves (59 → 51 → 54). Mean deviation at q82 is 2.5/255 on photographic texture, which is higher than the 0.72 a smooth gradient plate achieved and entirely expected — these are dates, fabric and hammered brass, not a gradient.
> - ⚠️ **This covers 10 of the 24 photoshoot shots**, so the remaining premium lines (اقط، دبس، طحينة، قهوة، مكسرات، مقلوبة، تمرية، الذهبية، قدوع، the cakes) keep the dark backdrop and **the catalogue is visually mixed until a further delivery arrives**. Flagged to the client rather than held back.
> - ⚠️ **Same destructive caveat as every other group: the importer REPLACES a product's images**, and re-running `photoshoot-2026-08` afterwards would put the dark photos back on these 13 SKUs. `photoshoot-light-2026-08` is the only group to run for them from here on.
> - **Verified against what will actually be SERVED, not against the source files:** dry run resolved **13/13 SKUs with zero unmatched warnings**, the real import stored 13 images, each product ended with exactly one image on a fresh UUID, all 10 product directories carry 4 files (original + thumb/card/detail), and a final contact sheet built from the stored **card variants** confirmed every SKU got the right photo and that all three duplicate pairs correctly share theirs. `pint --test` and `format:check` run **bare**, per this morning's lesson.
> - ⏭ **Not done:** the light set has not been pushed to production — it is a `catalog:import-images` run on the web service, same as the rusks. And `dates image (2)` stays out of the repo entirely rather than being committed unused.
