> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — ☑️ **Change log gained multi-select, with bulk revert and bulk delete.** Asked for as parity with nuor-steel, but retab's change log is **v2** (conflict detection, reverts as first-class mirror entries) where nuor-steel's marks `reverted_at` on the original, so this is adapted rather than ported. Its controller was read for the reasoning, not the code.
> - 🔴 **NEWEST-FIRST ordering is the whole feature, and it is proven rather than asserted.** A revert writes the entry's `old_data` back, so for two entries A (older) and B (newer) on one record, oldest-first reverts A, then B writes pre-B over it — the state A had already produced, i.e. **A's revert is silently undone**. Newest-first lands on pre-A, which is what selecting both promised. It bites harder here than in a simpler log because `revert()` also does per-field conflict detection, so oldest-first would see the newer entry as a conflict and refuse nearly every row. **Pinned by a test that was proven to fail against `orderBy('id')`** — the price came back `20.0` instead of `10.0`, which is exactly the silent overwrite, and a wrong order still returns 200 with a success flash, so only the value tells you.
> - **Bulk revert calls the SAME `ChangeLogService::revert()` as the single-row button**, so the two can never drift, and each entry is transactional on its own. Deliberately **not** one big transaction: a partial result is a legitimate outcome, and rolling back the reverts that did succeed would be worse than reporting honestly. The flash separates *blocked by a later change* from *could not be reverted*, and ids matching no row are counted as failures rather than dropped, so the numbers always add up.
> - 🔑 **Delete is ADMIN-ONLY (client's call), and deliberately NOT a grantable permission.** Worth recording why the obvious alternative was rejected: `Permission::preset()` iterates `SCHEMA` dynamically, so a new `change_log.delete` action needs no preset edits — but the **`manager` preset names `change_log`, so every Manager would silently inherit the power to erase audit history**. Gating on `isAdmin()` (route `admin` middleware + the same `auth.user.role` check `useCan` makes) keeps it with the owner and cannot be granted away by accident.
> - 🔴 **SMACC stock-import entries are EXCLUDED from bulk delete and reported as skipped.** The Inventory page lists the last 10 import logs and offers **Undo** on each, so deleting one from here would destroy that undo permanently with nothing on either page explaining where it went. ⚠️ The partition is done **in PHP, not with a `!=` where clause**: a SQL inequality also drops NULL rows, which would silently protect entries this guard was never meant to cover.
> - ⚠️ **`reverts_log_id` is `nullOnDelete`**, so deleting an entry a later revert points at silently strips that entry's "revert of #N" badge. It cannot fail or orphan a row, but the history reads thinner afterwards — noted in the controller docblock rather than blocked, since the alternative is refusing deletes on a large share of the table.
> - **Reused `ConfirmDeleteButton` rather than extending `ConfirmDialog`.** The dialog has a `details` slot but no way to gate its confirm button, so the first instinct was to add a `confirmDisabled` prop. `ConfirmDialog`'s own docblock points at the right answer: `ConfirmDeleteButton` already renders the button, the modal, and the type-to-confirm guard. **Three i18n keys written for the abandoned approach were deleted rather than left behind** — a stale key is exactly the leftover nobody finds later.
> - ⚠️ **Selection is a `Set` keyed by ID, never by index** (the list re-renders after every action and indexes would come to point at different rows), and it **clears whenever the visible page changes** — selecting rows then paging would otherwise submit ids the admin can no longer see. The clearing effect returns the *same* set when already empty, so an ordinary Inertia response does not churn state.
> - ⚠️ **`{{n}}` not `{{count}}` in the UI strings** — i18next reserves `count` for pluralization, which under Arabic means six suffixed variants these keys do not define. Same trap as 2026-08-17. Server-side counts are interpolated for the same reason rather than going through `trans_choice`.
> - 🔴 **CI caught a style failure I had claimed was green, and the mistake is worth naming: I ran `pint --test` with an explicit FILE LIST and left the new test file out of it.** CI runs `vendor/bin/pint --test` **bare, over all 363 files**, so a filtered local run is a different check wearing the same name. The finding itself was trivial (`fully_qualified_strict_types` + `ordered_imports`: the test referenced `\App\Services\Smacc\SmaccImportService::ACTION` inline instead of importing it), but a filtered gate that reports success is worse than no gate. **Run the quality commands exactly as `lint.yml` invokes them** — `./vendor/bin/pint --test`, `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint:check` — never a hand-picked subset. The same blind spot applies to `npx prettier --check <files>` versus `npm run format:check`.
> - ⚠️ **Two bash traps worth not re-hitting.** A `python - <<'PYEOF'` heredoc carrying JSX with mixed quotes died with `unexpected EOF while looking for matching '`; writing the patch script with the Write tool sidesteps shell quoting entirely and is the better fallback. And routes: the bulk paths genuinely cannot collide here (the per-entry routes carry a trailing segment, so they differ in length), but they are still declared first — nuor-steel hit exactly that collision, where `DELETE /change-log/bulk-destroy` matched `/change-log/{id}` and tried to delete an entry named "bulk-destroy".
> - **641 tests / 2995 assertions** ✓ (+5), **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, and Pint ✓ only after the repo-wide re-run described above. **Verified in a real browser in both locales**, including a genuine end-to-end delete rather than just the dialog: three throwaway entries created, selected, typed-to-confirm, deleted, flash read back as *"Deleted 3 log entries."*, rows gone, and the table back to its original 25. Arabic renders `dir=rtl` with every string translated and no raw i18n keys in either language; zero page errors.
> - ⏭ **Not built:** selection does not survive pagination (deliberate, see above), there is no "select all matching this filter" across pages, and bulk revert is capped at 50 with delete at 200 — raise those only alongside a queued job, since both run inline in the request today.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-31 — 🚦 **Rate limits get the branded error page (with a live countdown), and the branded page finally speaks the visitor's language — it never had.** Prompted by a raw Laravel 429 appearing on a local visit.
> - **The gap was narrow and entirely customer-facing: `429` was missing from the handler's status list** (`[403, 404, 500, 503]`), so every rate limit fell through to the framework's bare page. That list guards **~25 throttled routes**, including cart, checkout, coupon, contact, reviews and OTP, i.e. a shopper mid-purchase. The page, the wiring and the copy for the other statuses had existed since 2026-08-23; only 429 was unhandled.
> - **The page now counts down**, seeded from the response's own **`Retry-After`** rather than a guessed number, and rendered as a `M:SS` clock instead of a sentence — which sidesteps Arabic pluralization entirely (i18next would want six suffixed variants for an "N seconds" string). When the header is absent the timer is omitted rather than invented; at zero the copy flips to "you can try again now". ⚠️ The clock is `dir="ltr"` so bidi cannot reorder it around its colon, and deliberately carries **no `ltr:` utilities** — that variant resolves against the element's OWN direction and would switch on while the page is still Arabic (the 2026-08-11 contact-info trap).
> - **Inertia builds a FRESH response**, so `Retry-After` and the three `X-RateLimit-*` headers are copied onto the branded one by hand. Without that, swapping in a nicer page silently strips the only machine-readable signal a client or crawler backs off on.
> - 🔴 **THE REAL FIND, pre-existing and bigger than 429: the branded error page had NO shared Inertia props at all, so it ALWAYS rendered in Arabic** — 404s included, for every English visitor, since the page shipped. Measured in a browser rather than reasoned: homepage `dir=ltr`, error page `dir=rtl`, `locale` prop **absent entirely** (not wrong, *missing*). _Cause:_ `HandleInertiaRequests` shares props from inside the `web` group, but a **404 is thrown by the ROUTER before any group middleware runs**, and a **429 by `ThrottleRequests`, which Laravel's `$middlewarePriority` sorts ahead of unlisted custom middleware like `SetLocale`**. So at handler time nothing has been shared and `app()->getLocale()` is still the AR default. _Fix:_ a session-safe **`SetLocale::resolve()`** that the handler calls to set the app locale (fixing Blade's `<html dir>`) and to pass **`locale` as an explicit prop** (fixing the copy — `app.tsx` seeds i18n from it). ⚠️ It has to be **session-safe**: on the 404 path `StartSession` has not run either, so asking for a session throws; the long-lived plaintext `locale` cookie is the only source that still answers there.
> - 🔑 **The inline flash-back is now scoped to the ADMIN, and that asymmetry is deliberate.** A refused or too-fast admin action bounces back with a toast; a **storefront** one gets the full branded page. Reason: **the storefront mounts no global flash renderer at all** (only `admin-layout` mounts `<AdminToasts/>`), so bouncing a shopper back would make the button silently do nothing, which is worse than an error page. Pinned by a test in each direction. `password.update` is named explicitly in the check because the admin submits it from a modal whose path is `settings/password`, not `admin/*`.
> - **Dev behaviour split (client's call): `local` now brands CLIENT errors (403/404/419/429) and keeps the framework's trace for SERVER errors.** Previously the handler no-op'd in local entirely, which is both why the raw 429 appeared and why the storefront's commonest error states could only ever be seen in production. One line if it ever needs reverting.
> - 🔴 **GOTCHA that invalidated an existing test, and it generalises to any env override: `$this->app['env'] = 'production'` switches CSRF verification back ON.** `ValidateCsrfToken` opts out via `$app->runningUnitTests()`, which is literally `env === 'testing'`. So every POST/DELETE in `ErrorResponseTest` was **419ing**, and `test_a_refused_inline_action_redirects_back_with_a_flash` had been passing on the **page-expired** branch rather than the 403 branch it claims to test — a bare `assertSessionHas('error')` cannot tell the two apart. Fixed with `withoutMiddleware(ValidateCsrfToken::class)`, and every flash assertion now asserts **the message**, not merely that something was flashed.
> - ⚠️ **Three smaller testing traps worth not re-hitting.** (1) **`withCookie` ENCRYPTS the value** for `EncryptCookies` to unwrap, so for a cookie in the except-list (or on the 404 path, where that middleware never runs) it reads as garbage and silently falls back; use **`withUnencryptedCookie`**. (2) **`assertInertia` only understands a full-page response** — to an `X-Inertia` request Inertia answers with the page object as JSON, so assert `assertJsonPath('component', ...)` or you get "Not a valid Inertia response". (3) **Ignition's trace embeds the SOURCE of the file under test**, so a content assertion against a 500 matched the handler's own `'errors/error'` string and dumped a **900 KB** failure diff; turn `app.debug` off for that assertion.
> - ⚠️ **`php artisan test --filter=...` hung with no output** where `./vendor/bin/phpunit --filter=...` ran the same tests in 0.8s. Reach for phpunit directly when the wrapper stalls.
> - **All four new behaviours were proven to FAIL against the old code** before being accepted (removing 429 from the list reddens 2 tests; removing the `locale` prop reddens 2 more). Mandatory here, since a test that passes either way is exactly what let the 403 case rot.
> - **636 tests / 2983 assertions** ✓ (+7), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓. **Verified in a real browser in both locales**, which is the only gate that could have caught the Arabic-error-page bug: 404 and 429 each render the right language and `dir`, the countdown ticks (0:48 → 0:45), and there are zero page errors.
> - ⏭ **Deliberately not covered:** `419` still bounces back with a flash the storefront cannot render, left as is because the real recovery there is the reload picking up a fresh token rather than the message; and `405`/`401` still fall through to the framework page. Both are one entry in the same list if they ever show up in logs.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 🎞️ **Hero: Arabic headline lines given air (they were literally overlapping), and the carousel auto-advances every 6s.** Both client-reported.
> - 🔑 **The spacing complaint was measurable, not a matter of taste, and the measurement is the whole story.** At the shared `leading-[1.08]`, the gap from line 1's ink BOTTOM to line 2's ink TOP is **+17.2px in English and −2.8px in Arabic** at 1440 — the Arabic glyphs actually overlap. Arabic fills far more of its line box: deep descenders (ر, ك) under line 1 meeting tall ascenders (ل, أ, ك) on line 2, where Latin leaves that band empty. **Measure the INK, not the line boxes** — the em box says the two locales are identical.
>   - Fixed with `rtl:leading-[1.28]`, which buys Arabic ~0.15em of clear air at every width (11.3px at 1440 → 2.8px at 320, all positive). ⚠️ **Deliberately short of full parity with English's 0.24em:** the headline is two lines of ~70px type, and matching it outright adds ~38px to a hero whose height is the thing everyone complains about. The hero is aspect-locked so the section height did not move, and the 20px gap to the subtext is unchanged at every width.
>   - ⚠️ **A bug in my own probe worth not repeating:** the "restore" step was `h1.style.lineHeight = ''` behind an `if (lead)` guard, so it never fired and the trial value leaked into the next viewport's baseline. Two of six baseline readings were wrong before I noticed the ratio said 1.3 where the CSS says 1.08.
> - **Auto-advance: 6s** (`SLIDE_MS`, inside the 5-8s brief; measured intervals 5.9-6.1s), pausing on hover, on keyboard focus, and on a hidden tab, and **disabled entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`** — an auto-rotating carousel is exactly what that setting is for.
>   - **Any manual press restarts the clock** (client-asked, verified for the arrows specifically at 4.8s into a 6s tick: the next change lands 6.1s later, i.e. a full fresh interval, on mouse AND on touch). The effect is keyed on `index` **and a `nudge` nonce bumped by `goTo`** — ⚠️ `index` alone covers the arrows because they always move, but pressing the dot of the slide already showing sets state to the value it already holds, React bails out, nothing re-renders and the old timer keeps counting. One control in the row would silently not reset it.
>   - 🔴 **A tap paused the carousel FOREVER, and it only showed up on touch.** Measured: tapping an arrow dispatches a synthetic **`mouseenter` with no matching `mouseleave`**, so the hover-pause latched on and never released — on phones, which is most of this store's traffic. Both touch cases read as "never advanced" until the `onMouseEnter` handler was gated on `window.matchMedia('(hover: hover)')` (false on a touch device). **This is the same trap `.spotlight-card` in `app.css` is already scoped against**, which is a good argument for checking that file whenever adding a hover behaviour here.
>   - 🔴 **Bug caught by measuring: pausing on plain `focus` stopped the carousel PERMANENTLY after any click.** Clicking a dot focuses it, and moving the mouse away does not blur — 7.5s after a dot click it had still not advanced. The fix is `:focus-visible`, which is precisely the distinction wanted: a keyboard user tabbing in pauses it, a mouse click does not. Verified both ways.
>   - ⚠️ **Accessibility posture, stated rather than assumed:** WCAG 2.2.2 wants a pause mechanism for anything auto-updating past 5s. Hover + keyboard-focus pause + the reduced-motion opt-out is the mitigation here; a visible pause button would become furniture on a hero this size. Revisit if it is ever audited.
> - ⚠️ **Two harness traps, both of which produced a false "the app is broken" reading:** parking the Playwright pointer at y=880 to be "off the hero" put it **inside** it (the hero runs viewport 114–914 once the navbar is counted), so hover-pause was on for the whole run; and **`bringToFront()` does not flip `document.hidden` in headless Chromium** — no `visibilitychange` fires and `hidden` stays false, so the hidden-tab guard has to be verified by dispatching the event directly. Also worth knowing: headless Chromium does **not** default to reduced motion (`matches` is false), which I checked before blaming it.
> - **`visual-qa.spec.ts` now sets `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`.** A rotating hero means each run captures whatever slide the timer landed on, which defeats a sweep whose whole purpose is comparing runs by eye.
> - **602 tests** ✓, **55 JS** ✓, **41/41 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. Verified in a browser: ink gap re-measured at 6 widths × 2 locales, no collision with the subtext and no overflow anywhere, plus timing, hover pause, unhover resume, keyboard pause, simulated hidden tab, reduced motion, and the timer reset on **mouse arrow, touch arrow and the already-active dot**.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 🔍 **Site-wide product search in the navbar, and the matching is now Arabic-aware, typo-tolerant and cross-language.** Full design in Architecture → Storefront search.
> - ⚠️ **Correcting the premise, because it changes what had to be built:** a search box already existed — but **only on `/shop`**, and the navbar's magnifier was a plain link that dumped the shopper there. So searching from a product page meant leaving it. And the matcher was `name.toLowerCase().includes(term)` on the two name columns: no letter folding, no typos, no SKU, no category.
> - 🔑 **The single biggest win in Arabic search is NOT fuzzy matching — it is letter folding, and none of it is about typos.** أ/إ/آ vs ا, ة vs ه, ى vs ي, with or without harakat, with or without a kashida. «علبه» never found «علبة» and «سكرى» never found «سكري», and no amount of typo tolerance rescues that because nothing about it is a misspelling. Measured against the real catalogue afterwards, every cross-spelling and cross-language pair returns an **identical** count: `علبه`/`علبة`/`box`/`بوكس` → 5, `سكرى`/`sukkari` → 4, `تمور`/`dates` → 13, `٢٥٠ جرام`/`250 جم` → 4.
> - 🔴 **The trap that shaped the architecture: making the typeahead smart while the results page stayed dumb is WORSE than leaving both dumb.** `?q=` ran a raw SQL `LIKE %term%`, which cannot fold أ→ا — so a shopper would see five suggestions, press Enter, and land on "no results". The page now resolves ids from **the same cached, normalised index the typeahead uses** (free at 90 products, and the query stays in SQL so sorting and pagination are untouched).
> - **Typos are the one thing the two sides genuinely cannot share** (the client ranks with Damerau-Levenshtein; the server is a plain substring predicate). Rather than pretend otherwise, the overlay **asks whether the server could satisfy the query** and, if not, rewrites it — so "see all results" reads *«عرض كل النتائج عن «sukkari»»* and lands on 4 real results instead of an empty page. Verified end to end in a browser. If there is nothing to correct to, the link is not rendered at all.
> - ⚠️ **`normalize` exists TWICE and that is unavoidable**, because the server must normalise to build the index and the client must normalise the query. Both are pinned by **the same 18-case table**, duplicated by hand in `search.test.ts` and `SearchTest.php` — and I verified programmatically that the two tables are byte-identical, not merely that each suite passes on its own. Change one, change both.
> - **Synonyms are expanded on the PRODUCT side at index time, never on the query.** One list, in PHP, and the client's fuzzy layer sees the expansions for free. It earns its keep immediately: the catalogue writes "box" as **«بوكس»**, so «علبة» and «صندوق» match nothing without it.
> - 🔑 **An overlay, not an inline field, and the navbar's own comments are the reason.** Row 1 is a mathematically-centred three-column grid already documented as colliding with the logo at 320px when one extra control is added; row 2 is a `justify-between` nav an input would unbalance. The overlay also has room for thumbnails.
- **The icon sits beside the burger at EVERY width (client-asked, same day), and making it fit needed measuring rather than guessing.** Signed out the start cell also holds the sign-up pill, and adding the icon pushed the documented logo collision from <360px out to **−22px of overlap at 360 and −7px at 390 in Arabic** — i.e. onto the commonest phones. Fixed by reclaiming the space (row padding `px-6`→`px-4` below `sm`, tighter gap and pill padding below 430) and moving the pill's existing hide threshold 359→389. Result, measured at 8 widths × 2 locales signed in and out: **no overlap anywhere**, tightest clearance 9px at 390 Arabic, desktop numbers unchanged.
  - ⚠️ **Auto columns were measured and rejected again.** `auto 1fr auto` does clear the overlap, but it throws the 75px logo **31px off centre** in Arabic — which is exactly why the original author rejected it, and worth not re-proposing.
  - ⚠️ **Hit area is `p-1 -m-1`, not a bigger icon.** At `size-5` the target was **20×20**, under the 24px WCAG 2.5.8 floor and smaller than the 24px burger next to it. Padding takes it to 28px; the negative margin cancels the layout cost, which matters with only 9px of clearance — verified by the layout measurements coming back byte-identical. 4px of the 6px gap is absorbed, so the two hit boxes still do not overlap. _(The burger and cart are still exactly at the 24px floor — flagged in the 2026-08-19 sweep, unchanged here.)_
  - The drawer keeps its own search field: someone who opened the drawer to browse categories should not have to close it to search.
> - 🔴 **Portalled to `<body>`, and that is not optional.** The header writes `transform` on every scroll tick, and a transformed ancestor becomes the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants. The mobile drawer shipped broken exactly this way. Pinned by an E2E test that scrolls first, then opens, and asserts the overlay still measures the full viewport.
> - 🔴 **Bug found by driving it, not by testing it: Escape only worked if focus was already in the input.** Focus is moved in on a timer, so a fast Escape landed on `<body>` and the overlay stayed up **with the page behind it still scroll-locked**. Now a document-level listener. Pinned.
> - ⚠️ **Two X icons.** `input[type="search"]` draws Chrome's own unstyleable clear ✕, which landed right beside our close button — two crosses doing different things. Suppressed the native one app-wide.
> - ⚠️ **A collision I caused and caught in the same minute the client hit it:** the new static index accessor was called `index()`, and `ShopController::index()` is already the homepage action. PHP fatals on the class, so **every page 500'd**. `php -l` catches this instantly; a `tsc`-clean, Pint-clean repo does not.
> - ⚠️ **Three false alarms worth not re-chasing:** thumbnails "missing" in a screenshot (the shot fired before they loaded — all 7 render), a result count of 0 on the first query (my probe typed before the index fetch resolved), and a "wrong" view-all URL (`waitForURL` matched the page we were already on). **A browser probe that does not wait for the thing it measures reports app bugs that are its own.**
> - ⚠️ **eslint's `no-misleading-character-class` refuses `[ـً-ْ]`** even fully escaped, because kashida followed by a combining mark reads as one combined glyph. Split into two `.replace()` passes; the output is identical to PHP's single pass.
> - **Also closed:** `Category` now busts the search-index cache (the index folds category names in). No practical exposure — there is no admin UI for categories — but adding one later would otherwise quietly reintroduce a stale index.
> - **602 tests / 2806 assertions** ✓ (+30), **55 JS tests** ✓ (+34), **41/41 E2E** ✓ (+10 new specs), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. Verified in a real browser at 1440 and 390: overlay opens from the homepage and a product page, index warm, 13 query variants, typo correction end to end, keyboard nav, Escape, scroll-lock release, and zero page errors.
> - ⏭ **Not built:** no search analytics (what shoppers type and find nothing for is the best possible input to the synonym list and the catalogue itself, and it is currently thrown away); no recent-searches or popular-terms panel in the empty state; and descriptions are deliberately **not** indexed — they are long marketing prose and a fuzzy hit deep in one is usually a false positive. All three are levers if search ever needs to be better.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 🏷️ **Status pills rebuilt panel-wide: tones renamed for INTENT, colour moved into an icon, and one registry replaces eight per-page colour maps.** Prompted by the client asking for a unified treatment for "Awaiting confirmation", "Pending payment" and the rest now the dashboard has a look of its own.
> - 🔴 **The diagnosis is that colour had stopped meaning anything.** A shared `StatusPill` existed, but each page kept its **own** `Record<string, StatusTone>` — so one row of `/admin/orders` carried six hues (amber, grey, blue, indigo, green, purple) and the eye had nowhere to land. Worse, the same idea was drawn differently in different places: **the dashboard's Recent orders rendered order status as a plain grey chip while `/admin/orders` rendered it in five colours.** Eight independent maps could not have stayed in agreement.
> - 🔑 **Tones are now named for what the reader should DO** — `attention · active · done · stopped · idle` — because a colour name invites "which do I like?" while an intent name forces "does this need action?". **`attention` is rationed to the states that appear in the dashboard's action queue** (awaiting confirmation, a transfer to verify, a return to review, a complaint). That rule is checkable, it ties the pills to the urgency model the dashboard already uses, and it is the thing that stops amber becoming wallpaper.
> - 🔑 **Colour lives in the ICON, not the fill.** Every tone shares one faint surface and only `attention` tints, which is the same discipline direction B applied to the task cards. The icon is what preserves scannability once the palette is flattened — **a truck against a tick reads faster than indigo against green**, and it works without depending on the word, which matters in a panel that flips AR/EN.
> - **`components/admin/status-badge.tsx` is the single registry** (`order · payment · return · coupon · discount · changeLog · template · inquiry`), so a page names a domain and a value and never an appearance. `OrderStatusBadge` and `PaymentStatusBadge` are deleted; their exported label map was already dead. An unknown value degrades to `idle` with no icon **and still renders its label**, so a new backend state cannot crash a page or invent an urgency it has not earned.
> - ⚠️ **Deliberate re-classifications, each with a reason rather than a preference:** expired coupons and refunded payments left `red`/`purple` for `idle` (over is not failure, and money already returned needs no action); a hidden product review left amber for `idle` (it is a takedown staff *chose*, not an outstanding job); `pending` Meta template approval left amber (there is nothing to do but wait). 🔑 **`refunded` is `done` under `return` but `idle` under `payment`, on purpose** — for a return it is the successful resolution, for a payment it is just where the money ended up.
> - ⚠️ **A rule that fell out of doing this and is worth reusing: if a page's default filter shows only one state, do not tint it.** That is why "open" on Messages and Requests stays quiet while "Handled" is marked — tinting the default view is the definition of wallpaper.
> - 🔴 **The icons made a LATENT overflow bug visible, and it was live before this change.** The status pill is `whitespace-nowrap` inside a `table-fixed` column, so "Awaiting confirmation" (161px) simply **painted across the Payment column** of a 140px cell. Measured, not eyeballed: **9 overflowing cells** on `/admin/orders`. Fixed by sizing the column from the widest rendered pill plus the cell's 32px padding (status 140→195, payment 190→215, and 10px taken back off `order` so the whole table still fits a 1440 viewport at exactly 1150px), **plus `overflow-hidden` on both cells** so a column an admin narrows by hand clips inside itself instead of painting over its neighbour. ⚠️ Column widths are **persisted per admin** in `ui_preferences.tableWidths`, so anyone who has already resized keeps their old width — "Reset columns" is the fix for them.
> - **Also closed a pre-existing i18n gap the registry exposed:** `discount_apply` and `stock_import` are real `activity_logs` actions with **no translation and no tone**, so the change log had been rendering them as lowercase iconless fallbacks. Both now have entries in the registry and in both bundles.
> - **Products' completeness badges** (`No price` / `No image` / `No description`) were bespoke amber chips that wrapped mid-word in a 110px column; now quiet `idle` pills with icons in a 160px column. `idle` rather than amber because the row already says Hidden and the page has a Drafts filter.
> - **Verified by measurement in a real browser, in BOTH locales**, since none of the static gates can see a pill painting over a neighbour: 16 admin pages swept for raw i18n keys, console errors, page overflow and iconless pills (all zero), and **overlap re-measured as zero on six table pages in EN and AR** after having been 9 before. `data-status-tone` was added to every pill so the sweep could assert the whole vocabulary at once — and it is what E2E should select on rather than a colour class.
> - **572 tests / 2738 assertions** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (worth running: `status-pill.tsx` sits in the shared `components/` folder, though it is provably admin-only).
> - **Phase 2, same day — the rest of the panel's chrome, and it turned up a bigger bug than the pills did.**
  - 🔴 **EVERY table in the panel was misaligned in Arabic.** 17 `<thead>` elements carried the physical `text-left`, so with `dir="rtl"` every column header sat against the **opposite** edge from its own column's data. Screenshotted to confirm before believing it: on `/admin/orders` in Arabic the headers hugged the left while the order numbers, names and pills hugged the right. **`text-start`, never `text-left`** — the admin genuinely flips direction (`admin-layout.tsx` sets `dir` from `admin_locale`), whatever the older "admin is EN/LTR" notes in this file imply. All 17 now render from **`THEAD` in `lib/admin-ui.ts`**, which also collapses seven near-identical header recipes into one.
  - 🔴 **Raising a `minWidth` did nothing for anyone who had already dragged that column.** `useResizableColumns` hydrated saved widths **verbatim** (`{...defaults, ...saved}`), so the widened status columns had no effect on my own dev account — its stored `products.status: 110` survived and the pill went on overflowing. Now clamped to the column's constraints on hydration, so a future minimum actually takes effect; only an out-of-range saved value changes, so a deliberate resize is still preserved. **This is what makes the pill sizing robust instead of dependent on the client clicking "Reset columns".**
  - ⚠️ **That also explains a false alarm worth not repeating:** the browser sweep reported a `products` overlap in English that a fresh admin would never have seen. **I was measuring my own stale preferences, not the shipped default.** Check `ui_preferences.tableWidths` before treating a column-width finding as real.
  - **Filter chips → `components/admin/filter-chips.tsx`**, replacing three hand-rolled copies (orders, returns, customers). ⚠️ **The active chip was `bg-white` on a dark panel — the single brightest thing on the page, brighter than the amber status pills**, which put "which filter is selected" above work that needs doing. Now brand teal. Same stark white removed from the pagination active page, the two search submit buttons, and the products card/table switch.
  - **Change-log's paginator was a byte-for-byte copy of the shared `Pagination` markup** and is now the component.
  - **Card shells unified to `rounded-xl`** (24 files): 44 sites were at the old radius while the redesigned dashboard used the new one, so the dashboard read as a different app from every page it linked to. `CARD` in `lib/admin-ui.ts` is the recipe going forward.
  - **Remaining bespoke count chips folded in:** the discount-import preview's three saturated summary chips (matched/unmatched/invalid → `done`/`attention`/`stopped`) and the dashboard's stock "N left". ⚠️ Adding the Zap icon to the discount status pill tipped the Current-discounts table from 5px over its scroll container to 19px, i.e. a **visibly clipped "Action" header** — trimmed that table's cell padding to land it at exactly 522 = 522.
  - **Verified by sweeping all 18 admin pages × 2 locales** for RTL header alignment, raw i18n keys, pill overlap, page overflow and console errors: **zero findings in all 36 combinations**. **572 tests** ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓.
  - ⏭ **Deliberately left alone:** `Button`'s `success` variant is a solid green while `danger`/`warning` are soft outlines — that asymmetry is a **recorded decision** (solid = confirming a risky action), so changing it without being asked would overwrite a reason with a preference. Empty states still vary slightly (`py-8` vs `py-10`, `neutral-400` vs `neutral-500`) — invisible to a user, and the worthwhile version is a real icon + headline + action per page, which is copy work rather than a refactor. The shipping picker's gold "Cheapest" is a recommendation, not a status, and keeps its own look.
- ⏭ **Convention to keep:** filter chips are `rounded-full` and status pills are `rounded-md`, so **round means "press me" and square means "read me"**. They sit inches apart on every list page.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 💸 **Lapsed Tamara holds: prevention, detection, and a recovery that does not make the customer re-order.** Three pieces, prompted by asking what should actually happen when an authorisation expires.
> - 🔑 **The framing that shaped all of it: the hold expires because WE did not confirm in time.** The customer did nothing wrong, and their order still exists with its items and address — what died is the *payment session*, not the order. So "an error occurred, please order again" is the wrong message twice over: it charges the customer for a store-side lapse, and it throws away a basket we had already won.
> - **1 · PREVENTION — the expiry alert now reaches a phone.** It went to the bell and email only, while new orders and returns both got a WhatsApp fan-out. That was backwards: this is the one staff alert where simply not reading it in time loses the sale. New `admin_payment_expiring` template. ⚠️ **Needs Meta approval before it will actually send** — the code fails soft until then.
> - **2 · DETECTION — `TamaraService::reconcileLapsed()`, called hourly by the same command.** Previously an expired hold was only discovered when a human clicked Confirm and the capture threw; until then the order sat in the dashboard queue looking actionable when the sale was already gone.
>   - 🔴 **Tamara's answer marks an order dead, never our clock.** Our 48h (`TAMARA_AUTHORIZATION_HOURS`) is an *assumption* — nothing reads a real expiry from Tamara — so it is used only to decide **who to ask**. An unrecognised remote status is treated as "still fine", because marking a live order dead is far worse than being slow to notice a dead one. The terminal-status list was read from Tamara's docs, **not verified against a live response**, which is exactly why the unknown case fails safe.
>   - ⚠️ **Worth asking Tamara: what is the real authorisation window on this merchant profile?** The error is asymmetric — if the true window is 72h we merely warn early, but if it is 24h our 36h alert fires *after* the hold is already dead. Until it is confirmed, err LOW.
> - **3 · RECOVERY — reopen the order, don't recreate it.** `reconcileLapsed()` moves the order to `Failed` + `PendingPayment`, which is **precisely the shape `CheckoutController::pay()` already knows how to restart** (it clears the dead session and mints a fresh one). So the recovery needed no new payment code at all.
>   - 🔑 **The link is a SIGNED URL (`GET /orders/{n}/resume`), and that is the crux.** The storefront gates `/orders/{n}` on session state, so a guest who ordered days ago — or anyone opening the message on a different phone — would 403. The signature *is* the authorisation: unguessable, 3-day expiry, no login. It deliberately does **not** call `assertOwns()`; requiring the session too would defeat the reason the link exists. Residual exposure (whoever holds the link can reach that order's gateway page) is the same trade already accepted for the gateway-return route, and it beats locking out a paying customer.
>   - **Staff-triggered, one click.** A canned apology from the system that caused the problem reads badly, volume should be near zero if the alert works, and staff may want to attach a coupon. The *work* is automatic; the *sending* is a human decision.
> - ⚠️ **Removed a guard I had just written:** `customer_phone` is NOT NULL on orders, so the no-phone branch was unreachable. Same call as the contact-form phone guard on 2026-08-11 — an unreachable guard reads as a real one to the next person.
> - **572 tests / 2738 assertions** ✓ (+8), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. The signature tests were **proven to guard something**: removing `signed` from the route turns the tampered-link test red. Also strengthened one test that had been passing on a bare `assertTrue(true)`.
> - ⏭ **Not built:** a Tamara webhook handler for expiry events (the controller only handles `confirm`), so detection is polling-only; and the dashboard tile still counts genuinely-expired orders alongside at-risk ones until the hourly pass reclassifies them.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 🎨 **Admin dashboard rebuilt to "direction B" (brand-forward), and the sidebar went brand teal with it.** Three directions were mocked on a Claude Design canvas first (laptop + phone artboards each, real tokens, real counts); the client picked B.
> - 🔑 **The point of B is hierarchy, not decoration.** The old page was four identical KPI boxes, a bare chart in its own card, then a grid of same-weight panels — the eye had nowhere to land. Now **one number dominates**: 30-day revenue in gold on a brand-teal hero, with the chart and the three supporting figures (orders / AOV / today) living *inside that same panel* rather than competing as peers. Everything else is deliberately quieter.
> - **The sidebar is now `#133c40` with `#1b4e53` active chips** (`admin-layout.tsx`, so every admin page). This is what makes the panel read as Retab on sight — the brand tokens existed but only ever appeared as the odd gold icon on neutral grey. ⚠️ Two things had to move with it: `GROUP_LABEL` was `text-neutral-500`, which drops below a readable contrast on teal (now `#7f9fa1`), and the `border-neutral-800` rules vanish against it (now `#1b4e53`).
> - **Action queue restyled to carry urgency in WORDS, not just colour.** Each card leads with `TIME-SENSITIVE` or `TODAY`, the count is large, and **only the money-losing queues are tinted** — which is exactly what stops the tint becoming wallpaper. The heading gained an "N time-sensitive" summary so you know whether to hurry before reading the cards.
> - **Inventory stats became coloured leading rules** (`border-s-2`, so they flip with reading direction) instead of four centred boxes; **Top products gained a bar per row** scaled against the best seller, so the ranking reads at a glance rather than by comparing numbers.
> - **The greeting and date are computed CLIENT-side** on purpose. Server-rendering them would show the container's hour and locale — for a KSA client on a Railway box that is routinely the wrong half of the day.
> - ⚠️ **Deliberate deviation from the mock:** the supporting cards keep the panel's existing `neutral-900`/`neutral-800` surfaces rather than the mock's warm near-blacks. Warm surfaces on the dashboard alone would fork the design system and make this one page look like a different app from Orders. Worth doing as its own pass if the warmth is wanted panel-wide.
> - 🔑 **The redesign composes with the money gate rather than fighting it:** `kpis`/`trend` are null for editors, so the entire teal hero simply does not render and their page opens on "Needs attention". No separate editor layout was needed.
> - ⚠️ **A dashboard cannot be judged on empty data, and dev has none** (`revenue30 = 0`, all demo orders are 30-60 days old — which also explains the honest `↘100%`, checked before assuming it was a bug). Temporarily shifted 40 paid orders into the window to see the hero populated, screenshotted laptop and phone, then **restored every timestamp from a JSON backup and verified the totals returned to 0 / 31042 exactly**.
> - **Cleaned up rather than left behind:** `KpiCard` (the hero carries its own figures now), `trendPeak`, and the orphaned `admin.dashboard.trend.peak` string in both bundles — the hero header has no room for a peak figure, and a stale i18n key is exactly the leftover nobody finds later.
> - ⚠️ **JSX gotcha worth remembering:** a `{/* comment */}` cannot sit in a ternary branch beside the element it describes — `cond ? <p/> : ({/* … */}<ul/>)` is two expressions and fails to parse. Put it above the ternary.
> - **564 tests / 2716 assertions** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (worth running: `admin-layout.tsx` is shared by every admin page). Verified in a browser at 1440 and 390 with populated data — no overflow, no raw i18n keys, no console errors, sidebar measured at `rgb(19, 60, 64)`.
> - ⏭ **Not done:** the mock's warm surfaces (above), and the design canvas still holds directions A and C if the client wants to revisit — `design/` in the repo carries the working artboards.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 📋 **Dashboard "Needs attention" reworked: it offered work the viewer could not do, its Tamara counter disagreed with the alert that acts on it, and a backlog that never reaches zero sat among same-day decisions.**
> - 🔴 **It advertised 403s.** `tasks()` shipped all six tiles to every staff member with no permission check. Measured, not assumed: a **catalogue-only editor received all six**, and four of the destinations returned **403** on click. Now every tile is gated on the permission its own destination requires, and the count query is **skipped entirely** when the viewer lacks it, so it is a small perf win as well.
>   - 🔑 **The test that matters asserts the INVARIANT, not a key list**: for each preset, walk whatever tiles the dashboard actually offered and assert none of them 403. That keeps holding when a seventh tile is added; a hardcoded list would not.
> - 🔴 **The Tamara tile and the alert counted different orders.** The dashboard dated the hold from `orders.created_at` at a hardcoded **24h**; `payments:alert-expiring` dated it from the **authorization ledger row** at a config-driven **36h** (`authorization_hours 48 − warning_hours 12`). So the figure an admin read was never the set that got notified — on the one item where being wrong means a sale quietly stops being collectable. The command's own docblock had flagged the divergence rather than fixing it.
>   - **Fix: one definition, `App\Support\ExpiringAuthorizations`**, used by both. ⚠️ The `payment_expiry_alerted_at` filter stays in the **command only** — that stamp exists to stop the hourly job re-notifying, and an order that was alerted about but still not confirmed very much still needs attention on the dashboard. Pinned by a test asserting an already-alerted order still counts.
>   - Pinned the real hazard too: an order **placed 40h ago but authorised 2h ago** (the resume-payment route) must NOT count. Under the old `created_at` basis it did.
> - ⚠️ **Drafts moved out of the action queue into Inventory health.** 63 that never drops is a stock metric, not a decision due today, and at equal visual weight beside "2 bank transfers to verify" it teaches people to skim the whole panel — including the two amber tiles that cost money. Still one click from the drafts list. An existing test asserting the old task key went red, which is the honest signal the contract changed; it now asserts `inventory.drafts`.
> - 🔑 **Verifying the fix in a browser found the SAME bug one panel over, which reading the code had not.** With the tiles gated, a catalogue editor's dashboard still rendered **Recent orders** with links to `/admin/orders/RTB-…` — all 403. Now gated on the same permission.
>   - ⚠️ **Withheld data ships as `null`, not `[]`.** An empty array renders "no orders yet", which is a *false claim* rather than a withheld one; the same conflation made the empty tile list say **"All clear"** to someone who had simply never been shown anything. Both panels now hide outright when the viewer has no access, and keep their genuine empty states for genuinely-empty data.
> - 🔑 **Revenue is now ADMIN-ONLY (client decision, same day).** The entry above originally recorded this as deliberately left open — a policy question rather than a broken link. The client's answer is a flat rule: **money is admins only**. That covers the KPI cards, the daily-revenue chart, and the takings figure inside Top products; the product **ranking stays**, because what sells is operational and useful to whoever runs the catalogue while what it earned is not. Gated on `isAdmin()` rather than a new permission section, since the rule is flat — swap to a grantable section if that ever changes.
>   - ⚠️ **Withheld money ships as `null`, never zeroes**, and the panels hide on null. `0 SAR` would be a convincing lie rather than a withheld figure — the same distinction already applied to the task tiles and Recent orders.
>   - Verified in a browser for both roles, because making `kpis` nullable is exactly the change that white-screens a page on a null deref: an editor gets **5 panels, no revenue, no console errors**; an admin still gets all 6.
> - ⚠️ **IDE caught what the test run could not:** `assertSuccessful()` takes **no message argument**, and PHP silently drops extra arguments to userland functions — so the failure explanation was being thrown away while the test still passed. Replaced with an explicit status assertion carrying the message.
> - **561 tests / 2692 assertions** ✓ (+9), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓. Verified in a real browser **as both an admin and a catalogue-only editor**, with the temporary editor deleted afterwards. E2E not re-run — no storefront code touched.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-20 — 🚦 **Marketing and transactional work split onto separate queues. A campaign could delay every order confirmation behind it, and Redis would not have fixed it.** Found while re-examining the July "no Redis" decision after the client asked whether to add a Redis container on Railway.
> - **The Redis answer is still no, and nothing since July changed the arithmetic** — but chasing the one stated trigger ("high-volume queues") turned up a real ordering bug that is nothing to do with the driver. Confirmed the escape hatch is still real, too: **zero** references to `Redis::`, `Cache::lock()` or Horizon anywhere in `app/`, and Railpack already installs the `redis` extension, so a later switch genuinely is three env vars.
> - 🔴 **The bug: everything shared one queue.** Grepped for `onQueue` across jobs, notifications and mailables — **not one occurrence**. Meanwhile `SendWhatsappCampaign` chunks the audience 100 at a time but calls `sendCampaignMessage` **per user**, and that dispatches its own `SendWhatsappMessage`. So a 5,000-recipient blast is 5,000 individual jobs, and a customer who paid mid-campaign waited behind all of them for their confirmation, on one single-threaded worker. **Nothing errors** — it is purely an ordering failure, which is exactly why it had gone unnoticed.
> - **Fix: a `bulk` queue, keyed off Meta's own `category`.** `'marketing'` → bulk, `'utility'` → the default queue. Deliberately reusing the category argument that already exists rather than inventing a parallel flag, because that same distinction already governs opt-in rules and template approval, so the two cannot drift. Names live in **`App\Support\Queues`**; full writeup in Architecture → Queue isolation.
> - 🔑 **Only the exception is named.** Transactional sends never call `onQueue()`, so they take whatever `DB_QUEUE` is configured as — hardcoding `'default'` would break silently if that env var were ever changed. The test asserts `$job->queue === null` for transactional rather than a literal string, for the same reason.
> - **The orchestrator moved to bulk as well**, not just the messages it spawns: it iterates the whole opt-in segment, so on the default queue it would block transactional work for the length of the run regardless of where its children went.
> - 🔴 **DEPLOY CHANGE — the worker command is now `php artisan queue:work --queue=default,bulk --tries=3 --timeout=60`.** The list is load-bearing in both directions: **omit `--queue` and the worker serves only `default`, so campaigns never send at all** (no error, the jobs just sit there forever); reverse the order and the original bug is back. Laravel re-checks the list after every job, which is precisely what lets a confirmation jump ahead of a running blast. Updated in all three places this file documents it.
> - **`QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` is unaffected** — `onQueue()` is a no-op under the sync driver, so the interim no-worker option still runs everything inline.
> - **552 tests / 2603 assertions** ✓ (+5), and the two isolation tests were **proven to fail against the old code** before being accepted (a queue assertion that passes either way would be worthless here). `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓. No JS touched, so E2E was not re-run.
> - ⚠️ **Test fixture note:** `orders.shipping_address` and `whatsapp_templates.body` are both NOT NULL, so a minimal `create()` fails on SQLite. Copy the shapes from `AccountControllerTest` and `MarketingCampaignTest` rather than writing them fresh.
> - ⏭ **Left on the default queue on purpose:** `SendReviewReminder` — one delayed job per delivered order, low volume, and a customer-facing nudge rather than a blast.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-19 — ⭐ **Product-review moderation built at `/admin/product-reviews`, closing the gap found in the audit earlier today: customer reviews published publicly with no way for staff to see or remove one.** It auto-refreshes.
> - 🔑 **Deliberately a TAKEDOWN page, not an approval inbox, and that is the load-bearing decision.** `ReviewService::submit()` sets `is_approved => true` because only a verified purchaser can review, and the review-reward coupon is issued in the same request — holding reviews for approval would promise a customer a discount for something nobody can see, and delay the storefront nudge that asked them to write it. So the default stays "published on arrival" and staff hide the rare bad one. Nothing about existing behaviour changed; capability was added.
> - **Hide is the reversible verb and the one the UI leads with; Delete is for spam** and carries a confirm that names the consequence. ⚠️ **Deleting does NOT revoke a reward coupon already issued** — that is a separate, already-delivered promise — and the dialog says so, because the opposite assumption is the natural one.
> - **`stats.average` counts PUBLISHED reviews only.** An average that quietly included hidden ones would disagree with the number on the product page, and the whole point of the page is that the two match. Pinned by a test where a hidden 1-star must not drag a published 5-star average down.
> - **New `product_reviews` permission section** (view/manage), on by default and added to the `catalogue` + `manager` presets. ⚠️ It is a **fifteenth** section, so the preset-coverage test now guards it too: a section a preset omits falls through to `DEFAULTS`, which **grant** — so an omission silently *grants* rather than denies. Existing editors inherit it with no re-grant needed, which is the 2026-08-15 `hasPermission()` fix doing its job; there is a test asserting exactly that against a deliberately stale permissions row.
> - **Auto-update = `router.poll(45s, { only: ['reviews', 'stats'] })`,** the same partial-reload mechanism as the notification bell, so the server recomputes two props and the current filter, page and scroll survive for free. Proven rather than assumed: a browser run counted **2 partial requests in 50s** and the "last refreshed" stamp appearing.
>   - ⚠️ **It pauses while the delete dialog is open.** The list is newest-first, so an arriving review pushes every row down — refreshing underneath someone who is reading a row to decide whether to delete it is precisely how the wrong review gets deleted. Nothing is missed; the next tick catches up.
>   - 🔑 **`onBefore` is the hook that can cancel a poll, and it belongs in the SECOND argument.** `router.poll(interval, requestOptions, pollOptions)` — `PollOptions` is only `{keepAlive, autoStart, mode}`, so putting a callback there is a type error, and `onStart` cannot cancel because the request has already left.
> - **Also added a "How it works" drawer entry**, since the auto-publish model is the least obvious thing about the page and every other admin section has one. The drawer keys off `admin.help.pages.<navKey>` and degrades to no button when absent, which is why its absence was invisible.
> - 🔴 **GOTCHA that cost the most time here, and it generalises to every scripted edit: an indentation-only anchor can match as a SUBSTRING at the wrong depth.** Anchoring on `"            productRequests: {"` (12 spaces) matched *inside* `"                productRequests: {"` (16 spaces) at offset 4, so `str.replace` spliced the new block into the middle of another block's indentation — landing all 27 lines under `admin.help.pages` instead of `admin`, silently. `count(anchor) == 1` passed and gave false confidence, because the substring genuinely was unique. **Every key rendered as a raw `admin.productReviews.*` string in the browser while tsc, Pint and Prettier all stayed green** — only looking at the page caught it. **Anchor on whole lines including the leading `\n`.**
> - ⚠️ **Second gotcha: editing files with a Python script on Windows introduces CRLF, which Pint and Prettier then flag as `line_ending` on files whose content you did not change.** Read and write with `newline=''` to leave endings alone, and re-run `pint --test` / `format:check` after any scripted edit. Harmless but it muddies a diff and looks like an unrelated reformat.
> - ⚠️ **Test notes:** the product page ships `reviews` as `{summary, items, can_review}`, not a list, so assert on `reviews.items`; and `stats.average` of 5.0 serialises to the JSON **int** `5`, which Inertia's strict `where()` rejects against `5.0` — the same whole-float trap as the money assertions.
> - **547 tests / 2597 assertions** ✓ (+11), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓. Verified end to end in a real browser: hiding a review in the panel **actually removes it from the product page** and showing it puts it back (that round trip is the feature), filters by status and rating, product search, the confirm dialog, no overflow at 390, zero console errors.
> - ⚠️ **Three sample reviews were seeded into the DEV database** so the page could be verified with real data — including a deliberately spammy one to exercise Delete. Remove with `php artisan tinker --execute='App\Models\Review::query()->delete();'` (dev only; production has none).
> - ⏭ **Not built:** a bell notification and a dashboard count for new reviews (the page is polled, so staff who are on it see arrivals; someone who is not gets no signal), and the storefront still has no "report this review" affordance for customers.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-19 — 🛠️ **Admin panel audited on both axes the client asked for: does it reflect the storefront, and does it hold up as UI.** Swept all 22 admin pages × 4 widths in a browser, then traced every storefront event a customer can generate to whatever surface is supposed to catch it.
> - **The UI side is genuinely solid and that is worth recording, not just the defects.** Zero console errors, zero failed requests, zero broken images, no duplicate ids, no clipped tables, and **zero horizontal overflow at any width including 390px** — for a data-dense back office with wide tables, that is the hard one and it holds. Four things were wrong and all are fixed.
> - 🔴 **The notification history's type filter rendered two raw i18n keys.** `NOTIFICATION_TYPES` lists six types; the `types` block in both bundles had four. The two missing were **`order_cancelled` and `payment_expiring`** — precisely the pair added on 2026-08-17, i.e. the newest ones, and the two that matter most (a customer cancelling, and an authorisation about to lapse = money). 🔑 The existing note in this file says a shared renderer means "a new notification type can't render in one place and not the other" — true of the *body*, which was correctly translated, but the **filter labels are a second list that has to be updated too**. Adding a type means touching `NOTIFICATION_TYPES`, `ICONS`, the body strings **and** `types`.
> - **Four file inputs had no accessible name at all** (product create ×2, stock import, discounts import): no `id`, no wrapping `<label>`, no `aria-label` — a screen reader announces "file upload button" with no hint of what file. Each already had the right words in the section heading directly above it, so each now carries `aria-label={t(<that heading key>)}`; no new i18n surface, and what a screen reader hears matches what a sighted user reads.
> - **The dashboard's "most requested (out of stock)" list rendered rows reading "— 5 requests".** Cause is data, not code: eight `demand_events` seeded as demo data (all stamped `10:57:03`, backdated) point at product ids the real Zid import replaced. ⚠️ **The FK is `nullOnDelete()` and the query already skips NULLs, so a genuine product deletion cannot cause this** — only a truncate, a restored dump, or seeded rows can. The controller now drops rows it cannot name, because an action item naming nothing is worse than one fewer row. **This is a second symptom of the demo data already flagged for removal before launch (memory `retab-prod-demo-rollback`) — make sure that rollback covers `demand_events`, not just orders and customers.**
> - 🔑 **THE REAL GAP, reported not built: product reviews have NO admin surface whatsoever.** No page, no notification, no dashboard count, no moderation path. Customers write them, `ReviewService::submit()` sets **`is_approved => true`** ("verified purchase → trusted") so they **publish immediately and publicly**, and they trigger the review-reward coupon — real money. The `reviews.is_approved` column exists precisely to gate this and **nothing in the app can ever flip it**, so the only way to remove an abusive or mistaken review is a hand-written SQL statement against production.
>   - ⚠️ **Worse, the sidebar actively pointed the wrong way.** "Reviews" / «التقييمات» opens `/admin/client-reviews`, which manages the **`ClientReview`** model — the homepage testimonials, whose only import path is a pipe-delimited paste of **Google** reviews (`source => 'google'`). Two unrelated models, one label. Renamed the nav item **and** the permission-grid section to "Client reviews" / «آراء العملاء» (matching the page's own title) in both languages, so the absence is visible rather than disguised. **Renaming is not the fix, it just stops the panel lying about what it covers.**
>   - Shape when it gets built: mirror `ProductRequestController` (list + filter + a `reviews`-adjacent permission section), surface `is_approved` as the toggle, and decide whether new reviews should stay auto-approved with a takedown path (cheapest, matches "verified purchase → trusted") or move to hold-then-approve (safer, but delays the reward nudge the customer was just promised).
> - ⚠️ **`users.admin_theme` is a dead preference.** The column exists, defaults to `'light'`, is `$fillable`, and `AdminUserSeeder` explicitly writes `'light'` — while `admin-layout.tsx:252` hardcodes `className="admin-shell dark …"` with no toggle anywhere. So every admin gets dark regardless of a stored preference that says otherwise, and the decision recorded in this file ("admin panel supports dark + light themes") is unbuilt. Either honour the column or drop it; a stored preference nothing reads is worse than no preference.
> - **Everything else traces cleanly, which is the answer to "does it make sense of the storefront".** All six customer-generated events (order placed, order cancelled, return filed, coming-soon request, contact message, authorisation expiring) have a bell notification **and** a destination page; six dashboard action items carry live counts with the money-sensitive two flagged urgent; and the orders list ships **every** `OrderStatus` as a filter chip with counts, so abandoned `pending_payment` checkouts are findable rather than invisible. The only storefront signal captured and never shown anywhere is the **wishlist** — low priority, but it is demand data being discarded by a store that already built a demand-capture funnel for coming-soon products.
> - **536 tests / 2486 assertions** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓. Re-swept all 22 pages after the fixes: **zero findings**.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-19 — 🔎 **Storefront UI sweep: two customer-visible bugs found and fixed, both introduced by recent commits and invisible to every existing gate.** Drove a real browser over 17 storefront pages × 3 widths × 2 locales, then the interactive and authenticated flows the static pass cannot reach.
> - 🔴 **The homepage banner's main button rendered the literal string `hero.cta`.** `primary-banner.tsx` borrowed the hero's CTA label, and `58d25ae` (the carousel) moved that key into `hero.slides[].cta` — so the top-level key stopped existing and i18next fell back to printing the key. Live in **both locales**, on the busiest page on the site. 🔑 The fix is not just re-adding the key: the banner now owns `primaryBanner.cta` in its own copy block, because a label shared across two unrelated components is a refactor of either one away from breaking silently.
> - 🔴 **`/account/wishlist` 404'd for every signed-in customer.** The route is registered at the bare `/wishlist`, but `ACCOUNT_LINKS` in `navbar.tsx` guessed the prefixed path — and that constant feeds **both** the desktop account dropdown and the mobile drawer, so both entry points were dead. The account dashboard linked to `/wishlist` correctly, which is exactly why nobody noticed. Verified by clicking it, not by reading the route table: `/account/wishlist` → 404, `/wishlist` → 200.
> - 🔑 **Neither bug is catchable by the existing gates, which is the transferable part.** A missing i18n key is a *successful* render of the wrong string — `tsc` sees a valid `t()` call, PHPUnit sees HTTP 200, and no E2E spec asserts on that button's text. A wrong `href` is a plain string until someone clicks it. Both need a browser that reads what is actually on screen. Same blind spot as the 2026-07-30 prop-name mismatch.
> - **Two consistency fixes alongside them.** `login.tsx` and `register.tsx` were the last two forms still using native `required` without `noValidate`, so the browser intercepted an empty submit and every localized `InputError` on them was unreachable — a Saudi customer with an English browser got "Please fill out this field" instead of «حقل البريد الإلكتروني مطلوب.». Now server-validated only, matching contact/profile/checkout/returns (the 2026-08-11 lesson, applied to the two forms it had missed). And the **contact page had no `<h1>` at all** (it starts at `h2`; About carries one in its hero) — promoted, purely semantic since the styling is class-based.
> - ⚠️ **One finding was a FALSE POSITIVE and worth recording as a method note.** My probe flagged the login "remember me" checkbox as having no accessible name, because Radix renders it as a `<button>` with the `id` on the button and a separate hidden input. The browser's own aria snapshot says `checkbox "تذكّرني"` — the `<label for>` names it correctly, since `<button>` is a labelable element. **Check the accessibility tree, not a hand-rolled heuristic**, before reporting an a11y bug.
> - ⏭ **Not changed, flagged as a judgement call:** the phone header's menu and cart icons are **24×24**, exactly the WCAG 2.5.8 AA floor and half the 48px logo beside them; the hero dots are 8×8. They pass, but they are at the limit — worth enlarging the hit area (padding, not icon size) if taps get reported as missed.
> - **Clean across the board otherwise:** zero console errors, zero failed requests, zero broken images and **zero horizontal overflow** on any page at any width in either locale, and the catalogue search typeahead returns correct results with no broken thumbnails.
> - **536 tests / 2486 assertions** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (worth running: the login and register forms are exercised by `auth.spec.ts`). All four fixes re-verified in a real browser afterwards, including the Arabic validation messages now rendering.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-17 — 🧾 **Four customer-account gaps closed: cancel your own order, an alert before a Tamara hold lapses, unpaid orders you can act on, and a password for OTP-only accounts.**
> - **1. Customer cancellation (a BRIEF requirement that had no route at all).** `OrderConfirmationService::cancelByCustomer()` is literally named for this, but the only caller was the admin panel. Now `POST /orders/{n}/cancel` (own throttle bucket) + a quiet control on the order page.
>   - 🔑 **The window is asked of the ENUM, never restated in the controller or the page.** Restating it is precisely how the admin panel ended up offering a Cancel button in the one state the service always rejected (2026-08-15). `canCancel` is `$order->status->isCancellableByCustomer()`, full stop.
>   - **Two-step confirm rather than `window.confirm()`**, because a native dialog cannot be written in the customer's own language and cannot say what happens to their money. `cancelByCustomer()` releases funds as it goes: a Tamara hold is voided, a captured card refunded, bank transfer has nothing to release.
>   - ⚠️ **The exception message was a hardcoded English literal** (`'This order can no longer be cancelled.'`) reaching an Arabic-first storefront. Now `__('messages.orders.not_cancellable')`.
>   - 🔑 **Staff notification is the BELL first, WhatsApp second** — deliberately, because the WhatsApp fan-out needs credentials that are not configured in production, so a WhatsApp-only touchpoint would tell nobody anything today. The bell writes synchronously (`viaConnections`) and always works. Customer-side there is **no** message, per the brief's "notify indirectly": they are looking at the page they just cancelled from, and a cheerful confirmation would only advertise how easy it is.
>   - 🔴 **Two state-truth bugs found by driving it in a browser, not by testing.** A cancelled order still showed **✅ "Your order has been received"**, and worse, a cancelled BANK TRANSFER kept rendering the IBAN with "pay by bank transfer" — because `payment_status` stays `pending` on cancel (there is nothing to release), and the bank block keyed on that alone. Both fixed and pinned; same class as the unpaid-order heading fixed on 2026-08-16.
> - **2. Tamara authorisation-expiry alert — the project's FIRST scheduled task.** `payments:alert-expiring` (hourly, `--dry-run`), + `PaymentExpiringNotification`, + `orders.payment_expiry_alerted_at`. This is the only notification in the app where *not acting* costs money: the hold lapses, the order can never be captured, and nothing errors — no failed job, just a sale that quietly stops being collectable. The dashboard has counted these since it was built, but a count only helps someone already looking at the dashboard.
>   - ⚠️ **The hold is dated from the AUTHORIZATION ledger row, not `orders.created_at`** (which is what the dashboard counter uses). They are usually minutes apart, but an order paid through the resume-payment route is authorised long after it was placed — dating it from creation would alert on a hold that has barely started, or, on the other side of the same mistake, after it had already lapsed. Needed a new `Order::payments()` relation, which did not exist.
>   - **The stamp column is the point:** an hourly command with no memory re-alerts every run for the whole window, which trains staff to ignore the one alert that matters. Mirrors `review_reminder_sent_at`. Named for the concept, not the vendor.
>   - **Two knobs** (`TAMARA_AUTHORIZATION_HOURS` 48, `TAMARA_EXPIRY_WARNING_HOURS` 12) so the alert fires at 36h. Hours-left is rounded **down**, so the figure a human reads is never more optimistic than reality.
>   - 🔴 **DEPLOY PREREQUISITE: a `schedule:work` service on Railway** — a THIRD service off the same image, no domain and **no healthcheck path** (it listens on no port, so a healthcheck crash-loops it). Nothing here runs without it. Noted in `routes/console.php` too.
> - **3. Unpaid orders on `/account`.** The list showed them with no way to act, so an abandoned gateway checkout was recoverable only from a confirmation page the customer had already navigated away from. Now a notice at the top, a tinted row, and a Complete-payment button posting to the **same gated route** the order page uses (`assertOwns` passes for the signed-in owner, so no new authorisation path).
>   - 🔑 **The rule moved onto the MODEL** (`Order::isAwaitingGatewayPayment()`). Three surfaces ask it — the order page, the account list, and the pay route that enforces it — and three copies of a money rule is exactly the 2026-08-15 admin-cancel bug waiting to happen again.
> - **4. A first password for OTP-only accounts.** `POST /account/password`. The shared `password.update` requires `current_password`, which an account created through WhatsApp OTP has never had — so the store's *primary* sign-in method left customers permanently unable to add an email login.
>   - 🔴 **Guarded to `password === null`, and that guard IS the security model.** Allowing it where a password already exists turns a hijacked session into a permanent takeover with no knowledge of the old credential. Changing a password stays on the route that asks for the old one.
>   - ⚠️ **Refuses when the account has no email**, rather than silently creating an unusable credential: the login form is email + password, so a password with no email to pair it with can never be used.
> - ⚠️ **Repeated my own trap from earlier the same day and caught it: `{{count}}` is reserved by i18next** (it switches on pluralization, six suffixed variants in Arabic). Both new interpolations use `{{n}}`, the existing codebase convention.
> - **532 tests / 2457 assertions** ✓ (+25), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓, migration applied to dev MariaDB. **Verified in a real browser** as well: the cancel confirm names the money consequence, the cancelled order drops the IBAN and the tick, the account page shows the notice + one pay button on a tinted row, and a passwordless account can set a password and watch the card disappear. Dev test data cleaned up afterwards.
> - ⏭ **Not done:** a customer-facing WhatsApp/email on cancellation (deliberate, per the brief), and the expiry alert has no WhatsApp template — each new template needs Meta approval, and with WhatsApp unconfigured it could not send anyway.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-16 — 🔴 **Both gateways returned the paying customer to a 404. Neither return URL was ever routed.** Found while answering whether the Tamara portal needs a webhook registered; it does not, but checking how much we depend on the webhook exposed this.
> - **Verified against the running app, not inferred:** `route:list --path=checkout` had exactly two routes (`GET`/`POST /checkout`), and `/checkout/result?status=success` → **404**, `/checkout/success` → **404**. `TamaraService::buildCheckoutPayload()` sends the shopper to the first; `AppServiceProvider` bound Moyasar's `success_url` to the second. So a customer paid, was redirected, and landed on an error page.
> - 🔑 **The worse half is what it did to order state, not to the landing page.** With no return handler the **webhook was the ONLY thing able to advance a paid order out of `pending_payment`** — so a delayed, misrouted or wrongly-signed callback stranded a real paid order with nobody watching. The new route is deliberately a **second path to the same outcome**: the webhook stays authoritative (it re-fetches and verifies amount + currency), and the customer's own return now confirms too, so a webhook problem degrades instead of stalling. Both services are row-locked and idempotent, so the two racing is a no-op.
> - **One route for both gateways** (`GET /checkout/result` → `CheckoutController@result`), with Moyasar's binding repointed from `/checkout/success` — safe to change outright because Moyasar has never been live, so no outstanding invoice carries the old URL. A test pins that `route('checkout.result')` equals the literal path string the provider builds, since renaming the route would otherwise silently reintroduce the 404.
> - 🔑 **Identity comes from OUR session first (`placed_orders`), never the query string**, because the query string is attacker-controlled and the session is not. It survives the round trip: a **top-level GET redirect carries a SameSite=Lax cookie**, so the normal path never needs the fallback. Pinned by a test where a forged `?orderId=` belonging to another order is ignored and the visitor still lands on their own.
> - **The gateway reference is a fallback only** (session expired, cookies cleared, paid on another device), and it differs per gateway: **Moyasar returns `?id=<PAYMENT id>` while our `gateway_reference` holds the INVOICE id**, so it cannot be matched directly — `confirmFromGateway()` resolves the order from the payment itself. Tamara returns `?orderId=`, which is what we stored. ⚠️ The lookup is `whereNotNull('gateway_reference')`, or a bare `/checkout/result` with no session would match whichever order had no reference yet.
> - ⚠️ **Residual risk accepted knowingly:** arriving with a valid gateway reference grants `placed_orders` access to that order's confirmation page. Those references are server-generated UUIDs known only to us, the gateway and the customer, so abuse means guessing one — and the alternative is stranding a paying customer who lost their session, which is the exact failure being fixed.
> - **Confirmation is best-effort and skipped when already settled**, so the common case (webhook won the race) costs no outbound call, and a gateway outage still lands the customer on their order rather than a 500. Card orders use `reconcile()` (reads the invoice, needs no id from the query string); Tamara uses `confirm()`.
> - **Verified in a real browser end to end**, which matters because the failure was a redirect chain: placed a real order, then hit the return URL exactly as a gateway would → **HTTP 200**, landed on the same `/orders/RTB-…` page, order number rendered, no 404 text, no page errors. ⚠️ Note `?orderId=nope` with a live session lands on the visitor's **own** order rather than home — that is the session-first precedence working, not a miss; the home redirect is for a session-less visitor.
> - **507 tests / 2341 assertions** ✓ (+10), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓.
> - ⏭ **Tamara portal webhooks: deliberately NOT registered.** Our checkout payload carries the notification URL per order in `merchant_url.notification`, built from `APP_URL`, so it follows the domain automatically. The portal page being empty also confirms Zid never registered one, so nothing was routing away from us. Adding one pointing at `/webhooks/tamara` is harmless insurance if wanted (`confirm()` is idempotent) — but ⚠️ if the portal hook signs differently our endpoint 401s it, which would look like a broken integration and is not one.
