> **Last updated:** 2026-08-16 — 🔴 **Tamara had the SAME double-write bug as Moyasar, and fixing it turned up an error in the measurement I recorded yesterday.** Found while answering "what are we missing on Tamara".
> - **`TamaraClient::client()` carried `->retry(2, 200, throw: false)` and EVERY method used it**, including `capture`, `refund`, `createCheckout` and `cancel`. Measured against the real class: **2 requests on a dropped connection, and 2 on every error status**. A lost response is indistinguishable from a lost request, so a capture or refund Tamara had already executed was sent again — two movements on a customer's instalment plan, one row in our `payments` ledger.
> - **It differs from Moyasar in one way worth stating precisely:** Tamara's methods check `$response->successful()` and throw, so the caller sees an error rather than Moyasar's silent success. Better, because it is visible — but the duplicate has **already left**, and an admin who reacts by retrying by hand can make a third.
> - 🔑 **It survived for the identical reason: `TamaraPaymentTest` drives an anonymous subclass of `TamaraClient` that overrides every method**, so the real HTTP client never executes. Same blind spot, same shape, one class over. New **`tests/Feature/Payments/TamaraClientHttpTest.php`** exercises the real client over `Http::fake()`; all five write tests were **proven to fail against the old policy** before being accepted.
> - 🔑 **CORRECTION to the 2026-08-15 entry below: the retry table it records is WRONG.** It says 4xx is not retried. A bare framework probe (no class involved) says every status is: `400→2 404→2 409→2 422→2 429→2 500→2`. That earlier "measurement" was almost certainly leftover `Http::fake` state, the exact trap the same entry warns about. **The old behaviour was WORSE than documented, not better**, so the Moyasar fix was more warranted than the note implied. The fix itself was re-verified and still holds: Moyasar writes are 1 request in every case, reads retry only on 429/5xx. _Lesson: a measurement that contradicts a plain reading of the source deserves a second, isolated probe before it is written down as fact._
> - ⚠️ **Testing gotcha: `Http::assertSentCount()` counts RESPONSES, so a `ConnectionException` thrown from a raw `Http::fake(fn () => throw ...)` closure records nothing and the count reads 0.** Use `Http::sequence()->pushFailedConnection(...)->push(<success>)` instead, which is recorded — and make the second entry a **success** on purpose, so the test pins both the request count and the silent-success outcome a retry would produce.
> - 🔴 **Second fix, customer-facing: the product page advertised a DIFFERENT plan from the one checkout requests.** `product.tsx` hardcoded `effective_price / 4` and the copy read "split into 4 payments", while `TamaraService` sends `instalments` (config default **3**). A shopper was quoted "4 payments of 13.75" and landed on a Tamara page offering 3 of 18.33. The count is now a server prop (`tamaraInstalments`) driving **both** the divisor and the copy, read from the same config value the checkout payload uses, so they cannot disagree. Verified in a real browser: the block renders `قسّمها على 3 دفعات بقيمة 18.33` on a 55.00 product.
> - ⚠️ **The i18n placeholder is `{{n}}`, NOT `{{count}}`** — i18next reserves `count` for pluralization, which under Arabic means six suffixed variants this key does not define. `{{n}}` is already the codebase convention (`catalogue.resultCount`).
> - **Tamara credentials, from the client's portal (screenshot 2026-08-16):** merchant `retabstore.com/` ID `P0708313`, one **API Token** (masked), a **Public key** and a **Notification key**, all created 08/05/2024 by "System" (i.e. when Zid connected).
>   - 🔴 **The API token is UNRECOVERABLE and that is by design.** Tamara shows it once at creation then masks it forever; the portal's copy button copies the literal `*****`, which is not a bug. It exists only inside Zid's integration. **So reusing it was never an available option.**
>   - 🔴 **Do NOT press "Generate new token" before cutover** — it disables the old one, and the Zid store is still live and selling on it. That would switch off BNPL on the shop currently taking the money, for a token the new store cannot use yet.
>   - **The question to put to Tamara: can they add a SECOND merchant profile for `retab.com.sa` with its own token?** The portal's "Merchant name" column implies more than one row is possible. If yes, no overlap window at all. If no, regenerating becomes a cutover-minute step. ⚠️ Also worth confirming: the merchant is registered against `retabstore.com` while our checkout sends `merchant_url` values on `retab.com.sa`, and some gateways validate those against the registered site.
>   - **The Notification key is collectable now** (it is `TAMARA_NOTIFICATION_TOKEN`, independent of the API token) but ⚠️ **it is per-merchant-profile, so a second profile means a new one** — wiring it before Tamara answers may be throwaway. The **Public key is publishable** (browser-side) and is the proper long-term fix for the instalment quote: it drives Tamara's official widget, which renders Tamara's own calculation.
> - ⚠️ **Correcting my own claim from earlier the same day: I said there is "no webhook to register anywhere."** Our code does send the notification URL per checkout in `merchant_url.notification` (so it follows `APP_URL` automatically), but the portal **does** have a Webhooks section — worth checking whether Zid registered a global webhook there that some event types route to instead.
> - ✅ **Tamara HAS a sandbox** (`api-sandbox.tamara.co`, already wired via `TAMARA_BASE_URL`) with separate credentials, unlike OTO. So the full flow can be verified before any production key changes hands, which turns the token swap into a five-minute cutover step.
> - ⏭ **Still not built:** eligibility gating (Tamara is offered at any cart value, but Tamara enforces min/max order limits, so a below-minimum cart fails at `createCheckout` *after* the customer chose it — needs the client's actual contract limits), and **auth-expiry alerts** (the dashboard computes `tamaraExpiring` but nothing sends anything; an expired authorisation is money that quietly evaporates, and this would be the project's first scheduled task). Also noted: **Tamara does not implement the `PaymentGateway` interface** the way Moyasar does, contrary to what the Payments section of this file claims.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-16 — 👥 **Admins can now be created and un-created from the panel. Previously the ONLY way to make one was the seeder over a production shell.**
> - **Three places pinned it, not one:** `store()` hardcoded `'role' => 'editor'`, and both `updatePermissions()` and `destroy()` `abort_unless($user->isEditor())` — so there was no create path, no promote path, and no remove path for an admin. The practical cost: the client could not be given a second admin without a Railway shell, and their single admin locking out had no in-app way back.
> - 🔑 **The whole page now rests on one stated invariant — THERE IS ALWAYS AT LEAST ONE ADMIN** — and it is provable rather than merely likely: `store()` only ever adds, `destroy()` still only removes editors, and `updateRole()` refuses to demote the last admin. Zero admins would mean nobody can reach a page behind `admin` middleware, with only a production shell as the way back.
> - 🔑 **The two guards are ordered COUNT-then-SELF, and the order is the interesting decision.** The self-guard subsumes the count one (you must be an admin to be here, so any admin you are *allowed* to demote is by definition not the last), which would have left the count branch **unreachable and therefore untestable**. Checking the count first makes both branches reachable and gives the sole admin the message that actually tells them what to do — *"promote someone else first"* rather than a bare *"not your own role"*. Pinned by asserting the **message**, not merely that it failed: with the guards swapped the test reddens, which was verified by swapping them.
> - **Promotion deliberately LEAVES the stored permissions alone.** They are inert while somebody is an admin (`resolvedPermissions()` returns `[]` and `hasPermission()` short-circuits), so keeping them means a later demotion restores the exact grants that editor had instead of silently resetting to DEFAULTS. Conversely a **new** admin is created with `permissions => null`, since a stored map there is dead weight that reads as meaningful the next time someone opens the row.
> - **Removal stays editor-only, but a departed admin is no longer a shell job:** demote, then remove. Two deliberate steps, and the last-admin guard protects the first.
> - **UI:** a role picker in the add dialog (two chips, with the consequence spelled out under it and the submit button switching to "Create admin"), plus a Role row in the detail panel for admins and editors alike. ⚠️ **When a change is refused the button is not rendered at all and the reason takes its place** — a dead control the admin has to hover to understand is worse than a sentence saying why. Role changes get a confirm dialog where the permission grid does not: this one hands somebody every section of the store, or takes it away.
> - ⚠️ **`role` is REQUIRED on `POST /admin/users`, so the existing create test had to be updated.** That is the honest signal the contract changed, rather than defaulting the field and letting a role-less request through.
> - **Verified in a real browser, and the guard messages were confirmed to SWAP with the admin count** (which is what proves both branches are reachable in the UI too): with one admin, my own row reads *"only admin account"*; after promoting the editor, the same row reads *"cannot change your own role"*. Also confirmed the promote/demote round trip end to end (badge flips, the permission grid hides for an admin and returns on demotion), the confirm dialog naming the person, and zero page errors. **488 tests / 2289 assertions** ✓ (+9), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓. E2E not re-run — no spec touches `/admin/users` and nothing shared changed.
> - ⏭ Not done: emailing a new staff member their credentials, and an audit-log entry for role changes (`activity_logs` does not track staff today).
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-16 — 🛡️ **Staff & access reworked: the white-field / prefilled-credentials bug was Chrome autofill, plus generated passwords and permission presets.**
> - 🔑 **The two reported bugs were ONE cause: Chrome's password manager.** The "Add editor" fields already carried `bg-neutral-950`, yet rendered white and pre-filled with the signed-in admin's own email and password. A `type="password"` field next to an email field is exactly what Chrome treats as a **login form**, so it offered the saved credential — and `:-webkit-autofill` paints its own near-white background that **`background-color` cannot override** (the UA style wins). So the white was not a missing dark class; it was the autofill background.
>   - **Fix, two halves.** (a) Panel-wide CSS that covers the UA background with `box-shadow: 0 0 0 1000px <bg> inset` + `-webkit-text-fill-color` (the only reliable route) plus the long `transition-delay` companion trick, with a `.dark` variant. Applied globally, since any field the browser decides to fill has the same problem. (b) The form no longer looks like a login: `autoComplete="off"` on the form and the text fields, and the password field is `autocomplete="new-password"` — the documented signal that stops Chrome offering a *saved* credential.
> - 🔑 **The password is now GENERATED, not typed**, which fixes three things at once: the admin no longer invents a password for someone else (they pick weak ones), it is shown in clear with a **Copy** button because it has to be passed on anyway, and there is **no `type="password"` field left for Chrome to treat as a login**. `crypto.getRandomValues`, not `Math.random` — this is a real credential. ⚠️ The alphabet omits `0/O` and `1/l/I` **on purpose**: these get read aloud or pasted into WhatsApp, which is exactly where a misread character costs a support round trip.
> - **The green buttons were the wrong variant, not the wrong shade.** `variant="success"` is a solid `bg-green-600` that belongs to *confirming a risky action* (approve a return, apply a stock import). "Create editor" and "Update password" are simply the **primary** action of their form, so both became `primary` (brand-teal). The success variant is untouched where it genuinely means success.
> - **Permission presets** (`Permission::PRESETS` + `preset()`): 14 sections × 33 actions is a lot of switches, and the real roles are few — Operations, Catalogue, Manager, View only, Everything, Clear all. They fill the grid client-side and **save nothing**, so the stored value is always the map the admin actually confirmed.
>   - 🔴 **Presets list SECTIONS, and `preset()` expands against the live SCHEMA so every section is explicitly `true` or `false`.** An omitted section would fall through `resolvedPermissions()` to **DEFAULTS**, and most defaults are `view => true` — i.e. a missing key silently **GRANTS** where the preset means to deny. Pinned by a test asserting every preset covers every section and action.
> - **Verified in a real browser, not just by tests:** name/email empty with `autocomplete=off`, the password field `readOnly` + `new-password` + 16 chars, all three inputs computing to `oklch(0.145 0 0)` (neutral-950) rather than white, both buttons computing to `rgb(27,78,83)` (brand-teal), Regenerate producing a different value, an editor created end to end, each preset producing the expected grid (Catalogue → orders all off/products all on; View only → first action only; Clear all → nothing), and the saved map **surviving a reload**. Zero page errors. **479 tests / 2262 assertions** ✓ (+7), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓.
> - **Follow-up: the form moved into a DIALOG** (the shared `components/admin/modal.tsx`, already used in ~10 places) rather than expanding inline. Opening it used to push the staff list and the detail panel down the page, so the very thing you were about to configure slid out from under you. Measured: the admin's row now stays at **top 142 both before and after opening** — zero layout shift — and Esc + backdrop dismissal come for free from the shared component. Reopening re-seeds a fresh password and clears the fields, so a cancelled attempt cannot leak into the next one.
>   - ⚠️ **Verification trap, again:** `div[role="dialog"]` matches **two** elements on this panel (the modal *and* the always-mounted "How it works" drawer), so scope by content — `filter({ has: form[autocomplete=off] })` — or a correct render looks like a duplicate.
> - ⏭ Not done: emailing the new editor their credentials (needs a template family of its own), and a "last signed in" column — `users` has no `last_login_at`.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-16 — 🔴 **An abandoned card/Tamara checkout was UNRECOVERABLE; `POST /orders/{n}/pay` fixes it.** Flagged during yesterday's Moyasar audit, built while the application status is still unknown (it needs no credentials).
> - **The gap:** `orders.payment_url` was written by `initiate()` and **surfaced nowhere** — `grep payment_url` found the model, the two services, and nothing else. So closing the gateway tab, a declined card, or a failed `initiate()` left the order in `pending_payment` on a page with no way to pay and no way back. Every one of those was a lost sale, and this is the store's entire abandoned-checkout recovery.
> - 🔑 **The blocker was a data gap, not a missing button: `payment_method` was only written on the BANK-TRANSFER branch.** Card and Tamara set it when the payment *landed* (`markOrderPaid` / `markAuthorized`), so an unpaid card order carried **null**, and nothing could tell which gateway to resume against. It is now recorded at checkout for all three. ⚠️ Checked before changing it: **both** dashboard counts that key on this column (`bankTransfers`, `tamaraExpiring`) also filter on `payment_status`, so an unpaid card/Tamara order still cannot drift into them — and `OrderPlacedMail`'s bank block requires `BankTransfer` **and** `Pending`.
> - ⚠️ **A FAILED attempt must not be handed back the same invoice** — the gateway has closed it, so the customer would land on a dead page. `pay()` clears `payment_url`/`gateway_reference` on `Failed` so `initiate()` mints a fresh one; a merely **abandoned** (`Pending`) attempt reuses the still-open invoice, which is what `initiate()` already does. Pinned by a test each.
> - **Bank transfer is deliberately excluded** — there is no gateway to return to, and its IBAN instructions are already on the page.
> - **The page now tells the truth about state:** it showed **✅ "Your order has been received"** over an unpaid order, which is probably *why* an abandoned checkout never got finished — it reads as done. When payment is outstanding it is ⏳ "Your order is reserved" plus the pay block.
> - ⚠️ **Own throttle bucket (`throttle:10,1,order-pay`)** — a bare `throttle:N,1` rejoins the shared per-visitor counter (the 2026-08-06 revenue bug).
> - ⚠️ **Test gotcha: `Inertia::location()` answers 409 + `X-Inertia-Location` only to an Inertia request** and degrades to a plain 302 otherwise, so asserting 409 from a bare `$this->post()` fails misleadingly. Assert the **destination** (`assertRedirect($url)`), which is the decision under test either way and avoids the version handshake. Also note **`Product` has no factory** in this repo (only `UserFactory`) — build products with `Product::create()` like the cart tests do.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 👤 **Storefront account entry points: a sign-up button, a signed-in account menu, and the profile page rebuilt to the brand.**
> - 🔴 **Sign-up was unreachable from the storefront entirely.** `/register` existed and worked, but the header's only account affordance was one `User` icon hard-wired to `/login/whatsapp` — so a new customer could sign IN and never find how to sign UP. Now a filled `bg-brand-teal` pill in the header's end cell (desktop) and a filled button in the mobile drawer. Deliberately a **button, not another icon**: it is an invitation, not a utility, and the two must not look alike.
> - 🔑 **A signed-in customer also had no way to log out of the header** — logout existed only inside `/account`. The `User` icon now opens a menu (`ACCOUNT_LINKS` + a red logout), reusing the **same `group-hover`/`group-focus-within` mechanism as the row-2 category dropdowns** rather than adding open/close state. `ACCOUNT_LINKS` is shared with the drawer so the two lists cannot drift.
> - **The pill is desktop-only and the drawer carries the mobile equivalent.** Row 1 is a 3-column grid (icons · logo · language) and a pill among the icons reads as clutter; the end cell next to the language toggle is where account actions belong.
> - **Profile page rebuilt** (`pages/account/profile.tsx`): it predated the brand pass, so it was grey borders and a hardcoded `#2f4f4f` inside a storefront that is now teal/gold, Thmanyah, rounded cards. Now a `rounded-3xl` card with a shared `Field` shell, focus rings, `aria-invalid` + inline errors, and `noValidate` (per the contact-form lesson: native `required` swallows the submit and Inertia's errors never render).
>   - **Phone is shown read-only with its verified badge** — under the OTP identity model it *is* the login, so changing it here would move the account itself.
>   - **A "still missing" prompt appears only while name/email/city are incomplete**, because a WhatsApp-only signup arrives with nothing but a phone: this page is as much "finish setting up" as "edit".
>   - **Save is gated on `isDirty`**, so the button states whether pressing it would do anything instead of firing a no-op round trip. ⚠️ Incidentally confirmed while testing — a check that re-typed the *existing* name correctly left the button disabled; the test was wrong, not the gate.
> - **Verified in a real browser, signed out and signed in, both locales:** pill visible and pointing at `/register` when signed out and **absent** when signed in, drawer carrying both sign-in and sign-up, dropdown listing exactly `/account` + `/account/profile` + `/account/wishlist` plus one logout, a real edit enabling Save → flash → Save disabled again, an invalid email producing `aria-invalid` + the localized inline message, EN switching to `dir=ltr`, and no horizontal overflow at 390. Zero page errors throughout. **463 tests** ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓.
> - **Phone header follow-up (same day):** the account icon moved into row 1's end cell **beside the cart**, and the language toggle shortened to **EN/AR** below `md` (73px → **44px**), which is what makes room for three controls without crowding the logo. Measured clearance to the logo: **68-69px at 320-430**, no overflow at any width, header height unchanged at 69px.
>   - ⚠️ **The mobile account icon is a plain link, NOT the desktop dropdown** — a `group-hover` menu is unusable on touch, and the drawer already carries the full account list. Signed in it goes to `/account`, signed out to sign-in.
>   - **Both labels still name the language you switch TO**, so the short form is `EN` while reading Arabic and `AR` while reading English. Implemented as **two spans toggled by `md:hidden` / `hidden md:inline`**, not a breakpoint hook, so it stays a pure CSS switch with nothing for SSR to hydrate differently — and the full name stays on `aria-label` so a screen reader never hears just "EN". Verified: `EN` + aria `English` + `dir=rtl` → click → `AR` + aria `العربية` + `dir=ltr`.
> - ⏭ **Deliberately not built: setting a password from the profile.** `password.update` requires `current_password`, and an OTP-only customer has **`password = null`**, so the existing route cannot serve them — it needs a separate "set a password" path that skips the current-password check for exactly that case. Worth doing when email login matters more.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 🔴 **Moyasar audit: a lost connection on a REFUND re-sent it and returned success, i.e. a silent double refund.** Found while preparing the payment activation, with no credentials involved. Same class as the OTO retry bug, but this one moves money.
> - 🔑 **Measured, not reasoned.** 🔴 **⚠️ THE TABLE BELOW IS WRONG — see the 2026-08-16 Tamara entry above, which re-probed it in isolation.** The source reading was right all along: a bare `retry(2, 200, throw: false)` re-sends on **any** unsuccessful response, 4xx included (`400→2 404→2 409→2 422→2 429→2 500→2` on a bare `Http::retry`). The "4xx not retried" line was almost certainly leftover `Http::fake` state, i.e. the very trap warned about two bullets down. Kept here rather than silently edited, because the mistake is the instructive part: **the old behaviour was worse than this table claims, so the fix was more warranted, not less.**
>   ```
>   4xx (400/404/422)   1 request   — NOT retried     ← ❌ WRONG, they ARE retried
>   429 and 5xx         2 requests  — retried
>   lost connection     2 requests  — retried, AND RETURNS SUCCESS
>   ```
>   ⚠️ Two earlier probe attempts gave nonsense (cumulative counts, then zeroes) because **repeated `Http::fake()` calls in one process leak state** — the closures bind `&$counter` and earlier stubs stay registered. **One process per case**, or use PHPUnit's `Http::assertSentCount()`.
> - **The dangerous line is the third.** A lost response is indistinguishable from a lost request, so a refund Moyasar had already executed was issued again and `refundPayment()` **returned normally** — one row in our `payments` ledger, two refunds in the merchant account, nothing in our books to show it. A 5xx is the same ambiguity with a status code attached. Retab issues **partial** refunds (a return with or without the shipping fee), so there is usually refundable balance left for a duplicate to succeed against; a full refund would more likely be rejected for exceeding the remaining balance, but that is Moyasar's accounting saving us, not our design.
> - **Fix: split the client by SAFETY, not by verb.** `client()` (writes — `POST /invoices`, `POST /payments/{id}/refund`) has **no retry at all**; `readClient()` (fetch payment/invoice, `ping`) retries twice on `ConnectionException`/5xx/429 only. Invoice creation counts as a write too: a retry there leaves a stray unpaid invoice polluting reconciliation. A failed write now surfaces to the caller — checkout already flashes `payment.init_failed`, and the admin refund flow reports the error.
> - 🔑 **Why it survived this long: `MoyasarPaymentTest` drives a hand-written fake `PaymentGateway`, so it never executes this class's HTTP client.** Testing the orchestration through a fake of the very seam you want to check leaves the seam untested. New **`tests/Feature/Payments/MoyasarGatewayHttpTest.php`** exercises the real gateway over `Http::fake()`, and both write tests were **proven to fail against the old code** (`actual size 2 matches expected size 1`).
> - **463 tests / 2053 assertions** ✓ (+7), Pint ✓. No credentials needed for any of this; nothing in the payment *flow* changed.
> - 🔴 **Second finding, NOT yet fixed: a customer cannot resume or retry a payment.** `orders.payment_url` is stored and `initiate()` reuses it, but **nothing in the UI ever surfaces it** and there is no re-initiate route (`grep payment_url` → model, the two services, nothing else). So anyone who abandons the hosted Moyasar page, fails a card, or hits a failed `initiate()` lands on an unpaid order **with no way to pay** — the store's own abandoned-checkout recovery is missing. Needs a "complete payment" button on the order page plus a gated route. Worth doing before the keys arrive, since it is the difference between a recoverable and a lost order.
>

> - **How it is hidden:** `const SHOW_FOOTER_BANNER = false` in `pages/shop/index.tsx`, gating `{SHOW_FOOTER_BANNER && <FooterBanner />}`. **Restoring it is that one word** — chosen over commenting the JSX out (which orphans the import and trips eslint) and over deleting the mount (which loses the knowledge of where it went and in what order). The flag keeps the import genuinely used, so `tsc` and eslint still typecheck the component and it cannot silently rot while switched off.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 🔴 **TEMPORARY: 13 photography samples seeded as catalogue products via `php artisan catalog:preview-products`. REMOVE BEFORE LAUNCH with `--remove`.** The client supplied a new product-photography set and wants to judge it on a real card, in situ, rather than from a folder of files.
> - **Images: 2048² PNG → 1400² WebP q82, 81 MB → 3.0 MB**, committed to `database/data/preview-products/`. They have to live in the repo because the seeding runs **on production**, where `Media::storeImageFromFile()` pushes them to R2 — there is nowhere else the container could read them from. 1400 is the `detail` variant's width, so nothing is stored larger than the pipeline will ever serve.
> - 🔑 **Everything is built around being reversible**, since these will sit in a live catalogue: every row carries a **`PREVIEW-` SKU prefix** and a `preview-NN` slug (findable without this command still existing); `--remove` deletes the rows **and** their stored files through `Media::delete()` so the WebP variants go too and nothing is orphaned in R2; and it uses **`forceDelete()`, not `delete()`** — `Product` soft-deletes, and a trashed preview row would still occupy its unique slug and SKU, blocking a re-seed.
> - **Seeding is idempotent by SKU, and that guard is load-bearing rather than tidiness**: a second run that re-uploaded would orphan the previous R2 object with nothing left pointing at it. Verified: second run reports `0 created, 13 already present`.
> - ⚠️ **They are deliberately ACTIVE and therefore BUYABLE.** `is_coming_soon` would have been the safe choice, but it renders a badge and *hides the price*, i.e. it changes exactly the part of the card under review. The exposure is bounded by the order flow itself — **nothing ships without an admin confirming it** — plus the store is still noindexed. A stray order gets rejected in the panel like any out-of-stock item.
> - **Pinned to the top of `/shop` via `is_featured`**, because the catalogue sorts `orderByDesc('is_featured')->latest()`. Being the newest rows they also lead the homepage **new arrivals** carousel; the homepage's other strips are `is_active`-gated on real data and unaffected.
> - **Names are prefixed `[عينة]` / `[Sample]`** so nobody mistakes a sample for real catalogue copy, and each caption matches what its photo actually shows (checked against a contact sheet) — a plausible name over the wrong product would undermine the very judgement being asked for.
> - **Verified as a full round trip against the real dev DB, disk and variant pipeline**, which is stronger evidence than a mocked test for something whose whole job is to be undone: seed → 13 products, 4 files each (original + 3 variants) → `--remove` → **0 products, 0 orphan image rows, 0 files, back to 87 real products** → re-seed → 13 → seed again → 0 created. `--remove` on an empty state is a clean no-op. Pint ✓.
> - **To put them on production:** deploy, then `railway ssh --service retab-website` → `php artisan catalog:preview-products`. (Plain sub-commands relay fine; only `tinker --execute` gets mangled — see the 2026-08-11 note.)
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Product card titles pinned to a two-line box, so prices line up across a row.** Last of the mobile items.
> - 🔑 **`line-clamp-2` alone does NOT fix this, and that is the whole point.** It caps the overflow but still lets a short name occupy one line, so that card's price sits a line higher than its neighbour's and the row reads as broken. The height has to be *reserved*: `line-clamp-2 min-h-[2lh]`.
> - **`2lh`, not a rem value.** The title size is a `clamp(1rem,2vw,1.35rem)`, so a fixed height would be right at one width and wrong everywhere else. Two line-heights follows the computed size for free — measured 48px at 390 and 65px at 1440, both giving a single distinct title height across every card.
> - **The catalogue grid went `line-clamp-1` → `line-clamp-2`.** One line was already even, but it cut most Arabic product names within a few words (`خلاص أشيقر درجة أولى…`); two lines fit them whole and stay even.
> - **Verified by measurement:** in the carousel, names of 14/17/39/41 characters all produce `priceTop 227` at 390 and `371` at 1440 (one distinct value each), and the catalogue grid reports **0 uneven rows** across 12 cards at both widths. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, storefront + cart specs ✓ (13). Full E2E not re-run for a CSS-only clamp change.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 🔴 **The mobile burger drawer was rendering inside the HEADER's box instead of the viewport, so tapping the menu gave a stub strip across the top.** Client-reported. One-line fix (portal to `<body>`), but the mechanism is a trap worth knowing on any stack.
> - **Cause: a transformed ancestor becomes the CONTAINING BLOCK for `position: fixed` descendants — and an identity transform counts.** The navbar's scroll handler writes `header.style.transform` on every tick, so from the first scroll event onward the header permanently carries `matrix(1,0,0,1,0,0)`, and the drawer's `fixed inset-0` resolved against the header's own **68px-tall** box. The backdrop then dimmed only the header, and the panel rendered as a 288×68 stub with the page fully visible beneath it.
> - **Why it read as intermittent:** a freshly loaded, unscrolled page has `transform: none` and works perfectly. Measured both states: overlay **390×844 before any scroll, 390×68 after**. Anyone testing it without scrolling first would conclude the menu was fine.
> - **Fix: `createPortal(drawer, document.body)`.** Chosen over moving the drawer to a sibling of `<header>` because the layout wrapper carries `overflow-x-clip`, whose interaction with fixed descendants is not worth relying on — a portal to `body` escapes every ancestor unconditionally. **Safe under SSR** because `mobileOpen` starts `false`, so the server renderer never reaches `createPortal` (which it cannot render).
> - **Verified in both locales, before and after scrolling:** parent `BODY`, overlay **390×844** in all four cases, panel anchored right in RTL (`102..390`) and left in LTR (`0..288`), 11 links present, and a link click still navigates and closes the drawer. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (the navbar is on every page and a portal is exactly the kind of change that can break hydration).
> - ⚠️ **Generalises: any `position: fixed` UI nested inside this header is broken by construction** — a future search overlay, mega-menu or modal placed there will inherit the same containment. Portal it, or hang it off the layout.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Carousel arrows shown on phones and sale prices stopped wrapping — and exposing the arrows uncovered that they had NEVER worked in Arabic.**
> - 🔴 **The bug: `atStart`/`atEnd` inverted under RTL, so on the Arabic site (the default locale) the arrow that could page was the disabled one and the enabled one did nothing.** `measure()` collapsed the scroll position with `Math.abs()`, but **`scrollLeft` runs `0..max` in LTR and `-max..0` in RTL** — modern browsers keep 0 at the reading start and go negative toward the end. So "at the start" meant the **left** edge in LTR and the **right** edge in RTL, while the buttons are physical and never moved. Proven rather than reasoned: from a fresh load in Arabic, `scrollBy(-200)` moved the track to **-309** while `scrollBy(+200)` clamped at **0**, i.e. the left button was the working one, and it was the one rendered at `opacity-20` with `pointer-events-none`.
> - **Fix: keep the state as PHYSICAL directions (`canLeft`/`canRight`) and read the travel limits per direction** (`pos > (rtl ? -max : 0)` / `pos < (rtl ? 0 : max)`). Naming them start/end is what invited the collapse; a physical button needs a physical predicate. Verified in both locales at 390 and 1440: Arabic starts left-enabled and pages `0 → -366`, English starts right-enabled and pages `0 → +366` (exactly `clientWidth + gap`, so it still lands on a fresh pair).
> - ⚠️ **`i18n.language` had to join the effect deps**: the locale toggle flips `document.dir` **without a reload or a resize**, so nothing else would re-run `measure()` and the arrows would keep the previous direction's enabled states until the next scroll.
> - **Arrows below 640px sit in the page's side margin (`max-sm:-left-5`/`-right-5`), not over the cards.** There is no gutter to use (the track is full-bleed so two cards stay readable) and the cards are only ~160px wide, so an arrow parked at the container edge covered ~18% of a product photo. Pulled outward they clear all but ~6px, and a `bg-white/85` disc covers that sliver in case it lands on a dark image. The track was already swipeable; what was missing was any sign that more follows.
> - **Sale prices: `whitespace-nowrap` on every amount + stacked below `sm`.** `{price} {currency}` is a text node with a real space, so it broke as "100.00" over "SAR", and two prices never fit one line on a phone card. New price over struck-through old below 640, side by side above. Applied to **both** `product-carousel.tsx` and `product-card.tsx` (catalogue/search grids had the same markup).
> - ⚠️ **Gotcha — `getClientRects().length > 1` is NOT a wrap detector in RTL.** Every Arabic price reported **3** rects while sitting on a single 20px line: mixed Latin digits and Arabic currency split the inline box into bidi runs, one rect each. Compare the element's height against its line-height instead, or a working `nowrap` looks broken.
> - **Client reviews (same pass):** feature-card scrim 45% → **70% below `sm`** (55% above) because at phone width the copy fills the card and sits over the busy middle of the photo rather than the calmer edges it was measured against on desktop; and the reviews go **two per row from the smallest width up** (feature card spans both columns until `lg`). Cards land at 165px, about a third of their design width, so padding/avatar/type/stars all step down with them. Section **773px** at 390, from ~1140.
> - `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (worth running: the arrow change is shared logic on the homepage, and the storefront specs exercise it).
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Category tiles rearranged into a 3-over-2 pyramid below `lg`.** Five tiles in a 2-column grid left the fifth orphaned on its own row against the start edge; client asked for smaller tiles, three per row, with the second row centred in the gaps of the first.
> - 🔑 **The mechanism is a SIX-column grid with every tile spanning 2, not a three-column grid.** Three columns cannot centre a short final row at all — the orphan just sits at column 1. Six half-columns let the last row start half a tile across, which puts it exactly between the tiles above. **The alignment is exact for any gap size, not approximate:** with column `c` and gap `g`, row-one centres fall at `c+½g`, `3c+2½g`, `5c+4½g`, and an offset row-two tile at `2c+1½g` — precisely the midpoint of the first pair. Measured across 6 widths × 2 locales: **offset error 0.00px** everywhere.
> - **The offset is derived from the count, never hardcoded to five**, so the client adding a sixth category cannot silently break it: `inLastRow = count % 3 || 3`, then the first tile of that row gets a `col-start` of `1 + (3 − inLastRow)`. 4 tiles ⇒ the last one centres dead middle; 6 ⇒ no offset at all.
> - ⚠️ **The `col-start` classes must be whole literal strings** (`LAST_ROW_START` map), because Tailwind scans source text — a built `col-start-${n}` compiles to no CSS whatsoever and the row would silently fall back to the start edge.
> - **Applied below `lg`, not below `sm`.** The orphan existed at tablet widths too (3-col grid, 5 tiles ⇒ 3 + 2 left-aligned), so scoping the fix to phones would have left the same visual bug at 640–1023px. Above `lg` all five still sit on one line, unchanged.
> - **Also shrunk for phones as asked:** tile images `h-40 → h-28` below `sm`, labels stepped down to `0.8rem`, gaps tightened. Section is **474px** at 390 wide with a clean 3+2. ⚠️ At **320px** the English labels wrap to two lines (tiles are only 83px there) — harmless, since the fixed-height image row keeps every tile on a shared baseline, but it is why the label size is stepped rather than fluid.
> - `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓, screenshotted at 390 in both locales. ⏭ Not measured: a clean before/after section height — the comparison run kept failing on a stale dev server and it was not worth further time. The structural change (3 rows of 2 → 2 rows of 3, smaller tiles) is the substance.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Phone hero and banner trimmed from the top; both were slightly taller than a phone screen (client-reported).** Hero **848 → 780px** at 390 wide, banner **848 → 702px**.
> - 🔑 **Cutting from the TOP is the expensive direction here, and that has to be measured before it is done.** The subject in both portrait crops is anchored at the bottom, so a top trim is the only one that does not cut it — but the top band is *also* the empty sand/teal the copy sits in, so **every pixel removed is a pixel of copy headroom spent**. Measured by per-row luminance sd: the hero's man and fire begin at **y=371 of 874 (42.4%)**, the banner's product cluster at **y=539 (61.7%)**. Against that, the copy needs **220px (hero)** and **172px (banner)** in the worst case — which is **English at a 320px viewport**, not Arabic and not a common width.
> - **So the two crops differ because the compositions do, not for symmetry: hero −70px, banner −150px.** The hero had only ~95px of theoretical slack and 70 was taken to leave a real margin; the banner had ~320px and could have gone further, but 150 keeps the 320px case comfortable. **Measured clearance after the change: worst gap hero 11px, banner 74px** (both at 320px EN), rising to 36-138px / 110-236px at every real phone width. Zero overlaps and zero horizontal overflow across **9 widths × 2 locales**.
> - **Done in CSS — `max-sm:aspect-[402/804]` + `object-cover` + `object-bottom` — not by re-cutting the files.** Lossless, reversible, and the trim stays a single tunable number. Re-encoding would have saved a few KB and thrown away the master framing.
> - ⚠️ **The overlay band percentage is COUPLED to the crop and must be recomputed with it.** Trimming moves the subject's position *within what remains*: hero 42.4% → 37.4% (band 38% → **37%**), banner 61.7% → 53.7% (band 58% → **52%**). Leaving the band alone would have walked the copy straight into the subject. Both components carry an inline note saying so.
> - **Result on the fold:** navbar 69 + hero 780 = **849px against an 844px viewport** at 390×844, i.e. essentially exactly one screen, against 917px before. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓, screenshotted at 390 in both locales.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **HardRock build credit added to the footer's legal bar.** Client asked for a link to hardrock-co.com (the agency behind the build) and for it to look considered rather than bolted on.
> - **Deliberately the quietest thing in the bar.** It inherits the row's `text-brand-teal/70` at rest so it never competes with the client's own brand, and only earns colour on hover — **`brand-gold`, not the neighbours' teal**, so it reads as a different KIND of link (off-site) rather than another Retab page. Chosen over a monospace `< />` signature (clashes with the footer's Thmanyah elegance, and the device is a cliché), a full logo wordmark (too prominent on a client's site), and a centred colophon row (adds ~40px of footer height).
> - **The HardRock EMBLEM sits between the label and the name** (`components/hardrock-mark.tsx`), traced from the agency's master `HOR-BLACK LOGO.svg`. 🔑 **The emblem, not the wordmark** — in the master the mark occupies x 0–155.55 while the "HARDROCK" letterforms start at x=177, so it separates cleanly, and using the emblem means the SVG is not just repeating the adjacent text. Bounds were taken with `getBBox()` in a headless browser rather than eyeballed off the path data; a naive `x < 300` filter wrongly swept in the wordmark's first letter.
> - ⚠️ **Painted in `currentColor`, deliberately discarding the master's gradient.** It renders **9.8 × 13.8px** beside 12px type — at that size a two-stop gradient is not perceivable as a gradient, only as uneven tone, and it would be the single element in the cream-and-gold footer wearing a foreign colour. Inheriting instead means the mark mutes and warms to gold in step with its label (verified: `rgb(175,144,86)` on hover). Sized `h-[1.15em] w-auto` — **em not px**, so it stays optically matched if the bar's type size changes, and **not square**, since the emblem is 0.708:1 and a square box would squash it (rendered ratio measured at exactly 0.708).
> - 🔑 **The logo is NEVER mirrored; only the arrow is.** Confirmed per locale rather than assumed — the mark reports `scale: none` in both, the arrow `-1 1` under RTL. ⚠️ Checking this needs the standalone **`scale`** property: Tailwind v4 no longer composes `scale-x-*` into `transform`, so `getComputedStyle(el).transform` reads `none` even when the flip is working, which looks exactly like a broken variant.
> - **The underline is a background gradient sized `0% → 100%`, not a border**, so it *wipes* in rather than appearing. ⚠️ **It must start from the reading side or the wipe runs backwards in Arabic** — hence `rtl:bg-[position:100%_100%]`. Verified per locale: origin `0px 100%` in LTR, `100% 100%` in RTL.
> - ⚠️ **The arrow is always rendered, never toggled on hover** — it just brightens and steps outward. A conditional icon would reflow the legal bar on every hover. Measured: link width is **identical at rest and hovered** (101.1px AR / 116.3px EN). It is mirrored with `rtl:-scale-x-100` so "away from the text" stays away, and note Tailwind composes translate BEFORE scale, so RTL needs its own `-translate-x` rather than inheriting the flip.
> - **Links `https://www.hardrock-co.com` directly**, because the apex 301s to www — no reason to send every visitor through an extra hop. `rel="noopener noreferrer"` is not decoration on a `target="_blank"` link: without it the opened page gets a handle on this one through `window.opener`.
> - **Only the lead-in is localized** (`footer.builtBy` → "Built by" / "تطوير"); the company name stays Latin in both, being a brand rather than a phrase.
> - ⚠️ **GOTCHA while verifying: `curl https://hardrock-co.com` returns 403** — that is Cloudflare blocking a bare curl, not a dead site. With a browser User-Agent it is a clean 200. **Don't conclude a client's site is down from a curl 403**; send a real UA before believing it.
> - ⚠️ **Second gotcha: the cookie-consent banner intercepts pointer events over the footer**, so any hover check must pre-seed the `retab_consent` cookie — and the value shape matters (`{analytics, marketing, v:'1'}`; a guessed shape leaves the banner up). `e2e/fixtures.ts` already has the exact value; copy it rather than inventing one.
> - ⏭ **Open question for HardRock, not for this repo: whether the credit should be `rel="nofollow"`.** Shipped followed, since it is a genuine editorial attribution and that is the normal case. But Google's link-spam guidance calls out *"widely distributed links in the footers of various sites"*, and the risk lands on the **receiving** end — i.e. HardRock accumulating sitewide footer links across many client sites. One credit is fine; revisit the pattern if it becomes a portfolio-wide habit.
> - **Verified in a real browser in both locales:** present once, correct `target`/`rel`, underline wipe direction per locale, gold hover (`rgb(175,144,86)`), zero layout shift, screenshotted. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (the footer is on every page). No PHP touched.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Shipping history made readable, and the store's REAL carrier cost is now recorded (new `orders.shipping_cost`).** Follow-up to the carrier picker below, prompted by the client asking whether a ship → cancel → re-ship sequence is tracked and whether the cost is. Answer was "thinly, and no".
> - 🔴 **The timeline rendered a shipment as the literal word "tracking".** The carrier and tracking number were being stored in `order_activities.meta` all along, but `detailData()` **never shipped `meta` to the client**, and the React row falls through to printing the raw `type` string for anything that is not a `status_change`. So a ship → recall → re-ship read as `tracking` / `shipped → confirmed` / `tracking`: you could not tell which carrier was used, or that the middle row was a recall at all. Now renders **"Shipped via SMSA · TRK-1 · cost 19.50 SAR"**.
> - 🔴 **The carrier's price was fetched, used to sort, and thrown away.** `orders.shipping_fee` is what the CUSTOMER paid (the flat GCC rate); nothing recorded what the shipment actually **cost the store**. That matters beyond bookkeeping: the whole flat-rate decision is *"the client accepts absorbing the cross-GCC cost difference"*, and the difference was unmeasurable. New nullable `orders.shipping_cost` + the figure in each shipping activity's meta, surfaced as **Carrier cost** and **Margin vs fee** (red when negative) in the order sidebar.
> - 🔑 **This reverses a decision made hours earlier, and the reason is worth recording.** The picker originally skipped the quote on a manual pick — "validating the id would double the OTO calls, and a stale id is self-correcting". That was right when the id was all we wanted. But the **price exists nowhere else** (`createShipment` does not report it), so recording cost requires the quote anyway — and resolving the option out of the live list then validates the id **for free**, turning a rejected stale pick into a clear *"that carrier is no longer available, reopen the list"* instead of whatever OTO would have answered. `fulfill()` now takes the whole `DeliveryOption`, not an id. Automatic was already quoting, so the extra call only ever lands on a manual pick.
> - **Shipment recall is its own activity type (`shipment_cancelled`), not a bare status change.** "shipped → confirmed" is the only backwards move an order can make, so a plain status row *implies* a recall — but carries nothing about **which** carrier and tracking number were recalled, which is exactly what someone auditing a double carrier charge needs. from/to are still populated so the status timeline stays continuous.
> - ⚠️ **`shipping_cost` is CLEARED on recall, deliberately, while the activity keeps it.** The column describes the *current* shipment; a re-ship overwrites it. Keeping the figure only there would erase the cost of every cancelled attempt, so both the shipment and its recall stamp it into meta — that pair is what makes "did this order cost us two carrier charges?" answerable. Nullable rather than 0-defaulted throughout: orders shipped before this existed have an unknown cost, and 0 would assert we shipped them free.
> - **Also localized the four `ShippingService` exception strings** (`__('messages.admin.*')`). They are flashed straight to the admin via `$e->getMessage()`, and the panel supports Arabic — so an Arabic-speaking operator was getting English error text.
> - **Verified in a real browser, not just asserted:** the activity list renders `Shipped via SMSA · TRK-DEMO-9 · cost 19.50 SAR` and `Shipment recalled (Naqel) · TRK-OLD-1 · cost 23.00 SAR`, and the sidebar shows `Carrier cost 19.50 SAR` / `Margin vs fee 5.50 SAR`. **456 tests / 2036 assertions** ✓ (+5), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓. Migration applied to dev MariaDB.
> - ⚠️ **Local dev has NO `OTO_*` vars, so the picker legitimately shows `MISSING_REFRESH_TOKEN`** — that is the error path working, not a bug. 🔴 **Do NOT paste the live token into local `.env`:** it points at the client's real OTO account, so clicking Ship would create a genuine order and shipment and could dispatch an actual courier to the Riyadh warehouse. OTO's docs mention no sandbox — worth asking them. To exercise the populated picker locally, intercept `**/shipping-quotes` in a browser session instead.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Manual carrier selection on the Ship action, a real Cancel-shipment action, and 🔴 a fix for an admin button that could NEVER have worked.** Client asked for a carrier picker; auditing the surrounding cancel semantics to answer a follow-up question turned up two defects that had been live since the shipping layer was built.
> - 🔴 **DEFECT 1 — the admin "Cancel" button was offered ONLY in the one state where it was guaranteed to fail.** The UI gate was `'cancel' => status === Confirmed`, while the service behind it (`OrderConfirmationService::cancelByCustomer`) rejects anything outside `isCancellableByCustomer()` = `[PendingPayment, AwaitingConfirmation]`. **Those two conditions are exact complements**, so the button appeared precisely where the operation could not succeed, and every click flashed *"This order can no longer be cancelled."* It had never worked in any case. _Proven, not inferred:_ a throwaway probe against the real route on a Confirmed order returned `can.cancel: true`, `flash.error: "This order can no longer be cancelled."`, status unchanged. _Why nothing caught it:_ the only cancel test called the **service directly** with `PendingPayment` and never went through the route. _Fix:_ the gate now asks the enum (`$order->status->isCancellableByCustomer()`) instead of restating its rule, so the two can no longer drift apart.
> - 🔴 **DEFECT 2 — `ShippingService::cancel()` was fully written and completely unreachable.** No route, no caller anywhere in the app (grepped the whole tree). It recalls the parcel from OTO, clears tracking/carrier/label and returns the order to `confirmed` — almost certainly what the Cancel button was *meant* to call, given it was gated to exactly that state. Now wired to `POST /admin/orders/{n}/cancel-shipment`.
> - 🔑 **The conceptual fix is that there are TWO cancels and the panel exposed one button for both.** Cancelling the **ORDER** kills it and releases the money (voids a Tamara hold or refunds the card, `total` **including** the flat shipping fee). Cancelling the **SHIPMENT** recalls the parcel, moves **no money at all** — the order is still live and still owed, so refunding would be wrong — and returns it to `confirmed` so it can be shipped again. They are now separate buttons with different wording, styling (danger vs secondary) and confirm text that states the money consequence explicitly.
> - **Re-shipping after a recall works by construction:** `cancel()` clears `tracking_number`, which is exactly what `can.ship` (`Confirmed && ! tracking_number`) and `fulfill()`'s double-shipment guard both test. `oto_id` is deliberately **kept** so the re-ship reuses the order already pushed to OTO rather than colliding on a duplicate order number. ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED against a live parcel:** whether OTO's `cancelShipment` voids only the shipment or the whole order their side. If the latter, the re-ship fails at `createShipment` with OTO's own error (recoverable) and `oto_id` would need clearing here — confirm on the first real cancellation before putting the re-ship path in the client manual.
> - **Carrier picker** — `components/admin/shipping-picker.tsx`, opened by the Ship button. Defaults to **Automatic (cheapest)**, because the customer pays a flat rate regardless so the price gap is entirely the store's margin; the manual list exists for what price can't express (a faster carrier, or the only one serving a remote district). Shows each carrier's cost + ETA, badges the cheapest row, and carries an explicit note that **these are the store's costs, not the customer's** — without it the prices read like a charge to the buyer.
> - ⚠️ **Quotes are a JSON endpoint, NOT an `Inertia::optional` prop — and the reason generalises.** The optional-prop approach was written first and abandoned: `OrderDetailView` is **shared with the in-list modal**, which has no Inertia page of its own to hang a prop on. A plain `GET .../shipping-quotes` sits beside the `detail` endpoint that modal already fetches, so both surfaces load quotes identically. Gated on `orders.manage` rather than `orders.view` since it pushes the order to OTO and burns a live rate lookup.
> - 🔑 **That endpoint always answers 200, with any failure as DATA.** A quote crosses the network and can fail for reasons unrelated to the order (credentials, outage, an unserviceable destination). Throwing would give the operator a dead spinner; instead the provider's own message is rendered in the dialog — *"no options for this destination"* and *"credentials rejected"* demand completely different responses from a human — alongside a note that automatic shipping still works, which is true because `fulfill()` re-quotes server-side.
> - ⚠️ **Chosen carrier ids are deliberately NOT validated against a fresh quote.** That would double the OTO calls on every shipment, and a stale id is self-correcting: OTO rejects it, the admin sees the error and reopens the picker. Only `nullable|integer` is enforced.
> - **Verified in a real browser as well as by tests**, because none of the automated gates would catch a modal that fails to open: the dialog renders 1 automatic + 3 carrier rows with automatic preselected, the **Cheapest badge appears exactly once and on the cheapest row**, the no-ETA fallback and cost disclaimer render, picking Naqel posts `{"delivery_option_id":"11"}`, and with no OTO creds in dev the real failure path shows the error block plus the automatic fallback. Action buttons per state confirmed: a shipped order offers only **Cancel shipment**, a confirmed one only **Ship**.
> - ⚠️ **Two browser-check traps worth reusing:** `[role="dialog"]` matches **two** elements in this panel (the modal *and* the always-mounted "How it works" drawer — scope to `div[role="dialog"]`), and Playwright's `text=Cheapest` reported **3** matches for a single badge because the text selector also matches ancestor elements *and* the automatic row's "Cheapest carrier available…" hint. **Count the badge element by its own class, not by its text**, or a correct render looks like a triplicate bug.
> - **451 tests / 2014 assertions** ✓ (+12), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓.
> - ⏭ **Still missing and it is a BRIEF requirement, not a nicety: customers cannot cancel their own order.** The method is literally named `cancelByCustomer`, but the only route reaching it is the admin one — there is **no storefront cancel route or button**. The brief says "customer may cancel only before admin confirms → notify customer indirectly". The service and the enum guard are already correct, so this is a route + a button on the storefront order page + the WhatsApp touchpoint.
