> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 🚚 **OTO / Tryoto is LIVE in production — the first of the four external integrations to be activated.** Client generated the refresh token, the `orderStatus` webhook is registered, and the three vars are set on `retab-website`. Wiring the credential surfaced **two real bugs in code that had been sitting there since the shipping layer was built**, both of which would only have appeared in production.
> - 🔴 **BUG 1 — the access token was cached for SIX DAYS while OTO grants ONE HOUR.** `OtoClient::tokenTtl()` returned a hardcoded `60*60*24*6` on the assumption (stated in its own comment) that OTO issues long-lived tokens. It does not: probing the live `/refreshToken` returns `expires_in: "3600"`. So the first OTO call would cache a token, it would die an hour later, and **every** subsequent call — delivery rates, order push, shipment creation — would 401 for the remaining ~5 days. `forgetToken()` existed with a docblock reading "used after a 401", but **nothing called it anywhere in the app or the tests**, so the cache could never self-heal. Symptom would have been orders silently stuck at confirmed with no shipment and no user-visible error. _Fix:_ the TTL is now read from the response's own `expires_in` less a 5-minute margin (⚠️ it arrives as the **string** `"3600"`, hence `is_numeric`), with a deliberately short 30-minute fallback if the field ever goes missing.
> - 🔑 **The retry config was actively making it worse, which is the non-obvious half.** `->retry(2, 200, throw: false)` fires on *any* unsuccessful response, so a 401 was retried **with the very same expired token** — guaranteed to fail again, and it consumed the attempt that should have fetched a fresh one. Verified in a test: the built-in retry swallowed the 401 path entirely, so the new-token logic never ran. Retries are now scoped to genuinely transient faults (`ConnectionException`, 5xx, 429) and a 401/403 goes through `send()`, which forgets the token and retries **once** (one, deliberately — a genuinely bad refresh token would otherwise loop).
> - 🔴 **BUG 2 — `outForDelivery` callbacks were being silently dropped.** OTO's documented payloads are camelCase (`shipmentProcessing`), and `mapStatus()` **mixed conventions inside a single line**: it listed `pickedup` and `intransit` (camelCase-friendly, lowercased) but only `out_for_delivery` with underscores. A real `outForDelivery` event therefore fell through to `null` and the order stayed put. _Fix:_ strip all separators before matching, so `outForDelivery` / `out_for_delivery` / `out-for-delivery` collapse to one key and **no provider's casing has to be guessed again**. Both fixes are pinned by tests that were **proven to fail against the old code** (`518400 is not less than 3600`, and `'outForDelivery' did not map to shipped`) — mandatory here, since a test that passes either way would have been worthless.
> - **Token facts worth not re-deriving.** The refresh token does **not** exist until you press Save on Settings → Developers → API Integrations → Connect (the field shows a grey *"Click on the Copy button to get the token"* hint beforehand, and their FAQ's "generated automatically, copy it" reads like it should already be there). It is **REUSABLE** despite the exchange handing back a *new* `refresh_token` in every response — that looks exactly like Firebase-style one-time rotation, which would have broken the env-var approach entirely, so it was tested: three exchanges with the same original token, all 200. Storing one long-lived value in an env var is sound.
> - ⚠️ **TRAP — leave the dashboard's "Order Prefix" EMPTY.** Their docs describe it as *"removes specified prefix from orders before payload preparation"*, and `ShippingService::applyStatusUpdate()` resolves orders by an **exact** `order_number` match. A prefix would therefore strip characters off `RTB-260815-A7K2X` on the way out, the callback would name an order that does not exist, and the handler would just log "OTO status update for unknown order" and return null. Same silent stuck-at-confirmed symptom as bug 1, with a different cause. Our order numbers already carry their own `RTB-` prefix and are uniqueness-checked, so the field buys nothing.
> - **Webhook config (registered):** URL `{APP_URL}/webhooks/oto?token={OTO_WEBHOOK_SECRET}`, method **POST**, type **`orderStatus`** (the other two types are `newOrders`, for pushing into a separate WMS, and `shipmentError`; neither applies). The **Authorization Key** field is set to the same secret — OTO sends it verbatim in the `Authorization` header, which is its intended mechanism, so `OtoWebhookController` now accepts the secret from **three** places: `?token=`, that header (with or without a `Bearer`/`Token` prefix), or a `secret_token` body field. Belt and braces: whichever OTO actually sends, we authenticate, and it survives a proxy stripping either one.
> - **Secret Key deliberately left BLANK.** It makes OTO sign the body HMAC-SHA256 over `orderId:status:timestamp` — genuinely stronger, but that signing string is read off documentation rather than confirmed against a real callback, and enforcing it wrongly rejects **every** genuine delivery notification. A shared secret over TLS is adequate; noted in the controller as an upgrade once a live payload has been seen.
> - ⚠️ **`OTO_*` vars go on `retab-website` ONLY, not the queue worker.** Checked rather than assumed: every OTO call originates in a web request (the webhook endpoint, plus `pushOrder`/`createShipment` from `Admin\OrderController` on admin-confirm). `SendReviewReminder` is the only job that even mentions shipping and that is a docblock reference to where it gets dispatched from — it never resolves the gateway. Same reasoning as the R2 vars.
> - **Verified live, three ways.** `integrations:check` over `railway ssh` reports `OTO — OK — token exchange OK (HTTP 200)`; the production webhook was probed with a deliberately non-existent order number (so nothing could change) and returned **200 for the query token, 200 for a raw `Authorization` header, 200 for a `Bearer`-prefixed one, 401 for a wrong token and 401 for no credential at all**; and **439 tests / 1949 assertions** ✓ (+16), Pint ✓.
> - ✅ **Pickup location CONFIRMED present — and note this was answerable from here after all.** The worry was that `createOrder` needs somewhere for the courier to collect from, and that a missing one would leave every probe green while the first real order failed at confirm time. Rather than asking the client to hunt through the dashboard, `GET /getPickupLocationList` with the live token answers it outright: **1 warehouse, 0 branches** — code/name `موقع الإرسال الخاص بي`, city **Riyadh** (matching `OTO_ORIGIN_CITY`), contact سعود الجلعود. 🔑 **Reach for the provider's own read-only list endpoint before asking a human to check a dashboard** — it is faster, it cannot be misread, and it returns the exact identifiers the payload may later need.
> - ⚠️ **Correcting my own framing while asking: the load-bearing object is the PICKUP LOCATION, not the Brand.** `brandId` is documented as **optional** on `createOrder`, so the Brands page is irrelevant to this integration. Likewise `pickupLocationCode` is **not** universally required (a search snippet claiming otherwise was wrong) — the docs say it is required only when `serviceType` is `pickupfromStore` or when `createShipment: true` is passed *inside* `createOrder`. `OtoGateway::pushOrder()` does neither (we call `/createShipment` as a separate step), so OTO **auto-assigns** the location. That auto-assignment is only well-defined because exactly one exists.
> - ⏭ **Watch item: auto-assignment becomes ambiguous the moment a SECOND pickup location is added.** With one warehouse it can only pick the right one; with two, OTO chooses and couriers could be sent to collect from the wrong place, which is an operational failure with no error to see. If the client ever adds a branch or a second warehouse, pin it explicitly by sending `pickupLocationCode` in `pushOrder()` (worth an `OTO_PICKUP_LOCATION_CODE` env var then, deliberately not added now to avoid unused config). ⚠️ Note the existing code is **Arabic text**, so it needs care as a JSON/query value if it is ever passed explicitly.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **`<link rel="canonical">` added sitewide; it was missing on every page.** New `App\Support\CanonicalUrl` + a tag in `app.blade.php`.
> - **It needed policy, not a bare `url()->current()`**, because the catalogue is one path behind many query strings and each distinct URL is a separate page to a crawler. `category`, `on_sale` and `page` survive (they change *which* products are listed); `sort` (same products, reordered), `q` (unbounded search results) and every `utm_*`/`fbclid` a shared WhatsApp link accretes are dropped. `page=1` and a falsy `on_sale` collapse onto the bare URL, and params are **ksort**ed so the same page reached two ways cannot mint two canonicals.
> - 🔑 **Built through `url()->current()`, deliberately NOT `$request->url()`.** The former inherits `URL::forceRootUrl(config('app.url'))` and always names the real domain; the latter reads the **request** host, which would make the site canonicalise every duplicate to itself — precisely the self-canonicalising duplicate that `forceRootUrl` was added on 2026-08-09 to prevent. Pinned by a test that requests the page on the Railway hostname and asserts the tag still says `https://retab.com.sa`.
> - ⚠️ **Lives in Blade, not a page's Inertia `<Head>`.** Per the 2026-08-08 finding, Inertia head tags only reach the server HTML while the SSR sidecar is up, so a canonical placed there would vanish the moment SSR was off. Ordering is not a concern the way it is for `og:image` (which must stay out of the layout so a product page's own photo wins) because **no page emits a canonical of its own** — verified, so there is exactly one on the document.
> - **Verified on production after deploy:** `/shop` → `https://retab.com.sa/shop`, `?sort=price_asc&utm_source=test&fbclid=xyz&page=2` → `…/shop?page=2`, `/login` → no tag at all.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — 🔴 **`hasPermission()` and `resolvedPermissions()` disagreed, so a new admin section showed a sidebar entry that 403'd.** Found while going to grant editors the `contact_messages` permission; it turned out to be a code bug rather than a missing grant.
> - **The mechanism:** the admin sidebar renders from `resolvedPermissions()`, which merges stored grants **over** `Permission::DEFAULTS`, while route access went through `hasPermission()`, which read `users.permissions` **directly** (`$this->permissions ?? DEFAULTS`). The grid writes the *entire* schema on save, so a section added later is simply **absent** from an existing editor's stored array — the sidebar then fell back to the default (visible) and the route gate read the absence as a denial. _Fix:_ `hasPermission()` now goes through the same resolver, so the two can no longer disagree. This retroactively covers `product_requests` too, which this file had flagged as needing a manual re-grant when it shipped.
> - **The merge direction matters in both directions and is now pinned by tests:** an explicit `false` in the stored grants still revokes (because the grid writes every action), while a section the grants have never heard of inherits its default. Plus a test asserting the sidebar and the route gate return the *same* answer for a stale row.
> - ⚠️ **Honest scope: this was LATENT in production, not live.** Prod has exactly one staff user — an admin with `permissions` NULL — and admins bypass every check, so nothing was broken today. It would have bitten the first time the client created an editor account.
> - **Also done:** the two `contact_messages` smoke-test rows I submitted on 2026-08-11 were deleted from prod, along with the two bell notifications they generated (which had become orphans — the payload carries a `contact_message_id`, contradicting this file's earlier claim that it carries nothing linkable). ⚠️ The real customer message from that day was left untouched and is still the only row.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — ✅ **Media moved off `r2.dev` onto `cdn.retab.com.sa`, so product images finally get edge caching.** This closes the 🔴 item outstanding since the R2 cutover on 2026-08-09. The blocker back then was that an R2 custom domain requires the zone in the **same Cloudflare account** as the bucket, and `retab.com.sa` was still on Bluvalt; the nameserver move on 2026-08-10 dissolved it, which is exactly why the bucket was deliberately created in the client-owned account.
> - 🔑 **The measured payoff, which is the whole reason this mattered:** on `r2.dev` responses carried **no `CF-Cache-Status` header at all** — not a MISS, *absent* — meaning genuinely zero edge caching, so every image was an uncached round trip from KSA to the bucket's **Eastern Europe (EEUR)** region. The same object on the custom domain returns **`cf-cache-status: HIT`** with an `Age`, served from the **Amman** edge. Until now the WebP variant pipeline was doing its job while the delivery path undid it.
> - **One env var and no data migration, verified rather than assumed:** `R2_URL` is consumed in exactly one place (`config/filesystems.php` → the `r2` disk's `url`), `Media::url()` builds every URL at render time from that disk config, and **0 rows** in `product_images` store an absolute URL (paths are keys like `products/11/uuid.jpeg`). CSP needed no change either — `img-src 'self' data: blob: https:` already allows any HTTPS host.
> - **Ordering was chosen so no window exists where images can break:** test the new domain against real keys *before* touching config (needs no deploy, so a not-ready domain simply means don't proceed) → swap `R2_URL` → confirm the storefront → only then disable r2.dev. Pre-flight: the domain returned **403 while status was "Initializing"** (cert provisioning, DNS already live), then 200; bytes **MD5-identical** to r2.dev; **14/14** real keys scraped from the live catalogue returned 200. After the redeploy, `/shop` carried **24** `cdn.retab.com.sa` references and **0** r2.dev.
> - ⚠️ **Gotcha for the next time this is checked: local OpenSSL cannot test TLS 1.0 any more.** Probing the minimum-TLS setting with `openssl s_client -tls1` reported "refused" for every host — but the same command fails identically against `tls-v1-0.badssl.com`, which *does* accept it, with `no protocols available` from OpenSSL 3.5.4. **The client refuses to offer the protocol, which is indistinguishable from the server rejecting it.** Use SSL Labs or a browser, not local openssl. ⏭ Open: the R2 custom domain ships with **Minimum TLS 1.0**; raise to 1.2 (PCI DSS has required 1.2+ since 2018 and the Moyasar review is pending), and check the separate zone-level floor under SSL/TLS → Edge Certificates, which is the one that actually protects login and checkout.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-15 — **Phones get their own hero and "جودة نزرعها" banner art (designer-supplied portrait crops), which closes a long-standing mobile complaint.** Both new files are **402×874** (an iPhone 16 Pro viewport). The problem they solve was already logged on 2026-08-08 but never fixed: the landscape crops are aspect-locked, so at 390px wide the hero rendered **217px tall** and the banner **135px** — slivers with the copy crushed into them. The portrait crops render ~848px, about one phone screen.
> - **`<picture>` + `<source media="(max-width: 639px)">`, not two `<img>` toggled with `hidden`.** The browser resolves ONE source and fetches only that, so a phone never downloads the 145 KB landscape file it will not display. Verified per width: exactly one `/images/hero/` and one `/images/banner/` request, switching at 640 (639 → portrait, 640 → landscape).
> - 🔑 **The overlay MOVES, it is not merely rescaled, because the two crops put their empty copy space in different places.** Measured per-row luminance sd rather than eyeballed: the hero portrait is uniform sand (sd 13-24) to ~39% of its height, then sd jumps to ~49 where the man and the fire start; the banner portrait is flat teal (sd 2-12) to ~59%, then sd 66 where the product cluster starts. So the copy bands are the top **38%** and top **58%** respectively — and the banner's is the *inverse* of desktop, where the empty teal is on the right. Swept 11 widths × 2 locales: no copy ever reaches its subject, tightest being EN at 320px (CTA ends 38.8% against a 41% subject).
> - **The scrim direction follows the copy** — `bg-gradient-to-r` on desktop (text left), `max-sm:bg-gradient-to-b` on phones (text in the headroom above the subject).
> - 🔴 **The phone banner CTA had to invert, and it is a measured contrast fix rather than taste.** Sampling each crop *at the button's own measured position*: desktop lands at y≈71% on the beige floor where the teal pill scores **5.40:1**; the portrait puts it at y≈35% on the dark teal wall (#163639) where the same pill scores **1.39:1** — below the 3:1 floor for UI components, visible only because of its white ring. White there scores **12.91:1**. So phones get a white pill with a teal label.
> - ⚠️ **That exposed a trap in `.cta-shimmer`: it paints the label through `background-clip: text` + `-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent`, so it OVERRIDES any Tailwind `text-*` class.** Setting `max-sm:text-brand-teal` alone would have silently produced a white label on a white pill. The colours are now variables — but note the second half of the trap, which the first fix walked straight into: 🔑 **declaring `--shimmer-base: #fff` inside `.cta-shimmer` shadows the override**, because a custom property set on the element itself beats one inherited from the parent button. Caught by measuring (the phone still reported `--shimmer-base: #fff` while the pill was white). The fix is a **fallback inside `var()`** — `var(--shimmer-base, #fff)` — and no declaration at all, so the parent's value is inherited and the default still applies when unset. Reduced-motion uses the variable too, verified as teal rather than hardcoded white.
> - ⚠️ **Removed dead `max-[480px]` rules rather than leaving them.** Once `max-sm` was added, those never applied: measured at 460px the h1 computes to **34.5px** (max-sm's value), not the 32px an active `max-[480px]` rule would give. **The named `max-sm` variant is emitted after both arbitrary `max-[…]` ones and wins throughout** — worth knowing before adding another arbitrary max-width step to any of these components. Below 640 there is now one size set, not three.
> - 🔴 **Follow-up (client-reported): the ENGLISH hero headline was a broken lockup at every width, not just on phones.** The design is a two-line block — teal line over white line — which Arabic holds by kashida-elongating both lines to equal width. English has no such device, so it just wrapped, leaving `land` and `table` orphaned on lines of their own: four ragged lines against Arabic's two. _Two independent causes, both measured in the real font rather than eyeballed:_
>   - **Phones — the copy was too long.** At 390px the lines measured **374px and 348px against a 342px column**. Note the second was over by only 6px, which is the kind of near-miss that looks like a rendering bug rather than a copy problem. Shortened to **"From the finest land" / "to your finest table"** — 285px and 267px, fitting at every phone width down to 320px, and near-equal to each other so the block reads as balanced, which is exactly what the Arabic gets from its tatweel. Copy length is now load-bearing here, so there's an inline note giving the ~270px budget for any future rewrite.
>   - 🔑 **Desktop — the ramp was mathematically incapable of fitting, at any width.** The headline needed ~0.604 of the viewport width while the column only offers ~0.51 of it (56% minus padding), and since both scale with `vw` the ratio never changes: it could not have fitted on any monitor. Dropped the ramp `6.2vw → 4.9vw`. ⚠️ **Widening the column instead is not available** — per-column variance on the landscape crop puts the subject's left edge at **x=58.3%**, and the column already runs to 56%.
>   - ⚠️ The desktop wrap **predates this work** (the old copy was 1124px against a 718px column at 1440px, so far worse) — worth stating plainly, because the shortened copy alone made 1920px fit and could easily read as having caused the remaining wrap rather than having halved it.
> - **Verified:** the sweep above (11 widths × 2 locales, zero horizontal overflow everywhere), the per-width source check, contrast sampled from the actual image pixels, the shimmer variable confirmed on phone/desktop/reduced-motion, and the headline re-checked at **13 widths × 2 locales — every span exactly one line** (320→1920). Screenshots at 390px and 1440px in both locales. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓. No PHP touched, and no test or spec asserts on the hero copy (checked before changing it).
> - ⚠️ **Flake note:** one full E2E run came back 30/31 with `admin-notifications.spec.ts › the bell links to a full history page` failing — untouched by any of this. It passed **3/3 in isolation** and the next full run was **31/31**, so it is a flake, not a regression. Worth knowing that this file is the flaky one locally: CI has `retries: 1` (see `playwright.config.ts`) but a local run has none, so a flake there shows up as a hard red. Re-run the single spec before believing it.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — **Contact-message inbox built at `/admin/contact-messages`, reversing the "no admin page" decision made hours earlier the same day.** Client received a real message, got the bell notification, went looking for it — and there was nowhere to read it. The original call (staff reply directly by email/WhatsApp, so a workflow page would be building ahead of the need) was defensible, but it rested on a claim about the code that was simply **false**.
> - 🔑 **Correcting this file: the earlier entry said "the notification's payload carries the full message + the customer's own contact details." It does not.** `toArray()` stores `name`/`email`/`phone`/`inquiry_type` and **no `message` key**, and the bell body renders a *static* i18n line ("A new message arrived through the Contact Us form"). So the message text existed **only in the staff email**, the bell named a sender without their message, and a click fell through to `/admin/dashboard`. The row was safely in `contact_messages` the whole time — it was unreachable, not lost. **Lesson: a "deliberately deferred" note is only as good as its claim about what the fallback actually does; verify the fallback, don't describe it from memory.**
> - **Shape mirrors `ProductRequestController`** (list + open/handled filter + mark-handled + a `contact_messages` permission section with view/manage) because that is the established template. ⚠️ Editors get it via `DEFAULTS`, but **existing editors keep their stored permissions** and need it granted — same caveat as `product_requests` when that shipped.
> - **Expandable rows rather than a detail route:** the body is the whole point, so clicking a row opens it inline with reply-by-email and reply-on-WhatsApp buttons. Keeps one route and one query; expansion state is an **array of ids**, not a single id, so two messages can be compared side by side. `dir="auto"` on the body decides direction **per message**, so an Arabic enquiry renders RTL inside the LTR-pinned admin.
> - **The WhatsApp link is built server-side through `OtpService::normalize()`**, the app's single phone normalizer, rather than re-rolling digit-stripping in the page (verified live: `+966 55 088 3845` → `https://wa.me/966550883845`).
> - ⚠️ **"Reply by email" is a `mailto:` link, and `mailto:` is not dependable — client-reported the same day it shipped.** Choosing Gmail as Chrome's handler, then picking an account, ended with **no compose window at all**: the hand-off depends entirely on each machine's browser/OS protocol-handler registration, and web-mail users are exactly where it breaks. It cannot be fixed from our side (a Gmail-specific compose URL would just break for Outlook staff instead). _Fix:_ the sender's **email and phone are now click-to-copy** via the existing `CopyText` component (which already carries a non-secure-context `execCommand` fallback), so replying never depends on machine config — `mailto:` stays as a convenience for whoever does have a working desktop client. ⚠️ The email copy button needs its own `stopPropagation`, since it sits inside the row that toggles the message body; the phone one does not, because the expanded body row has no click handler. **Worth remembering for the Arabic client manual: document copy-then-paste as the primary reply path, not the email button.**
> - ⏭ **Deliberately no subject prefill on the `mailto:`.** `contact_messages` has no `locale` column (unlike `orders.locale`, added for exactly this reason), so we cannot know whether to write the subject in Arabic or English — and a wrong-language subject is worse than none. Add the column if a prefill is ever wanted.
> - 🔑 **The message body is still NOT in the notification payload, and that is deliberate.** Notification rows are kept forever and fan out per staff member, so duplicating a 2000-character message into every recipient's row — as an uncorrectable snapshot — is the wrong home for it. The page reads the live record; the notification just gained a `url` that deep-links to it.
> - **`ContactMessageController::INQUIRY_TYPES` went `private` → `public`** so the admin filter and the storefront validation read one list. Pinned by a test asserting the shipped prop equals the constant, so a new type can never be selectable in one place and rejected in the other.
> - ⚠️ **Two things the tests caught that reading the code did not:** the shared admin `Button`'s `onClick` takes **no event argument**, so "mark handled" could not call `stopPropagation()` itself — the handler moved onto the actions `<td>`, or clicking the button would also toggle the row; and `contact_messages.phone` is **NOT NULL** in the schema *and* required by validation, so the null-phone branch I had written was dead code and its test failed with a QueryException. Removed rather than left as an unreachable guard.
> - **Verified in a real browser, not just by tests:** logged into the panel, confirmed the sidebar entry, that rows start collapsed, that expanding reveals the Arabic body plus both reply links, and that marking handled does not toggle the row. **414 PHP tests / 1899 assertions** ✓ (+10), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, build ✓.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — 🔴 **`$request->ip()` was returning a RAILWAY EDGE IP for every visitor in production. Every `throttle:` bucket in the app was collapsed.** Went looking for the theoretical spoofing gap flagged earlier the same day, found a live functional bug instead. Fixed by `App\Http\Middleware\TrustProxiedClientIp`; Turnstile's wiring verified end to end in the same pass.
> - 🔑 **The finding: the visitor's IP is not in `X-Forwarded-For` at all on this stack, so no `trustProxies` value could ever have fixed it.** Railway's edge **replaces** whatever XFF arrives with its own chain, `<ip that connected to Railway>, <Railway edge hop>`. With Cloudflare in front that reads `<Cloudflare egress IP>, <Railway edge IP>` — the visitor appears nowhere. Meanwhile `trustProxies(at: '*')` does **not** mean "trust anything": Laravel maps it to `setTrustedProxyIpAddressesToTheCallingIp`, i.e. **REMOTE_ADDR only** (the `100.64.x.x` private hop). Symfony then filters trusted entries out of the chain, `array_reverse`es what's left and returns index 0 — the **rightmost untrusted** entry, which is a Railway edge IP. So every visitor resolved to the same handful of addresses.
> - **Impact, in the app's own terms:** each rate limiter keys on the client IP, so all visitors through one Railway edge node shared one counter. This is the **same class of failure as the 2026-08-06 shared-throttle revenue bug** (one shopper's cart activity able to 429 another's checkout), reached by a different route — and there the prefix fix assumed per-visitor keys that were never actually per-visitor. Also meant `product_requests.ip`, `contact_messages.ip` and the audit logs recorded a Railway address, so they were worthless for forensics.
> - **The fix reads `X-Real-IP`, and that is trustworthy here — both halves proven by probing production, not assumed.** (a) Sending a forged `X-Real-IP` **and** forged `X-Forwarded-For` straight at the Railway edge, bypassing Cloudflare, arrived with **both overwritten** by the true connecting address: Railway sanitises them, so they cannot be spoofed. (b) A forged **`CF-Connecting-IP`** on that same direct request **passed through untouched** and did *not* influence `X-Real-IP` — Railway only honours Cloudflare's header when the connection genuinely comes from Cloudflare. Hence the middleware reads `X-Real-IP` and **deliberately never reads `CF-Connecting-IP`**, which is forgeable by anyone who reaches the origin directly. It is also gated on REMOTE_ADDR being private/CGNAT (loopback deliberately excluded), so a request that reached the app without traversing a proxy can never nominate its own IP.
> - ⚠️ **The header must be REPLACED, not prepended to.** Putting the real IP in front of the old chain leaves the Railway hop as the rightmost untrusted entry, so Symfony keeps returning *that* — the bug survives a fix that looks correct. Verified by working through `normalizeAndFilterClientIps` in the vendor source rather than reasoning about intent.
> - **`trustProxies(at: '*')` is deliberately KEPT.** Narrowing it to a CIDR allowlist is pointless (Railway's edge IPs are neither published nor stable) and actively dangerous: it would drop `X-Forwarded-Proto`, leaving Laravel to treat real HTTPS traffic as plain http. A test pins that proto stays trusted, since the failure mode there is silent http:// URL generation.
> - 🔑 **The earlier "spoofing gap" framing was wrong and is now corrected in place.** The origin *is* directly reachable (a `--resolve` to a Railway edge IP with `Host: retab.com.sa` returns **200**, bypassing Cloudflare entirely — and note Cloudflare **403s** a request carrying a forged `CF-Connecting-IP`, so it defends its own header), but XFF spoofing was never possible because Railway strips it. **Also worth recording: there is no longer any `.up.railway.app` domain attached** — both `retab-website-production.up.railway.app` and the CNAME target `rjgqm6py.up.railway.app` return 404, so only the two custom hostnames route.
> - **Verified by replaying production's ACTUAL captured header sets** (three requests, three continents) through the real middleware stack in `tests/Feature/Security/ClientIpResolutionTest.php`, plus the regression that matters most: two visitors behind one Cloudflare edge now get **distinct** identities. 🔑 **And the tests were proven to fail without the fix** — disabling the middleware turns 4 of 7 red with `212.102.36.193` (a Railway edge IP) in place of the visitor, which is the whole point; a test that passes either way would have been worthless here. **404 PHP tests / 1833 assertions** ✓ (+7), Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ — the browser suite matters for a change to the *global* middleware stack, since it exercises every request path end to end (it stays unaffected in dev because the middleware no-ops on loopback). No JS/TS was touched, so `tsc`/eslint are untouched by this change.
> - 🔑 **The two tasks turned out to be coupled, which is the argument for doing them in this order: `TurnstileVerifier::verify()` passes `$request->ip()` to Cloudflare's `siteverify` as `remoteip`.** With the old resolution that meant reporting a **Railway edge IP** for a token Cloudflare had just issued to the visitor's real address — asking Cloudflare to validate a token against the wrong client. Enabling Turnstile *before* this fix risked verification failing (or validating against nothing) on every guest submission, which on the Contact form and the OTP gate is a hard block on real customers. Same shape elsewhere: `LoginRequest::throttleKey()` is `email|ip`, so the login limiter was effectively per-email only, with no working per-IP component.
> - **Turnstile: no code changes needed, wiring confirmed correct end to end using Cloudflare's published test keys** (`1x…AA` always passes, `2x…AA` always blocks — noted in `.env.example` for next time). Drove a real browser against a real Cloudflare response: the always-passes pair produced a token and stored the message (303 → thank-you panel); swapping in the always-blocks **secret** while keeping a passing **site** key rejected it (302 back) and rendered the form-level banner. That also re-validated the same-day error-keying fix under genuine conditions: **no field marked `aria-invalid`**, the customer's typed message preserved, and nothing written to the DB.
> - ⚠️ **`.env.example` now spells out the asymmetry that would bite in production: SECRET set alone rejects every guest submission.** The widget only renders when the SITE key is present, so no token is ever produced and the verifier fails closed. **Set both or neither.** Both are read at runtime (a shared Inertia prop), so no rebuild is needed. Coverage stays the three guest forms that cost real money or messages (WhatsApp OTP send, Coming-Soon request, Contact Us) — `register` is deliberately left on its throttle alone, since a rejected signup costs nothing per attempt.
> - ✅ **Confirmed LIVE in production, and the verification is worth reusing because it needs no DB access and only one IP.** All three services deployed at `249b65b`; then three GETs to the rate-limited `/shop/search-index`, alternating network path — Cloudflare's Amman edge, then **direct to a Railway edge in a different region** (bypassing Cloudflare), then Cloudflare again — returned `x-ratelimit-remaining: 59 → 58 → 57`. A **single shared counter across two different paths can only happen if all three resolve to the same client IP**, i.e. the real one. Before the fix the Cloudflare route and the direct route resolved to *different* Railway edge IPs, so request 2 would have started its own bucket and reported 59 again. 🔑 **`x-ratelimit-remaining` is the cheap way to ask "what does the server think my identity is?" in any environment where you cannot read the DB.**
> - **Turnstile widget theme set to `dark` on the contact form** (`theme="dark"`, not the component's `light` default and not `auto`): that section's teal is fixed regardless of the visitor's OS colour scheme, so `auto` would hand a light-mode visitor a white widget box on dark teal. The product page and the WhatsApp login keep the light default — their forms sit on light cards.
> - ⚠️ **GOTCHA — Cloudflare's Turnstile TEST keys never render the visible widget, so they cannot be used to check appearance.** With the always-passes sitekey the container mounts with only a hidden input holding the literal token `XXXX.DUMMY.TOKEN.XXXX`; **zero iframes**. Even the documented "forces an interactive challenge" key (`3x00000000000000000000FF`) renders no iframe — it just leaves the token **empty**. So the test keys prove the *wiring* (token plumbing, server siteverify, pass/fail branches) and nothing about the *visuals*; the `theme="dark"` choice above is reasoned from the section's background and typed contract, and **still wants a human glance once a real sitekey is in**. Silver lining: the empty-token case incidentally confirms the form **fails closed** when a challenge never resolves.
> - ⚠️ **GOTCHA — a Turnstile widget that renders NOTHING, with no error, is very likely the local network, not the config.** Symptom after setting the real keys: `render()` runs (the hidden `cf-turnstile-response` input is created) but **zero challenge iframes, empty token, and `window.frames.length === 0`**, persisting 30s+ in both headless *and* headed browsers. Ruled out one at a time: the sitekey **is** in the page props (`curl | grep turnstileSiteKey`), CSP raises **no `securitypolicyviolation` events**, and — the decisive one — wrapping Cloudflare's own `api.js` to intercept `render()` showed the **`error-callback` never fired at all**. That last point is the useful diagnostic: a wrong hostname or bad sitekey raises a code (**110200** / **110100**), so *silence* means the config is fine and the challenge is simply stuck. _Root cause:_ Turnstile escalated to an interactive challenge (reasonable — it was an automated browser) whose per-challenge host is a wildcard subdomain like `brunhild.challenges.cloudflare.com`, and **those subdomains publish `AAAA` records ONLY, no `A`** (confirmed identically via the router, `1.1.1.1` and `8.8.8.8`, and for an invented subdomain too), while this dev machine has **no working IPv6** (`curl -6 https://cloudflare.com` → 000). The base `challenges.cloudflare.com` does have IPv4, which is why the script and the first platform calls succeed and only the escalation stalls. **So a dev box cannot confirm the widget renders; that check needs a real browser on a normal visit.** Worth watching in production: a stalled challenge yields no token, and the verifier fails closed, so an affected visitor gets the "couldn't verify you're not a robot" banner and cannot submit — if that is ever reported, the widget mode can be moved from Managed to Non-interactive in the Cloudflare dashboard with no code change.
> - 🔴 **Still needs the client: create the Turnstile widget** at dash.cloudflare.com → Turnstile → **"Add widget manually"**, listing **both** `retab.com.sa` and `www.retab.com.sa` as hostnames, mode **Managed**, then set the pair on the **web service** (the worker never serves a form). ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11** — widget created, both keys set on `retab-website` only, confirmed reaching the client in the page props. ⚠️ **Do NOT use the dashboard's "Set up with Spin" button** — Spin is Cloudflare's agent whose whole job is to write the integration *into the codebase* (embed the widget, wire server-side siteverify, migrate reCAPTCHA). All of that already exists here and is more careful than a generic integration: guest-only gating, the `turnstile`-not-`message` error key, and ref-based re-arming after a burnt single-use token. Letting it edit the repo would duplicate or regress working code.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — **Canonical domain flipped to the apex (`retab.com.sa`), Resend is fully live, and a stale Railway CLI read got corrected before it caused damage.** Three infrastructure changes, all verified against the real production container rather than assumed from config.
> - **Domain:** `retab.com.sa` (apex) is now canonical, `www.retab.com.sa` redirects to it — the reverse of every earlier mention in this doc, which had `www` as the live URL. Mechanism: Cloudflare **CNAME-flattens the apex** to the Railway target (apex records can't normally be a CNAME, Cloudflare synthesises the flattening), and a Cloudflare **Redirect Rule** ("redirect from WWW to root", 301) handles `www→apex`. ⚠️ **Redirect Rules only fire on Proxied (orange-cloud) traffic** — the rule silently no-ops on a DNS-only record, which is why the editor's own "may not apply to your traffic" warning showed up once before `www`'s record was confirmed Proxied; it cleared once verified. `APP_URL` set to `https://retab.com.sa` on **both** `retab-website` and `queue-worker`, confirmed live post-deploy via `php artisan integrations:check` over `railway ssh` (see below) rather than trusted from the dashboard.
> - ⚠️ **Gotcha: the Railway CLI's project link silently drops mid-session, and it fails quiet, not loud.** `railway variables --service queue-worker` returned `APP_ENV=local` / `APP_URL=http://localhost` — the raw Laravel scaffold defaults — which read exactly like a service that was never configured. It wasn't: `railway status` right after returned `No linked project found`, i.e. the CLI had lost its link and was returning defaults instead of erroring loudly. Re-linked with `railway link -p "Retab Stores Main Website" -e production -s queue-worker` (get the exact project name from `railway list` first — non-interactive linking needs an exact match). Re-queried after relinking: `queue-worker`'s real vars **already matched `retab-website` correctly** on every field that matters (`DB_*`, `MEDIA_DISK=r2`, `APP_URL`, `MAIL_MAILER`, `RESEND_KEY`) — nothing was actually misconfigured, only the earlier read of it. **Lesson: a `railway variables` result that looks like scaffold defaults is a link failure, not a real answer — check `railway status` before trusting it.**
> - **Resend:** `retab.com.sa` now shows fully **verified** (DKIM + the SPF/MX pair), using DNS records that were already sitting in Cloudflare from earlier work — triggered Resend's on-demand verify via its API directly rather than waiting on the async background check. `MAIL_MAILER` flipped to `resend` on both services; `MAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` is now the real `info@retab.com.sa`, not the `onboarding@resend.dev` placeholder this doc previously said was the only option pre-verification.
> - 🔑 **Correcting two stale claims already in this doc (2026-07-30 entries): the domain needing verification was never `retabstore.com`, and the key is not uniformly "restricted."** The real domain of record is `retab.com.sa`. And there are **two different `RESEND_KEY` values in play**: the one committed to the local dev `.env` (`re_RZm…`) genuinely **is** restricted send-only — confirmed directly, `GET /domains` on it returns `401 restricted_api_key` — while production's actual key on Railway (`re_YrKW…`, confirmed via `railway variables`) is a **different, fuller-access key** that returns a real `200` with verified domain data. **Don't test Resend behavior against the local `.env` key and assume it describes production** — they're not the same credential.
> - **Verified for real, not just "looks deployed":** submitted the live Contact form on production via Playwright (`https://retab.com.sa/pages/contact`) → `303` → thanks panel rendered, zero console/page errors. `railway logs --service queue-worker` shows `App\Notifications\ContactMessageReceivedNotification … DONE` in 274ms for that exact submission (a Resend rejection fails the queued job rather than completing it, so a clean `DONE` is real signal, not just "no crash"). Independently, `php artisan integrations:check` run live over `railway ssh --service retab-website` reports `Resend — live check OK — key valid — domains: retab.com.sa [verified] (HTTP 200)`. Three independent signals (browser, queue log, live API probe) agree.
> - ⚠️ **Gotcha: `railway ssh -- php artisan tinker --execute="..."` reliably mangles anything with parentheses or semicolons** (`echo 1+1;` alone came back `Unexpected end of input`) — whatever relays the command to the container appears to rejoin/reparse the argv rather than preserving local quoting. Plain sub-command invocations with no special characters (`php artisan integrations:check`) work fine and return promptly; piping a script into `php` via stdin over the same SSH connection just hung past the timeout instead of erroring. For anything beyond a bare artisan command, this needs a real one-off artisan command file, not an inline `--execute`.
> - ✅ **`trustProxies` — investigated immediately after and it was worse than described here: a live functional bug, not a spoofing risk. See the entry above for the real diagnosis and the fix.** For the record, the framing in this paragraph was wrong on two counts: XFF spoofing was never possible (Railway strips the header), and the `.up.railway.app` fallback domain is not even attached any more. Still genuinely pending from this session: a `rel="canonical"` tag (confirmed absent app-wide) is worth adding while the canonical decision is fresh, and a few harmless test rows sit in production `contact_messages` from these verification passes, safe to leave or delete.
> - ✅ **Domain setup verified end to end:** the apex serves **200 with zero redirects**, and `www` returns a **single-hop 301** to it with `Server: cloudflare` — i.e. the rule fires at the **edge** and the request never reaches Railway. No redirect loop, which also confirms Cloudflare's SSL mode is compatible with `URL::forceScheme('https')` (a "Flexible" mode here would loop forever). 🔑 **Reversing an earlier suggestion in this file: KEEP `www.retab.com.sa` attached as a Railway custom domain.** It looked redundant once the edge rule existed, but it is the thing that keeps a valid origin certificate for that hostname — if the Redirect Rule is ever disabled or bypassed, Cloudflare would try to reach the origin for `www` and fail the TLS handshake. Duplicate-content risk from keeping it is already covered by `URL::forceRootUrl()`, which pins every generated link to the apex.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — **Contact form revised on client feedback: shrunk to fit the screen, and validation/error states designed for real (client-reported, same day as the build above).** Two asks, one real bug caught along the way.
> - **Section height 1560px → 738px at 1440px width (53% shorter).** The first pass built the design's own numbers 1:1 (93px fields, 32px type) on the stated principle that a functional form shouldn't be shrunk the way decorative About sections were — the client's follow-up overrides that judgement call, so it's gone. Not a fraction of the original numbers (unlike the About sections' 0.7–0.85× compression): field type dropped to a conventional ~16px UI scale and padding/gaps were rebuilt from scratch around it, landing fields at **~48px tall — still above the ~44px tap-target floor** the original decision was protecting, just with far less air around it. Textarea rows 5 → 3.
> - 🔴 **Real bug found while building the error states: `required` on every input made the browser's native validation swallow the submit before Inertia ever saw it.** Clicking Send Message on an empty form produced a browser tooltip, not a single one of the field errors just built — **zero of six** `errors.*` ever reached the page. Every other form on this site (RequestSection, return-request, checkout) already skips `required` for exactly this reason; this form just hadn't been checked against an actual empty submit yet. Removed from all six controls — `noValidate` was already on the `<form>`, it just had nothing to prevent.
> - **Error design, since the source SVG has no error state to trace** (a static mock has nothing invalid to show): invalid field → red border + red label + `AlertCircle` + message underneath, all driven by the SAME `errors.<field>` Inertia already supplies — no new state, just a fuller render of data that existed from the first build. `aria-invalid` + `aria-describedby` wire each error to its control, ids via `useId()` (not a random number — this renders through the SSR sidecar, and a `Math.random()` id would mismatch between server markup and client hydration, per the SSR gotchas already logged for this stack).
> - 🔑 **Second bug caught in the same pass, unrelated to either ask: the Turnstile-failure error was keyed `errors.message`, copied from `ProductRequestController`'s pattern where that key is safe because phone is the form's ONLY field.** Here `message` is a real, distinct textarea — a bot-check failure would have attached itself to the message field's error slot, making a perfectly valid message look invalid. Re-keyed to `errors.turnstile`, a name with no matching form field, and given its own form-level banner (same red/icon/text language, centred, above the button) rather than borrowing a field's slot. Pinned by a new test that configures a fake Turnstile secret, fails the mocked `siteverify` call, and asserts the error lands on `turnstile` and NOT `message`.
> - ⚠️ **Verifying "the border turned red" needed a different check than expected.** Comparing the computed `border-color` against an `rgb(...)` string always failed — Tailwind v4 resolves colours to `oklch()`/`oklab()`, not `rgb()`, so a format-specific string match is fragile in either direction. Fixed by capturing the border colour BEFORE submitting and asserting it changed, rather than pattern-matching a colour space that can legitimately change between Tailwind versions.
> - **Verified:** field heights confirmed ≥44px at the smallest tested breakpoint, error state driven by a real empty-submit round trip (not simulated) with `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby`/icon/message all asserted on the live DOM, a 5-width × 2-locale overflow sweep, and the turnstile-key fix covered by a dedicated PHPUnit test. **397 PHP tests / 1824 assertions** ✓ (+1 test), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, Pint ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — **Contact Us page → "أرسل رسالتك" (contact form) built, end to end.** New `contact-submit.tsx`, mounted below `ContactInfo` on `/pages/contact`. Unlike every prior "next section" build, the hand-off was SVG-only (no PNG composite) and the section is a real form, not a decorative card grid — both changed the approach.
> - 🔑 **Every field/placeholder pair is the SAME text layer, twice, at the SAME measured size.** The SVG draws a label above each field and identical placeholder text inside it — six pairs, one canvas-measured size (≈32px, weight 700; kashida on every string means only ink HEIGHT is trustworthy, same as contact-info). That collapses to one i18n key per field serving both the `<label>` and the `<input placeholder>`.
> - **Two deliberate departures from the literal file, both logged inline and both worth the pattern:**
>   1. **The submit button is flat `#D9D9D9` with no label in the source** — reads as an un-skinned Figma default (every *other* interactive surface on the site picks a real brand colour) rather than a considered choice. Built as a white pill + brand-teal text with real copy, matching the site's established light-on-dark button convention instead of shipping a literal grey blank.
>   2. **The Logo 2 watermark replaces what the file actually embeds**: a large rotated-150°, 8%-opacity instance of the FULL logo lockup (mark + wordmark), flattened to a raster and used as an SVG pattern fill — the same mechanism as why-us's background plate, confirmed by decoding the embedded base64 and finding a legible "Retab / رطاب" wordmark inside it. Per the explicit request, this uses the existing pre-faded Logo 2 **knot** (already shared by journey/why-us/contact-info) instead, for one consistent watermark across the page. ⚠️ Unlike contact-info's, this placement was **not diff-verified against a bare plate** — none was supplied for this section — so it is a judgement call, flagged as such in the component.
> - ⚠️ **NOT height-compressed like the decorative About sections.** Photo/card grids have no functional floor to protect; input fields do (WCAG's ~44px tap-target guidance). Shrinking a form to hit a shorter section is the wrong trade when the section's job is to be *used*, not looked at — so every size here is a clamp() built straight from the design's own pixels with no extra 0.7–0.85× factor, and still shrinks fine on phones via the vw term alone.
> - 🔑 **RTL row order fell out for free.** Row 1's two name fields are mapped in plain reading order (first name, then last) with no physical left/right hardcoding — under `dir=rtl` the grid's own first-child-goes-right behaviour puts `first_name` on the visual right, exactly matching the design, and the same markup mirrors correctly under LTR. Verified by measuring rendered `x` positions, not by eye.
> - **Scope call: this shipped as a genuinely working form, not a static shell.** A blank "next section" request that happens to include a submit button is a live P1 on a real page if the button does nothing, so the full path was built: `contact_messages` migration + model, `ContactMessageController@store` (Turnstile-gated for guests only, mirroring the Coming-Soon "I want this" convention — signed-in visitors skip it), `throttle:5,1,contact-submit` (own bucket, per the 2026-08-06 shared-throttle revenue-bug lesson), staff notified via `ContactMessageReceivedNotification` (bell + mail, `viaConnections(['database' => 'sync'])`, mail conditional on the recipient having an email — the established `NewOrderNotification` shape), and the new `contact_message_received` type registered in the admin bell + history filter (`notification-content.tsx`, both i18n files).
>   - **Deliberately NOT built: a dedicated admin inbox page.** `ProductRequestController`'s admin list+permission-section pattern was the obvious template, but replicating it here would have been building ahead of the need. The notification's payload carries the full message + the customer's own contact details (so staff can reply directly by email/WhatsApp without a workflow), and it ships **no `url`** in its data — `Admin\NotificationController::open()` already falls back to `/admin/dashboard` on a missing/invalid url, so this is a real, tested fallback rather than a dangling link. Add the page later if volume ever asks for one.
>   - **Inquiry-type options are invented**, not sourced from the design (native `<select>`s carry no design-time option list): order / product / complaint / partnership / other. Keys live in both `ContactMessageController::INQUIRY_TYPES` and `contact.form.inquiryTypes`, kept in step by convention (like return reasons) rather than generated from one source, since there's no server-driven copy elsewhere on this page.
> - **Verified:** structure/geometry/colour assertions in a real browser (field count, RTL column order, button width matching a row-1 half-field within 5% — design has 446 vs 445, not a coincidence — watermark natural size, section background exactly `#1b4e53`), a full functional run (fill every field, submit, land on the thank-you panel), and a 6-width × 2-locale overflow sweep, all green. **396 PHP tests / 1820 assertions** ✓ (+9), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, Pint ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (including the admin bell suite, since the new notification type touches shared bell code).

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-11 — **Contact Us page started: "معلومات الإتصال" (contact info) built.** New `AboutController` twin `ContactController` → `resources/js/pages/shop/contact.tsx` + `components/store/contact-info.tsx`, registered at **`/pages/contact`** (the URL the nav, footer and sitemap already use) declared *before* the `/pages/{slug}` CMS catch-all, same trick as `/pages/about` and `/pages/branches`. ⚠️ That shadows the `contact` content_pages row, which held only placeholder copy, so nothing real was displaced. Built strictly to the **Architecture → "Building a designed section from a Figma composite"** recipe, which gained four new techniques and four new traps from this section.
> - 🔑 **This hand-off included the SVG, so the geometry is EXACT rather than measured, and the pixels then confirmed it:** card **591×295 rx 73** (aspect 2.003:1), columns at x 119..710 / 747.5..1338.5 (gap 37.5), rows at y 326..621 / 674..969 (gap 53). Decoding needed care — every card is authored 295×591 and `rotate(90)`-ed about its own corner. ⚠️ The side margins are **119 left / 101.5 right**, i.e. the block sits **8.75px right of centre**; that is drift, so it is centred here on the mean margin (container 84.69%, gap 3.08% of it).
> - 🔑 **The type spec is the most solidly evidenced yet: once size and weight were fixed, all SEVEN measured strings matched the design's ink height to within 1px.** Heading **121px/900/teal**, titles **53px/700/white**, bodies **53px/400/gold**, body line pitch **1.30** (solved twice from two-line ink blocks, agreeing at 69.0 and 68.0px). Titles and bodies are again the *same size* with weight and colour carrying the hierarchy, as in why-us. Both card colours turned out to be the existing tokens exactly (`#1b4e53`, `#af9056`), so nothing new was added.
> - **New technique worth reusing: stroke thickness pins the WEIGHT, which ink height cannot** (height varies only ~5% across 400→900). Median horizontal ink-run gave design stems of **18 / 6 / 3px** against canvas renders at w900=17, w700=6, w400=4. 🔴 **But the thresholds on both sides must match:** a strict "near the ink colour" test on the composite against canvas's 50% cut under-measured every stem by ~1px and pointed at Medium instead of Bold. Nearest-colour classification is the 50% criterion and is directly comparable.
> - **Second new technique: with an overlay asset but no plate, PREDICT the background and diff that.** Compositing the watermark over white at candidate placements scored **0.02/255** at natural size at (0,0) versus 1.28 stretched, 3.45 mirrored, 3.08 absent — so the designer had exported the already-rotated layer at frame scale, and it needs **no CSS opacity** (the file is pre-faded to 7.9%, and the composite's `#F9F7F2` is that colour over white on all three channels exactly). ⚠️ Then, comparing the render back, **tint and coverage are independent**: tint matched at any size while coverage was 16.5% against 20.9%, because a compressed section scales a height-anchored image down. Swept the height (16.5/18.8/20.0/21.7/25.0% at 100→140%) and settled on **125% → 19.5%**, deliberately a little under rather than overshooting.
> - 🔴 **The composite's email reads `info@retabstore.com` — the OLD Zid domain, already wrong.** So the phone and email render from the **admin-editable footer settings** (the same shared prop the site footer reads), not from i18n; only fixed display copy lives in the translation files. Transcribing a composite verbatim ships whatever was stale in it, and this is now step 6 of the recipe.
> - ⚠️ **Bug I introduced and caught by measuring: `ltr:` resolves against the element's OWN `dir`.** `dir="ltr"` on the phone/email paragraph (there to stop RTL reordering a leading `+` or an `@`) *also* switched on its `ltr:` size, so two of four cards silently rendered the smaller English size while still in Arabic. The isolation moved down onto the value's `<span>`; pinned by asserting all four cards share one size per locale.
> - ⚠️ **English wrapped and that resizes the whole section, not just one card:** with `auto-rows-fr` a single 545px line in a 520px box grew every card and took the section from **797px to 910px**. Fixed by shortening the EN copy (`Across the Kingdom`, not a literal translation) *and* giving English its own slightly smaller body size, so there is headroom rather than a 25px margin.
> - **Height compressed to 797px** (911 with the navbar) from the 1126px frame, whitespace roughly halved and content scaled 0.82, every `clamp()` capping at 1440px. Consequence accepted deliberately: cards end up **2.33:1** against the design's 2.003:1, because matching the aspect would mean narrowing them and opening a large central gutter (the same trade as why-us). `auto-rows-fr` is **not** gated to `sm:` so the four cards stay equal on mobile too.
> - **Verified:** geometry / type / weights / colours / heading alignment / watermark all measured against the composite, section height and per-card overflow and line-wrap checked at **1920/1440/1280/1024/768/390 in both locales**, screenshotted at 1440 AR, 1440 EN and 390 AR. **387 PHP tests / 1791 assertions** ✓, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, Pint ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓.
> - ⏭ Open: the design's heading sits 43px further right than the card block, on a right margin matching nothing else in the frame, so it is aligned to the card container instead. Opening hours are i18n copy, not a setting, so the client cannot edit them without a code change; worth a `settings` key if they ask. And the remaining Contact sections (form, map) are still to come.

> - **New bottom bar** (thin top rule, `text-xs`, `text-brand-teal/70`): copyright · privacy policy · cookie settings (with a lucide `Cookie` icon) · the handle. The handle previously sat under the social icons as **bold gold text**, which read as a heading for the block beneath it rather than as a byline; at bar scale it is unambiguously an aside. Layout is `justify-between` rather than Cloudflare's left-clustered row, because this footer is a centred three-column design and a spread bar suits it better; it stacks centred below `sm`.
> - **The cookie control MOVED out of the quick links, it was not duplicated.** It is not a page, and a footer offering the same button twice makes the reader hunt for the difference. `/pages/privacy-policy` was added while here: the page exists and is seeded, but was previously reachable only from inside the consent banner itself.
> - ⚠️ **Copy gotcha caught by reading the rendered text rather than the template:** `'© {{year}} {{company}}. All rights reserved.'` renders as **`Retab Alwatan Dates Factory Co.. All rights reserved.`**, because the EN company name already ends in "Co." Both locales now use a **middot** separator. Arabic was unaffected, which is exactly why checking only the AR default would have shipped it.
> - ⚠️ **The year is `new Date().getFullYear()` on the client, deliberately.** The SSR sidecar renders in the container's timezone and the visitor's browser in theirs, so for a few hours around New Year they disagree by one and React patches the text on hydration. Cosmetic, and the alternative (shipping the server's year as a prop) would show a KSA visitor the *wrong* year for those same hours.
> - **Verified in a real browser** at 1440 and 390 in both locales: logo is `<a href="/">` and actually navigates home, the cookie button re-opens the consent banner, the handle and the button are each present exactly once, no horizontal overflow, and the bar screenshotted in all four combinations. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓. No PHP touched.
