> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — 🔴 **Third and final instance of the navbar blank-strip bug: a fast scrollbar drag exposed up to 94px of page background.** Client-reported. One-line fix in `navbar.tsx`, but the lesson is bigger than the line.
> - 🔑 **The rule was already right; it was being applied to the wrong quantity.** The safety guard compares the header's translate against the scroll position, but it read `offset` — the last **target** — instead of where the element actually **is**. Those two are identical only while nothing is in flight. During the reveal's 750ms `transition-delay` they disagree by the *entire* travel band, because the element has not moved at all yet.
> - **Why a scrollbar drag specifically, when a wheel is fine.** The drag's first event lands while still far down the page, which earns the reveal, sets the target to 0 and starts the delay. Every later event in the same drag then sees `offset === 0`, so the guard's `offset > y` term is false **forever**, and the header sits parked off-screen while the scroll position falls inside the band. A wheel cannot reach the top inside 750ms; a drag (or a scrollbar-track click) can. **Measured before the fix: 54px of `DIV.flex.min-h-screen` at the viewport top at y=60, 94px at y=20**, for the remainder of the delay.
> - **Fix:** read the rendered translate (`-header.getBoundingClientRect().top`) instead of the cached target. ⚠️ That is a layout flush, so it is read **only when `y < band`** — past the band the value is capped at `band <= y`, so no amount of transition lag can violate the invariant and the cached target is a fine stand-in. Confines the read to the first ~114px of the page.
> - 🔑 **Against the rendered value the guard becomes provably complete rather than merely careful:** `next` is always `0` or `min(y, band)`, hence always `<= y`. So a violation (`rendered > y`) implies `next <= y < rendered`, which *is* the guard's condition. **Every violation is therefore corrected in the frame it appears** — there is no longer a class of scroll input that can slip past, which is what the two previous fixes could not claim.
> - ⚠️ **Worth internalising for any lag-based animation:** a "current position" variable that is really a *target* is a silent lie whenever a transition has a delay. Duration alone hides it (the element at least starts moving); a delay means zero movement while the variable claims arrival. **If a correctness invariant depends on where something is, measure it, do not infer it.**
> - 🔑 **Third occurrence ⇒ it is now PINNED, in `e2e/navbar-scroll.spec.ts`.** The previous two fixes were verified by hand and by definition did not stay fixed. This is browser-only — invisible to `tsc` and to PHPUnit — so the E2E suite is the only gate that can hold it. **Validated in both directions rather than just written:** green on the fix, and re-broken deliberately (`rendered = offset`) to confirm it fails with `Received: 114, Expected: <= 60`, the exact strip. ⚠️ Note the **single-jump** case (scrollbar-track click, `y=3000 → 10`) **passes even with the bug**, because one event evaluates the guard correctly — only a multi-event drag defeated it. Kept as a guard anyway, but do not mistake it for the detector.
>   - The assertion is the invariant `translate <= scrollY`, not a timing threshold, so it is CI-stable: any violation is corrected within a frame, so the only way it reddens is a real regression. ⚠️ The 120ms settle is bounded on **both** sides — long enough for the handler's rAF, short enough to still be inside the 750ms reveal delay, which is precisely where this bug lived. A third test pins that the header still hides at all, since the invariant alone would also pass on a header that never moves.
> - **Verified by sampling, not by feel:** **152 frames across 1440×900 and 390×844, worst strip 0px**, and `elementFromPoint` at the viewport top never returned the page wrapper in any frame. Both previously client-accepted behaviours confirmed intact — the hide from the top still **eases** (T trails y: 0/4/14/25/38/52/66 against y=20…140) rather than snapping 1:1, and the reveal is still gated (120px up ⇒ still hidden; 240px ⇒ hidden at t+120ms, fully in by t+1150ms). `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **31/31 E2E** ✓ (+3; every storefront spec touches this header). No PHP touched, so the PHP suite is unaffected and was not re-run.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — 🎉 **`www.retab.com.sa` is LIVE** (nameservers moved to Cloudflare, Railway issued the cert). First bug found on the live site: the homepage showed **four category tiles instead of five**. Fixed by data migration `2026_08_10_100000_backfill_category_tile_images`.
> - 🔑 **It looked like a missing image but it was a missing DB value, and the reason it looked that way is the interesting part.** `الشوابير` (slug `rusks`) had `categories.image = NULL` in production. `ShopController::index` builds `featuredCategories` with **`whereNotNull('image')`**, so the row is **dropped from the query entirely** — it never reaches `categories-section.tsx`, which would otherwise have rendered its 🌴 placeholder. A null image therefore presents as a *missing tile*, not a broken picture. **A filter in the query and a fallback in the component are not the same contract**; here they disagreed, and the query won.
> - **Diagnosed against the live site rather than guessed**, by pulling the Inertia `data-page` payload off production: the homepage returned ids **3, 4, 6, 7** (id 5 absent), while **`/shop` returned all six chips including id 5 `rusks`** — proving the category exists, is active, and has products, so only the column was empty. `rusks.webp` was also already serving **200 / 28,808 bytes** on prod, so the asset was never the problem.
> - ⚠️ **Categories have NO admin UI** (there is no `Admin\CategoryController`) — every value comes from `ZidCatalogImporter::CATEGORY_MAP` or `CatalogSeeder`. So the client cannot fix this class of gap themselves and it can only be corrected in code. Worth remembering when triaging anything category-shaped.
> - **The migration only fills rows where `image IS NULL`**, which makes it idempotent (Railpack runs `migrate --force` on every deploy) and means it can never overwrite a deliberate choice if category editing is added later. Values are **hardcoded rather than read from `CATEGORY_MAP`**: a migration is a frozen record, and reading the constant would let a later edit to the importer silently change what an already-run migration meant. `assorted` is deliberately left null — the importer maps it to null so it stays off the homepage.
> - **Verified:** 6 new tests in `tests/Feature/CategoryTileImageTest.php` pinning fill / never-overwrite / assorted-stays-null / *dropped-not-placeheld* / appears-after-backfill, plus an assertion that **every mapped tile file actually exists in `public/`** (a backfilled path that does not resolve would just swap one broken tile for another). Applied to the dev DB as a confirmed no-op. **387 tests / 1791 assertions** ✓ (+6), Pint ✓.
> - **To fix prod:** it applies automatically on the next deploy. To do it immediately from the Railway console instead: `php artisan migrate --force` (or the one-row equivalent, `DB::table('categories')->where('slug','rusks')->whereNull('image')->update(['image'=>'/images/categories/rusks.webp'])`).
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **Cursor-following spotlight on the why-us cards** (`.spotlight-card` in `app.css` + `lib/spotlight.ts`). A warm glow tracks the pointer inside the hovered card and the border brightens with it. Taken from a 21st.dev bento component, of which only the `FlowCard` hover behaviour was wanted.
> - **Only the effect was ported, and that component in particular should not be pasted.** Its injected CSS is **malformed**: the `@media` blocks are spliced into the middle of the `.bento3-icon::before, ::after` declaration block, so under CSS nesting they resolve as descendants of those pseudo-elements and **every responsive rule inside them is dead**. It also injects a `<style>` tag into `document.head` at runtime and ships a second theme system (its own `dark` class + `localStorage` key + `MutationObserver`) that would fight the project's.
> - ⚠️ **`getBoundingClientRect()` is read inside the rAF callback, never in the handler.** `mousemove` fires far more often than the screen refreshes, and measuring per event forces a synchronous layout each time. The original does exactly that; ours coalesces to one measurement and one style write per frame.
> - **A plain module, not a hook, and deliberately so.** One delegated `onMouseMove` on the container resolves the card with `closest('[data-spotlight]')`. A hook would have to be called per card, which would force every card in a `.map()` to become its own component purely to host it.
> - **Drawn on `::after`**, so it costs no extra DOM per card; `border-radius: inherit` keeps it inside the rounded corners.
> - ⚠️ **Gated behind `@media (hover: hover)`.** On touch, `:hover` can stick after a tap, which would leave a glow burnt into whichever card was last touched. Also dropped under `prefers-reduced-motion` — not motion in the vestibular sense, but a light chasing the cursor is the kind of restlessness that setting asks to be spared.
> - **Verified by driving the real pointer** to three known points inside a card: `--spot-x/--spot-y` tracked to within **0-1px** each time, `::after` opacity ramped 0.41 → 0.90 → 1.0 through its 400ms fade, the border went from alpha 0.25 → 0.45, leaving the group cleared the variables, and both gates held (`display: none` under reduced motion, opacity stuck at 0 under `hover: none`). `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **Rotating gradient border on the values pills** (`.gradient-border` in `app.css`). The gold outline is a conic gradient that turns, so two highlights sweep round it like light on metal; the four pills are phase-offset so the group shimmers as a wave. Client picked this over a first attempt at a single travelling bead, prompted by a 21st.dev component.
> - 🔑 **`@property --gradient-angle` is MANDATORY, not boilerplate, and its absence is the usual reason this effect ships broken.** An unregistered custom property has no type, so it **cannot be interpolated** — the angle jumps discretely instead of sweeping. The widely-copied version of this snippet omits it (and also omits the animation classes its own component references, so it renders a static border). Verified rather than assumed: sampling the computed value gives a clean ramp, 14 distinct angles across 14 samples.
> - ⚠️ **Ascending colour stops.** The circulating snippet has `secondary 37%, accent 30%, secondary 33%` — CSS clamps a stop up to the preceding one, so those three collapse into a hard edge at 37% rather than the intended soft highlight. Ours ascend.
> - **The technique:** two stacked background layers — the element's flat fill clipped to the **padding** box, the conic gradient clipped to the **border** box — with `border: Npx solid transparent` letting the gradient show through. `--gb-fill` must therefore be set to the element's own fill (`bg-white` stays underneath as a fallback).
> - 🔑 **This shape tolerates a conic sweep only because the whole border is coloured.** A conic gradient sweeps by **angle from the centre**, so on a 3.1:1 pill the caps sit ~160px out against ~51px for the long edges — the highlights cover roughly **3× more distance per degree at the ends**. As a *sheen* that reads as metal; as a **single travelling dot it reads as broken**, because the eye tracks one point and sees it accelerate. A dot on this shape needs an arc-length animation (`stroke-dashoffset` with `pathLength="100"`) instead. Worth knowing before reusing either.
> - 🔴 **The abandoned bead attempt taught one thing worth keeping: light colour is surface-dependent, and I got it wrong twice in the same way.** A pale cream arc is right over the dark why-us plate, but on a **white** pill it was nearly invisible and read as the **gold border breaking up** rather than lighting up — on a light surface nothing can be brighter than the background, so a highlight has to be more **saturated**, not lighter. Switching the core to teal fixed it, and then the identical mistake resurfaced in the halo (translucent gold on white is paler than the border, so the bead looked like a dark dot with a bright smear beside it). **The gradient border avoids the whole problem** because it contains a dark stop (`--gb-deep`) as well as a pale one, so it stays legible on white throughout.
> - **Phase offset via a NEGATIVE `animation-delay`** (`calc(var(--gb-index) * -1.25s)`), so each pill starts immediately but a quarter-sweep apart rather than waiting its turn. Measured 90° apart: 304.8° / 34.8° / 124.8° / 214.8°. ⚠️ Note this is a *continuous shimmer on all four*, not the card-to-card relay originally described — a rotating gradient has no obvious start point for a hand-off to read against.
> - **Reduced motion stops the rotation but keeps the gradient**, which is a perfectly good resting state — unlike a travelling dot, which has to be hidden outright because a light frozen mid-border looks like a fault.
> - **Verified:** `--gradient-angle` interpolating (see above), 5s duration, transparent 4px border with `background-clip: padding-box, border-box`, per-pill phases 90° apart, and under `prefers-reduced-motion` `animation-name: none` with the conic gradient still present. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓ (no spec visits `/pages/about`, and the visual sweep captures rather than pixel-compares, so a looping animation cannot destabilise it).
> - ⏭ The bead implementation was **deleted rather than left unreferenced**. If the relay behaviour is wanted later, it is an SVG pill path + `pathLength="100"` + `stroke-dasharray: 7 93` + `animation-fill-mode: backwards` (that last one is required: without it every element shows a stationary bead through its delay).
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **About Us → "ليش رطاب" (why us) built, and the section-building method written up as a reusable recipe.** New `components/store/about-why-us.tsx`: four glass cards on the designer's dark teal plate, each a white title over two gold lines. The method is now **Architecture → "Building a designed section from a Figma composite"** — read that before starting the next one; it exists because the client asked for it to be captured and because every miss on these four sections traced to a guessed value.
> - 🔑 **The designer supplied the bare background plate as well as the composite, and diffing the two is the highest-leverage trick in this whole workflow.** In the gutter between the cards they agree to **0.17/255**, which *proves* the plate is the real background rather than a lookalike; the same diff then showed the knot watermark is **not** baked into it, and measured the cards' glass fill directly at the same pixel with the background gradient cancelled. That fill turned out to be almost nothing — a median implied white alpha of **0.023**, rising from ~0 at each card's top edge to ~4% at its bottom, hence a gradient rather than a flat tint.
> - **No SVG this time, so all geometry came from pixels.** Card edges were found as **border luminance spikes** on scan lines chosen to sit inside a card but clear of its type — generic step detection kept locking onto the watermark and the glyphs instead. Result, symmetric to the pixel: `margin 69 + card 569 + gap 165 + card 569 + margin 70 = 1442 (≈1440)`, centres at ±366.5. Unlike the values section these numbers **are** usable as a grid, because each card holds its own text instead of sitting beside it.
> - **Type size resolved a genuine ambiguity.** Only **two** lines in the design carry no kashida, and those are the only ones whose width-derived and height-derived sizes agree — they do, within 0.5px, across every weight. Both land at ~40px, which proves the **card title and body are the same size**, with weight and colour carrying the hierarchy. Everywhere else the width figure is corrupted by the designer's elongation, so height is the only trustworthy dimension.
> - **Height compressed to ~782px** (896 with the navbar) from the 1136px frame, pre-empting the complaint raised on the values section. ⚠️ Different arithmetic was needed here: values was **47% whitespace** so halving gaps did the work, whereas here the cards dominate, so the cards had to scale too. Consequence accepted deliberately: the cards end up **2.28:1** against the design's 1.635:1. Scaling their width to match would have left a 315px gutter between two narrow cards, which is worse.
> - ⚠️ **Two bugs I introduced and caught by looking at the render.** (1) **`-z-20` on the background image rendered the whole section bare cream** — `position: relative` does not create a stacking context, so a negatively stacked child paints behind the nearest *ancestor* background, not merely behind its siblings. Plain `absolute` first in DOM order plus a `relative` content wrapper is enough. This also made every white title invisible, so the section now carries a **fallback `bg-[#013E46]`** sampled from the plate — white-and-gold type over a failed image is an invisible section. (2) The watermark was **too strong and too large**: the asset is pre-faded to exactly **7.9%** (every shape pixel at alpha 117/127) but the composite renders it at a cream alpha of **5.7%**, so it needs `opacity-[0.72]` on top — confirmed twice over, 5.7/7.9 = 0.72 from the assets and a rendered-vs-design brightness lift of +12 vs +8.8 = 0.73. Re-measured after the fix at **+8.0** against the design's +8.8. Its width also had to drop 52% → 42%, because a fixed-aspect image sized off the *width* fills a vertically-compressed section and reads as a graphic element rather than a texture.
> - **`why-bg.webp` is 8 KB, down from a 1.27 MB PNG** — checked for banding rather than assumed: mean difference from the master is **0.724/255 with no pixel off by more than 3**, and the flat runs in the gradient are present in the PNG too. PNG is simply poor at smooth gradients.
> - **Verified:** `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓, card box measured at **569×250 at x=69 / x=802** (the design's exact positions), section height and line-wrap checked at 1920/1440/1280/1024/768/390 in both locales, screenshotted at 1440 AR, 1440 EN, 390 AR. No PHP touched.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **About Us → "قيمنا" (values) built.** New `components/store/about-values.tsx` mounted on `/pages/about`: four gold-ringed pills (الجودة / الأصالة / الابتكار / رضا العميل) each over a one-line explanation. Copy lives in `about.values.items` as an **array of objects** in both locales, so a fifth value is a copy change, not a code change (same pattern as `journey.steps`).
> - 🔑 **The composite's horizontal numbers are exact but CANNOT be used as a grid, and the trap generalises.** Measured off the 1440×1136 frame: `margin 181 + pill 376 + gap 326 + pill 376 + margin 181 = 1440`, dead on. But a 376px column is **narrower than the descriptions it must hold** (the widest is 537px of ink), so text at the design's size would wrap. What actually has to be preserved is the pill **centres**, at ±351px from the middle. For a 2-column grid that offset is `(column + gap) / 2`, so `column + gap` is pinned at 702 while the pair trades off freely — **620 + 82** inside a 1322px container puts the centres exactly right and leaves room for the copy. **Reproduce the constraint, not the numbers.**
> - 🔴 **Client-caught: the section was too tall to fit one screen.** The source frame is 1136px, which cannot fit a laptop viewport once the 114px navbar is counted. Fixed by rendering at **0.85 of the design's type and pill size with the whitespace roughly halved** → **728px** (842 with the navbar). The split is deliberate rather than uniform: **nearly half the frame's height, 531 of 1136, is padding and gaps**, so cutting whitespace buys the most height for the least fidelity, while scaling type *and* pill by the same factor keeps the pill's **3.13:1** proportion — squeezing height alone would have flattened it into a wide tag. Every `clamp()` caps at exactly 1440px, so the height **stops growing on larger monitors** instead of scaling itself back out of the viewport (verified identical at 1920).
> - ⚠️ **`whitespace-nowrap` on the label is load-bearing.** The pill is a fixed width, so a wrapping label does not reflow inside it — it grows the pill's height and **spills the second line past the rounded ends**. "Customer Care" did exactly that. English also needs its own smaller size (`ltr:text-[…]`) because Latin words are far longer than these Arabic labels yet must fit the same pill, and the EN descriptions were **rewritten shorter** ("Nothing but the best", not a literal translation) so every row stays one line. Checked by measuring `scrollWidth > clientWidth` on all four pills at six widths in both locales.
> - ⚠️ **Mobile needed its own pill width, found by looking rather than by measuring.** `w-full` in a single column produced a **358×34 bar** — 10:1, no longer reading as a pill at all. `w-[42%] sm:w-[51.6%]` plus raised clamp floors hold the proportion everywhere (3.33 at 390px, 3.11 at 768px, 3.13 at 1440px).
> - **Type sizes came from ink measurement, each confirmed by two independent dimensions agreeing** (width-derived and height-derived sizes landing within ~1px): labels **59px**, descriptions **58px** — nearly the same size, which is the design and not a mistake — heading **110px**. Kashida runs are hand-tuned to the outlined type: `قيــــــمنا` renders 373px of ink where the design has 376, at the design's exact 132px ink height.
> - ⚠️ **Two measurement traps, both of which produced confidently wrong numbers first.** (1) A plain "darker than the page" ink test reported each label's box as the **whole pill interior**, because the pills carry a faint **inset shadow** — the SVG exports `fill="black"`, a Figma placeholder, and sampling the PNG shows a white interior shading #F6F6F6 at the ring to #FEFEFE at the centre (Figma does not export effects to SVG, same as the hero card's frosted blur, so it is rebuilt in CSS). (2) Switching to a colour-keyed test still leaked, because **gold scores 0.52 against a teal target** and cleared a 0.35 threshold — the ring was being measured as text. Fix: sample well inside the ring at a 0.80 threshold.
> - ⚠️ **The kashida broke my own tooling, which is worth knowing before writing a selector or an assertion:** `'قيــــــمنا'.includes('قيم')` is **false**, because the tatweel characters sit between the letters. Selected on the heading's computed colour instead (it is the only gold `h2`; journey's is teal). The same applies to in-page search and to any test matching on this copy.
> - ⚠️ **Copy change to flag: the design writes `الاصالة` without the hamza; shipped as `الأصالة`.** Not a guess — the measured 53px ink height matches the hamza-less form (52), not the hamza form (58), so the omission was real rather than a rendering artifact. Reverting is one string.
> - **Verified:** `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓, section height and label overflow measured at 1920/1440/1280/1024/768/390 in **both** locales, and screenshotted at 1440 AR, 1440 EN, 390 AR. No PHP touched.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — 🔴 **Every production browser tab was reading "… - Laravel". Removed the scaffold's brand-suffix title template.** Surfaced by the client noticing `VITE_APP_NAME` was not set on either Railway service.
> - **What was live:** `resources/js/app.tsx` carried the starter kit's `const appName = import.meta.env.VITE_APP_NAME || 'Laravel'` + `title: (title) => \`${title} - ${appName}\``. Read straight out of the deployed bundle, it had compiled to `const Qw="Laravel"; ({title:n=>\`${n} - ${Qw}\`, …})` — **zero occurrences of "Retab Stores"** — so the About tab hydrated to **`من نحن | رطاب للتمور - Laravel`**.
> - 🔑 **The fix is NOT to add the variable, and this is the part worth internalising.** Every page's own `headTitle` **already** carries the brand in the correct language (`من نحن | رطاب للتمور`, `About Us | Retab Dates`), so setting `VITE_APP_NAME=Retab Stores` would only have changed the bug to `من نحن | رطاب للتمور - Retab Stores` — brand twice, in two languages. The suffix itself was the defect. **Deleted the callback entirely**, which also deletes the env dependency instead of papering over it.
> - **It also silently fixed an SSR/CSR disagreement nobody had noticed:** `ssr.jsx` never had a title callback, so the server emitted the clean title and the client rewrote it on hydration. Removing the suffix makes both paths agree — which matters because crawlers see the SSR title while users see the hydrated one.
> - ⚠️ **`VITE_*` is a BUILD-time variable, not a runtime one.** Vite inlines it into the bundle at `npm run build`; setting it on a running container does nothing until the next build. So "add it to the Railway service" would not have taken effect without a rebuild — and on the **queue worker it is meaningless in any case**, since a worker never builds or serves frontend assets. `.env.example` now says exactly this in place of the deleted var.
> - ⚠️ **Why local dev never showed it:** local `.env` **does** set `VITE_APP_NAME="${APP_NAME}"`, and `${APP_NAME}` is a **dotenv** interpolation, not a Railway one — so it silently resolved locally and silently vanished in the Railway build. A var that only misbehaves in the environment you cannot see is the worst kind.
> - **A red herring while diagnosing:** with SSR off, `curl` shows `<title inertia>Laravel</title>` — that is the **Blade** placeholder (`app.blade.php`, `config('app.name')`, and local `APP_NAME` is still `Laravel`), which Inertia replaces on hydration. It is not the bug and prod shows `Retab Stores` there. Read the *hydrated* `document.title` in a browser, not the curl'd HTML.
> - **Verified in a real browser** (not inferred): home `رطاب للتمور | تمور فاخرة`, about `من نحن | رطاب للتمور`, shop `المتجر | رطاب للتمور`, exactly **one** `<title>` element each, and an AR→EN toggle correctly re-titling to `Retab Dates | Premium Dates`. The rebuilt bundle contains **0** occurrences of `Laravel`. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓ — worth running because `admin-notifications.spec.ts` asserts the unread-count title badge (`/^\(\d+\)\s/`), which shares the title machinery.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — ⚠️ **GOTCHA (no code change): the `.up.railway.app` URL renders unstyled with dead React until the custom domain's certificate is issued.** _Symptom:_ opening the Railway-generated URL gives a broken-looking front end, which reads exactly like a failed `npm run build`. _It is not the build._ `URL::forceRootUrl(config('app.url'))` (added 2026-08-09) pins **every** generated URL to `APP_URL`, and that includes the `@vite` asset tags — so the page served on the Railway host asks for all 13 of its assets from `https://www.retab.com.sa/...`. That host resolves (the `www` CNAME is correct) but Railway is still serving its wildcard fallback cert, `CN=*.up.railway.app`, which does not cover it, so **every** asset request dies at the TLS handshake (`SEC_E_WRONG_PRINCIPAL`). No CSS ⇒ unstyled; no JS ⇒ React never boots.
> - **How to tell the build is fine in one command:** strip the host and request the same path off the Railway origin — `curl -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://<svc>.up.railway.app/build/assets/app-<hash>.js`. Here it answered **200, 473,747 bytes** (and the CSS 200, 125,496), while the identical URL on `www` answered **000**. If the origin serves the asset, the problem is the *host in the URL*, never the bundle.
> - ⚠️ **Do NOT diff hashes across requests during a deploy.** Half of this investigation was chasing a phantom: the HTML I first fetched referenced `app-Dcp9oUt3.js` while `/build/manifest.json` advertised `app-BYoodYoN.js`, which looks damningly like a stale manifest. A deployment had simply landed between the two requests. Re-fetching six times gave one stable hash matching the manifest. **Confirm the deploy is settled before treating a hash mismatch as real.**
> - **Ruled out, so nobody re-checks them:** DNS is correct on *both* Bluvalt nameservers (`www` CNAME → `rjgqm6py.up.railway.app`, `_railway-verify` TXT intact), there is **no CAA record** blocking Let's Encrypt, and no A record conflicting with the CNAME. So the cert stall is on Railway's side of the fence, not the zone's — it should clear on its next check or on the nameserver move.
> - 🔑 **This self-heals the moment the cert issues** — it needs no code change, and the pinning is still the behaviour we want (a self-canonicalising duplicate store is the thing `forceRootUrl` exists to prevent). **The cost to accept is that the Railway fallback URL is not a usable preview while `APP_URL` points at a host that does not serve yet.** If a working preview is ever needed before DNS lands, temporarily point the web service's `APP_URL` at the Railway hostname — but that also moves `og:image`, the Ziggy base URL, the sitemap and every webhook/email link, so it is a revert-at-launch footgun in the same category as `SITE_INDEXABLE`.
> - ⚠️ **Before removing/re-adding a Railway custom domain to force a re-check, write down the current CNAME target.** A re-add can mint a *different* target, and updating the CNAME at Bluvalt needs the engineer — turning a five-second nudge into another multi-day wait.
> - **Verified live on the current deployment:** the merged About hero, the journey section (with the `text-start` heading fix) and the trimmed navbar (`.h-12` present in the CSS) are all being served, and the SSR sidecar is rendering the current bundle — so the deploy itself is healthy.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **Navbar height trimmed: 130 → 114px desktop, 81 → 69px mobile.** Logo `h-14` → `h-12`, row 1 `py-3` → `py-2.5`, row 2 `py-2` → `py-1.5`.
> - 🔑 **A STATIC height change here is safe; a scroll-dependent one is not.** The load-bearing rule is that the header's height must stay **constant while scrolling** (an in-flow height change feeds the scroll-anchoring loop documented above), not that the number itself is fixed. Trimming the padding is fine.
> - **The travel band follows for free**, because it is the header's *measured* `offsetHeight` rather than a constant — so the header now also hides after 114px instead of 130px, which compounds with the earlier "hide sooner" requests. Verified: it comes to rest at exactly `T=114` on desktop and `T=69` on mobile, with `T <= y` still holding.
> - ⚠️ **Found and fixed a coupled value:** `shop/product.tsx` had `scroll-mt-24` (96px) on the `#reviews` anchor, tuned to clear the sticky header because the post-delivery WhatsApp nudge (`SendReviewReminder`) **deep-links straight to `#reviews`**. At a 130px header it was already under-compensating by 34px, so the reviews heading landed partly behind the navbar. Now `scroll-mt-24 md:scroll-mt-32` — two values because the header is two rows on desktop and one on mobile, and a single number either hides the heading on desktop or leaves a large gap on mobile. **Grep for `scroll-mt` before changing this header's height.**
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **"من نحن" badge optically centred, and a reusable technique for it.** _Symptom (client):_ the label sat low in its teal pill. _Cause:_ symmetric padding centres the font's **em box**, not the ink, and **Thmanyah Sans reserves a large ascent for stacked diacritics** these labels never use, so the glyphs land low.
> - 🔑 **The measuring technique is the transferable part, because the obvious measurement lies.** My first pass used canvas `actualBoundingBoxAscent/Descent` positioned via the line box and reported the text as **0.8px off, i.e. already centred** — flatly contradicting what the client could see. The reliable method is to **render the label to a canvas at the browser's real baseline** (`paddingTop + halfLeading + fontBoundingBoxAscent`) **and take the alpha-weighted centroid of the pixel rows**. That is the *optical* centre. It reported **10.8px low**, and the plain ink bounding box reported 4.5px, so the bbox understates the real error by more than half. Use the centroid, not the bbox, and never trust arithmetic over rendered pixels here.
> - ⚠️ **The correction is direction-scoped, because the two locales genuinely differ.** Both render in Thmanyah Sans, but the ink distribution does not: Arabic `مــن نحن` sat **0.200em** low while English `Who we are` sat only **0.065em** low, since its ascenders (`W`, `h`) pull the mass upward. A single shared value would have over-shot English by ~7px and turned one client-reported bug into another. Implemented as `[--nudge:0.2em] ltr:[--nudge:0.065em]` with `pt-[calc(var(--pad)-var(--nudge))] pb-[calc(var(--pad)+var(--nudge))]`, so the pill's height is unchanged and only the text moves, and it scales with the `clamp()` font size for free. **Result: 10.8px → 0.3px (AR), 3.5px → 0.0px (EN).**
> - **Navbar thresholds retuned on client preference:** `REVEAL_AFTER` 240 → **180px**, `REVEAL_DELAY_MS` 1000 → **750ms**. Verified: 120px of upward scroll leaves it hidden, 240px total triggers it, computed `transition-delay` reads `0.75s` on the reveal and `0s` on the hide.
> - **Verified:** `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓, plus the badge screenshotted at both locales.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 — **Navbar reveal made deliberate: two notches of upward scroll plus a 1s delay** _(thresholds later retuned to 180px / 750ms, see above)_**.** Client wanted the header to stop reappearing on the slightest correction. Hiding is unchanged (still leaves on the first pixel); only coming back got harder.
> - **`REVEAL_AFTER = 240px` is an ACCUMULATOR, not a bigger `DIRECTION_DELTA`, and that distinction is the whole point.** A per-event threshold would work for a wheel (one notch ≈ 100-120px in Chrome, so 240 ≈ two notches) but would **break trackpads entirely**: they emit a stream of small deltas, so no single event would ever exceed 240px and the header could never come back at all. Summing *consecutive* upward movement treats a fast wheel flick and a slow two-finger drag identically. The total resets the moment the user scrolls down, so slow jitter can never creep up to the threshold.
> - **`REVEAL_DELAY_MS = 1000` is a CSS `transition-delay` on the reveal only**, never on the hide — a delayed hide would leave the header sitting over content the user has already scrolled past. Pleasant side effect: because the element has not moved when the delay is still running, flipping direction back down inside that second simply changes the target, so a brief scroll-up mid-gesture cannot flash the header.
> - **Escape hatch preserved:** scrolling to the very top still shows the header even without earning 240px, because inside the travel band the offset is `min(y, band)` and that reaches 0 on its own. Otherwise a user who scrolled up 200px and stopped would be stuck with no navigation.
> - **Verified by sampling** rather than by feel: one notch up leaves it at `top:-130` (hidden) even after 1.6s; the second notch gives `t+0ms −130 → t+500ms −130 → t+1200ms −41/0.68 → t+1800ms 0/1.00`, i.e. the delay elapses then it animates; and 120ms after scrolling down it is already at `−49/0.62`, confirming the hide has no delay. **28/28 E2E** ✓ (worth running — every storefront spec touches this header), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-09 — **About Us → "كيف بدأت رحلتنا" (our journey) built.** New `components/store/about-journey.tsx` mounted on `/pages/about`: four photo cards (البداية / التطوير / الجودة / اليوم), each a stage of the brand story, over the cream brand-knot watermark. Copy lives in `about.journey.steps` as an **array of objects** in both locales, so a fifth stage is a copy change rather than a code change (same pattern as `whoWeAre.paragraphs`).
> - 🔑 **Geometry was MEASURED off the 1440×800 composite, not eyeballed**, and it came out exact: `4 × 295px + 3 × 58px gaps + 2 × 43px margins = 1440`, i.e. **20.49% / 4.03% / 2.99%** summing to precisely 100%. Expressed as percentages so the designer's rhythm survives any width. Verified in the browser: cards render **295×634 at a 1440 viewport**, to the pixel, matching the composite's measured card box (y 129→763).
> - **Cards are `aspect-[295/634]` with `object-cover`.** The supplied plates are 295×531 — exactly card *width* but ~100px shorter than the card — so anything other than cover would leave a strip of bare card at the bottom.
> - **Two scrims, not one** (`from-black/45 via-black/10 to-black/65`). The copy sits at BOTH ends of the card, so a single overlay dark enough for the top headline would flatten the middle of the photograph.
> - **Grid, not flex**, collapsing to 2 columns below `sm` (four 20%-wide cards on a phone are unreadable). ⚠️ RTL row-major order was checked in a real browser and is correct: البداية top-right → التطوير top-left → الجودة bottom-right → اليوم bottom-left, so the chronology still reads right-to-left.
> - **The watermark is `LOGO 2`** — this resolves the open question from earlier the same day about where that asset belonged. It ships **pre-faded by the designer** (sampled: essentially all pixels are near-transparent cream), so it needs no opacity of its own. Positioned **physically top-left in both locales**, deliberately unlike the navbar's logical `end-0`: the crop is baked to bleed off that corner, so mirroring it for LTR would turn the cut edge inward and read as a clipping bug.
> - ✅ **All four plates now shipped** (the missing التطوير production-line photo arrived 2026-08-10). Copied in **byte-for-byte, not re-encoded**: a second lossy pass over an already-lossy WebP would save ~40 KB at the cost of visible quality, and 174 KB across four lazy-loaded cards is not worth optimising. ⚠️ **PHP stat-cache gotcha while verifying the copy:** `filesize()` returned the PRE-copy sizes for every file because the earlier `file_exists()`/`filesize()` calls had already populated the stat cache in the same process, so the output looked like nothing had been copied. `clearstatcache(true, $path)` (or an md5 comparison, which is the better check anyway) is needed after writing a file you already stat'd.
> - 🔴 **RTL BUG, mine, client-caught: the heading and card copy were aligned with `text-end`, which is inverted in BOTH locales at once.** These are logical properties resolved against the **reading direction**, so `end` means **left in Arabic and right in English** — the precise opposite of what a design wants for a heading. The fix is `text-start` (right in AR, left in EN). Worth internalising because the failure mode is deceptive: `text-end` *sounds* like "the far side", it reads as deliberate in code, and my own comment confidently asserted the wrong rationale. **The tell was in a screenshot I had already taken and reviewed** — the Arabic heading sat top-LEFT against a Figma that clearly has it top-RIGHT — so this is also a reminder that rendering a screenshot is not the same as checking it. Now verified by measurement rather than eye: AR gives `gapRight=0` (flush right) and EN `gapLeft=0` (flush left).
> - ⚠️ Hand-off note, third instance of the same pattern: the accompanying zip was again an **empty archive** (210 bytes, uncompressed size 0), which is why extracting it produced empty `about us/` and `ABOUT US EDITED/` folders. The real files arrived as loose files. Also unexplained and unused so far: `ABOUT US EDITED/…/about us section-{ar,EN}.{png,webp}` at **2560×900** — a much wider ratio than anything on this page, so probably a different section entirely; do not guess at it.
> - **Verified:** `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, and screenshotted at 1440 AR / 1440 EN / 390 AR.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-09 — **Storefront navbar hides on the first pixel of scroll instead of after its own height.** _Symptom (client):_ it took an unnaturally long scroll to hide; from the top, one 120px wheel notch did nothing at all. _Why the number was there:_ not a tuning choice. The header is `sticky`, so its box stays in normal flow and a transform does not remove it; translating it away while the viewport is still inside that box exposes the box as a blank strip (the 2026-08-08 bug). The previous fix pinned it open for its **measured height** (~130px desktop / 81px mobile), which is exactly what made hiding feel slow. _Fix:_ inside that band it now translates by exactly **`-scrollY`** instead of staying pinned. Its box sits at page `0..H` = viewport `-y..H-y`, and the header offset by `-y` lands on precisely the same rectangle, so it **covers its own box at every position** rather than revealing it. Visually that is just a normal header scrolling away, starting on the first pixel; past `H` the offset caps and the handoff to the hidden state is continuous.
> - 🔑 **1:1 is the geometric maximum safe speed.** Translating faster (say `-2y`) to hide sooner would put the header above its own box and the blank strip returns immediately. Hiding *earlier than the header's height* is impossible while anything reserves space at the top of the document — the only way is an overlay header with content running underneath, which was tried and rejected earlier.
> - 🔴 **When to animate took THREE attempts, so the final rule is derived from the constraint rather than guessed.** The whole component reduces to one invariant: **the header may never be translated further up than the page has scrolled** (`T <= y`). Its `sticky` box sits at page `0..H`, so at scroll `y` the visible part of that box is viewport `0..(H-y)`, and a translate of `T` covers `0..(H-T)`, leaving a blank strip exactly when `T > y`. From that, animating is **safe whenever the header moves further OUT of view**, because the destination is clamped to `min(y, band)` and `y` only grows while scrolling down, so the eased value trails the target which trails the scroll. It is unsafe **only** when the header must come back DOWN while already above the scroll position (the page scrolled up inside the band) — that one case snaps. Condition: `next < offset && offset > y`.
>   - **Attempt 1 (wrong):** compare the offset delta against the **scroll** delta. A fast flick has a big delta, so a reveal measured as "smaller than the scroll", was treated as tracking, and snapped in with no animation, getting worse the faster you scrolled.
>   - **Attempt 2 (wrong):** skip the transition for the whole tracking phase. That made every hide from the top a rigid 1:1 follow with no easing, which reads as the header vanishing instantly while animating correctly further down the page. Client caught it.
>   - **Verified by sampling every frame, checking `T <= y` as an assertion, not by feel.** Hide from the top now eases `T0 → T24 → T66 → T99 → T118 → T120` (previously it jumped straight to T120); hide from revealed eases `T0 → T39 → T91 → T120 → T130`; the small-upward-nudge case snaps `T100 → T60` in a single step, and `elementFromPoint` at the viewport top returns header content (`nav`, then the logo) rather than the page wrapper in every frame of every scenario. Hide duration is **260ms** against the reveal's 400ms, so it keeps up with the page instead of floating behind it.
> - 🔴 **The continuity test compares the offset against where tracking WOULD have put it last frame, NOT against the scroll delta.** The delta version (`|Δoffset| > |Δscroll| + 2`) was my first attempt and shipped briefly — it is subtly wrong: a fast flick has a large scroll delta, so a reveal (offset 130 → 0) measures as "smaller than the scroll" and is treated as continuous tracking, skipping the transition entirely. **Symptom: the header appears instantly, and it gets worse the faster you scroll up** — a slow scroll animates correctly, which is exactly what makes it easy to miss. Client caught it. Now verified by sampling the computed transform mid-flight rather than eyeballing: slow (-60) and fast (-600) flicks trace the identical curve, `-130 → -104 → -74 → -53 → -30 → -12 → -2 → 0` with opacity `0.00 → 0.20 → 0.43 → 0.59 → 0.77 → 0.91 → 0.99 → 1.00`.
> - **The fade is back, but only on the discontinuous moves.** During tracking opacity is pinned at 1: a header dissolving as you scroll reads as a glitch rather than as one leaving the page, and by the time the tracked offset reaches the cap the element is off-screen anyway.
> - ⚠️ **The offset is written straight to the DOM node in the rAF callback, not held in React state** — a per-pixel value in state would re-render the whole header every scroll frame. React still owns `scrolled` (the shadow), which flips rarely. The old `show` boolean and the opacity fade are gone; it is a pure slide now.
> - **Verified by measurement, not by eye** (every previous fix in this component looked right and was not). Downward tracking is exactly 1:1 — `scrollY=40/80/120` against `headerTop=-40/-80/-120`, i.e. **`lag=0px`** at every step. The safety probe, `elementFromPoint` at the very top of the viewport, never returns the page wrapper at any position (`y=40` → the logo, `y=80`/`y=120` → the nav row, `y=240` → real hero content), so there is no blank strip at any point. Confirmed at 1400px and 390px. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **28/28 E2E** ✓.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-09 — **About Us: the two sections merged into one, and the seam fix moved to the source image.** The banner and "من نحن" were two sections with two background photographs, and the join read as a visible break line (client-reported). That was previously papered over in CSS with a cross-fade: the lower image bled upwards 10vw and masked transparent→opaque so real pixels from both photos blended. The designer has now re-shot the lower half as a continuation of the same frame and supplied **one 1440×1600 image**, so `about-banner.tsx` + `about-who-we-are.tsx` collapse into a single **`about-hero.tsx`** and the cross-fade is **deleted rather than tuned** — there is no seam to hide because there is no join.
> - 🔑 **Verified which half actually changed before adopting it**, rather than assuming a full redraw: sampling both files, the new **top** 800px differs from the shipped banner by a mean of **2.30/255** (identical, just re-encode noise) while the **bottom** 800px differs by **24.09**. So the hero is untouched and only the ground was replaced. Worth doing on any "here's a new version" hand-off — it turns a guess about scope into a number.
> - **`object-cover` is now safe in a way it specifically was not before, and this is the whole architectural point.** The old lower section could not use it because its scaling had to agree with the aspect-locked banner above, and on mobile it disagreed (width-driven vs height-driven) which is exactly what the cross-fade was masking. With ONE section there is no second image to disagree with, so a crop can never produce a misaligned join — and unlike the old `<img>` at intrinsic aspect ratio, it can never leave the bottom of the section with no background (the mobile-only bug from 2026-08-08 that desktop hid).
> - **The crop direction differs by viewport and both directions are wanted.** Desktop (section 1457px at 1440 = 1.012× width) is *shorter* than the photo's 1:1.111 aspect, so it trims ground off the bottom. Mobile (574px at 390 = 1.471× width) is *taller*, so cover scales by HEIGHT and trims the **sides** — which zooms the composition and makes the dates box read properly at 390px instead of shrinking to quarter size. That incidentally answers the "banner is a desktop crop that renders only ~217px tall on a phone" note from 2026-08-08 without needing a separate mobile asset.
> - ⚠️ **Constraint REVERSED, and it is a trap for the next edit.** The old lower section could *not* be `overflow-hidden` or its upward bleed was clipped and the hard line came back. The merged section **must** be, because the banner's teal band is bled sideways by 100vw.
> - ⚠️ **Caught in my own code before building:** moving the headline column into normal flow (it was `absolute inset-y-0 left-0`) made it **direction-relative**, so it would have jumped to the RIGHT in Arabic and landed on the dates box baked into the right of the photo. It must stay absolutely positioned at `left-0` in both locales, with a `min-h-[55.56vw]` wrapper (800/1440 of the frame) reserving the space instead. Flow layout inside a flex row is *not* a drop-in replacement for physical positioning in an RTL app.
> - **Encoded from the PNG master, not the designer's WebP**, to avoid a second lossy generation: 292 KB at quality 84, against 286 KB for the two files it replaces, so one image at no extra payload. ⚠️ Hand-off note (a repeat of the favicon episode): the accompanying `.zip` was **210 bytes with uncompressed size 0** — an empty archive containing only a folder entry, which is why extracting it produced an empty directory. The real images arrived as standalone files alongside it.
> - **Verified in a real browser** at 1440 / 1920 / 390 in **both locales**: seam gone, no background gap, headline physically left in AR *and* EN while the card badge correctly follows the reading direction to the left in EN. `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client + SSR builds ✓. No PHP touched; no E2E spec references this page.
> - ⏭ Still open: `LOGO 2.png` (the knot watermark) does not appear in this composite, so it likely belongs to a later "our journey" section. English banner line 1 wraps to two lines where Arabic fits one (looks fine, but it is a deliberate difference, not a bug).
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-09 — **R2 is LIVE: production product images fixed. Plus three pre-domain hardening changes ported from Sky Amman.**
> - **R2 media cutover done.** Client attached a card, so the bucket **`retab-media`** was created in the **client-owned** Cloudflare account (deliberate: an R2 custom domain requires the zone in the *same* account, and that account is where DNS should eventually land). `php artisan media:push` copied **652 files / 162.3 MB** and verified all 163 `product_images` rows *plus their variants* resolve on the target. `MEDIA_DISK=r2` + the five `R2_*` vars are set on the **web service only** (the SSR sidecar never touches a disk — image URLs reach it as finished absolute strings in Inertia props). **All 87 products now render real photos instead of the 🌴 placeholder**, verified by curl: `200 image/webp`, 14,966 bytes for a `card` variant, our one-year immutable `Cache-Control` intact, served from the **Amman** edge, and 24 R2 URLs present in the production catalogue HTML. Zero DB changes, because prod's paths were already byte-identical to local. **This also stops the underlying bug**, which was not a one-off: with `MEDIA_DISK` unset, every admin upload was landing on the container FS and being destroyed by the next deploy.
> - ⚠️ **Launched on the `r2.dev` public URL, and that must NOT stay.** ✅ _Resolved 2026-08-15 — see that day's entry; the blocker below dissolved once DNS moved to Cloudflare on 2026-08-10._ A custom domain was impossible **at the time of writing** (`retab.com.sa` was on Bluvalt, not Cloudflare). Cloudflare's warning lists three limits and they matter very unequally: the rate limit is a non-issue at this volume, the missing WAF is irrelevant for public product photos, but 🔑 **there is no CDN caching**, so every request goes to the bucket origin and a KSA shopper pays a transcontinental round trip *per image*, which directly undercuts the whole point of the WebP variant pipeline. **Confirmed empirically, not just from the docs: the response carries no `CF-Cache-Status` header at all.** Swap is one env var (`R2_URL`), no migration, then disable the r2.dev URL. Full state in memory `retab-r2-media`.
> - **`trustProxies` → `at: '*'`** (was a Cloudflare CIDR allowlist). The production domain is a **direct Railway CNAME with no Cloudflare in front**, so the allowlist matches nothing and Laravel ignores every `X-Forwarded-*` header. **Higher stakes here than in Sky Amman:** every `throttle:` bucket keys on client IP, so all visitors would share one identity and the limits would collapse into a single global bucket (the same class of failure as the 2026-08-06 revenue bug). `'*'` is safe *specifically because* the container is only reachable through Railway's edge. ⚠️ **Honest status: this is a precaution, not a reproduced bug.** Retab's old list did include the RFC 1918 ranges, so it may have been working; two external probes (session-cookie `Secure` flag, spoofed `X-Forwarded-Host`) were both **inconclusive** — Laravel treats a null `session.secure` as false regardless of the request, and Railway's edge appears to overwrite forwarded-host. Verify after deploy by checking `product_requests.ip` shows varied client IPs rather than one repeated address.
> - **`URL::forceRootUrl(config('app.url'))`** added to the production branch (we had `forceScheme` only). Laravel builds `url()`/`route()` from the **request host**, so the app stays reachable on the Railway hostname and would serve canonical tags, `og:url`, robots' `Sitemap:` line and a sitemap of all 87 products **pointing at itself** — a self-canonicalising duplicate store, which is especially damaging mid-migration off Zid. Pinned by `SeoTest::test_production_pins_generated_urls_to_the_canonical_host`, which exercises the provider's real production branch (`$this->app['env'] = 'production'` then re-`boot()`).
> - **`SITE_INDEXABLE` noindex switch** (`config/retab.php`, default **true**) so the store is reachable for Moyasar's review while staying out of Google. 🔑 **The subtle part: noindex mode deliberately keeps the site CRAWLABLE — there is no `Disallow: /`.** robots.txt governs *fetching*, noindex governs *indexing*, and a crawler blocked from fetching can never read the noindex, so a linked URL can still be listed as a bare result and is then slow to remove. Letting the crawler in to **read** the `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` is what actually works; only the `Sitemap:` line is dropped. Sent as a header (in `SecurityHeaders`) not a meta tag, so it covers the sitemap and other non-React responses with no frontend change. Defaults to **true** on purpose: a forgotten var that deindexes the *live* store is a worse failure than one that indexes a staging URL. 🔴 **Flip it back at launch.**
> - ⚠️ **Found while probing: `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` is unset in production**, so session + XSRF cookies ship **without the `Secure` flag**. `config/session.php` reads it with no default and Laravel treats null as `false`; it does **not** infer it from the request being https. Documented in `.env.example`; needs setting on Railway. Also aligned `.env.example`'s `APP_NAME` to `Retab Stores` (prod was already correct).
> - **Verified:** **381 tests / 1765 assertions** (+4), Pint ✓. No JS touched.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-08 — **Designed About Us page started (Figma): hero banner + "من نحن".** New `AboutController` → `resources/js/pages/shop/about.tsx` with `components/store/about-banner.tsx` and `about-who-we-are.tsx`. **Registered at `/pages/about`, the URL the nav, footer and sitemap already use**, declared *before* the `/pages/{slug}` CMS catch-all — the same trick `/pages/branches` uses — so nothing needed redirecting. ⚠️ **That shadows the `about` content_pages row**, but the row held only scaffold placeholder text ("Edit this content from the admin panel"), so no real copy was displaced; the row still exists if the prose should later become client-editable.
> - 🔑 **The two sections use deliberately different height models, and the reason generalises.** The banner is **aspect-locked** (`<img>` + absolute overlay, like the homepage hero) because its composition *is* the artwork. The who-we-are section is **content-driven** (background `object-cover`, height follows the text) because its artwork is only a sand texture: aspect-locking it would leave a 217px band on a 390px phone with nowhere for the copy. **Lock to the artwork only when the artwork is the content.**
> - **Figma numbers that are load-bearing, not decoration:** the banner's text column is **50.5%** (the design's 727/1440 teal rectangle) because the dates box starts at ~52% of the photo — a first pass at 56% dropped the Arabic headline straight onto it. Verified by measurement rather than eye: band edge 50.5% and band height 16.4% at 1440, holding at 1920. Type and padding are in `vw` not `rem` throughout, because an aspect-locked photo's rendered height scales with viewport *width*, so a fixed unit drifts from the design as the screen grows.
> - **Assets:** the designer's `about us - banner.svg` (3.2 MB) is the whole composition with the photo embedded as base64 and **zero `<text>` elements** — reference only, never shipped, so the copy stays live HTML and translatable. `Rectangle 22/24.svg` were just `#1B4E53` (already the `brand-teal` token) and `#FFF8F8` at 11% radius 66, i.e. CSS rather than assets. Photos converted to WebP: banner 2021 KB → **175 KB**, texture 1567 KB → **111 KB**. ⚠️ Figma does not export *effects* to SVG, so the card's frosted look had to be reproduced with `backdrop-blur`.
> - **Direction handling differs per element, on purpose:** the banner's text column is anchored **physically left** in both locales (the dates box is baked into the right of the photo, so mirroring would collide), while the who-we-are badge uses **logical** `ps-`/`text-start` and correctly moves to the left in English. Both confirmed in a real browser at AR and EN.
> - ⚠️ **Three client-reported fixes worth generalising.** (1) **The banner carries NO scrim.** The homepage hero's white gradient was copied over reflexively; here the headline is dark teal on bright sand and already reads, so the wash only drained the photo's colour. Check the actual photo before adding a contrast layer. (2) **The seam between the two sections is CROSS-FADED, because the two files are different photographs and can never butt cleanly.** Diagnosed by measuring, not by eye: the average colour at the joining edges differs by **22/29/10/23 across four horizontal bands** — an *uneven* gap, which is the signature of different source images (a scale mismatch misaligns content while tone stays continuous; both render 1:1 at 1440). Fix: bleed the lower image **upwards** over the banner by 10vw and mask it `transparent → opaque`, so real pixels from both photos blend instead of two mismatched edges meeting. Requires dropping `overflow-hidden` from the section or the bleed is clipped and the line returns. (3) 🔴 **`top` + `bottom` does NOT stretch an `<img>`.** A replaced element with `height:auto` resolves its height from the **intrinsic aspect ratio**, so the masked background rendered 217px tall inside a 357px section and left the bottom with no background at all. **Desktop hid the bug entirely** (the auto height happened to exceed the section there) and it only appeared on mobile — state the height explicitly, `h-[calc(100%+<bleed>)]`.
> - ⏭ **Still open:** `LOGO 2.png` (the knot watermark) does not appear in this section's composite, so it was left out rather than guessed at — it likely belongs to `about-us-our-journey`. The banner is a desktop crop that renders only ~217px tall at 390px, so a mobile art-direction variant is worth requesting. English banner line 1 wraps to two lines where Arabic fits one.

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-08 — **Storefront navbar: one scroll down now hides it outright, and the two-step turned out to be a real bug, not a style choice.** _Symptom (client-reported):_ scrolling down once from the top made the header **shrink** to its compact state, and only a second scroll actually hid it. _First attempt was wrong:_ the two thresholds did disagree (compact entered at `y>48`, hiding only engaged past `y>80`, leaving a 48–80px window where the compact header was still on screen), but narrowing that window **did not fix it** — a measured wheel notch from `y=0` still ended at `y=80`, compact and visible. _Real root cause:_ collapsing the header shrank its own height by ~40px, and because it is **`sticky`, i.e. still in normal flow**, that shortened the whole page; the browser's **scroll anchoring** compensated by *reducing* `scrollY` by roughly the same amount, and the direction detector read that synthetic jump as a genuine scroll **UP** and re-revealed the header. So an in-flow height change simply **cannot coexist** with direction-based hide/reveal. _Fix:_ the header height is now **constant** — the padding collapse is gone and `scrolled` drives only the (layout-neutral) drop shadow, which also means its hysteresis pair is no longer needed. Behaviour is now two states: pinned open within 24px of the top, hidden by one downward scroll, revealed full by scrolling up. ⚠️ **Anything that reintroduces an in-flow height change here** (a collapse, an announcement bar) brings the artifact straight back; animate colour/shadow/opacity instead. **Unexpected bonus:** scroll events per 300px gesture dropped from **15 to 1** — the anchoring loop had been generating ~14 spurious events per scroll, so this was a real jank source, not just a visual nit.
> - 🔴 **Follow-up the same day — the hide exposed a blank strip, and the constant height made it worse.** _Symptom (client-reported):_ a white band above the hero on scroll down. _Cause:_ `sticky` keeps the header's box in **normal flow**, and a transform does not remove that box, so translating it away while the viewport is still inside the box just reveals the box. Proven with `elementFromPoint(x, 5)`: at `y=60` the topmost element was the page wrapper, not the hero; from `y=140` it was hero content. Pinning to a fixed 130px height had made the strip **130px instead of the old compact 90px**, so the previous change amplified a pre-existing flaw. _Fix:_ the pinned band is now the header's **measured `offsetHeight`** (cached, re-measured on resize — reading it per scroll frame would force a layout flush), so the header only hides once hiding reveals real content. This adapts across breakpoints for free: desktop ~130px (two rows) vs mobile ~81px (row 2 is `md:flex`), where a fixed number would either leave a desktop gap or over-pin on mobile. ⚠️ **Consequence to accept:** on desktop one 120px wheel notch no longer hides it (120 < 130) — the header cannot hide sooner without exposing its own box. The alternative is running the hero *underneath* an overlaid header, which was tried and rejected in the abandoned slide-in experiment. Verified gap-free at 1400px **and** 390px, plus a screenshot of the hero flush to the top edge. _Verified by measuring `scrollY` / header `top` / height / opacity at each wheel notch (not by eyeballing):_ `y=0` FULL → one notch → HIDDEN → scroll up → FULL, height constant at 130px throughout, plus **28/28 E2E green**, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓. Nothing in the PHP or E2E suites referenced the compact state.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-08 — **Staff can change their own password, and every password field now has a show/hide toggle.** Two small features, three decisions worth recording (full writeup under **Architecture → Admin UI conventions**).
> - 🔑 **The Staff page alone would have shipped this broken for half the audience:** `/admin/users` sits behind `admin` middleware, so **editors can never open it**, and the admin layout had no user menu at all (just a logout button). So the shared form is mounted **twice** — inline on your own Staff row, and behind a **key icon in the top bar** that every staff member can reach.
> - **Reused the existing `password.update` route** instead of adding an admin endpoint, so a password still only changes in one place. It already validated `current_password` + `Password::defaults()` + `confirmed` and returned `back()`, which lands each caller where it submitted. Added a **success flash** (the admin toast layer reads `flash.success`; without it the admin form would succeed silently) and **`throttle:10,1,password-update`** — a `current_password` rule is a credential-guessing oracle for anyone with a stolen session. ⚠️ Note the **bucket prefix**: a bare `throttle:10,1` would rejoin the shared counter (the revenue bug from 2026-08-06). Two regression tests pin the limit and its isolation from the login bucket.
> - ⚠️ **The new `PasswordInput` had to stay uncontrolled-capable.** The login form deliberately uses Inertia's uncontrolled `<Form>` so password-manager autofill can't desync the submitted credentials; a controlled-only toggle component would have quietly reintroduced that "first login attempt fails on a fresh browser" bug. It owns only the `type` attribute. Its labels are **props** rather than translated internally, because a plain `useTranslation()` inside the admin resolves against the storefront i18n instance.
> - Also fixed while verifying: nothing, but two false alarms worth noting so they are not re-chased — the dev admin login "failing" was my check reading `page.url()` before Inertia's navigation settled, and `[role=dialog]` matching twice is the always-mounted "How it works" drawer, not a bug in the modal.
> - **Verified:** **377 tests / 1751 assertions** (+3), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, and the whole thing driven in a real browser: the login toggle flips `password`→`text`→`password` with the Arabic aria-label and the value preserved without submitting the form, the toggle renders at the **inline-end (left) under RTL**, the Staff section appears only on your own row, the top-bar modal opens with three fields, the save button gates on dirty, and a wrong current password returns the inline "The password is incorrect."

> **Last updated:** 2026-08-08 — **Designer's favicons + OG share card wired in, and it exposed a launch blocker.** The site had **no favicon at all** (`public/favicon.ico` was the scaffold's 0-byte placeholder, with zero icon links in the layout) and **no default share card**. Both fixed; full writeup under **Architecture → SEO**. The finding that matters most: 🔴 **Inertia `<Head>` tags are invisible to WhatsApp and Facebook unless the SSR sidecar is running** — they are applied client-side after hydration and those crawlers do not run JavaScript, so every `og:*` tag on the site previews blank without SSR. Proven by curl, off and on. That makes the SSR sidecar a **hard requirement for the store's main marketing channel**, not a performance nicety. Second gotcha, which cost real debugging time: a **sidecar left running keeps serving the OLD bundle after a rebuild**, and a second one fails with `EADDRINUSE` instead of taking over, so the stale process silently wins and a correct code change looks broken. **Deploys must restart the SSR service too.** The hand-off itself had two defects worth remembering: every icon was exported **one pixel oversized** (Figma fractional-frame rounding), and two filenames used a **non-breaking space**, which is what made Windows Explorer skip them and look like a corrupt zip. Minor cleanup noted: **`APP_NAME` is `Laravel` in the local `.env` and in `.env.example`** — cosmetic in dev, but worth aligning the template. **Production is already correct (`Retab Stores`)**, set as a Railway variable on both the web and SSR services. ⚠️ Worth knowing if it is ever changed on a live site: `APP_NAME` feeds `config/session.php`'s cookie name via `Str::slug`, so editing it invalidates every active session. Verified: **374 tests / 1746 assertions**, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Prettier ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, and all icon/manifest routes serving 200 with correct MIME types.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-08 — **`media:push` built: the fix for production's missing product images.** Prod was running with `MEDIA_DISK` unset, so every upload went to the container filesystem and was destroyed by the next deploy; the DB kept all 163 `product_images` rows, so all 87 products served the 🌴 placeholder. Diffing prod's paths against local proved all **163 are byte-identical** (same import on both sides), which reduces the fix from a re-import to a **pure file copy with zero DB changes** — worth checking before assuming a migration is needed. New `php artisan media:push` streams the 652 local files (163 originals + 489 variants, 162 MB) to R2 via the already-installed flysystem S3 adapter, preserving keys, and verifies at the end that every image **and its variants** resolve on the target. Full writeup + the R2 public-access constraint under **Architecture → File Storage**. Two gotchas banked: `Storage::fake()` leaves `config()` pointing at the real disk settings (so config-inspecting guards misfire in tests — replaced with a live write probe, which is a better guard anyway since a *wrong* key passes a blank-check), and **each `expectsOutputToContain()` matches a separate ordered write call**, so two substrings from the same output line can never both pass. Verified: **374 tests / 1746 assertions** (+6), Pint clean repo-wide. _Still needs the client: an R2 bucket + API token before the upload can run._
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-06 — **Cart page rebuilt — and it uncovered a live revenue bug.**
> - 🔴 **The bug first: every `throttle:` route shared ONE rate-limit bucket per visitor.** A shopper who added ~11 items to their cart (limit 60, allowed) then clicked checkout (limit 10) got a **429 and could not pay**. Cause: Laravel's unnamed throttle keys on `sha1(domain|IP)` — the route URI is not in the key — so the strictest route rejected on the visitor's *combined* request count. Fixed by giving every throttled route a prefix (`throttle:60,1,cart`, `throttle:10,1,checkout`, …) across `routes/web.php` **and** `routes/auth.php`; two regression tests pin the isolation. Full symptom→cause→fix under **Security hardening**. Found because new cart E2E specs pushed the shared counter over and the checkout spec started 429-ing — the browser gate earning its keep again.
> - **Cart page:** real product images (the 🌴 was a *backend* gap — `CartService::summary()` never shipped image data; now serves the `card` WebP variant, eager-loading `items.product.images` so it isn't a query per line), a **`− n +` stepper** reusing the product page's pill (the old number input PATCHed **once per keystroke** — typing "10" sent 1 then 10; now click-only with an in-flight guard), and a real **money breakdown** (subtotal / discount / shipping / total) replacing the vague "shipping added at checkout".
> - **Empty state:** icon + headline + browse/offers buttons (Offers auto-hides via the existing `hasOffers` prop) **plus a best-sellers row**, so an empty cart isn't a dead end. Required extracting `ShopController`'s private `card()` + `bestSellers()` into **`App\Support\ProductCards`** — one definition, two consumers.
> - **Coupons on the cart** (client's pick over checkout-only). The risk was a cart promising a discount checkout then refuses, so `previewCoupon()` and the real `resolveCoupon()` now **share one `assertCouponUsable()`** — they can only ever agree. Preview takes no row lock (checkout still validates under lock, so a coupon exhausted in between is caught there); the applied code lives in the session, pre-fills checkout, is **re-validated on every cart render** (dropped + explained inline if it stops qualifying), and is cleared once an order is placed. Free-shipping coupons waive the fee rather than discounting goods, mirroring `placeOrder`.
> - ⚠️ **Also fixed a customer-facing localization bug:** the coupon errors were hard-coded **English literals** flashed to Arabic shoppers. Now `__('messages.checkout.coupon_*')` in both locales.
> - ⚠️ **Two test gotchas worth remembering:** PHP serializes whole floats as JSON **ints** (`100.0` → `100`) and Inertia's `where()` is strict, so money assertions must use ints; and the Arabic currency symbol **`ر.س` contains periods**, so stripping non-digits in a Playwright assertion yields `"11.50.."` → `NaN` (match the first number instead).
> - **Verified:** **368 tests / 1730 assertions** (+2 suites), `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, Pint ✓, **28/28 E2E** (5 new cart specs), and both cart states screenshot-checked at desktop + mobile.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-03 — **Repo-wide format pass + formatting is now actually enforced.** Ran Pint (67 files) and Prettier (92 files), then closed the hole that let them drift. ⚠️ **Root cause worth remembering:** `lint.yml` ran Pint and Prettier in **WRITE mode** with the `git-auto-commit` step **commented out** — so CI dutifully reformatted its own checkout, threw the result away, and reported green forever. Formatting was enforced by nobody. Worse, its trigger list named **`develop`, a branch that has never existed here**, so the workflow only ever fired on `main` at all. Both fixed: the job now runs **`pint --test` + `format:check` + `lint:check`** (read-only, fails on drift), triggers match ci.yml (`main`/`construction_phase`/`deployment_phase`), `permissions` dropped to `contents: read` (nothing writes back now), PHP aligned to **8.2** (was 8.4; composer requires ^8.2), and `npm install` → `npm ci`. Added a `lint:check` script (`eslint .` without `--fix`) so CI can't silently auto-fix. **This is why the customer-email diff needed 63 files reverted by hand** — any formatter run drags the whole repo in. _Verified:_ **358 tests / 1592 assertions**, `tsc` ✓, eslint ✓, 21 JS ✓, client+SSR builds ✓, **24/24 E2E** (Prettier touched every page component, so the browser gate mattered here), and all three check commands re-run exactly as CI invokes them → clean. _Flagged, not changed:_ **`tests.yml` is a starter-kit leftover that duplicates ci.yml's PHP tests** (PHP 8.4, xdebug coverage, raw `phpunit`) — on a `main` push the suite runs twice. Delete it or fold it in when you want.
>
> **Last updated:** 2026-08-03 — **Customers finally get email.** The app sent zero customer mail until today (three staff notifications, nothing outward), which hurt most on **bank transfer**: the IBAN and the order number the customer must quote as the transfer reference lived only on the confirmation page. Built three queued Mailables — receipt / confirmed / shipped — behind `App\Services\CustomerMailer`, hooked in beside the existing WhatsApp calls. Full writeup in the new **Architecture → Customer-facing email**; the parts worth knowing up front:
> - 🔑 **New `orders.locale` column**, snapshotted at checkout. These are queued, so `app()->getLocale()` at render time is the *worker's* locale (AR) — without the snapshot an English shopper gets an Arabic receipt, and guests have no user record to read a preference from. `OrderMail` pins `$this->locale` from it; Laravel's `withLocale` wrapper covers the subject line too.
> - **The receipt is split by payment method on purpose:** bank transfer sends at checkout, **card/Tamara send on payment confirmation** — abandoning a hosted gateway page must not produce a receipt for an unpaid order. ⚠️ That exposed an asymmetry: `PaymentService::markOrderPaid` already early-returns when paid, but **`TamaraService::markAuthorized` had no guard** and re-runs on every repeated webhook, so it now captures the first `pending_payment → authorized` transition explicitly.
> - **Templates are hand-rolled** (table layout, inline styles, `dir` on `<html>` *and* the outer table) rather than Laravel's markdown theme, because an Arabic-first store has to flip the whole document and the published theme bakes in LTR. IBAN/tracking numbers are `unicode-bidi:embed` so RTL can't scramble them.
> - ⚠️ **Test gotcha:** asserting a subject via `envelope()` directly bypasses `withLocale` and makes a correct subject look broken — use `assertHasSubject()`. Cost one red test before the cause was clear.
> - ⚠️ **Pint note (not a code change):** running `./vendor/bin/pint` reformats **65 files nobody touched** — the installed Pint 1.29.3 preset (`concat_space`, `fully_qualified_strict_types`, …) disagrees with how the repo was formatted. CI never catches it because `lint.yml` runs Pint in **fix** mode with the auto-commit step commented out, so formatting is effectively unenforced. Those reformats were reverted here to keep the diff reviewable; **a repo-wide Pint pass is its own commit** if you want it.
> - **Verified:** **358 tests / 1592 assertions** (+10), `tsc` ✓, **24/24 E2E green** (the guest bank-transfer spec now runs through `CustomerMailer`), migration applied to dev MariaDB, and all six template variants rendered and inspected (AR→rtl, EN→ltr, bank block only on unpaid transfers, tracking only on shipped).
